# F1–F86, triaged

`FINDINGS.md` is a log: eighty-nine entries in the order they were hit, each accurate about
what it found. Past thirty, *filed* stops meaning much — a reader cannot tell which entries
are one change and which are forty, or which to do first.

This is the other view. Same findings, no new claims: **grouped by mechanism, and ranked by
how many independent surfaces reached for the same thing.**

**The ranking is consumer count, and that is the whole argument.** A gap two independent
surfaces reached for is a gap the next surface will reach for too, and it is the only
evidence available that a fix generalises. One consumer is a request; three is a design
error. Nothing else here is a judgement about importance.

**Two things override the count.** A group that changes a **public type** (⚠) sorts above
one that does not, because the freeze makes those expensive after publication and cheap
before it. And within the ⚠ groups the count used is **open** consumers, not total: a
consumer whose finding is closed is evidence the shape is real, but it no longer costs a
type change.

> **This file was last redone at F55.** Twenty-six findings landed under it — steps 8
> through 13 — and four of the thirteen groups below did not exist at that time. The
> divergence from `docs/ROADMAP.md`, which was drawn from the F55 version, is named
> entry by entry at the end.

---

## 1 · The consumer cannot reach a fact the framework holds — **closed** · 27 closed · 2 with no verdict ⚠

**F999** — **the type a consumer had to index for, and the sibling twelve lines above it.** F505 asked for `Camera` on the published surface. Checking its premise first: the alias it cites had had **no consumer since F509** removed the orbit and the function that read it, so the finding's own argument — *the consumer that would justify it is the workaround above* — had expired, and exporting on its strength would have added an export nothing consumes. **And `Camera` was not the worst instance**: twelve lines above sat `export type PlotForm = Plot["form"]`, the framework's own type re-declared in an app file under the framework's own name, with 15 call sites. The population that finds the right ones is not *unpublished types* — 50 of those, most correctly indexed — but **the consumers' index expressions**, four across both example apps, two instances and two controls. Both exported, C24 I34 and T2.21, and the row's residue arm watches the consumers where the resolution arm watches the surface. **Two defects in the resolver, found by its own fabricated violation**: the first capitalised token answers `Partial` for the finding's own instance, and a line-start anchor misses every one-line declaration. **Closed**

**F509** — a live part cannot delta-time itself: `ProducerContext` carries `width`, `height`, `capabilities` and `measure` and **no clock**, while the driver that builds it holds `deps.clock()` and computes `now - part.startedAt` forty lines away for the elapsed counter. So the reference app turns its 3D figures on a **tick counter** — `phase += 1` per refresh, `azimuth + phase * 0.35` — and the tick period is `every` + fetch + render, which makes the angular rate a function of how expensive the frame is: **1.71 rad/s on most rungs, 0.36 on the bunny, against the framework's own 0.52**. C22 I74 rules on exactly this one layer down and states the defect in the same words — *a step per wake turns the plot 25% fast … its speed decided by something it has nothing to do with*. **The remedy is not the missing field**: the framework's orbit is correct, delta-timed and bound to `o`, and the app's captions name none of the six controls that ship · **Closed** (F991) — commit `f13c15da` — the tick drives the data and the caption names the keys

**F505** — the published surface exports `Plot` and not `Camera`, so a consumer typing anything over a camera has to index the block: `NonNullable<Plot["camera"]>`. It compiles and it misnames the thing — the type is about a **view**, which C04 I75 is at pains to keep off the block. Invisible from inside the package, where nine files import `Camera` from `data/viewmodel` directly; found by writing the consumer, which is the only instrument that reaches it · **Closed** (F999) — `Camera` **and** `PlotForm` exported, C24 I34 and T2.21; the alias F505 cited had had no consumer since F509, so its own argument for the export had expired

**F421** — *`a=T` replaces at an id* is asserted in four files, all ours, and measured in none; the probe that could answer it gives every case a digest-derived id, so no two transmissions it has sent ever shared one. Now instrumented — a pair at `i=911`, appended so `probe.py` picks it up unedited. Calcium's own id is `imageId(imageKey(block))` where `imageKey` is the **picture's** digest (C04 I74). Two frames of one animated plot are one block at one position and two ids, so the whole placeholder grid is rewritten with them — the id travels in each cell's foreground colour. Measured at 40×10: **13 996 B a frame, 89% of it placeholders**, against **1 596 B** if the id were stable, and dearer than the braille dither at 8 974 B. `#sentImages` is never cleared, so a hundred frames leave a hundred images resident. **Not a patch** — the digest identity is right for its own reason, and what an animation wants is a *placement* identity that is the block's with the digest kept as the transmission's cache key, which is F413's split one field over. **A seam we could open, not a protocol refusal** — `transmit(id, …)` takes the id as a parameter and it is derived at four call sites, three in L4 and one in L1. **Open, and it decides the question it was asked**: no live image arm until C09 §4c rules the placement identity · **Closed by F1036** — the reason it stayed unmeasured, *which picture is displayed needs a human in front of the terminal*, is the claim that was wrong: it is a pixel count. In kitty 0.41.1 under Xvfb, `a=T` at a live id replaces and the screen follows **with no cell rewritten** — 11 224 red to 11 224 green — an unaddressed transmission is accepted, draws nothing and stays resident, `v=1` loops six turns, and `a=T` discards the frames the `a=f`s uploaded. Calcium's half had already closed under F987; this is a standing e2e row rather than a one-off

**Closed** (F987) — C09 I66: the placement's identity is the block's within its entry and the picture is what is cached at it, one derivation in `placementIdOf`. The `a=T` reading stays unmeasured and is stated as such; the probe's pair at `i=911` is the instrument.

**F987** — **the placement identity was the picture's, so a frame of an animation rewrote all 400 placeholder cells.** Two identities, both right, one member doing both jobs: `imageKey` is the picture's and is the right key for *has this been sent*, and it is the wrong key for *where does this go*, because the id it hashes into is written into every placeholder cell's foreground colour. Measured at 40×10 on two frames differing by one in the red channel: 1 367 B of transmission and **12 809 B of placement**, 14 176 B a frame, and a digest left in the sent set per frame. Both walk artefacts, because the component has state and structure — the table is where the **scope** came from (a block id is unique within a document and not across the transcript, so `img-${file}` in two entries collides) and no sequence could have reached it. **Closed** (C09 I66, C04 §3g.2) — the code and the after figures owed at the code commit.

**Still the largest open group, and steps 9–13 changed what kind of group it is.** At F55
every instance was an app *duplicating* a Calcium module. It is now three shapes.

| | the fact | the consumer | the workaround |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F413** | **a picture the terminal can decode and we cannot** | any reader on a kitty terminal | F410's box gated the **block**, and only one arm of three needs pixels: `imageCells` wants the aspect, the rasterisers want RGBA, and the transmission wants neither — the identity is a byte digest and the payload is the bytes unchanged. `decodePng` reads the IHDR **before** it refuses, so the extent survives Adam7 and 16-bit intact. **Fixed** — `extentOf` for geometry, `pixelsOf` for rasterising, and the refusal on the arms that cannot run · **Closed** (F991) — commit `14f4e434` — `extentOf` and `pixelsOf` |
| **F410** | **why a PNG did not decode** | any reader of an image block | `decodePng` names each refusal — *interlaced PNG (Adam7)*, *bit depth 16*, *IDAT does not inflate* — and `pixelsOf` collapses all of them to `null`, so a corrupt file and a deliberately-unbuilt format draw the same `alt`. **Fixed** — the fault goes through the `status` box whose `error` gloss was widened this arc to cover exactly it, with `alt` kept beneath as the caption · **Closed** (F991) — `image/codec.ts` names its faults and `image.ts` draws the status box over `alt` |
| **F406** | **the failure box — a ladder with no rung for a framed box, and twelve sites that never used the kind** | every app: `errorDoc`'s twelve call sites, and every live part that declines to override | a `notice`, and a `status` at a rung that looks like one. **Reported by a reader with two screenshots** — the containment box beside a live part's failure, which is a red line of text and indistinguishable from what it replaced. C09's height ladder **couples the tag to the border**, so a box inside `b.live`'s panel could read as a line (h2), draw a second border with no tag (h3), or draw the tag at two nested borders (h4). C23 I51 picked the first with the reason recorded, and the reason is right about both alternatives — **a choice between two wrong answers is evidence a third is missing**. `framed` is a second ladder on the same axis: no border, the rows spent on tag and content. And `errorDoc` built `notice` for spawn, handoff, transport, pipeline and refusals, because `error`'s **gloss** — *the definition's renderer threw. A bug.* — was narrower than the kind its own table describes. **Fixed** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `fc6c8424` — `framed?: boolean`, C23 I51, T1.40b |

The casts stay visible in the tree as the fabricated violation. **Closed** — C04 I119 and C24 I35. The stated blocker resolved at HEAD before anything was built: all three questions were already answered at their own sites, each citing F1, and nobody had carried them back. `Block` is `BlockKinds[keyof BlockKinds] & Gap & Floor` over a closed `KnownBlockKinds`, and `faulty.ts` compiles with **no** casts — it carried three, not the two counted here. `AnyBlockDefinition` is the half this row did not see: five `as unknown as BlockDefinition` casts leave `src/` and one arrives, at the map keyed by kind. Three things the build found that the walk did not — the variance runs the other way, every declared kind carries `Gap & Floor`, and MG27/MG28 went vacuous because they read the union textually, which their fabricated violation is what caught. Both consumers are in `test/` on purpose: an app never re-checks `src/`, measured — the example compiles clean against a reverted table while this repository's build fails |
| **F402** | **eight `b.plot` members, four of them a form's only datum** | both examples, and every future one | four forms — `gantt`, `waterfall`, `pairplot`, `smallmultiples` — unbuildable through the published builder, and three more reduced to one variant. **Seven of the eight exemptions carried one sentence**: *step 0 scaffolding — no plot-builder shorthand yet, and **no plan owes one***. F335 owed it, the roadmap cited it, and the reason had been **reworded once** without being re-checked. `emptyMessage`'s was worse — *no surface has an empty plot* is a claim about the surfaces that existed, offered as a reason a member cannot be set, while the renderer read `block.emptyMessage ?? "No data."` at two sites throughout. **Fixed** — all eight declared; `refusals()` empty; `/all` reads 46 · 46 · 60 · **106**. `FigureBuilder.setFacets` stops differing. The row that asserted the refusals is **inverted rather than deleted**, since an empty set would have passed over nothing. **Fixed** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `6c61593d` — all eight declared, `refusals()` measured from the table |

MG27 could not see it: it asks *a field no builder can set* and is keyed `Kind.field`, so a kind with **no** builder has no member rows to be missing, and seven `status.*` reasons described a builder that did not exist. **Fixed** — `b.status(err, retryInMs, attempt)`, the override's own parameters in its own order; `state` and `height` derived, `elapsedMs` and `spinner` still the framework's. The two framework literals now call it, so the default and the override cannot drift. **Fixed** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `9254c293` — `status` is in the builder export list |

| **F85** | who owns a `RenderContext` field | every caller of `render-lines.ts` | supplies **two fields the registry overwrites**, one a stub that **throws if called** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `bea5fcd1` — `RenderContextInput` omits the four seam members |

**F77 is what makes the group structural rather than incidental.** Every earlier entry says
*a producer is missing a fact*. F77 says **asking anything at all forces a route change**,
and the route costs the whole of C07: `meta`, the failure arm, the invocation record, the
spawn. Three different needs have now reached for `local` — the dashboard's ring, the events
window's, and `ctx.ask` — and the escape hatch charges full price each time.

**F13 and F58b are one fact from two sides, and only one of them was visible.** `Adapter.adapt`
returns a `ViewDocument`, so the compiler demands ten `meta` fields; `authoritativeMeta` keeps
three. The local route hand-writes nine and means none of them. Measured: an adapter returning
`exitCode: 999` produces a document reading `0`.

**F85 is F58b reached by a second component, which is what says the shape generalises.** An
adapter must compute ten `meta` fields and the registry honours three; a caller must supply
eight `RenderContext` fields and the registry honours six. Both times the compiler demands a
value the consumer discards, so the only way to satisfy the type is to write something untrue
— a `?? 0` in one case, a throwing stub in the other. **The throwing stub is the more honest
and the more dangerous**: it is correct only because the overwrite is unconditional, and a
comment is the whole of the guarantee.

**The fix is not nine exports, and F58b's is a *narrower* type, not a wider one.** The adapter's
return wants a `meta` restricted to the three honoured keys, so that supplying `exitCode` fails
to compile rather than failing to matter. The rest is one contract question — what a producer is
handed — and it wants a ruling, which is why it is stated as a group rather than patched as nine.

**Its own counter-argument, recorded**: `AdapterContext.width` says *"Never a layout decision —
C11's"*, and handing a producer a height invites exactly that. The reply is that a **pushed
view's** producer is defined by the region and has no other bound.

**F1021** — **the detection record answered ten questions in four voices and printed them identically, and one of the five kinds it needed was a value that already existed twice.** F418's fourth instance had been named and instrumented rather than ruled; checking the part the entry calls closed found it had drifted — `identifyTerminal` is read by **four** capabilities now, not three, `keyboardProtocol` having joined under C02 I12 after the entry was written, and the tmux half closed under F432, so the entry's own sentence is no longer true of the tree. What was actually open is that a consumer cannot tell a guess from a statement. Ruled as `sources`, a third member naming five kinds for **what would falsify the answer** — `declared`, `stated`, `inferred`, `assumed`, `unreachable` — **produced by the rule that produces the value**, so a fourth list cannot drift into a component just repaired for having three. **The walk's best find was that the tidy summary is false**: *`declared` beats `stated` beats `inferred` beats `assumed`* does not hold, because `TERM=dumb` with `COLORTERM=truecolor` answers 1 — an `assumed` rung outranking a `stated` one — and it was stated as a non-claim before any code existed. **Ten mutations, four survivors, all one mechanism**: a *nothing carried the fact* arm flipped to `stated` is invisible because `assumed` is produced by four other fields and every set-shaped assertion stayed green — a set over sites is blind to redistribution. Repaired, re-run kills all four, and the control still survives. T5.8 puts the identity table to real terminals for the first time — kitty 0.41.1 and XTerm(398) under Xvfb, four for four · **Closed** — C02 I13 and commitment 16, rows T1.14/T2.9/T3.14/T5.8/T6.14/T6.15. Stated limits: `TERM=dumb` is a statement recorded as `assumed`, `unreachable` is `TMUX`-only, and `sources` has no diagnostics consumer yet — `construct.ts:583` is its only production caller

**F1022** — **`view` on a `local` tool is inert, and the arm's own comment was stale in both directions.** F23 and F129 are one gap, ruled here as a refusal at parse in C05 I20's shape, reading both declaration sites. Three measurements settled it and each disproved something the entries rested on. The `shellOnly` docblock carried *the `view` arm has been usable only on `local` tools*, **false in both halves**: `shellOnly` closed F108 so a spawned verb can express it now, and `isViewInvocation` is read on the `app` route only, so the local route never could. §13a's forcing argument for a declaration is **C23 I3, an app-route obligation** — `runLocal` appends once, after its handler returns, so a local verb may decide on seeing its own result, and `/profile` already does exactly that. And F23's ground — *`/dashboard` is local and S6/S7 will want views* — **has no file**: `DOCKER_TUI_SURFACES.md` S1 rules `/dashboard` explicitly *not* a pushed view and draws S6/S7 as transcript entries. **Zero manifests in the tree declare the pair** · **Closed** — C05 I20, commitment 17, §8a rows 16–18, T1.23 and T6.16. The refusal forecloses a surface nobody has drawn, and that reservation is written in C05 rather than watched by a gate; `LocalContext` has no view owner, so `/profile`'s shape stays framework-only

**F1023** — **a fold runs on a version, so an attempt that failed is not counted, and the argument for leaving it was a transient signal read as a cumulative one.** F137's hedge — that the error arm already says what a retry count would say — is measurably false: the error arm is **transient**, replaced by the next successful poll, and the caption is **cumulative**, so after a recovered stall the caption asserts *no attempt was missed* while nothing contradicts it. They are not two signals for one event. Ruled the third arm — `compute` becomes `(data, prev, attempts)`, which is `renderError`'s existing widening applied to its sibling member, and additive, so every fold in the tree compiles unchanged · **Closed** — C23 §3c, I47, commitment 40, T1.41b and T6.45b. **One mutation survived and indicted this finding's own prose**: T6.45b had claimed the parameter-versus-field mutation is observable and it is not, so the row and the test comment now describe that arm as a control rather than a discriminator. One line is owed in another component — `LiveSpec.derive.compute` still declares two parameters, so the third is delivered at run time and no app can name it

**F1026** — **the entry's defence was about the axis that needs a 298-column terminal, and the refusal has another that does not.** `placementRows` refuses on `cols > 297` **or** `rows > 297`, and *at 298+ cells a still was already the honest answer* is only about the first. `imageCells` clamps `cols` to the frame width, so that axis is reachable only above 297 columns — measured over 770 combinations, it occurs at widths 298, 400 and 600 and nowhere below. **`rows` is the block's declared height and is bounded by nothing under the registry's 2 000-row cap**, so a 16×400 GIF at `height: 400` is `{cols: 32, rows: 400}` at **80 columns**: a 32-cell-wide picture on an ordinary terminal, taking the same still. Read in colour, because stripped text reports it as a still either way — at `kitty`, one ink over 990 ms; at `imageProtocol: "none"`, the control on the same document, two. **And the seam the entry was refused on was never owed**: there is no chosen arm to carry back, because the arm is a pure function of `(block, width, capabilities)` and travels **down** as a question. The family already ships twice in the same two files, and `transmits()`'s own comment names the defect — *the alternative is a second `imageProtocol !== "kitty"` in `session.ts`* — which is exactly the line that was there. **The classification table found a cell no entry names**: the transmission seam gates on the capability too, so a refused placement still wrote **4 APC escapes, 317 B** against **zero** placeholder cells in the frame, and a still PNG writes 4 270 B the same way · **Closed**

**F1036** — **the reason this stayed unmeasured was the claim that was wrong, and it is a pixel count.** *Which picture is displayed needs a human in front of the terminal* held F421 open; in kitty 0.41.1 under Xvfb it is four measurements. `a=T` at a live id replaces and the screen follows **with no cell rewritten** — 11 224 red to 11 224 green; an unaddressed transmission is accepted, draws nothing and **stays resident**; `v=1` loops six turns; and `a=T` discards the frames the `a=f`s uploaded. Calcium's half of F421 had already closed under F987, so what was left was the protocol's, and it is now a standing e2e row rather than a one-off · **Closed** — **two instrument defects were caught by controls rather than by review**: a `latin1` write that read zero pixels *everywhere including the controls*, and a backgrounded tty reader stopped by `SIGTTIN`. A capture that reads nothing in every case including its controls is a broken reader, not a negative result

**F1042** — **the defect needs no ambiguous input at all, because the marker the framework appends is itself Ambiguous.** Handed over as *a bordered `status` overflows when its message carries Ambiguous characters*, and the mutation swapping every Ambiguous character in the fixture for `µ` **still failed** — U+2026, the ellipsis `truncate` appends, is Ambiguous. So the condition is not *ambiguous input*; it is *a message long enough to be cut*, which is every long message on every terminal at the wide convention. C09's `fit` took `Pick<TerminalCapabilities, "unicode">`, forwarded it to `truncate`, which cuts at the session's convention, and then called `pad` with none — **the two halves of one function disagreeing** · **Closed** — the class in `src/presentation/blocks/` is 15 `pad` sites: 9 already correct, 2 literals, 4 live, all four closed

**F899** — a fallback frame is recorded whole — reason, wait, work, spans, `outcome: "fallback"` — and `report()` builds both `timeline` and `worst` from `drawn`, filtered to `outcome === "frame"`. Measured: `timeline` and `worst` both `["frame"]`, and the only published trace of the frame that gave up is `excluded.fallback: 1`. **Which frame, when, after what wait, with which spans open — all recorded, all dropped at the projection**, and a fallback is the interesting frame: it is where composition failed. One filter serving two purposes and only one required — C28 I6 asks for exclusion from **durations**, and nothing states the exclusion from the timeline. The cost is that `FrameRecord.outcome` is a published field whose non-`frame` value no consumer can observe, which is also why no row could have caught it from the report: the value is not there to disagree with. Not repaired — the remedy changes what `timeline` means, which is C24's surface; T3.7 asserts the absence so a projection carrying it fails the row · **Closed by F1020** — two filters, because they answer two questions: `timeline` filters by cause, the durations by cause **and** outcome. C28 §2 gains `timeline` (published and absent from the interface block entirely), I6 narrows to *durations*, I54 and commitment 35 hold the ruling, §9c holds the walk. The surface premise was checked and did not hold: `ProfileReport` is C24's export, but C24 I31 delegates the report's shape to C28, so the decision was C28's throughout

**F1020** — **one filter served two projections, and every rule that could have said so is satisfied by the filter itself.** F899's remedy was deferred on a premise — *the remedy changes what `timeline` means, which is C24's surface* — and the premise was true about the export and false about the owner: C24 I31 delegates the report's shape to C28, so C28 could always have ruled it. What decided the ruling is in no document: at a ring of one, a 40 ms drawn frame followed by a fallback publishes `timeline []`, `frames 2`, `dropped.frames 1` and `latency.work.count 1` — **the bound is spent on the fallback either way**, which evicts the frame that drew and then filters the fallback away, leaving four figures no reader can reconcile. And `session.ts:918` writes the fallback screen *before* `endFrame("fallback")`, so *the frames that drew* was never the name of the set the exclusion was taken over. **Two further defects, both from instruments rather than assertions.** T1.35's title says *an ordinary frame's record carries no tree member at all* and all three of its assertions are about the frame that **was** retained — removing I32's retention bound outright leaves it green; repaired, then re-mutated red. And MG24 is satisfied by the line that hides the value: deleting `outcome: frame.outcome` from `toNdjson`, the only read outside the recorder, leaves **MG24 silent**, because it counts the recorder's own construction and the filter's read as consumers, while MG30 asks only whether the union member is produced. **Each rule is satisfied by the half of the path that works, and neither asks whether a produced value can reach a reader**; the wide reading that would catch it covers 27% of the surface, which is why MG24 declines to gate on it. No rule should have caught this as framed — T3.15's third arm does. Six mutations by hand, none survived; the one that indicted a test rather than the code is the T1.35 case · **Closed** — spec first, then code; `excluded.selfInflicted` still undercounts a self-inflicted frame that falls back, ruled deliberately and recorded in I54 rather than repaired

## 2 · A complete mechanism, unreachable from the other side of a seam — **closed** · 81 closed · 6 with no verdict ⚠

**F1015** — **`expand` was the one op of four that did not descend, and its refusal stated something false about the document.** `replace`, `merge` and `reserve` resolve a `blockId` through `rewrite`'s depth walk; `expand` used `doc.blocks.find`, so expanding a table inside a `panel` — the arrangement `b.live` itself produces — answered *expand: no block "tbl" in the document* about a document holding `tbl`. The dispatcher's half said the same in its own register. **The mechanism is the enumeration**: C04 §4a stated the addressing rule over *`replace` and `merge`*, written when there were two, and `countId`'s doc comment carried the same pair — the rule recorded twice and both records naming the same two subjects. C23 §3a held a **true** sentence justifying the wrong decision (*`expand` needs no resolution — it names a row on the entry it came from*: true of the entry, false of the block), with its own refutation five paragraphs below in `view`'s clause. **Fixed** — the rule now states over the field, `actions.ts`'s two arms read one `reachable` walk, and `expand` gained I14's duplicate-id refusal it had silently lacked. Ten before/after probe rows, five mutations with five distinct failing sets, M5 reddening T4.63 — a row written months earlier for the other arm.

**F1010** — **the read and the write into a pushed view descended differently, and the `false` that meant *unreachable* was read as *the host is gone*.** Three functions reach a view's held document: `patch` resolves through C04's `applyPatch` to any depth, and `blockAt` and `putBlock` scanned the top level — so a block inside a `group` was invisible to both. **This is F408 stopping one delegation short**: F408 fixed `currentPanel`'s *entry* arm, where the write was already right, and the view arm delegates both halves out of the file. **Worse than filed**: `put`'s view branch reads `updateView`'s boolean as one bit and returns `{kind: "hostGone"}`, which `landed` answers with `release(part.host)` and no `deps.fault` — so F1002's silent sibling teardown came back through the walk, meaning *`putBlock` did not find the id* rather than *the layer has gone*. Both sides resolve through `childBlocks` now, the compiler-checked question `tree.ts` owns, with no new recursion — and the `.map` that replaced **every** top-level block sharing an id is gone with it, which `applyPatch` refuses under C04 I14. **The mutation pass had a green cell on the first attempt**: reverting the read alone failed nothing, because `put` writes without reading, so T4.87 carries `gapBefore` as a third figure — the only value a working write cannot supply. **Residue named, not closed**: `applyPatch`'s `expand` arm is the same class and says something false — `expand` on a top-level table is `{ok: true}` and the same table in a `group` reports *no block "tbl" in the document*, while `replace` and `reserve` on it both succeed. **Closed**

**F962** — **docker-tui sets `profile: { tier: "counters" }`, the lowest tier at which `/profile` opens.** F953's residue: the framework's seventh verb refused in the reference app because nothing on the app's side of the seam asked for a profiler. `counters` is decided by C28 T4.1 — `tier: "off"` and `profile: undefined` are the same object to the runtime — and by C28 §3a measuring `off`/`counters` in one row at nil; measured through a PTY, the greeting's median moved from 935 ms to 862 and the overview drew. Closed.

**F944** — **the ⌃c ladder pops a view and never tells its owner.** C16's `pushedView` rung answers with `overlays.pop()` and calls no owner; every view owner kept state beside its layer and exposed a `pop()` that only `Esc` reached. Measured on the document view: stack `[]`, `openFor` still `"/watch api"`, a motion returning `false` and a second `open` accepted over the stale state, while `keys.ts` routed view keys by `openFor`. C15 I25 is the seam written from it — every removal emits one change carrying the id, synchronously, before the removing call returns — and all three owners now tear down from that stream (C28 T1.94/T4.6, C22 T4.65, C25 T2.15). Closed. · **Closed** (F991) — commit `701d4ed7` (spec `43bf3566`) — C15 I25, and three owners tear down from the subscription

**F624** — a GIF wider than 297 cells at `kitty` has its placement refused (G3) and falls to the half block — a rasterising arm — but the shell chose not to gather frames by *capability* while the block fell through by *width*, and nothing carries the block's choice back to the shell. The result is a still at frame 0, C09 §8b G11 and C22 §6j row 7. Recorded rather than repaired: at 298+ cells a still was already the honest answer. Open · **Closed by F1026** — **the defence was the sentence to test and it fails.** The refusal has **two** axes, `cols > 297` *or* `rows > 297`, and *at 298+ cells a still was already the honest answer* is only about the first: `imageCells` clamps `cols` to the frame width, so that axis needs a terminal wider than 297, while `rows` is the block's declared height bounded by nothing under the registry's 2 000-row cap. A 16×400 GIF at `height: 400` is `{cols: 32, rows: 400}` at **80 columns** — an ordinary terminal, a 32-cell-wide picture, the same still. And **no upward seam was owed**: the arm is a pure function of `(block, width, capabilities)`, so it travels **down** as a question, and `transmits()`'s own comment already named the line that was there

**F586** — `SgrStyle.underline` sat on MG24's equality list under a reason written about `italic` — *nothing sets it yet* was true of both, and only one was named as waiting. The list is by equality, so writing the italic writer fired the rule on both, which is the rule working. A style member C10 resolves and nothing writes is this group's shape. **Fixed**, with the writers

**F538** — three readers of `liveId` were three copies of one constant — `liveElements`, `focusedBlock`, `focusFor` — so the stored location had no entry to carry and nothing outside the live entry could be focused. One pull, `focusedEntryId()`, and the entry on `StoredFocus`'s `liveBlock` arm (C26 I21, I22). **Fixed**

**F539** — a notice appended over the live entry left focus at `liveBlock` with `liveElements()` answering `[]`: `↓` did nothing, `↑` and `Esc` left. Reachable from every notice path and asserted by no row, because every row entered the block and then did something to it. **Fixed**, T3.42

**F541** — `CSI Z` was discarded as well-formed-but-unknown, so the `⇧tab` row would have named a key nothing sends — C16 I17's fourth instance and the first found before the row shipped. The first fix admitted `CSI 999 Z` and router-decode T3.13 caught it in the same run. **Fixed**, bare form only

**F542** — `mergeBlock`'s throw would have refused its first consumer on every key it has; C16 I27 routes colliding keys to `interaction` and free keys to `liveBlock`, both listed by `/help`. **Ruled**, and the mode still has no producer in `src/` — a route is not an inhabitant

**F543** — C23 I18's refusal was reachable from nothing a reader could press — the only route in was a test calling the dispatcher directly. §4g's `⏎` on a settled row is the first keyboard route, and pressing it forced the ruling to finish: every kind refused, the notice naming the command, `rerunEntry` firing it through submit. **Fixed**, T3.41, T1.17b

**F544** — `rowActivate` with `liveId` as origin fires a settled row's action against the live entry — a `fill` lands in the prompt, an `expand` walks the wrong document. The origin is `focusedEntryId()`; the mutation restoring `liveId` puts `pick 1` in the prompt. **Fixed**

**F545** — a move after eviction resolved against the live entry and wrote the evicted id back — highlight on one entry, store on a ghost, I10 violated by the keystroke that should have repaired it. Every move carries the resolved entry now. **Fixed**, T3.43

**F546** — `cursorPositions` had eleven green rows and no writer in `src/`, because `plot-interaction` injected the field itself; with the writer removed every `cursor-positions` row fails and those eleven pass. C22 I71 — the field and its writer land together. **Fixed**

**F547** — a plot declares an element only with a camera (C12 I85), so the crosshair's first reachable subject is a 2D plot wearing `camera: {}`; a `cursorable` predicate exists on the barrel and `construct.ts` does not import it yet, so a cursor on a non-positional form is stored and unread — C22 I76's residue. **OPEN** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `aa6ebbfd` — C12 I85 widened, `cursorable` exported and gated in `construct.ts`

**F532** — `RenderContext.onAction` was a required member no renderer ever read; every frame rendered against a no-op while the working route ran beside it as `KeyDeps.onAction` → `pipeline.onAction`. Removed; C09 §2 and C23 §3a/I16 name the real route. **Fixed**

**F533** — `MetaSpec.resultId` was a shell-side writer for a producer-owned field, with zero writes across `compose`'s three callers. Deleted. **Fixed**

**F535** — `TuiConfig.transport` took a `TransportRouter` the entry could not produce — no constructor for any of C06's three arms, and no `createRouter`. All four exported; T2.11 is the consumer, and its first failure was the busy-latch wrapper answering for the arm. **Fixed**

**F528** — `Annotation.whiskers.points[].x` is read by neither arm: the terminal spreads by index and the SVG crossing agrees rather than rules. **OPEN**, I109's stated blind spot · **Closed** (F991) — commit `aa6ebbfd` — both arms place by `x`, with the `whiskers-placed` fixture

**F470** — `elementsIn` walks into a container that declares no elements of its own, so focus lands on a block inside a `panel` or a `group`; every effect in `construct.ts` resolved that block with a **top-level `find`**. A table inside a panel could be focused and not paged, and a 3D plot inside one focused and not turned — a key consumed, nothing drawn, no error. `session.ts`'s `blockById` walks `descendants` for the same question one file along, which is what made the two read as agreeing. **`b.live` builds a panel**, so the arrangement the framework itself produces is the failing one and every hand-written fixture put the block at the top level. The class is **a recursion added on one side of a pair**, and the signal is that the fixed side's comment names the other side's symptom — `animationIntervalOf`'s does, in a file the effects never import. Fixed with one shared resolver over C04's `descendants`; T4.17n covers it · **Closed** (F991) — commit `f262d76b` — the walk is C04's `descendants`, T4.17n

**F471** — the same walk one file along, and **grepping from the other direction is what found it**: not *which effect resolves a focused block* but *who resolves a block id at all*, which returns two more lines, both in `patch-view.ts`. A `patch` inside a `panel` answers **`no block \`p1\` in this entry`** about a block that is in the entry — a refusal that is *false* rather than silent, which is the direction that makes it worse than F470 rather than a copy. **Both sites move together**: `open` resolves the target and `live` re-reads it behind every motion, so fixing one gives a view that opens and then dismisses itself as `anchorEvicted` on the first keypress. **No enumeration reaches it** — `tree.ts` exists because six sites enumerated `panel | group`, and this site enumerates nothing and does not recurse at all, so `CONTAINERS` has no caller to fail on. `b.live` builds a panel and C23 I34 replaces every refreshed part with one, so the document is the framework's own output. Fixed at both sites through `descendants`; T3.60 covers it and T6.73 is its revert · **Closed** (F991) — `patch-view.ts` and T3.60 with its control

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **F443** | **the half-block rung, from its second consumer** | The rung has shipped since C09 with **one glyph**: `HALF_BLOCK` carrying a foreground and a background, which is everything a photograph needs because a photograph inks every cell. A **scatter** is mostly empty, and there the two halves disagree about whether they are drawn at all — so the sparse raster needs four cases where the dense one has one, and a *bottom only* cell cannot be `HALF_BLOCK` with no foreground, because the terminal paints the top half in the current foreground and a blank half comes out as a solid bar. **The unstated assumption in the first arm was density**, and nothing in the module said so because nothing had ever been sparse. `HALF_BLOCK_LOWER` lands beside `HALF_BLOCK`, on that file's own recorded reason for holding the first. **Found by writing the second consumer from the public surface** — the instrument that found three of docker-tui's eleven. **Fixed** |
| **F409** | **the half-block rung, from the kitty placement refusal** | `placementRows` refuses a placement past the diacritic encoding's span, and the renderer fell to `ditherBraille` — the **bottom** of the glyph ladder. A kitty terminal is non-`ascii` and at least 8-bit by construction, so the one terminal that reaches this path is the one qualifying for every rung of it. The code was correct when the ladder had one rung below kitty; **the new rung is what made the jump a defect**, and no frame shows it because both arms draw a picture of the right size. Found by C09 §8b's table before the rung existed. **Fixed** |
| **F399** | **the monitor, and any live part with a fallible render** | The wrapper notice drew and the live part drew **nothing** — no error, no empty state, no text saying why — while `b.plot` threw F398's refusal inside `render` once a second. `b.live` hands the app the error, the countdown and the attempt; declining to render them is declining to be told. Written without one because the source is `os` and *reading a local file cannot fail* — true about the **fetch**, silent about the **render**. A second duplicate-id refusal (C04 I14) was found on the way, hidden the same way. **Fixed** |
| **F398** | **`b.plot`, for every matrix past eight rows** | Refused an eleven-row heatmap that the terminal renders fine and the validator accepts, under a comment calling it *the same refusal the validator makes — two expressions of one rule*. They were not one rule: the validator carries an exemption stating *C04 I50a is a rule about colour, so it binds where colour is drawn; eight rows is not a matrix*, and the constructor never got it. **And the exemption named one form where its own rationale covers ten** — `IS_MATRIX` lists heatmap, calendar, correlation, confusion, spectrogram, latency, density2d, utilisation, contour, quiver — so the validator refused nine of them and the builder all ten. Both read `IS_MATRIX` now; verified both ways. **A reimplemented rule keeps its birthday clauses**, and the comment asserting equivalence made the drift read as deliberate. **Found by writing a consumer** — a monitor with a row per core on an eleven-core machine; no corpus fixture has a matrix past eight rows. **Fixed** |
| **F397** | **the variant table, and the guard it claimed to have** | `const VARIANTS: Partial<…> = Object.freeze({…})` — and `Object.freeze` returns a value rather than a fresh literal, so excess property checking never runs and `layout: "stacked"` typechecked inside a variant. **The same hole this file's header records catching once**, in the `plot` helper, reproduced by a different mechanism ten lines below the warning. `satisfies` fixes it; the lookup widens by checked assignment, never `as`. **And the first control was vacuous**: the fabrication reported no error because the file was already failing to compile for an unrelated reason. A fabricated violation run against a red tree proves nothing. **Fixed** |
| **F396** | **`/all`, for every form with more than one presentation** | The caption said *every form the type declares* and the count said 46 forms · 42 drawn. Both true, both about **forms** — the corpus has **188 variants**, `violin` alone has 19, and `/all` showed one of each. **53 rungs added, all through `b.plot`**; now 46 forms · 42 drawn · 53 rungs · **95 figures**. **The build refused eleven** written from memory — `plotDetail` on forms with no ladder (C12 I34), violin compact rungs at heights the band floor rejects (C04 I56), callouts without `yAxis: "right"` (C04 I60), and four field values that do not exist. **Eight more drew their own default** — `plotCorners: "sharp"` is a no-op on five forms, `bar`'s base was already vertical — and were removed rather than counted. `T-rungs` asserts both counts by equality and compares each rung against the default it sits beside; fabricated by name. **Fixed** |
| **F395** | **any app wanting to profile the framework** | A chrome function runs **once per frame** and is handed `session · now · columns · copyMode`, so **frame cadence is observable** — successive `now` values are inter-frame intervals, and that dashboard is buildable today. **Render cost is not**: nothing times compose/measure/paint, `src/` holds no `performance.now` or `hrtime` at all (the clock is injected, correctly), `FrameScheduler` carries windows and budgets but no elapsed, and `SessionSnapshot` carries no timing. Three misses of different sizes — per-frame render cost, the `CommitReason` that drove it, per-block `measure` cost. **The tell**: tier 5's `T5.2` asserts input-to-frame latency at p95 **from outside through a PTY**, so the suite goes around the framework to ask the framework's central question. **Cadence half buildable now; cost half deferred** on *a recorded duration on `ChromeContext`* — a C24 change, and *what* to time is a C03/C22 design question rather than a demo's · **Closed** (F991) — C28 shipped and C24 I31 publishes it; the coalescing half is C03 I16 |
| **F394** | **every terminal without kitty, for `/compare`** | The pane is captioned `svg · pixels` unconditionally, and `b.image` descends a ladder whose **second** rung is an ordered braille dither. `detectImageProtocol` says `kitty` only for `TERM=xterm-kitty`, so **VS Code, Cursor, Terminal.app and every `xterm-256color` shell** get the dither — under a label claiming otherwise, which reads as *the SVG renderer is broken*. It is not. `ctx.capabilities.imageProtocol` was one field away on `LocalContext` the whole time. **Fixed**, both arms measured under a PTY: `xterm-256color` names the dither, `xterm-kitty` says pixels and puts six APC escapes on the wire. Type named from `TerminalCapabilities["imageProtocol"]`, so a new rung is a compile error in the consumer. **And the same function held a deferral whose condition was met** — *a local handler is not handed a width*, while `ProducerContext.width` is non-optional on that same `ctx`; comment corrected, constant kept for the measured reason |
| **F393** | **the reader, one command after F392's fix** | The correction to a stale usage block was wrong in the same way. `npm run build` from `examples/plots` — where the previous line's `cd` had put the reader — gives `Missing script: "build"`; from the root it gives `Unable to resolve @typescript/typescript-darwin-arm64`, because `node_modules` is bind-mounted from a Linux container. **And the step was unnecessary**: `dist/` is plain JavaScript and the demo drew on the host with the existing one while the build could not run there at all. The instruction was measured against a belief about the state rather than the state — `npm start` was never tried alone before a build was put in front of it. **A correction to a usage block has the same standing as the error it replaces, none, until it is run verbatim.** **Fixed**, and the new block was run from a clean shell first |
| **F392** | **a clean clone on Node 22.0–22.17, for two of three examples** | `examples/docker` passes `--experimental-strip-types`; `minimal` and `plots` ran `node main.ts` bare. Node runs a `.ts` entry without the flag only from **22.18**, and all three declare `engines: >=22` — so the same clone ran one example and the other two died with `ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION` before drawing. **The devcontainer cannot see it**: CLAUDE.md sends every command through a container on 22.23 where stripping is default, and R01 R4.4 commits that a clean clone works *without* one — so the one claim the container cannot test is the one it hides. Found by a reader on a host, running the README's own command. The README was stale too, listing five commands from the original plan that were never built. **Fixed**; EX1 discovers `examples/*` and asserts the relation, fabricated |
| **F391** | **the second renderer, for `bandwidth`** | `violin/bimodal-sharp` and `violin/bimodal-default` are the same fifty samples at two bandwidths — a pair that exists *because* the estimate differs — and they drew **byte-identical documents**: `densityFigure` called `densitySeries(sr)` with no `adjust`, so `block.bandwidth` reached the terminal's estimator and never the figure's. Every `ArmDecisions` column read `agree`, because the picture was one. **I75's mechanism doing its job** — a dropped input no per-property comparison can see. And the record had classified it wrongly: the note said the density family's four collision groups were *every one legitimate*, and this was a defect. **A collision reads as a resolution fact or as a dropped input and the two look identical**; naming the member per group is what separated them. **Fixed** |
| **F390** | **`plotToSvg`, for every consumer** | Measured after F383: 46 forms plus five degenerate blocks — no series, empty series, `flame` with categories, `tree` with no hierarchy — and `plotToSvg` returns `null` **zero** times. A published return type offering a case its callers can no longer meet, with every consumer still writing `?? ""`. **Recorded, not fixed** — narrowing a published return is a C24 change on F335's precedent, and it has to answer *unreachable* vs *unreached*, which the corpus and the `body.length === 0` guard answer differently. Five suite rows were asserting the opposite as **bounds** (`> 0`), which cannot say *this side is empty*; all five are equalities now |
| **F389** | **the second renderer, for the violin's summary** | The box landed and still read as missing, for two reasons a reader agrees with twice. **The ink**: every other distribution form draws over the *ground*, so its marks take the slot's colour — a violin **fills** its slot, so the median went in as a coloured mark on a field of the same colour. Two inks now, from opposite ends of seaborn's one: `surface.bgDeep` for the whisker and box, `tone.default` for the median and mean. **The composition**: `distributionFigure`'s set was reused on the argument that *a violin is a box plot that also shows the distribution* — true about the **data**, taken as a claim about the **drawing**, so the caps and the slot-wide median bar came out wider than the body they annotate. Every reference draws four things and no caps; the whisker's ends **are** min and max. Also: `layer: "annotation"` draws a polyline **dashed**. **Fixed** |
| **F388** | **the second renderer, for every density curve** | A Gaussian kernel is defined everywhere, so a curve evaluated over a shared axis returns a vanishing density far outside its own data — drawn, **a flat line the width of the frame with a lump on it**. `kde.ts` has always cut it at two bandwidths (seaborn's `cut`), *privately*, under a comment describing the exact picture the other arm was drawing. **The terminal's rasteriser owned a piece of the estimate rather than of the drawing** — F384's shape on the same file's other half. Moved to `derive.ts`; `densitySeries` returns the support it used. **Fixed** |
| **F387** | **the second renderer, for every multi-series density** | `densitySeries` estimates over each series' **own** support and discards the sample grid, so laying the samples on the **shared** axis stretched every distribution to fill it: sd 2.2 and sd 7 drew the same width in the same place — the comparison the form exists to make, inverted. **The box is what made it visible**: alpha's quartiles at 29–32 against a body spanning 25–57, in one picture. A defect found by fixing a different one. `density` passes a single series and is byte-identical. **Fixed** |
| **F386** | **the second renderer, for `ridgeline`** | Two defects in one expression. **It left the frame**: the top row has one slot of headroom and the reach was `1.25`, and this arm has nothing to clip against — the curve was drawn out through the border and off the canvas. Conditional, and that is why it hid: only the *globally tallest* curve reaches full height, and in every fixture the tightest distribution was at the bottom. **And the rows were inverted** — `ridgelineArea` stacks from the floor, this arm from the top, so both arms drew the same curves in opposite orders and both labelled them confidently. `AD` was blind to it: every column it records was equal. **Fixed**; the label-on-baseline residue is recorded, since closing it costs the overlap that is the form |
| **F385** | **the second renderer, for a density with an empty series** | `densityFigure` filtered to the series with finite samples and then numbered the **survivors**, using that index for both the colour slot and the row position — so a leading empty series shifts everything: row 1 draws in slot 0's ink while `identity[0]`, unfiltered, names a curve one place down. Every series holds a slot now, as the terminal's layout does. Found by reading, not by a frame — no corpus variant has an empty series beside a full one. **Fixed** |
| **F384** | **the second renderer, for `violin`** | `definition.ts` says *a violin **is** a box plot that also shows the distribution, so the box is not optional* — above the function that computes it. **The second arm drew the outline and nothing else**, so one block rendered twice said two different things about where the middle of the data was. **Placement, not omission**: `summaryOf` was private beside the terminal's rasteriser, and `figure.ts` cannot import `definition.ts` without a cycle — so the arm could only reproduce it or go without, and went without. `distribution.ts`'s own header says that is the wrong home. Moved to L0 as `summariseSeries`. **Fixed** |
| **F383** | **the second renderer, for 2 forms and 6 variants** | Three causes, and only one was work. **`origin`**: a guard whose reason had expired — `projected` already read `figure.facing`, so deleting one line gives four correctly-oriented documents. **`flame`/`icicle` with categories**: the wrong family, not a gap — `tilesFigure` needs a `hierarchy` and the terminal draws plain bars, so the datum picks the family now. **`violin`/`ridgeline`**: real, and the blocker was *resolution* — `violinColumn` evaluates the kernel at the renderer's row count, while `densitySeries` is fixed-resolution and already crosses for `density`. The figure carries the outline and each arm rasterises it. **The ridgeline came out upside down with the right number of marks**, found by rendering it. **46 of 46 forms, 0 refused variants**; terminal baseline unmoved |
| **F376** | **`plotToSvg`, for every consumer of the second renderer** | Exported by name and described at the entry point as *the second renderer*; `ResolvedTheme` and `loadTheme` both interior, so it resolves and cannot be called. **The one published route does not reach the callers who want it** — `RenderContext.theme` hands one to a *synchronous* `render`, which is the one place an SVG cannot be rasterised, while an adapter, a local handler and a `b.live` renderer get none. `svg-baseline.mjs` says it loads the theme *"the way a consumer loads it"* using a function no consumer can import — the sixth blind spot at its cheapest, in the tool whose job is to model the consumer. **Third instance of I19's class** after `CompletionContext` and `ProducerContext`, and the first where the function itself was already published: that is what made it C24 I29 and MG29. **Fixed** — four types published, which makes existing exports callable rather than adding capability |
| **F377** | **`b.plot`, for four of forty-six forms** | F335 and F371 state the gap in members; the question a consumer asks is *can I draw this chart*. Measured over the catalogue's 46: **39 buildable in full, 3 reduced** (`bar` no `layout`, `histogram` no `layout`/`binning`, `line` no `xScale`/`emptyMessage`), **4 not buildable at all** — `gantt` needs `offsets`, `waterfall` needs `totals`, `pairplot` and `smallmultiples` need `facets`. Those four do not degrade, they become a different chart. **MG28 cannot see it**: it reads whether a builder *threads* the union field, and `form` is threaded, so all 46 arms are reachable as far as that rule goes — the arm is reachable and the form is not, because reaching it needs a second field. Recorded; the remedy is F335's owed C24 ruling · **Closed** (F991) — commit `6c61593d` — the four datum members on `b.plot` |
| **F371** | **`b.plot`, for a bar's only multi-series reading and eleven datum types** | F335 exact at HEAD, hit by writing a third consumer. **The hard refusal is `layout`** — `TS2353`, and `stacked`/`normalised` are the only values that draw, because the collision sweep shows *(absent) = overlap = grouped* is one byte-identical frame, ruled in C12 §3ak. So the plan's own `layout: "grouped"` fixture would have **stated a claim its block does not make**, F350's rule caught on the figure chosen to demonstrate the gap. **The soft one is new**: eleven of `Plot`'s datum types — `HierarchyNode`, `QuartileSummary`, `Segment`, `OHLC`, `VectorSeries`, `Annotation`, `ColormapName`, `Graph`, `ScaleType`, `Origin`, `AxisCross` — are declared on the builder as `Plot["…"]` and never published, so a literal passes and **only the consumer who factors** is refused. Nothing inside the package can notice, because every internal declaration already indexes off the type it publishes. C24's, recorded rather than fixed · **Closed** (F991) — commit `6c61593d` — `layout` among the eight, asserted present by the example's own row |
| **F356** | **the whole numeric abscissa, for the second arm** | `Figure.positionAxis` is a **boolean** — *is the row drawn* — and the only thing it gates in `svg.ts` is `block.xLabels`, three captions the caller supplied. The terminal computes a numeric abscissa instead: `xDomain(block)` from `xMin`/`xMax`, then `xTickRow(domain, width, xFormat, caps, xScale)`. **Six blocks differing only in the abscissa draw six terminal frames and one document** — 1225 bytes, byte-identical, `xScale: "log"` included. So **four members** have zero readers in `svg.ts` *and* in `figure.ts` (`xMin`, `xMax`, `xScale`, `xFormat`), and `xTitle` is a fifth; F355 counted the first four as crossed because they reach `axes.ts`, and the only thing this arm imports from `axes.ts` is `formatValue`. **And a member that does cross is drawn wrongly because of it**: `plotFrame: "grid"` gives the terminal `┊` at every position tick and `┄` at every value tick, and gives this arm five horizontal `<line>`s and no verticals — under a comment in the same file saying *both ways, and drawing one was half of what the style means*. The member sweep cannot see that, because it asks whether a member is read in both arms and `plotFrame` is. **Remedy is the value axis's shape**: `Figure.value` carries a niced `ValueAxis`; the position axis carries a boolean, and the four members are four inputs to one computation that never moved · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I78 and §3ak.44, with rows on both arms |
| **F351** | **`stackBands`' clamp, for the terminal's own stacking walk** | The shared fold states the rule — `v === null || !Number.isFinite(v) ? 0 : Math.max(0, v)`, a null is a zero-width contribution and not a gap — and `stackedBarRow` does `?? 0` and nothing else, so a negative fill reaches `String.prototype.repeat` with a negative count. `layout: "stacked"` with any negative reading throws `RangeError` and the registry draws an ERROR panel; both layouts. **F329's class arriving as a crash rather than as a disagreement** — *the corpus that separates two implementations is the corpus neither arm has*, and no variant has a negative stacked reading, so the path has never run. Found by asking what the fold's rule was while giving the second arm a stack, which is the only reason the two were read side by side. Recorded · **Closed** (F991) — the fold is called rather than restated — `categorical.ts` and `stack.ts`, FB8 |
| **F342** | **`layout`, for every stacked or normalised bar and histogram** | `bar-stacked.svg` and `bar-normalised.svg` are **byte-identical** — `ef00e027b0d2c0eb6e638834180670c9` — and `histogram/two-series-stacked` draws grouped. `layout` is read at `definition.ts:2265` and nowhere else in the renderer, so three distinct terminal figures collapse to one document. **The shared layer carries the value and drops it**: `derive.ts:485` computes *`overlap` cannot mean draw the first one* for the derived histogram block, and no `Figure` member holds it. No column of the matrix can see it — same labels, same legend, same border, same counts — so **the paired sheet is the only instrument that reaches it**. | **F346** | **sixteen of 105 `Plot` members** | Asked mechanically instead of one form at a time: which members does a terminal renderer read that neither the shared layer nor the second arm does. `plotDetail`, `fieldDim`, `glyphInk` are **ruled** (§3ak.26); `matrixAnchor` is **expressed as a refusal**; `yCallout`, `aspect`, `align`, `axisCross`, `emptyMessage` and `layout` are read by one arm with **no ruling anywhere** — `yCallout` has nine mentions in C12 and not one about crossing. **`layout` was missing from the first sweep** because `svg.ts` has a *parameter* of that name, so `\.layout\b` reported the field as consumed by an arm that has never read it: MG24's name-exactness problem inside the instrument written to measure MG24's subject. Recorded |
| **F344** | **the dumbbell's two ends** | Terminal: four series inks, one per row, ends told apart by filled and hollow. Second arm: two inks, one per series, connectors in the rule's grey — so nothing says which row is which and the shape channel says nothing. Not `rowSlot`: `distributionFigure` emits literal slots `0` and `1` and the polyline carries no `seriesIndex` at all. **Fixed** (§3ak.42) — and the remedy this finding proposed, *a hollow shape the role vocabulary has not got*, was **already refused in `roles.ts` with its reason**: *giving either a role of its own would make `GLYPH_SHAPE` describe eight things where the figure says seven*. The figure says eight and has in both arms all along; `meanOnMedian` stays beside it on a reason the far end never had — one cell holding two marks is a composition, not a shape. No terminal frame moves |
| **F335** | **the public builder, for six forms in the public union** | `b.plot`'s spec declares **48** of `Plot`'s **58** members, and eight are simply absent — `facets`, `offsets`, `totals`, `layout`, `binning`, `xScale`, `yScale`, `emptyMessage`. **Four of them are a form's only datum**, so `gantt` has no starts, `waterfall` no totals, `smallmultiples` and `pairplot` no children, a grouped `bar` no layout, and a `histogram` no binning strategy. It **refuses rather than drops** — `TS2353 — 'offsets' does not exist` — which is the good direction, and what the caller is told is that the sanctioned entry point does not reach the form. **`FigureBuilder.setFacets` exists one file along**, so the capability is in the component and absent from the function C24 publishes. **Found by writing a second consumer from the public surface**: the catalogue and every test build through `block({ … })`, which is transparent to any field, so no artefact in the repository goes through the builder for these forms. C24's, recorded rather than fixed — widening a published signature wants its own ruling · **Closed** (F991) — commit `6c61593d` — the eight declared under C24 I30 |
| **F263** | **a builder gap I got wrong, and MG27 is what disproved it** | Claimed: `quartiles`, `categories`, `segments`, `bands` are on `Plot` and absent from `b.plot`, so five forms are *buildable by nothing public* and **MG27 passed throughout**. Field by field the first is **true**; the other two are **false**. `b.figure(…).setQuartiles([…])` and its three siblings have been there all along, and all four fields were on `BUILDER_OMISSIONS` with a stated reason — whose **bidirectional arm refused the commit** the moment the builder set them. So this is scheduled work arriving early, not a defect. **How the wrong claim was reached**: one builder file was read and there are two, and *the rule's own doc comment records exactly that about itself* — the limitation was written down in the file cited as evidence, and the rule's silence was cited instead of its list. A grep answers *is it in this file* and was read as *is it reachable*. **The instrument that caught it costs nothing**: an exemption compared by equality in both directions, third entry disposed of that way after `plot.yFormat` and MG25's `isUsable`. **Landed** — the shorthand beside the chain, four expired entries removed |

The type carries the field, the renderer honours it, the tests cover it, and no consumer can
reach it. **Seven closes is the strongest evidence in this document that a shape generalises.**

| | the mechanism | state |

F23's ground had no file: `DOCKER_TUI_SURFACES.md` S1 rules `/dashboard` explicitly *not* a pushed view |
| **F227** | **`RenderContext.tick` — declared, honoured by `steps`, covered by C09 T6.13, and never advanced.** Ten real frames drew `⠋⠋⠋⠋⠋⠋⠋⠋⠋⠋`, **one distinct**, against the same block through `measurable({tick})` giving **ten**. Three links: nothing calls `commit("spinner")` in `src/`; `visibleRows` omits `tick`; the line cache has no tick axis. **Two of the three were already written down** — C22 I60 and §6c row 10, correctly and as a coupled pair — **and filed as a hypothetical**: row 10 reads *not reachable*, which is exactly true of the cache defect and false of the animation, and one phrase names both. The unrecorded link is the first, and it is what makes the other two look dormant. **Patching link 2 alone moves nothing**, which is *two blockers reading as one*. **Fixed** — all three wired, re-measured at eight distinct frames over ten samples against one before, and C03's 100 ms window is recorded as a floor under the set's own 80 ms, which nothing said before | **fixed** |
| **F165** | **`ErrorLike.code`, `.stage`, `.details` — set by three producers, the adapter mapping and the app's own twelve sites; rendered by nothing.** The first thing roadmap 48's residue was read for, and it listed one of the three: the other two were *cleared* by the app naming them, which is the instrument's direction working as described. **The twelve sites were then read, and they split the three**: `stage` is a per-file constant restating the kind of function it sits in, so the framework already knows it (F13's class, group 1); `code` and `details` come off the far side's own envelope and are the one place narrowing costs something | **partly fixed** — `code` is rendered beside the message and all three members are kept. **The removal of `stage` was ruled and the compiler falsified it**: eight framework sites write real runtime values (`parse`, `spawn`, `handoff`, `local`, `transport`), so the axis keeps the field and moves the app's twelve writes. **Fixed** — the twelve are gone, and the app's suite is 308 before and 308 after: fourteen assertions touch `.error` and every one reads `message`, so the field was write-only for its whole life. What moved is the instrument — roadmap 48's residue went 88 → 89 candidates, and the one member that moved is `ErrorLike.stage`, cleared until now by the app's own writes |

**F78 is the sharpest instance yet and it is worse than the others by a specific mechanism.**
`b.live` throws when neither `fetch` nor `stream` is given, and throws again when both are —
two throws policing a choice **where one option is inert**. An unimplemented field nobody
mentions is a field nobody uses; a constructor that *insists* you choose reads as a decision
you are being asked to make. Step 11 chose the unimplemented one first, for `docker build`,
which is exactly the case it looks written for. It then fails in the worst direction:
`render(null)` is a plausible empty panel, so a build that streams nothing looks like a build
that produced nothing.

**That pair of throws should not survive whatever the ruling is** — it is A03 §2's vacuity
class expressed as an API.

**Verified rather than taken from the ledger**: nothing in `src/` reads `spec.stream`. The
`.stream` references in `src/shell/execution.ts` are `transport.stream`, an unrelated
mechanism.

**F1032** — **`maxWidth` on a column with no `flex` is a ceiling on nothing, and 26 of 26 declarations in `examples/` are that shape.** F50 said *a column with no `flex` gets its minimum and nothing more*, which is exactly what C11 specifies and exactly what `planColumns` does — the finding was right about the behaviour and wrong about where the defect sits. Measured over **1,000 plans**, five shapes × 200 widths, with and without the cap: **0 differing**, against a control where the same cap on a flex column moves it 59 → 20. So the option is declared, typed, documented and unreachable from the side that declares it. §3 step 7 said *"clamp each column"*, and that is the sentence the 26 declarations were written from. **Deliberately not repaired the other way**: making `maxWidth` imply growth moves 26 declarations and every layout and contradicts step 8, so T1.25 pins the deep equality with its flex control and makes the next change a decision rather than a drift. Separated from F1019 by measurement rather than by argument — strip the capabilities and the header disagreement is unchanged; set narrow and the plan sweep still shows zero difference · **Closed** — §3 step 7 reworded to *flex*, §4's indicator sentence extended, I21, I22, commitments 19 and 20, rows T3.20 and T1.25. Group 3's heading carried *F50 relocated to §7* and no §7 entry ever existed, which is this register's own subject arriving in its index

**F1062** — **the gather takes the run's width and the seam takes the frame's.** F1026 moved `visibleRows` to measure each image at the run's width, which is what made the 32-cell picture on an 80-column terminal correct; `transmitFrame` still computes its `c=`/`r=` box at the **frame's** width while a card's body renders at `width − 4`. Two halves of one decision taken against two different widths. **Latent rather than live** — the disagreement needs one of the two to cross 297 while the other does not, so a card-nested image at least 302 columns across, and over the 770 combinations F1026 swept the column axis is reachable at 298, 400 and 600 and nowhere below · **Open** — the remedy is a seam and not a line: `transmitFrame` receives the document and would need the layout, which is a different argument from a different producer and wants a C22 ruling before it wants code. Its `rows` half is unmeasured rather than measured-and-clear · **Closed** — ruled as C22 **I98**, commitment 68 and T1.60: `transmitFrame` takes one group per non-blank `EntryRun`, each declaring the width its blocks render at, one group per run rather than a width beside the document because an entry is two runs at two widths. **And *latent* was wrong.** The 297 figure came from a sweep of the *refusal* arm and was generalised to both: measured directly, the **box** disagrees at **80 columns** — seam 80×2 against run 76×2 — so the picture is drawn into a placement four cells wider than anything addresses it and its right 5% is never drawn, a wrong picture rather than none. The refusal disagrees only in the window **298–301**, where the renderer places and the seam refuses. `rows` moves too, 8 against 7 at 300 and 301. **A bound stated without the ordinary case measured is how a live defect is filed as latent**, and the tell is that the figure quoted came from a sweep of the other arm. The row's own fixture had no `digest`, so `imageKey` returned `undefined`, the seam `continue`d and every assertion would have been made against `""` — checked before it was trusted

**F1055** — **the reason `logs` was kept stopped being true six days after it was written.** The surface document still says `logs` is the only kind that windows and *`raw` has none*; `Raw.window` landed 2026-09-04 and **seven** kinds window today. The residue itself re-derives exactly — one caller, a contract row — and what survives is the three-column record and the level tone, whose only reader is the `logs` renderer. **The count is a second lesson**: grepping the block-kinds directory returns five, because two of the seven register from other components, so *a set over sites is blind to redistribution* landed in the instrument that would have watched it — five went into the spec and the registry measurement corrected it before it shipped · **Closed** — the windowing set asserted **by equality**, which is the watch that would have caught the premise going false. What is actually left is C04's and is **F1065**

**F1065** — **no patch operation appends a line, so a growing log is a whole-block replace per tick.** The op set is exactly `append · replace · merge · status · expand · reserve`, asserted by equality; `append` appends a **block** and nothing appends a line to one. **The kind's missing consumer is the streaming route declining an operation that does not exist, not a surface declining the kind** — F141 read the absence of callers as evidence against the block kind, and it is evidence against the patch vocabulary one layer down, which is indistinguishable from the caller's side · **Open** — filed rather than ruled, because the cost is real on both sides and the measurement that would decide it is the one nobody has: how large a `logs` block gets in practice, and what a whole-block replace costs at that size. **Running *ask where this is written down* on the entry before filing it found a home** — the logs surface document already says the incremental operation is *owed and additive*, in the paragraph after the one F1055 corrected, and names the working alternative the reference app uses. So the register's contribution is not the claim but that nobody was watching the word *owed* · **Closed** — ruled *no operation*, and the measurement reversed both premises. The endorsed alternative is the **slower** arm by ten times: at 8 000 ticks `raw`-per-line costs **5 138 ms** against the whole-block replace's **496 ms**, because `append` grows the *document* and every later patch walks it. A line-append op would buy the producer's array copy — **36 ms of 532** — and nothing else, since an immutable append **is** a copy. What was actually costing was `deepFreeze` re-walking already-frozen lines on every patch (**F1078**, 9× end to end, 200× on a re-sent block), one layer below the vocabulary — which is the same relation this entry claims F141 had to it. The residue is the shape rather than the op: both arms stay quadratic, and an unbounded stream wants the bounded block S12 already specifies

**F1078** — **the walk that establishes immutability re-walked what it had already frozen, on every patch.** `applyPatch` deep-freezes the document it returns and `deepFreeze` built its `seen` set fresh per call, so a streaming entry re-froze every line of its block on every tick. Held at a fixed **8 000** lines with the same array re-sent, a thousand patches cost **1 140 ms**; memoised, **5.7 ms** — 200×, and flat where it was linear. A second cost was the walk's own allocation: `Object.getOwnPropertyNames` builds an n-string array per array node, and a document is mostly array nodes. **`Object.isFrozen` is the wrong memo and passes the obvious test** — the tree shallow-freezes in a dozen places and a shallow freeze says nothing about depth, so reading one as a hit leaves `blocks[0].rows[2]` mutable, which is the failure I1 opens with reintroduced by the optimisation for it · **Closed** — 4 502 ms to 496 ms end to end, three mutations failing one row each, and the narrowing the array path costs is tested rather than written down. **The whole suite was green over the unmemoised walk for the component's entire life**: 6 111 tests pass either way, because a suite indexed by correctness cannot see a cost

**F1079** — **half of `make check` had no corpus, and the procedure that verifies a commit doubles it.** `eslint .` lints every `.js`/`.mjs` it can reach and the config carried **no `ignores`**, so **745** files decided that gate — 371 `dist/`, 87 scratch under `out/` — and **999** with `out/verify` in place, which is the worktree recipe used to check that a *commit* is green. **The rules reach none of it and parsing reaches all of it**: the one rule block is `tools/**` relative to the config, so a fabricated `var x = 1; if (x == 1)` under `out/verify/tools/` fires nothing with `no-var`, `prefer-const` and `eqeqeq` all configured — while a stray paren in a scratch probe took the gate red on a tree whose sources were clean. The two `dist/` warnings it had been printing all along are the same fact in its quiet form · **Closed** — 282 files, named rather than derived from `.gitignore` (`out/` is not in it, and *committable* and *lintable* are different questions), and T2.129 asks `isPathIgnored` rather than reading the config as prose. Its control is the ignore-everything arm, which satisfies every exclusion assertion and makes the gate a no-op

## 3 · A block cannot express what the surface needs — **closed** · 9 closed · none with no verdict — F50 closed by F1032, and the *relocated to §7* clause it carried resolved against no entry there

**F428** — `table`'s window, built and reverted. The walk had four interactions; the implementation found three more. **`window` did not take `measureChild`**, and the argument for it was written on the neighbouring member, about this kind and this quantity — so `table` could not divide for want of a parameter rather than a rule. **A header-only window measures 2 against a range of 1**, because C11 §5's empty-table rule fires on a window that holds no rows — C09 I26 meeting C11 §5, each right about its own case. And the blocker: **`Windowed`'s `skipRows` is leading only**, while a range ending inside an expanded row overhangs at the *end*. `patch`'s slack leads, `logs` and `keyValue` have one-row units — **`table` is the first kind that can overhang**, so this is C09 §2a's contract and not `Table`'s field. Reverted, then ruled: a window failing the invariant written to make windows safe is worse than a kind declaring none. **The header case and the overhang are one thing** — both surpluses fall after the rows, where `skipRows` cannot reach — so the field whose name collided with `emptyMessage` was never needed, and `dropRows` names a drop `session.ts` has made on every frame since the seam landed (C09 I26, I26a; C11 §5a). · **Closed** (F991) — `dropRows` landed on the child seam, and both conformance harnesses read it

| | | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **F33** | `Comparison` cannot label its columns | open — both drawings show labelled columns · **Closed** (F991) — commit `37d6d74e` — `Comparison.labels`, C04 I40 |
| **F34** | a comparison's verdict is colour and nothing else | **half fixed** — the checker's blind spot is closed; `better`/`worse` still want a glyph on `ComparisonRow`, a C04 change · **Closed** (F991) — commit `37d6d74e` — `verdictGlyph` derives the mark from the named fact, so no glyph member |
