## 4 · A change axis distinct from `Tone` — **closed** · 5 closed · none with no verdict — C04 I35, I36 and I38 answered all five

**Three surfaces with no knowledge of each other hit the same absent concept, and step 11
made it four.**

| | the surface | what it wanted |
|---|---|---|
| **F30** | `/drift`, `/compare` | `same \| better \| worse \| changed` mixes a *change* axis with a *judgement* axis; a drawing used a fifth verdict (`added`) the type cannot express · **Closed** (F991) — commit `1a7feaba` — `change` split from `verdict`, C04 I36 |
| **F49** | `/diff` | `+ - ~` wanted three colours meaning **change kind**. C04 I6 refused the mapping, correctly — a deleted file is not a fault · **Closed** (F991) — **Ruled correct as built** — C04 I35, and §3's sentence says so in as many words |
| **F51** | `/events` | `EventLine` has no tone at all, so `die · exit 137` draws the same as `create` · **Closed** (F991) — commit `1a7feaba` — `Events…tone`, pinned by T1.4e after the surviving mutation |
| **F81** | **`/build`** | **a cached step and a step that ran are different kinds of thing, not different grades** · **Closed** (F991) — **Ruled correct as built** — C04 I35 |

**F81 is the clearest statement of the gap in the repository, because it puts both axes in one
row.** The same `b.row` call takes a tone on one cell and cannot on the cell beside it:

```ts
step: s.error !== undefined ? { text: s.name, tone: "error", glyph: "error" } : s.name,
how:  s.cached ? "cached" : s.completed !== undefined ? "ran" : "…",
```

Failure *is* a goodness axis; caching is not. So the finding is not *this block needs a colour
it does not have* — it is **this row needs two axes and the model has one**, demonstrated by a
cell of each, built by one expression.

**What has to be ruled** is unchanged and is the largest design question on the list: whether
the answer is a second axis on the block, a widened `Tone`, or a theme-level concept both
resolve through — and **what the 1-bit rendering is**, which is the constraint that makes the
design rather than decorating it. `/diff` shipped `+ - ~` as *text* for exactly that reason,
and F81 shipped a *word in a column*, which is the same answer reached independently. Two
surfaces solving it the same way without coordinating is worth more than either.

## 5 · The far side's shape is not the framework's contract — **closed** · 9 closed · 2 with no verdict

**F1006** — **`--json` was a convention the framework had read as a fact.** It was appended to every invocation of every far side with no manifest member naming it, so Calcium could not drive `docker` — which spells it `--format json` — without a shim, and the module's whole doc comment was about the *dedupe*, a careful answer sitting on an assumption nobody had written down. The party who knows is the app author, which `interactive` and `persist` both rule in as many words; the difference here is that **the framework had already guessed**. The seam was built one field up: `Invocation.streams` carries *from the manifest; C06 does not read C05; the caller does*, and the tokens take that route. **Row 5 of the walk is the cell two correct statements overlap in** — a user who typed `-o yaml` against a declaration of `["-o","json"]`, where matching the whole sequence appends after it and silently overrides them, and matching the first token leaves both I4's argument and D16's intact. Three states, with `[]` a declaration of *no tokens* rather than an absence. **And T3.25's first draft was caught by the fixture transport**: it asserted every transport reports the declared tokens, and a replayed `argv` is a historical fact about the data — the spec row had the same wrong premise and was corrected from the failure. **Closed**

| | the mismatch | whose |
|---|---|---|
| **F1** | Calcium appends `--json`; docker has no such flag | app — the shim · **Closed** (F1006) — C05 I26 and C06 I25: a `jsonFlag` on the manifest and on the verb, resolved by the caller onto the `Invocation`, with the dedupe reading the **first** token so a user's own value is not silently overridden |
| **F26** | `docker stats` streams by default | app — the shim · **Closed** (F991) — absorbed app-side by `bin/docker-json` |
| **F46** | the stdout/stderr split is a JSON-CLI assumption, and a log verb inverts it | app — the shim · **Closed** (F991) — absorbed app-side — the shim inverts the channel |
| **F45** | an app cannot render a stream that is not JSON | **fixed** in Calcium · **Closed** (F991) — `malformed` is read twice and the lines are offered to `adaptPatch` after `degraded` |
| **F39** | a **rendering** flag (`--raw`) is transmitted to the far side, which exited 125 | **fixed** in Calcium — `shellOnly`, C05 I21 · **Closed** (F991) — `shellOnly` (C05 I21) and `AdapterContext.flags` |
| **F80** | **`interactive` is a property of the verb, and `docker run` is not** | **ruled** in Calcium — C05 I23 · **Closed** (F991) — **Ruled** — C05 I23, `FlagDef.interactive` |

**F80 is a type that cannot describe its subject**, and that half held. `ToolDef.interactive`
has one slot; `docker run` has two terminal contracts chosen per invocation — `/run -it alpine
sh` needs the terminal and `/run -d nginx` must not take it. Ruled as C05 I23: `FlagDef` carries
`interactive` too, and an arm equal to the tool's default is refused at parse, so the arms on a
verb cannot disagree and there is nothing to arbitrate.

**Three of the finding's claims did not survive being measured, and this paragraph carried all
three.** It is amended here rather than left as written, because a summary that keeps a body's
claim after the body has been corrected is F86, F89 and F92's mechanism — and this is the file
those three are about.

- *"a per-flag `interactive` lets two flags disagree, and C05 already rejects that shape for
  `view`."* C05 I20 does the opposite: `view` is declarable on both and an invocation is a view
  if **either** says so — a disjunction, under which disagreement is impossible.
- *"more likely a predicate over the invocation."* The manifest is JSON and T2.7 round-trips it;
  a predicate does not survive that.
- *"wrong that way is a flicker, wrong the other way is a hung session."* Both measured, both
  false, and **inverted**: `docker run -it` without a terminal exits 1 at once, C21 gives every
  non-handoff child `/dev/null` on stdin so the named hang cannot happen, and the *safe*
  direction is the one that writes a container id to a screen repainted a frame later.

Two further findings came out of checking those claims rather than out of looking for anything:
**F118** — I20's refusal covers one of the two ways to declare the pair it forbids — and
**F119** — an interactive verb was routed above the validation gate and spawned unvalidated.

**F39 stays separate from the shim for the same reason it always did**: no shim makes it go
away for the next app, and it was invisible to twelve passing rows because they hand argv to
the adapter and never spawn anything.
