**F1039** — **F753 and F800 are one measurement, and asking whether the owed rows are measurable at all was the whole of the work.** Both residues are *what does a real emulator put on the wire under 1003 + 1006*, answered by one fixture with one drive, so F1040 is not a separate finding. Of the four owed emulators exactly **one exists here** — kitty 0.41.1; Ghostty and WezTerm have no Debian candidate and iTerm2 cannot run here at all, which makes it a **permanent limit** whose row expires on a macOS runner or never, rather than a deferral. Kitty agrees with xterm on both questions, and two facts fell out that neither entry has: **xterm reports the horizontal wheel as press *and* release where kitty reports press only**, byte-identical over two runs each and latent because only `wheelUp`/`wheelDown` drive anything; and **T5.9's drag gesture does not survive on kitty**, which is a harness defect that would have read as *kitty does not report drags* · **Open** — nothing landed, because the rows belong in `test/e2e/mouse.test.ts`. Owed: the tracking-mode arms extended to kitty, a wheel row asserting `64 65 66 67` on both with the asymmetry as its own assertion, and the measured set `["kitty", "xterm"]` **compared by equality** so a third emulator is a failure rather than a quietly shorter loop. Stated blind spot: two emulators is a pair and not a table, and both are xterm-lineage in their SGR encoding — Ghostty and WezTerm are the interesting rows precisely because they are not · **Closed** (F1044) — the rows landed in `test/e2e/mouse.test.ts`, and one premise of this entry was itself stale in the other direction: C16 §2 still said *no other emulator has been measured… the container has no Ghostty, kitty or WezTerm* while kitty 0.41.1 was installed

**F1044** — **the spec said no second emulator had been measured and one was installed.** C16 §2 carried *no other emulator has been measured… the container has no Ghostty, kitty or WezTerm* while kitty 0.41.1 was in the image, which is the stale-claim class arriving inside a component spec rather than a plan. Kitty agrees with xterm on every question either residue asked, and the one place they differ is the one place *ctlseqs* does not say: **xterm reports the horizontal wheel as press and release, kitty press only**, asserted as `["66m","67m"]` against `[]` because a row phrased *at least the four presses* is satisfied by both and records nothing. The set is compared by equality **twice** — before any capture and after the loop — so a third emulator is a failure that names itself rather than a quietly shorter loop · **Closed** — T5.9 extended and T5.10 new; the drag arm stays xterm-only with its **harness** diagnosis in the row, because left unstated it reads as *kitty does not report drags*; iTerm2 recorded as a permanent limit rather than a deferral, since a deferral names a condition something can watch

**F1040** — **a finding number was allocated, spent and folded, and this row is what stops it being handed out twice.** F753 and F800 were dispatched as two findings because the register holds them as two rows; they are one session, and F1039 has the measurement. The entry exists because a number that names nothing is worse than one that names a redirection: two documents carry the sentence *F1040 is not a separate finding*, and SP5 reads any `F\d+` as a citation — correctly, since nothing distinguishes a citation from a disclaimer. It is the reserved-name hazard at one remove, a finding number being a name in a namespace every document shares · **Closed** — no surface, no code, no test; the alternative repairs were rewording until the number is unspellable, which leaves the allocation unrecorded, or an exemption in a rule that has no other holes

## 8 · Absence indistinguishable from failure — **closed** · 50 closed · 1 with no verdict

**F1002** — **three reasons, one bit, and the teardown it triggered was the host's.** `renderPart` read `if (put(…)) return true;` and `put` answered `ok` for a patch the document refused, a host that is gone, and a part that has ended — so the shell released `part.host`, which is the **host's** teardown: one part that could not draw stopped every sibling that could, and froze the entry's elapsed readout (C23 I53). Measured at **2 fetches over six one-second ticks, and its well-formed sibling also stopped at 2** against 6 with no collision. A **classification table** (§8h H1–H8), because nothing happens between a patch and its answer, and it found four things reading could not: `"patch"` covers two structurally different states, told apart by asking the host rather than by parsing C04's message; **`"settled"` is unreachable here**, since C13 gates it on `origin: "farSide"` and a part patches as `"shell"` — half a justifying comment describing a state no caller can construct, with T3.68 as its fabricated violation; the report cannot be the part's own panel, because a patch is what failed; and **H8 is the implementation falsifying the walk** — a housekeeping release and an I33 teardown share one name, and a card carrying a one-shot part froze at 2s against two controls at 6s, older than the ruling. **Four arms have a hazard a boolean did not**: `if (put(…))` is always truthy, and three of seven call sites were left that way by the lane's own first pass with no row able to fail. **Closed**

**F1005** — **one problem, two remedies, and both are right at different layers.** F898 left open whether a zero-filled loop-delay histogram should be emptied or qualified. The ruling is that the **recorder** keeps what it holds — a histogram with no observations is a real state of the instrument — and the **consumer** omits, because the two figures are not the same kind of claim: a `p50` below the resolution is a statement about the instrument and the qualification does its work, where a `max` of 0 is an existence claim about the loop that no resolution printed beside it can qualify. I13 gains a second clause and `ResourceSample` gains `loopDelaySamples`, which is what separates a floor from an absence — without it the two are the same three numbers. **The defect it found was in the row written to close it**: T3.3's new arm read `toContain("— no window sampled yet")` and survived deleting the maximum's own branch by hand, because the notice sits on two rows and the p50's copy satisfied it. An assertion over a pane's whole text is a proxy for the row it means. **Closed**

**F960** — **the coalescing plot's height grew by one per reason and its rows by two.** `ov-coalesce` was a grouped bar of commits and frames per `CommitReason` at `height: max(4, reasons + 2)`; a grouped bar draws two rows per category, so at the type's five reasons the plot hid `stream` and `spinner` — the two coalescing is about — behind `+4 more`, and every fixture recorded one reason. Now a table with a *saved by coalescing* column, one row per reason, asserted over every reason by T1.100. Closed. · **Closed** (F991) — C28 I52 bounds the overview, T1.100

**F947** — **the overview pane is taller than the region it is shown in, and C15 clips it in silence.** Measured at width 80 through `measureSequence`: the overview with its one-row header is 20 rows on an empty ring, 26 at twelve frames and 28 with spans and a counter recorded, over a 24-row region; `frame`, `distribution` and `memory` fit at 5, 10 and 4. The pane a reader opens first is the one that does not, and C15 I8 clips rather than saying so; the view windows by block and re-windows on its next tick, so the clip lasts at most one interval (C28 §9b S8, T3.14). Those figures were the bare registry's (F959): through the one that draws it the overview was 40 and 51 rows, and `distribution` 26 and 37, paging under I51. **Closed** — C28 I52 bounds the overview at 23 pane rows at 80 columns, a 24-row terminal minus the header, and it measures 9, 17, 18 and 22 on the four reports T1.100 holds; the two unbounded tables moved to `frame`, whose subject they are, and the overview says so in its last row. · **Closed** (F991) — C28 I52 — the overview is bounded at 23 pane rows and the window is I51's

**F522** — an all-zero waffle drew three swatches and three names with no reading, and the pie called the identical list `No data.` — two answers to *what is a zero total* inside one family. The waffle reads `0%`; the follow-through ruled a zero total *every segment at nought, never an empty list* (C12 §3ak.26 finding 5), so the pie draws its rim. **Fixed**

**F556** — every completion source failure was dropped silently in the product — `onSourceError` supplied by every test and by nothing in `src/`, C19 T3.6 asserted against an array the test built. `createSourceErrorSink()` lands in `engine.ts`; the `construct.ts` wiring is requested, and both halves sit on the equality lists until it lands. **Built, wiring owed** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `a7eff1fb` — wired in `construct.ts`

**F561** — an overflowed child on the adapter route produced a document with no record of the cut, and the shell route recorded it under `meta.truncated`, which nothing draws — two routes, two opposite defects under one sentence that guarded a third place. A `notice` in `finish` (C07 I22), the id suffixed until free; the stream route landed after. **Fixed**, three of three

**F424** — C09 I25's `window` seam is built, composed and wired at `session.ts:921`, and **two kinds of five declare one**: `patch` and `logs` do, `code`, `table` and `keyValue` do not. Declining is silent by design — `windowSequence` keeps a non-declaring kind whole and pays for it out of `skipRows` — so the only symptom is a number: **914 ms to paint forty rows of a 50 000-row `code` block against 0.65 ms for the same `logs`**, 1 400×, with every assertion passing and the frame correct. Wants a **named list compared by equality** rather than an assertion, because which kinds divide is a judgement (the plot never will, C12 I1). **The survey was wrong twice before it was right** — a glob of `blocks/kinds/` missed `patch`'s definition in `presentation/patch/`, and a rebuilt fixture measured 1 row for three kinds; the reliable question is asked of the registry, not the tree. · **Closed** (F991, F1009) — T2.136 is the divisible-kinds list, asked of a **registry** with `table`, `plot` and `patch` registered, compared by equality in both directions with every entry carrying its reason. **And the count above was wrong by this finding's own mechanism**: seven kinds divide, not six — `table` is registered through `register` exactly as `patch` is, so a survey shaped by `DEFAULT_DEFINITIONS` plus the exception one remembers misses it

**F414** — the shipped transmission sets `q=2`, which suppresses every reply including errors, so a transmission the terminal rejects is silent at the protocol level as well as on screen. Measured by flipping one token: Ghostty answers `EINVAL: invalid data` for a corrupted PNG and `OK` for four good ones, so the information exists and is one character away. **Open, and `q=2` is right today** — the reply arrives on stdin and `decode.ts` has no APC arm, so it would reach the line editor as a lone `ESC` and literal text. The blocker is named as a symbol; `q=1` is the middle rung the protocol already defines. · **Closed** (F1057) — **and the blocker four documents named was never the one in the way.** The arm landed and nothing about detection moved: **a reply is unreadable until raw mode** — four queries to both emulators returned **0 bytes in ~1 500 ms** canonical against under 1 ms raw on XTerm and 13–23 ms on kitty — and raw mode is `acquire()`'s, which runs after the record is built, read at eleven sites and handed into six objects. **The bytes are held and echoed while they wait**, so an OSC reply lands as 26 printable characters: F414's symptom, by a mechanism no entry named, in the window early enough to matter. `q=2` stays, and the refusal explicitly does **not** rest on danger or on latency, with both figures in the spec so a reader who checks finds the argument standing elsewhere

Every instance is invisible by construction: a green suite, a plausible frame, no diagnostic.

| **F372** | **a live part, when the shell's own patch is refused** | `renderPart` does `if (put(…)) return true; release(part.host)`, and `put` returns `outcome.ok` — so `PatchOutcome.reason`'s three values collapse to one. `unknown` and `settled` mean the host is gone and releasing is right; **`patch` means the shell built something the document cannot take**, and it takes the same silent teardown. `put`'s comment justifies the collapse and names only the two, which is MG24's shape in the comment recording the reasoning. Measured at `every: 120`: **2 fetches against 24** for an identical part whose child id differs. **The panel keeps drawing its last child** — right value, no notice, no stall — for the rest of the session, and `applyPatch`'s exact message is discarded at the one place that could report it | open · **Closed** (F1002) — C23 I70 and a four-armed `PutResult`; **worse than filed**, because `release(part.host)` is the host's teardown, so one part that could not draw stopped every sibling that could and froze the card's elapsed readout |

And `columnLabels` drops a name **and its tick** when it would overlap its predecessor: at four series with five-cell names, **3 ticks for 4 categories, the tallest bar unlabelled**. C12 I8 is the rule and is written about *rows*; the horizontal arm obeys it visibly (`+3 more · 2560w · layout · …`) and the column arm has no equivalent. Neither is in the catalogue, whose bar fixtures are one and two series | open · **Closed** (F991, F374's own entry) — C12 I8 gains a fourth arm: the vertical arm drops for **width** rather than for rows, so none of the three reached it. Measured worse than the record said — **one tick and one name under four bars at width 30**, not three of four. The count goes in the label row itself because a second furniture row changes the block's height and C12 I1 forbids it, with the placer reserving its cells to a **fixed point** before it places anything. `LC4` had been asserting the silence; `LC7` asserts ticks + the count = the categories at seven widths. Three `notice` cells opened in the arm-disagreement record and the reader was repaired to file a trailing `+N` as a withholding rather than as a category name |
| **F350** | **three corpus variants named for a field their block does not vary** | `line/legend-right` is **byte-identical JSON** to `line/multi-series` and sets no `legend`; `heatmap/palette` sets `colormap: "viridis"`, which is `rampOf`'s answer for `heatmap` already; `histogram/default` and `histogram/scott` produce the same bins. All three assert nothing and every gate agreed — a golden frame records what a fixture produced and cannot ask whether it varies what its name says. **The fabricated-violation discipline pointed at a fixture.** The fourth collision, `slope/default` ≡ `slope/six-readings`, is **legitimate and is I74's proof**: six readings whose first and last are the other's two must draw the same figure, so a rule flagging it would ask the corpus to falsify what it was built to demonstrate. Recorded, not repaired — widening two of them moves frames in both arms · **Closed** (F991, F350's own entry) — **and the three are three different defects, which is why *widening two of them* was the wrong plan.** `heatmap/palette` had the field set to the **default** (`viridis` is `rampOf`'s heatmap answer) → `magma`. `histogram/scott` sets a **real** non-default value over a sample where the rules agree — the category the finding's sentence had no room for — so the shared `bell` went 200 → **300**, the smallest measured size at which all three strategies disagree. And **`line/legend-right` was not a defect**: adding `legend: "right"` moved four frames and all four were worse, because below the colour floor the stacked arm labels its own strips and `legendPlacement` declines for a stated reason — an explicit value overrode a correct decline and drew the name twice. Reverted; the collision is legitimate beside `slope`'s. Collisions 4 → **2**, distinct frames 237 → 239 and documents 178 → 180, 55 goldens moved |

| **F40** | a view's window, measured a block at a time — C15 cut the excess silently | **fixed** |

C09 enforces I1 nowhere: a kind measuring 1 and rendering 3 returns three lines. `visibleRows` reconciles by `rows.slice(0, ve.takeRows)`, and `takeRows` cannot exceed C14's index — so the over-drawing block keeps its first row and **the block after it is not on the frame**. No fault: `BlockFault`'s three arms are `measure`, `render` and `elements`, and this is none of them. **The repair exists one level up** — `paint.ts` made the region's trim a `FrameError` with a comment about the trim *quietly reconciling* two quantities, and the per-entry trim still does. Unreachable today at **15 kinds × 4 widths, zero divergences**, and the deferred height change is what makes a disagreement ordinary | open, and the height change lands with its report · **Closed** (F991) — C22 I70, T4.54 and revert T6.78 |

Two answers about one block, and the pair costs the subtree rather than the block: measured **0 elements against a control's 4**, with `↓` skipping the lot and nothing said. `scroll` is a shipped kind that declares `elements`. **And the ruling it was meant to satisfy names an outcome that does not exist** — C26 I12's *focus falls to the block level*, where `FocusState.rowId` is `string | null` and `null` is constructed **nowhere in `src/`**, so an atomic block is skipped rather than focused. C12 §3ah's class in another spec: right about the interaction, wrong about a mechanism it assumed. **Ruled** — C09 I30 and a corrected C26 I12; T3.37 asserts both arms, because the throwing arm alone passes at 0 and 4 alike · **Closed** (F991) — commit `d40ab050` — C09 I30, both answers out of `#elements` |

| **F67** | **below 16 rows the shell draws nothing, says nothing, and stays alive** | **closed** — the gate existed and neither half ran (C01 I12b, C22 T4.21) |

Under it, `Series.values` and `Cell.spark` were never element-checked at all, so a string in a numeric array passed with or without a round trip. The docker ring had been building such a block for a commit and nothing noticed, because a constructed block never reaches the validator. **Fixed** — C04 I46a, C12 I4, T1.16, T2.19 |
| **F171** | **a ramp step that draws as padding** — `RAMP_BRAILLE` began at `U+2800`, so at `ambiguousWidth: "wide"` every sparkline drew its *minimum* as whitespace, which the right-anchor already uses for *fewer samples than cells*. `cells()` counts it as one and no golden frame renders that arm, so every width and length row passed. **Fixed** — C12 I16, T1.15, a golden frame for the arm |
| **F172** | **an unknown `ColourRef` resolves to no style, silently** — `resolve()` returns `NO_STYLE` when a palette or slot is missing *and* when a decoration palette collapses at 1-bit, so *this reference does not exist* and *this reference means nothing here* are one value. The empty-block class in the resolver, and entry 3's fourth palette family is what makes it reachable. **Closed** (F986; 3ac95376, C10 I30) — a missing family throws `ThemeError` at resolution, where contrast failures already go |
| **F240** | **a degradation ruled for two rungs with a reason forced at one** | C09 §3a rules *the pair degrades together — at 4-bit and 1-bit there is no ground and no ink*, I31 cites it and **T3.46 asserts both halves' absence** two-armed and on purpose. **Filed with the wrong headline**: the code, the tokens, the maps and four frames were measured and the instrument not run was the one that asks where the behaviour is written down. **The finding survives, better** — the reason is right about the *pair* and silent about the *rung*. At 1-bit C10 I8 forces it. At 4-bit the ladder has a rung and `diffAdd`/`diffRemove` are text-bearing grounds curated at that depth in the same file: the pair is not degraded, it is **unfilled**. `FourBitMap` is `Readonly<Record<string, number>>`, so an unanswered slot and a deliberately unpainted one resolve identically — **three of ten missing in all three themes**: `selection`, `errorGround`, `errorInk`. And three missing arms are probably three cases: `selection` is a wash behind text it does not own, the pair is a tag that brings its own ink. **Ruled** — the 4-bit arm lands, 1-bit is untouched, T3.46 splits its two depths, and `selection` is named as roadmap 23's rather than folded in · **Closed** (F991) — the 4-bit arm landed (C10 I32) and T3.46's second arm was rewritten |

**F67 is the new instance and the first where the *framework* is the one saying nothing.**
Measured with the size set on the PTY master before the child draws: 100×12, 100×15 and 30×16
all produce **zero bytes on both channels, process still running**. Not a crash, not a refusal,
no exit. The floor is two-dimensional, so it is some minimum region rather than a row count,
and **nothing anywhere refuses a region too small to use.**

**That is C02 I7's argument with a different subject.** A terminal that cannot open the
alternate screen must be told so on the primary screen rather than left dark. A terminal too
small to draw into reaches the same situation by a different route and gets the opposite
treatment.

**F61 is the class arriving through the instrument**, which nobody predicted: nothing was
absent and nothing had failed, and the picture was the same one — with the prompt gone,
because a pushed view takes it, so not even a cursor suggested the app was alive.

**F15 remains the one that matters**, because it is the mechanism the others were seen
through. `appendAndCommit`'s bare catch is documented and deliberate (C23 §5); the consequence
is that a malformed document is indistinguishable from a verb that did nothing. Fixing five
instances did not fix the thing that hid them.

**The app-side half is a rule, not a gap**, and two unrelated verbs found it independently:
`/drift` (a container identical to its image) and `/config` (files that agree) both produce an
empty block that reads as a call that failed. Six app surfaces now write a sentence for
*nothing to show*. **`/port` is the one that shows the class is not what it looked like**: a
container publishing nothing and a stopped container are two successful worlds with one
rendering, so the rule is not *say when you are empty* — it is *an empty rendering is a claim,
and the claim has to be one the producer can support.*

---

**F813** — a verb submitted while the greeting's dashboard fetch is in flight loses its result: the row is echoed and nothing follows, for the life of the shot; six seconds later the same verb draws three hunks · **Closed by F1024** — diagnosed, and the premise was wrong: **the result is not lost.** `grep hunks` hits the byte stream in every capture including the losing ones, and three PageUps find the diff as the transcript's *first* entry with the banner below it. It is F158's mechanism, and F813 had measured the wrong side of the race — `/config`'s fetch, not the greeting's

**F912** — a recorded PTY session would not replay, and the divergence moved five times: an unpaced driver firing `^D` before the far side answered; the initial size recorded as a `resize` and delivered as a `SIGWINCH`, which C03 reads as contamination; a pacing baseline that absorbed the three frames a session draws during `start()`; the replay hard-coding `tier: "spans"` while the regime had carried the recorded tier since it was written. **Four of five were the harness**, and the fifth wore the shape of an impossibility result — *a frame reporting its own cost cannot replay byte-identically* — which is true and was the wrong diagnosis. C01's `signalExit` calls `process.exit` directly, so `recording.end()` never ran and the batched clock reads were lost with it; past the end of a clock stream a replayed session's durations hold still, the chrome row carrying one has no delta, and **the frame is never composed** — an elision no mask can reach. Nine lines, flushing from `process.on("exit")`, and six runs across two tiers then came out identical over every frame. **Broken by reading the two event orders side by side**: four causes produced the same appearance in the frames, and the orders disagreed structurally — an extra `resize`, a missing `end`, neither visible in any frame. The drive's counters are reported beside the comparison for the same reason: `stalled: 0` is what proved the pacing worked, and the frames had been byte-identical while all eight waits stalled · **Closed** (F991) — commit `dc79ab21` — all five recorded

**F910** — the two sides of the comparison were captured at different points: recorded frames from `lifecycle.writer`, replayed frames from the fake `stdout`, so frame 0 diverged on the alternate-screen enter that only one side had. Two populations, and every downstream figure was about the difference between them. **Fixed by having the replay record itself to a mirror**, so both sides come from the same tap — which falsified C22 I94's *may not be set together*: a replay that does not record cannot be compared, and what the gate should refuse is the **same path**. The asymmetry then returned once more in the *pacing* signal, counting every write where the recording counted only the writer's, and did not read as the same finding until the counters were

**F909** — with recording on, a session under a PTY drew **nothing at all**. C01 I9 captures `stdout.write` at construction and replaces it with a debug redirect, so the recorder's tap on `config.stdout` sat inside the redirect and every frame went to the debug sink. Silent, total, and the configuration reads as obviously correct — tap the stream the frames are written to. C01's ownership of `stdout` is not a fact a reader of `config.ts` can see · **Closed** (F991) — commit `dc79ab21` — the tap moved off `stdout` to `lifecycle.writer` (C28 I46)

<a id="9"></a>

**Keyed here by the F142 inventory sweep** — the second cohort, filed after this document's own count was last derived. Placed by mechanism from the entry; SP6 proves coverage and not placement, which is its stated limit.

| | why this group |
|---|---|
| **F135** | a malformed greeting is swallowed twice and the session shows nothing · **Closed** (F991) — closed by F15's containment, which its own body asks for |
| **F138** | every notice composed as an error was an invalid document · **Closed** (F991) — `noticeDoc`'s error field, C23 T3.38 and the `c23-faults` run |
| **F254** | Calcium's own suite — **an assertion that sees one encoding of a thing reports its absence.** IO3 asks whether the overlay is *absent* below 8-bit or drawn in a vocabulary that says something it should not. It read the frame for `\[38;2;` — the 24-bit SGR form — and reported **nothing drawn at 8-bit**, which is where `continuousColour` quantises to the 256-cube and emits `38;5;N`. **A control separated the readings**: the same row asserts the picture's glyphs are unchanged, and they were, so the cells existed and were invisible to the instrument rather than missing from the output. **The fixture rule pointed at an assertion** — a matcher is a fixture, and one that sees a single encoding cannot distinguish *the rung is absent* from *the rung is a different escape*. **The degradation ladder is the worst place for it**, because every rung below the top is a different encoding by construction | **fixed** · **Closed** (F991) — the matcher was fixed and the first draft recorded, row IO3 |
| **F319** | **a bin that is not drawn and not named** | C12 I8 is *a series that cannot be given a row is named in a legend, never dropped silently*, and `categoricalForm` opens with `const labels = cats.slice(0, areaRows);`. The sibling branch honours the rule in twenty lines with a comment saying **a series dropped in silence is the failure this branch exists to avoid**; categories get the slice. **Three of 45 row-bearing variants in the corpus are short and all three are histograms** — `default` and `scott` lose one bin of nine, `freedman-diaconis` **three of eleven, 39 of 200 samples, the whole right tail** — and the frame reads as a clean unimodal distribution that has ended. **The rule was written about the subject that had the defect**, and a histogram's rows are bins a *strategy* chose rather than categories an author wrote, so the count is not a number anybody could check against the height. Invisible to all three gates: the 1810 terminal frames are byte-identical because it has always done this, the matrix is unchanged in every cell across a change that replaced 240 bars with 18, and `plotAreaRows` is the terminal's so the second arm never had a limit to hit. **Found by reading the frames F317 moved** — the remedy producing the finding the original could not. **Fixed** — I8 widened to name a *row*, the notice spent from the declared height before the drawing, 30 of 1810 baseline frames moved and 0 of 181 SVG · **Closed** (F991) — I8's third subject implemented — `definition.ts`, C12 I8 |


**F897** — `c22-footer-budget` reports `T4.28 · visibleRows bypasses entryLayout` as **SURVIVED** twice and **CAUGHT ELSEWHERE** on a third run of the same unmodified file; two drivers built by extracting its `MUTATIONS` verbatim (`diff` clean) also catch it, and by hand the mutation fails `T4.12` six times of seven. **Two of five runs report a live mutant that is not live**, in the direction the harness's preamble calls worse than useless — a survivor reads as thoroughness. **Both hypotheses died on their own controls**: `mutate.mjs` exports an atomic tmp+`renameSync` writer and 150 of 163 run files define a plain `writeFileSync` instead, none using the atomic one — but the plain-writer driver catches it too. Order excluded by swapping. **One mechanism was seen once**: vitest printed a `Failed Tests 1` section, `Worker forks emitted error`, and a summary reading `Tests  49 passed (73)` — no failed count, which is the only thing `killed()` reads. That is the class the harness's own preamble records (ANSI codes hiding the count, *eight caught mutations reported as uncaught*) with a second cause. **The fix is not shipped**: the case will not reproduce, and MH1's comment is that testing this function against a string the test wrote itself is the defect. **T1.41 is a real survivor and a finding about the mutation** — `|| blocks.length < 2` is redundant, `cardBody([])` and `bodyRuns([])` make the card path byte-identical for a one-block card, so the mutation cannot change anything; it has read as a test gap since F815 · **Closed by F1018** — the reproduction was never the blocker. `process.kill(process.pid, "SIGKILL")` inside a test reproduces it on demand, and the signal is the **arithmetic** rather than the `Errors` line: `Tests 2 passed (6)` says `reported < collected`, which is answerable from vitest's own summary bracket per run and needs no golden total. `tally`/`incomplete` and an `INDETERMINATE` row, with refusals at the clean and control moments

**F903** — `suspended` had a declaration, a consumer and **no writer**. `recorder.ts` holds `let suspended = false` and passes it to `probe.sample(suspended)`; nothing anywhere assigns it, so every sample in every session said `false` and a `handoff()` running `vim` for two minutes produced a series of near-idle readings correctly labelled as running. **The sequel to F900 and the same invariant**: F900 found the consumer missing, and writing the consumer is what made the missing producer findable — a flag with a producer and no consumer is a wasted field, a flag with a consumer and no producer is a wrong answer, and the first is what hid the second because the mechanism looked half-built either way. Read one statement at a time all three parts are correct; what is absent is an **edge**, and the grep that reaches it is for an assignment rather than a mention — two hits, neither a write. **Fixed** — `Profiler.setSuspended` is the writer and C22 decorates `lifecycle` once at construction, so `execution.ts` runs a handoff without naming a profiler; set before the release and cleared after the reacquire, with an unwind on a refused `suspend()` because a stranded flag labels every later sample unreadable · **Closed** (F991) — commit `370d2a29` — `setSuspended`, decorated once (C22 I93, C28 I28)

**F900** — `suspended` has been on `ResourceSample` since the first commit and **no consumer has ever read it**: one grep hit across the panes and the exporter, and it is a comment. The memory pane took every headline figure — loop delay max and p50, gc pauses and kinds, cpu, timing entries — from the newest sample whatever its flag, so a sample taken while the session was suspended presented a stopped clock as the present state and **an idle machine and a paused one drew the same picture**, which is the reading C28 I27 names in as many words. The invariant's two clauses had the same appearance and different fates: the first is a parameter being stored and cannot fail; the second quantifies over *every consumer* and is satisfied trivially while there are none, then becomes false the day one is written from the sample's fields, where `suspended` reads like one more. **Fixed** — the headline comes from the last running sample, the caption counts the suspended ones and says the series is not continuous, and an all-suspended report refuses rather than drawing. **Third pane instance of one class this session** after F895's two: a figure whose meaning depends on a flag the drawing code does not read · **Closed** (F991) — C28 I27 with its consumer in `panes.ts`

**F898** — C28 T3.3 asks for *the histogram is empty rather than zero-filled*; measured, a sample over no window gives `max: 0` and both percentiles `0.000511` at a 10 ms resolution. **I13 does not ask for that**: its remedy is that the figure travels with its resolution, which is built and asserted over every pane. So two remedies exist for one problem — omit, or qualify — and the row names one while its invariant names the other. **A test row strengthened past its invariant**, which is the harder direction to see than F895's opposite: a row is read as an assertion about the code, so a row the code fails reads as a defect in the code. Left open rather than conformed, because `max: 0` over an empty histogram says *nothing was ever delayed* — a statement about the loop, which the resolution beside it does not qualify · **Closed** (F1005) — C28 I13's second clause, and the ruling is **both remedies at different layers**

**F1035** — **`q=2` was the second half of a blocker whose first half is wider than the protocol it was found in.** F414 deferred on a symbol — an APC arm in `decode.ts` — and the symbol is genuinely unmet at HEAD: there is an arm for `ESC [` and one for `ESC O`, none for APC, and `q=2` is still on all five transmissions in `kitty.ts`. **So the deferral has not silently expired, which is worth recording as the outcome that instrument is usually run to disprove.** `q=1` is confirmed on a real terminal — errors reported, successes suppressed. But **F414's prediction of the consequence is wrong in the direction that matters**: there is no lone `ESC` reaching the line editor, because the Meta arm consumes it and emits `Alt-_`, a *bindable* key, with `Alt-\` closing. And the hole is **every string-terminated reply** — APC **and** DCS **and** OSC — not APC's alone, which puts `XTVERSION`, C02 §8's own Phase 1B probe, behind the same missing arm. Every CSI reply decodes to **0 events**, so `synchronisedUpdate` and `keyboardProtocol` are one C02 ruling away from being measurable and `imageProtocol` is two · **Open** — measured, not landed: the fix is a string-sequence arm in C16's `decode.ts` and then `q=2`→`q=1` in C09's `kitty.ts`, two components away from where the finding was written · **Closed** (F1043) — the arm landed, and three of this entry's own statements were corrected by measuring rather than by reading: the class is **five** ECMA-48 introducers and not the three that happened to be queried, **both** terminator forms occur and XTerm mirrors the query's, and the `BEL` form closes with `Ctrl-G` rather than `Alt-\`. Its *a blocker on asking rather than a live defect* is retired by **F1045**, which is the same bytes arriving through a paste with no terminal asked anything

**F1043** — **a terminal's answer arrived as a keystroke, and the arm the ladder was missing is five introducers wide, not three.** F1035 named the fix as a symbol — a string-sequence arm — and the symbol was three names short: ECMA-48 gives five introducers, **both** terminator forms occur, and which one you get is not the terminal's property. XTerm 398 **mirrors the query's terminator** — three `BEL`-terminated OSC queries came back `BEL`-closed while its DCS `XTVERSION` came back `ST`-closed **in the same capture** — so an arm reading one form is wrong on one of the two emulators here. And the `BEL` form closes with **`Ctrl-G`**, readline's `abort`, not the `Alt-\` F1035 named. Eight real replies: **164 events → 0** · **Closed** — the arm, a 256-byte cap whose argument is direction rather than odds, and the walk's row f: **the hazard is created by the fix and not repaired by it**, which is a shape neither artefact indexes because both index the accepted paths. Twelve mutations, twelve killed — the survivor indicted the test (`toEqual([])` is the same green for *consumed* and for *still waiting*) and then a sentence of the spec written an hour earlier

**F1045** — **I12 held on one ESC arm of four, so a pasted hyperlink dispatched four bindable keys and lost two bytes of its payload.** `decodeCsi` checked `paste.mode`; the SS3 arm, the Meta arm and the lone-`ESC` window did not. A pasted OSC-8 link — `ls --hyperlink=auto`, `gh`, `delta`, a `PS1` carrying `OSC 0` — emitted `Alt-]`, `Alt-\`, `Alt-]`, `Alt-\` **before** the paste event, and the payload arrived with its `]` and `\` already eaten, so C17 I5's one-undo-unit rule is applied to text two keystrokes have been taken out of. **Live at HEAD with nothing asking a terminal anything**, which retires F1035's *a blocker on asking rather than a live defect* · **Closed** — the branch moved into `bufferPayload`, which every ESC arm calls before it emits, the `CSI` arm included: **a reimplemented rule keeps its birthday clauses**, and a second copy is exactly how the `CSI` arm came to be the only one that had it. Four mutations, one per arm, each killing T3.19 on that arm alone — a helper applied to three arms of four passes every row about the other three

**F1024** — **the verb's result was never lost; it is above the greeting, and the greeting's own append scrolls it off.** Eighteen PTY captures. `grep hunks` hits the byte stream in **every** capture including the ones that "lose" it, and three PageUps find the diff as the transcript's *first* entry with the banner below — so the framework did what it was asked and the frame is the only thing that disagrees. It is F158's mechanism: the async greeting appends whenever its producer resolves, and a ~33-row entry in a 35-row region scrolls everything before it off. Greeting lands at 2.58–4.47 s, the verb at type_at + ~2.5 s, once **152 ms apart**; the prediction *the final frame holds the result iff the greeting landed first* holds **12 of 12**. F813 had measured the wrong side of the race — `/config`'s fetch, not the greeting's · **Closed** — built as C22 I99, and the prescription landed without amendment: `session.ts` reserves a streaming entry synchronously before awaiting the producer, and `greeting(doc, slot)` settles into that id. Three seams, four rows, each mutated until it failed and each failing alone; dropping the reserve puts the greeting at row 54 against the result at 41. The walk added two clauses the diagnosis did not have — a rejected producer releases the slot, because C13 never evicts a streaming entry, and a `/clear` in the window makes `settle` answer `unknown`, an outcome this call site was discarding. C22 I44's *produces no entry* was falsified by the fix and corrected in place. 458 golden frames move by zero. Stated blind spot: the addendum's other symptom — `/ps` never echoing, a command sitting unsubmitted — did **not** reproduce in any of the eighteen captures and is not covered here

**F1057** — **the reply channel's blocker was never the input path, and the record is spent before a reply is readable.** F414 deferred on an arm in `decode.ts`; the arm landed and **nothing about detection moved**. A reply is unreadable until raw mode — **0 bytes in ~1 500 ms** of canonical mode on both emulators, against sub-millisecond on XTerm and 13–23 ms on kitty raw — and the bytes are **held and echoed** rather than dropped, arriving in full the instant raw mode is entered while the line discipline draws them as printable text. Raw mode belongs to `acquire()`, which runs after the record is built, read at eleven sites and handed into six objects, and C22 I58's cache key omits it **because** it never moves. So a capability answered from the wire has nowhere to go · **Closed** — ruled, with the reason **replaced** rather than added to and both weak reasons priced in the spec: not danger, because C16 I32 makes a reply harmless, and not latency, because a DA1-terminated burst is one round trip. **The watch is on every route into raw mode**, not on the token. And the mutation that survived is the finding inside it: the control matched the **wrapper's** call sites, so deleting the syscall left it green — a control that matches the wrapper has stopped watching its subject

**F1058** — **the planned probe's window prices the terminal's silence where a sentinel prices the capability's.** §8 has said *an opportunistic 50 ms query* since it was written and the number was never measured. A burst closed by DA1 resolves when the slowest **answering** query has answered — under 1 ms on XTerm(398), 13.3–23.4 ms on kitty under software rasterisation — and **a silent capability costs nothing**: XTerm answered neither of two queries and its DA1 came back in the same read. So only a terminal answering no DA1 at all pays a window, and 50 ms is **barely twice** kitty's local worst case in a file whose own paragraph names remote sessions as the motivating environment. **A short window does not fail loudly; it keeps the guess** · **Closed** — both rows rewritten

**F1059** — **the plan's own choice of query is what put the plan behind the missing arm.** §8's stated blocker was that the version query's reply is DCS-shaped and unreadable until an arm exists. True, and self-inflicted: it is the only one of the four whose reply is DCS-shaped, **and it answers a name where DECRQM answers the capability** — *identification is not capability* is C02's own §3 heading, unapplied to §8. §3's table had already measured DECRQM answering on both emulators, including the reply meaning *not recognised*, which is still a reply. Anchored there the plan would never have been blocked on the decoder: the CSI arm has swallowed a DECRQM reply since it was written · **Closed** — the row rewritten

**F1071** — **the deferral's instrument had an output file, the file had no reader, and the file was not in the repository.** F1060's remedy built: `probe.py` gains an optional argument and defaults to `results/<terminal>.txt`, three records land tracked — Ghostty 1.3.1, kitty 0.41.1 and **XTerm 398, which speaks no graphics protocol at all** — and TP7 reads every one back through `detectCapabilities`, failing by filename when a recorded verdict disagrees with `IMAGE_PROTOCOL`. **XTerm is why the comparison means anything**: with three agreeing records a comparator that fires only on *measured ahead of the table* is indistinguishable from one that also fires on *the table claims what the terminal refused*. TP6 counts the arms because an exit status is the same bit for clean and for an empty corpus, TP8 shows the comparison firing in both directions, and a fabricated `wezterm` record turned TP7 red carrying the whole verdict. Five mutations, one survivor, and the survivor was a finding about the row's *message* rather than a licence · **Closed** — and F1060's own verdict cell was wrong in the direction that mattered: `result.txt` was gitignored and untracked, so the evidence was absent from every clone rather than merely overwritable. C02 §3 corrected with it — it named **Konsole**, which `TerminalName` has never had a member for. Limits recorded: TP7 reads one line of each report, and the `iterm2` and `sixel` arms are unreachable from any query the probe sends

**F1074** — **a finding number was allocated, never spent, and this row is what stops it being handed out twice.** Four numbers were pre-allocated to round 8's Lane P against two subjects, because two lanes discovering the same next number is a collision nothing detects; three were used. Nothing was measured under this one and nothing should be. **A number that names nothing is worse than one that names its own absence** — without the entry the next round hands F1074 to something else while a lane report already spends it, and SP5 reads any `F\d+` as a citation because it cannot tell a citation from a disclaimer · **Closed** — F1040's precedent applied to the other cause: that one was spent and folded, this one was never spent at all, and both want the same record because a finding number is a name in a namespace every document shares

**F1060** — **the deferral has an instrument, the instrument has an output file, and the file has no reader.** The image-protocol table's comment distinguishes itself from a hope — *run the probe there and read the verdict* — and the verdict lands in a file the repository holds, from the run that put Ghostty on the kitty arm. **Nothing in `src/` or the suite reads it**: the only mentions outside the tool are two lines of ledger prose, and the probe's own eight rows assert the build script's **source text** rather than any verdict. A single output path also means a second terminal's run overwrites the only evidence the table rests on · **Open** — right about the instrument and silent about the loop, so the expiry still depends on a person noticing. Closing it wants a per-terminal results directory and a row that fails when a recorded verdict disagrees with the table, which is a build rather than a ruling · **Closed by F1071**, which also disproved this row's own first clause: the verdict landed in **no file the repository held** — `result.txt` was gitignored and untracked, so a reader on CI or a fresh clone had no evidence at all, and the single record the image-protocol table rested on existed on one laptop. Wrong in both directions, and the reason it was unread is stronger than the reason given

**F1094** — **a missing package arrived as a colour reading of minus one, twice, and this reader had already been wrong this way once.** The first `full` job this branch's work has ever had said *the red picture drew: expected -1 to be greater than 1000* on two rows that drive a real kitty under Xvfb and count coloured pixels — a sentence about a terminal that failed to draw, where the fact is a package that was never installed. `image-protocol.test.ts` screenshots the root window with ImageMagick's `import` and counts with `convert`; the job installs `xvfb xdotool xterm kitty` and **neither of those**; both are spawned with `stdio: "ignore"`, so a missing binary is silent, no PNG is written, and the reader's sentinel for *no screenshot* is minus one in all three channels. **Nothing here is a defect on its own** — the ignore is deliberate, the sentinel honest, the assertion right — and what is wrong is that **the sentinel covers more failures than it names**. **Deterministic, which is what separated it from the noise in the same job**: `full` failed twice on this head and the failing set moved between samples, a kitty CSI-u row red then green and a scroll row green then red, while these two were red in both with the same numbers — the stable pair is environmental and the moving set is F812's. **Invisible in the devcontainer because the image has both**, `/usr/bin/import` and `/usr/bin/convert`, so the rows are green locally every time: the same shape as F1087 and F1093, a fact about the machine discoverable only on the machine that was never allowed to disagree, third in a day. **And the file records its own first instance of the shape** — `drive`'s comment says a latin1 write once made the probe read *zero coloured pixels in every case including the controls*. Same reader, same silence, different cause: **three distinct failures reach one number**, no screenshotter, no screenshot, and a screenshot with no colour. **Open** — the package in the job, and a precondition refusing by name before anything is driven, so minus one keeps the meaning it should have and loses the one it should never have carried; driven with a shim `import` that exits one, the rows now fail naming the tool · **Closed** — `image-protocol.test.ts`, two rows, twenty-nine point two seconds green on the runner, on pull request 46's `full` job and on `main`'s own run after the merge. **A fact about a machine is closed by that machine and by nothing else**, which is what F1087 and F1093 both had to wait for and is why this class took three instances in a day to see; the devcontainer's greens were as green before the fix as after it. `mutation-sweep.yml` deliberately does not get the package — that decision is F1095's sixth instance, and the mechanism rather than the list is what covers it

## 9 · The instrument was wrong — **closed** · 68 closed · 67 with no verdict · new at F80 · **six more from the residue pass, two of them the brief's own grep · two from the owed three, both reporting before they had a subject · five from the seven owed items, each a fixture or row measuring something other than its subject · four from the three lanes, three of them a check whose expectation comes from its own subject · three from the three owed items, one of them the framework's own width measurer used as the authority for a width · seven from lanes5, three of them an instrument whose corpus or anchor was not the subject it named · eight from lanes6, two of them a gate that read a Unicode code point as a citation and invented a finding · two from lanes7, one of them a mutation that indicted its fixture rather than its rule and one an anchor that only re-running could tell from a live one** · **one from the deferral backlog, a third state the mutation harness had no name for** · **one from the uncited-invariant backlog, a fake reimplementing the rule it stands for and inverting it** · **one from the profiler's owed list, a row reader that could not match a vitest summary carrying a failure (F949)** · **three from the six open items — a registry with no `plot` measuring a pane's JSON (F959), a PTY painter that paints `DECSCUSR` as text (F966), and two mutations written to be quadratic that were linear (F958)** · **two from the T1.83 diagnosis — a replay stand-in that read no clock and was served every later value two reads stale (F963), and a red row whose whole evidence was *expected false to be true* (F965)**

**F1014** — **a memo turned a timing row's instrument into a cache-hit timer, and the row kept passing because two numbers below the clock's resolution divide to anything.** T3.15 asserts that laying out a megabyte is linear: time the half document, time the full one, require the ratio under 3. It printed **`0.00 ms → 0.00 ms, ratio 6.75`** — and the same comment block records the figures it was built on, *320–390 ms and 645–880 ms*, so the file carried the before and the after of its own decay with nobody reading them together. **The earlier repair wrote down the premise that broke**: the minimum of N is the least-contended estimate *of a deterministic computation*, and `displayRows` now returns `this.layout(...).length` through a memo keyed on text, width and gutter — so readings two through five are **cache hits** and `Math.min` takes one. Measured: **0.0005 ms shared against 259.53 ms fresh**, five orders of magnitude, which is why it rounds to `0.00` rather than looking fast. **The diagnosis it invites is the wrong one** — this reads as group 12's contention class, and contention only decides which side of the bound the noise falls on. **One of its two assertions still worked**, the row count, because a count is not a timing, and one live assertion beside one measuring nothing reads like two. Repaired with an editor per reading, three rather than five at **4.78 s of a 10 s budget**, and a floor — *the smaller walk is above the clock's resolution* — whose fabricated violation fails at 0.0005 against 1. **The general shape**: a row that divides two measurements needs a floor on the denominator, because `expect(ratio).toBeLessThan(3)` is satisfied by two zeroes. **Closed**

**F958** — **two mutations written to be quadratic were linear, because a V8 slice is a view and `containing` is lazy.** The cluster cursor's pass indicted two of its own rows before it indicted the code: the mutation meant to restore F937's cost copied nothing, so the linearity rows stayed green against it and would have read as a rule with no violation. Replaced by `graphemes(rest)[0]`, measured quadratic, every expectation in the run applied by hand. Closed.

**F959** — **F947's height was the instrument's.** The 20/26/28 rows F947 carried were measured through `createBlockRegistry()` bare; `table`, `plot` and `patch` are not C09 defaults, an unregistered kind falls to `raw`, and a plot measured as the wrapped lines of its JSON. Through the registry `construct.ts` builds the overview was 40 rows at twelve frames and 51 on a full report — and `test/support/overlay.ts`'s registry has the same gap, so T1.98 and T3.14 were windowing JSON. Closed: the pane's tests build the registry that draws it, and the fixture gets the three definitions.

**F966** — **the PTY harness paints `DECSCUSR` as text.** `test/support/pty.ts`'s `COMPLETE_ESCAPE` accepts a CSI as parameters then a final letter and has no intermediate class; the cursor shape is `ESC [ Ps SP q`, so F962's drive read the prompt row as `❯ [0 q`. The framework's tier-5 rows write the shape only at start and release; docker-tui changes the beam at the prompt. Closed: three copies, not two, built from one alternation now with the general CSI grammar; PS16 feeds the beam and the kitty keyboard protocol's private parameter, which the old class did not name either.

**F963** — **the profiler's replay was two wall-clock reads behind its recording.** C06's subprocess transport reads the session's wall clock twice per invocation, before spawning and for `durationMs`; `replayTransport` served the recorded value and read nothing, so under positional clocks (C28 I14) every later read was served the value the live session took two reads earlier — the resize repaint's header got the answer frame's last stamp, and a wall-clock second boundary in that ~30 ms gap was `:27` on one side and `:28` on the other. Red only inside chains because the gap lies between two of the session's own reads and widens with load; reproduced alone five times once the verdict travelled with the assertion (F965). Closed: the stand-in reads the clock where C06 does, and T5.1c asserts read parity by count — 52 against 54 before, 54 against 54 after.

**F965** — **what the red row could not say.** Four red instances of T1.83 and T5.1b, and the sum of their evidence was *expected false to be true*: the fixture's verdict went to a stderr the reporter dropped, and the recording sat in a `mkdtemp` nobody could name. Every replay row now carries `explain()` — both paths, the drive and replay verdicts, the clock counts, each PTY read's arrival and size, both event orders — and `CALCIUM_VERDICT_LOG` keeps a green row's figures too. Three assumptions measured false on the way: `^D` never ended a recording (C16 I16 opens a confirm), the far side was never the slow part, and the profiler file ran fifth of nineteen rather than last. Closed.

**F971** — **the mono residue was the sampler, and a periodic reader cannot sit on a positional channel.** Every mono read tagged by its caller, live against replay: `clockCostNs` 2 000 and three more at construction on both sides, spans 438 against 438, frames 8 against 8, and the sampler's stamp 2 live against 0 replayed at three seconds, 5 against 0 with a three-second hold — one read per second of session, F963's mono half. Not a count to match: a tick lands at a different position between two sessions and shifts every read after it. Closed: `ProfileOptions.sampleClock`, the untapped `elapsed` the root's config hands down, the recorder stamping the sample and `node.ts` reading no clock (C28 I53); T5.1c asserts mono parity beside wall — 2 908 against 2 908 at a second's pause, with a three-second hold, and behind a 1.3 s far side.

**F974** — **a stand-in that answers when asked never draws the wake frame.** T5.1d records behind a far side sleeping 1.2 s, so the readout wakes and a frame sits in the recording ahead of the `far` line; the replay's transport served the answer at once, that frame was never drawn, and the row diverged at frame 5 with `stalled` 3 and both channels short. Closed: `driveRecording` releases each far answer in turn through `farGate()` and the stand-in awaits its release; the fixture's tick bound raised to 3 000.

**F1018** — **exactly two places in this repository parse a vitest summary to decide pass or fail, and both had two outcomes where there are three.** The census the register kept asking for: `mutate.mjs` and `instruments.mjs`; `waitfor.mjs` reads a sentinel and `proof.sh` greps npm, so neither is a reader. F897's mechanism reproduces on demand — `process.kill(process.pid, "SIGKILL")` inside a test, vitest 4.1.10 — and yields `Tests 2 passed (6)`, on which `ran`, `unbuilt` and `killed` each answer **correctly** and compose into `SURVIVED` for a run that lost four of its six tests. **Every part is right and the composition is the falsehood**, which is why no assertion about a part could see it. The signal is the arithmetic, `reported < collected`, not the `Errors` line — and F929's owed count turns out to live on vitest's own summary bracket, per run, needing no golden 562 at all. `tally` and `incomplete` were added with an `INDETERMINATE` row, and refusals at the clean **and** control moments, because the control pair alone is satisfied by a blind run: a walk finding no classification table reaches that, and it is the third state collapsing onto *nothing to do* for the third time in this register · **Closed** — F929's other half was already fixed by F949 and is extended rather than replaced; its residue was that the counter read the buckets and not the bracket, so a crashed child read as *errored after its rows*, and the sixth state is `lost rows`. Note the conflation F929 and its brief both inherit: `profile.mjs` reports nothing — `instruments.mjs` was the reporter

**F1041** — **four residues all said *and nobody wrote it down*, and three of the four wanted an assertion rather than a sentence.** F608, F606, F774 and F873 share a shape — the rule is right, the instance is fixed, the convention is in no document — and taken together they answer a question this register keeps asking badly. Only F608 was really prose. F606 wanted a refusal, and **its first draft fired on four legal constructs**: a plain comma test reads `"C1 (R1.2, R5.1)"` — a row name followed by the requirements it cites, and the verbatim title of a real test — as two rows, which is a gate that gets an exemption and stops being read, caught by the corpus and not by review. F774's *writer still unnamed* was unanswerable, because **no file in this repository chmods anything**; the answerable question is whether the set is what we think it is, and the census found fourteen executables, thirteen with a shebang and one without, whose mode had flipped three times including once mode-only in the commit closing F56. F873's refusal is sound and written in **three** places, each naming the same symbol, and the day that directory lands all three become false together with no gate saying so · **Closed** — one sentence in `CLAUDE.md`, a paren-depth refusal in the anchors sweep, an expiring assertion on C26's exemption, and a new file asserting the executable set by equality with a reader control, since both of its rows are satisfied by a reader that returns nothing. **My own edit broke a mutation anchor and the sweep caught it in the same run**, which is the instrument answering for its author

**F1046** — **four of the five disagreements a residue left open already had their ruling on the record, by that residue's own standard.** F934 resolved SS37 against a spec citation and then did not reapply the method to the other five. SS33 resolves to `C01 I9`, whose `why` clause is the rule word for word — I8 being about `release()` while suspended; SS35 to `C04 I26`, where C04's commitment 31 already says *enforced by SS35* and the old `C04 §4` both resolves and is titled *4. Patches*; SS48 wanted a findings citation; SS59 to `C28 T2.4b`, T2.4 being SS58's row. **Only SS56 needed an A03 edit and there the code was right.** The census moves 31/11/4/1 → **35 agree, 11 code-more, 1 contradiction**, and *five contradictions* turns out to be four plus a one-directional gap — a bundled row, where N subjects means N blockers and the shared one is rarely any of them. **And `A03 §701` does not exist**: F934's closer cites it in three places, all the same sentence, and the passage it means prescribes no generated column — invisible because SP8's corpus excludes the ledger · **Closed** — a reconciler with its debt list compared by equality in **both** directions *and* on the separating tokens, four fabricated violations including an empty-corpus vacuity control, and a per-row corpus guard. Thirteen mutations, **one survivor**, which found a dead branch in the reconciler's own tokeniser; repaired and re-mutated red

**F1047** — **a residue that is reported and not gated is a ratchet, and the ledger recording it sits outside every rule that could check it.** The unowned bucket went **574 → 622** while the row read as addressed; a counter that is printed and never compared records drift rather than resisting it. Off one green `enforce` run the class is **six counters on one screen and exactly one compared by equality — SP9's, which is the one sitting at zero**, so the only counter that cannot drift is the only one with nothing to drift. Two measurements the entry did not have: **2 462 finding citations in `tools/` and `test/` — 19% of the repository's — are outside SP5's corpus**, and they are clean today, so widening lands green rather than red on arrival; and **SP5 cannot see a collision**, only an absence — `F164` is duplicated in the ledger, the first of the pair a truncated stub, which is precisely the failure mode a double-allocated finding number produces. Three other apparent duplicates are the recorded follow-up convention, and checking that is what stopped a false claim · **Closed** for this file's own slice, 3 → 0; the corpus widening is named with its figures and owed in a file this lane did not own

**F1048** — **the roadmap's citation checker cannot see the table where seven of seven citations are wrong.** One evidence table, seven citations, none resolving — and one of them on a blank line. `roadmap-status` exits 0 because **63 of 145 citations are outside its corpus**, so the checker is green on a document where its subject is 43% unread. That is the gate-phrased-over-a-corpus class: a rule inherits its corpus's blind spots, and a corpus that covers 57% of the document is a rule that covers 57% of the claim · **Closed** — measured with the figures; the corpus fix is one edit in a file this lane did not own

**F1049** — **cross-reference into F1047, and the reason it is not its own entry.** SP8's bare-§ resolver and SP8's owner arm are two halves of **one printed sentence in one file**, so they are one ratchet measured twice: the dangling arm went **134/83 → 321/170** on the same run that took the unowned bucket 574 → 622. Filing them apart would have produced two entries citing each other and one fix · **Closed** — recorded here so the number resolves and cannot be handed out again

**F1050** — **the corpus a negative claim was closed with is checked by nothing, so it lost a kind while the claim stood.** F427's rule is exact — *for a claim of the form nothing does X, the file holding it cannot be the one making it* — and its own residue named one absentee. Measured: **two**. `patch` and `terminal` are both outside the corpus, and **nothing anywhere compares `CORPUS` to `DIVIDES`**, so the set the claim is quantified over is maintained by hand and read by no rule. A negative claim inverts a resolver's verdict: *there is no X* passes hardest on the day the corpus stops containing X · **Closed** — the comparison is the remedy and it is named with both absentees

**F949** — **F929's `0 rows` was the harness reading `1 failed | 5 passed` as no counter at all.** The runner's vitest reader was `/Tests\s+(\d+) passed/`, which cannot match a summary line carrying a failure, so exactly the runs with one failing row read as *no rows*: 556 against 562 is the whole file's six read as zero, not one row lost. `execFileSync` throws on a non-zero exit and the harness already read `e.stdout`, so nothing was starved and nothing was lost — it was misread. Run alone under a saturating load (12 `yes` loops on 11 cores, load 16–17) it reported 562 rows, all green. The reader now names five states, distinguishes *did not run* from *diverged*, and the runner has its own fixture (IN1–IN7). What T1.83 and T5.1b diverge on after a `make e2e` is still open, in group 12 · **Closed by F1038** — it was closed by **F963, forty rows further down this same file**, a read-count asymmetry under positional clocks, and the residue sentence outlived its answer by one day. A residue named in a summary is a claim like any other; this one cost a grep.

**F936** — **the profiler's largest number was the one it could not decompose.** Run to re-derive its own round's defect list, `make profile` reported `assemble` at **358.2 ms of self time, 58.4 % of a frame's work**, against `react`'s 151.9 — overturning the plan's prediction that React+Yoga was *almost certainly where the frame goes* — and then could say nothing more, because `assemble` was one span over row-building, compositing and the SGR base pass. Its own comment defends that, correctly, about the **name**; what the name being right did not settle is whether the span should have **children**. P4 had specified the split and that half never landed. Splitting it moved 48 % into `body` and left `assemble` at **0.8 ms**, `composite` at 0.7 and `based` at 0.3 — so `assemble` *was* `body`, and the two parts named beside it are a thousandth of it each. `body` then held 48 % with nothing under it, so the same question applied again: `visible` (the transcript actually rendering) is **5 %** and `transcript` (fitting rows to the width) is **53 %**, which is F937. **Three levels to reach an actionable line, and the tell each time was one span holding half the work.** Nothing in the suite could say so: C28's rules ask whether a span is *opened* (I39) and *when* (I41), never whether it is *divisible*. Read shares and never absolutes — four runs of one fixture measured 533, 613, 710 and 854 ms of work, a 60 % spread on the machine F929 records as contended, and every ratio here is stable across them. **Closed**

**F926** — `localContext`'s `ask` is the seam a consumer's handler asks a question through, and C24 I26 says it defaults to the **decline** path — *"C23 I36's own semantics, so a handler tested without a scripted answer takes the route `Esc` takes rather than a stub's."* It took the **first** choice. The shell's rule is `defaultStart`: *the marked one, else the **last***, with the reason written down — *"for a destructive verb the safe option is conventionally last (`yes`, `no`), and a default that silently means the first thing offered is the wrong way for this to fail."* On `[{yes},{no}]` unmarked the shell answers `no` and the fake answered `yes`. **The justification is true and answers a different question**: *"`ask` resolving with nothing is the second representation of nothing happened that C23 I36 exists to refuse"* argues against returning `""`, and not at all for first over last — the class CLAUDE.md names, where review checks whether a justification is true and this one is. **And it is aimed at the population it hurts**: `defaultStart`'s own note says every caller in this repository marks a default, so the two records cannot disagree inside the repo, and the callers who forget are consumers, which is who `src/testing/` is for. A handler tested here deleted where a user pressing `Esc` would have cancelled. `confirm.ts` had already written the rule about its own two copies — *"a question that opens on `no` and escapes to `yes` is the worst possible pair"* — and the fake calls `defaultStart` now. C24 I26, T2.16. **Fixed**

**F922** — `c21-pty.mjs`'s wrapped-error row reported **SURVIVED**, and the summary said what it always says — *a finding about the tests, or about the sentence they were written from*. The `to` opened a `try` it never closed. **Three of four suites failed to collect and the fourth ran green**, so vitest printed two lines that disagree: `Test Files 3 failed | 1 passed` above `Tests 10 passed | 2 todo`. `ran()` found a summary; `killed()` found no failing test; both answered correctly and nothing asked whether the suites had **loaded**. **The third instance of a class this file has grown twice already** — `NO SUMMARY` for a buffer cut under `pipefail`, `ANCHOR MISSED` for an anchor rewritten by the change it was written for, and now `DID NOT BUILD`: each a state that is not a kill, is not a survivor, and had no name, so it was counted as the only other thing available. The signal is the **disagreement between the two summary lines**, not the `Transform failed` above them — that wording is esbuild's, and a module throwing at import fails identically without it. The same gap sat one level up: the clean-tree gate calls `ran(clean)`, which a tree with one unloadable suite passes, and every mutation that suite covers then reads as a survivor (MH8b). MH8's fixture is the captured bytes, with a real kill and a real survivor as controls, because a predicate answering true to everything reads exactly like one that works. **And the probe nearly cleared the harness**: run over two files that both import the broken module, *all* of them fail, no `Tests` line is printed, and `NO SUMMARY` already covers it — the new state lives exactly in the middle, where enough of the suite still runs to look like a result. **Fixed**

**F728** — widening `RC3`'s collision threshold from `SVG_FONT_SIZE` to **four times it** was expected to suppress uncontended labels and fail the row's set assertion. At the fixture's `height: 8` it killed **nothing** — all 173 rows passed — because the right column's tick pitch there is **137.6 px** and four glyph heights reach no neighbour. **The mutation was indicting the fixture, not the rule.** `RC3` gained a second fixture at `height: 40`, where the pitch is 15.29 px, and the same mutation now kills `RC3` **alone** — which is the row's real point, `RC1` being satisfied by a right column that has been deleted. **A second prediction was wrong the other way**: inverting the side was expected to leave `RC1` standing on the reasoning that the right column would be clean, and it does not — the right label survives *and* the callout still lands on it. Only running the pass said either

**F730** — `c12-svg-right-margin.mjs`'s `RM3` anchors on the `walk(...)` call, which gained the row collector under this change. **Re-anchored and re-run by hand rather than re-anchored on faith**: it still kills `RM3`, and now `RM4`, `RC2` and `G11a` with it. **A stale anchor and a dead mutation read the same green**, and the harness reports whether an anchor *exists* rather than whether it still selects the mechanism — F712's finding one turn along, with the re-run as the thing that distinguishes the two. A sweep of every `from:` anchor in the seven run files naming `src/presentation/plot/svg.ts` found no other stale one

**F693** — `isWide`'s 17 hand-written blocks were expected to be an incomplete list, so a repair is a union. Against the property: **8,619 code points are `W` or `F` and measure one cell** in 65 runs (Tangut 7,529, Kana Extended and Nushu 687, Yijing 64, Hangul Jamo Extended-A 29, ~35 emoji singletons) **and 369 measure two cells and are not `W` or `F`**. The second number is what refuses a union — **eight of the 369 are U+3248..U+324F, which the property calls Ambiguous**, so the coarse `0x3041..0x33ff` was over-counting *inside* an under-counting table, and a union satisfies every row about the 8,619 while keeping all eight. **Deriving both tables from one file makes the disagreement impossible rather than fixed**: the property's classes are disjoint and `WIDE_RANGES ∩ AMBIGUOUS_RANGES` measures **0**. The width measurer used as its own authority, one table on from F682; F697 is the deviation's figure moving under it

**F695** — the generator read `; W` and `; F` rows, and **the property's `W` defaults live in header prose above `@missing: 0000..10FFFF; N`** — the unassigned code points of three CJK blocks and everything undesignated in planes 2 and 3. A row parser cannot see them. Checked: in 17.0.0 those blocks are listed in full, so the defaults contribute **0** beyond the rows and the generated table is complete today. Recorded in the docstring anyway, because the day a revision stops listing them a row-reading generator loses tens of thousands of cells **silently and in the under-counting direction** — a premise that is true now and whose falsification is a data release

**F699** — T1.28's first draft took **one representative code point per run**, 27 of them. Collapsing `0x17000, 0x18cd5` to `0x17000, 0x17000` — which removes **7,382 Tangut ideographs** from the table — left **all 36 rows green**, because every representative had been written as its run's *first* member and a collapse onto element zero keeps exactly that. *Assert the set, not its first member*, arriving in a table of ranges. Rewritten as 28 `[lo, hi]` runs asserted at **both bounds and the midpoint**; the mutation now fails T1.28, and the mirror mutation (a collapse onto the *last* member) is row 5 of the run file

**F700** — writing `U+F900..U+FAFF` in a docstring — the third of the property's default-`W` blocks — was expected to be ordinary prose. `make enforce` reported **two SP5 violations at that line**: the pattern excludes a preceding word character, so `0xF900` and `\uF900` are exempt because `x` and `u` are letters, and **`+` is not**, so the code point read as a citation of a finding past the end of the ledger. The collision set is small — `U+F000..U+FFFF` written in that form — and `src/presentation/text.ts` is the one file in the tree likely to write one. Worked around in the lane by naming the block; F723 is the fix and the reason it was made a named lookbehind rather than a wider class

**F708** — the geometric row `RM1` — *every string inside its own viewBox* — was expected to be the row that fails if the margin fix is reverted. Measured by hand: setting `rightRoom` back to 0 fails `RM2`, `RM3`, `RM4`, `RM5` **and `G11a`** and leaves **`RM1` green**, because the `fitLabel` at the drawing site still cuts the callout to the page — the frame reads `alp…` rather than running off. **Only reconstructing both halves of the shipped state** — fixed margin *and* no cut — fails `RM1`. Reverting the advance to 0.6 also fails it, which is what makes the bound load-bearing rather than decorative. A containment row cannot see a reserve going missing when a second mechanism hides the overflow, and the mutation is the only thing that says which row holds which half

**F712** — `c12-arm-seam.mjs`'s gutter anchor is a one-line string and had been unique for as long as it existed. **Growing the right margin with the *same expression* on the other side of the box made that line match twice** — the anchor check reported `1 ambiguous` and the mutation would have silently mutated whichever came first. The run file was not edited and its anchor was still correct English. Second instance of F679 in two passes, from the other direction: there a new *function* wrote the same line, here a **correct symmetry** did. Re-anchored on the gutter's own preceding line

**F720** — T1.36 derives the admitted picture alphabet from the shipped goldens rather than from the constructors' own tables, and was expected to cover the alphabet. Hand-running the `ALPHABET-WIDE` mutation — widen `isPictureGlyph` to *anything not alphanumeric* — **T1.36 survives** and T1.35 fails. **A row indexed by what the corpus contains sees a narrowing of the admitted set and is blind to a widening of it**, because everything the corpus holds is still admitted. Both rows are load-bearing and neither substitutes for the other; recorded on the mutation entry rather than repaired, the asymmetry being a property of the instrument and not a defect in the row

**F723** — A03 SP5's citation pattern excluded a preceding **word character**, so `0xF900` and `\uF900` were never citations because `x` and `u` are letters — **and `+` is not**, so `U+F900` read as a citation of the finding those digits spell and the rule reported a citation some two hundred past the end of the ledger, on a docstring naming a block of the East Asian Width property (F700). **This is the loud failure rather than the quiet one**: it invents a citation rather than dropping one, which is the only reason it surfaced at all — the quiet twin, a real citation the pattern cannot see, is what the other two exclusions do and nothing would report those. Fixed with its own named lookbehind `(?<!U\+)` rather than by adding `+` to the class, so **the exclusion says which form it is about**; a fabricated violation and a control both die when the lookbehind is removed, and F700's body carries `U+F900..U+FAFF` in the ledger as the live control

**F672** — the mutation's `expect` anchor named **T2.4** — the row that recomputes every floor from the shipped tokens — as the check that would kill the wide arm. T2.4 stays **green**: it recomputes its own ratios and never calls `validateTokens`, so a theme that is *rejected* does not fail it. What fails is everything needing a store, first among them `T2.1 (I1): a thousand calls return identical styles`. **A rejected theme is not a failing ratio**, and an anchor written from the rule rather than from a run names the wrong check as the one holding the line

**F673** — `slotsByHex` in `test/contract/theme.test.ts` assumed a hex names one slot. I17 forbids two slots of **one** palette sharing a value and says nothing across palettes, and the shipped set has **8 cross-palette collisions on light and 10 on high-contrast** — `tone.default` = `syntax.punctuation` = `spectrum.outline`, `tone.muted` = `syntax.comment`. A `Map<hex, slot>` keeps whichever came last, so **T2.27 had been resolving axis furniture (`tone.muted`, floor 2.5) to `syntax.comment` (floor 3)** and asserting a number it did not choose. Nothing failed because both floors clear on the shipped values. Found by T2.29b on its **first run**; the map returns every slot now and T2.27 takes the strictest floor
