**F942** — **the cap's two measures are two questions, and the repeat P11 read there is one line up.** `#form`'s `total` is measured of the resolved block and decides whether a form exists; `shown` is measured of the block `window` returned and is what the marker prints. Two blocks, two questions, the second taken only over the cap. The repeat is `#measured` re-measuring after `#form`: an uncapped windowable block is measured twice per `measure` and three times per `render`, a capped one three and five, so F914's *measured once* is false. Ruled as C09 I62; the adjacent repeat is recorded and not fixed this round.


**F914** — P11's seven measured defects, resolved against HEAD and then measured. Three had already been fixed or were never defects (the `flatMap` at F889, `based()`'s per-call regexp, and `#form`'s two measures, both of whose answers are used); three were real and cost **1.2% between them** — `editor.layout` at 4.97 calls a frame for 30 ms of 4986, `group.place` 18 ms, overlay placement 8 ms. The top three costs are `react` 44.9%, `assemble` 25.3% and `local` 18.5% and **none of the seven is in any of them**: a list built by asking *what runs more than once* finds what runs often, which is not the set that costs. **The one that mattered was invisible to the instrument that found the others** — the O(entries) scan at `session.ts:1293` has no span, because it is a loop body's first line rather than a phase, so no table sorted by cost could show it; it was found by resolving the claim against the tree, which is F884's method one round on. And **invisible to the fixture**: at the tool's default 400 entries both fixes move the median frame 2.48 → 2.30 ms with the run spreads overlapping, and at 4000 they move it **4.22 ms ± 0.07 → 2.41 ms ± 0.04**, three runs a side with no overlap, total span time **69.8 s → 49.8 s (−28.6%)**, `assemble` halved and `react` untouched. Nearly all of it is the index; the `layout` memo is the 0.6% and is kept on the argument rather than the figure

**F916** — P11's defect 2 was real and its remedy was measured after being built rather than before: a `(block, width)` memo in C09's registry reached a **75% hit rate** — 577,071 hits against 188,258 misses, every miss `absent` and not one `width` — and bought **34 ms of 49,811**. The `measure` call count does not move at all, because the span brackets `#measured` and the memo returns from inside it, so the bracket survives every hit and only the walk is saved; 577,071 hits against 34 ms is **59 ns per avoided walk**. The whole-session difference is 4.2% against a without-arm spread of 5,246 ms, which three runs a side cannot separate. **Reverted** — 52 lines, an invalidation on `register()` and a new coherence hazard in the layer everything draws through, for 0.07% on the one span that can see it. *A fix that changes nothing indicts the diagnosis*, pointed at the diagnosis: the defect was correctly identified as repeated work and wrongly assumed to be expensive work. **A hit rate is a property of the access pattern, never of the saving**

**F902** — the profiler's `it.todo` rows were grouped by the clause after their marker: 38 rows, **33 naming a condition already met**. Read as a work item that is 33 conversions; resolved per row against the tree it is not. Two of the 33 name a subject that does not exist — `ChromeContext.lastFrame` (C24 I32) has no member, and C22 I95's resolution was never written (F901) — so a met condition splits into *the row that is now writable* and *the row whose real blocker was never stated*, which look identical from the marker and of which the second is the expensive one: it reads as ready, and whoever picks it up writes a test against a member nobody built. **A shared blocker is accurate exactly while it is the only one** — every row took its neighbours' clause in the commit that wrote them, when nothing distinguished them — and it stops being accurate silently, one row at a time. **The count is not the finding**; the instrument is per row: resolve the subject, not the marker. Both were built rather than re-marked
**F727** — F713 said `gutterRoom` reserves from `labels` while the emitter writes `labels[i] ?? String(tick)`, so a short `labels` array reserves for nothing and draws a number — with **no instance in the corpus**, which is what made it look like a latent defect rather than an impossible one. **Asking where the claim was written down**: `ValueAxis` is produced in exactly one place, `valueAxisOf` returning `{ ...axis, labels: tickLabels(...) }` with `tickLabels` mapping over `ticks`, at three call sites and with **no object literal anywhere in the tree**. So `labels` is total over `ticks` **by construction**, the fallback branch is unreachable, and **the two reserves are the same function** — the mirrored fix would have changed nothing, a fix indicting its diagnosis. **Wrong in both directions, which is the shape**: the hazard is real and stated differently — a **silently ellipsised** gutter label if the two ever part, `fitLabel` cutting rather than overflowing so an under-measured reserve reads as a shorter number — and nothing watched it. F713 corrected in place with its claim kept visible; `RC6` watches the hazard now

**F694** — narrowing **369** code points from two cells to one was expected to be the dangerous half of the repair, an under-count being what wraps a row. Measured rather than argued: **302 of the 369 are the text-presentation emoji of plane 1** — U+1F321..U+1F32C, U+1F5A5..U+1F5FA and neighbours — which the property calls Neutral because a terminal draws them one cell until a variation selector asks for the emoji form. Cross-checked against `emoji-data.txt` 17.0.0: **of the 369, none has `Emoji_Presentation=Yes`**, so no glyph a terminal draws double-width lost a cell, and `cells("\u{1F321}\u{FE0F}")` is still 2 through `clusterCells`'s existing promotion. The remaining 67 are unassigned gaps the coarse blocks swallowed

**F696** — `DRAWN_AS_GEOMETRY`'s recorded justification carried **its own re-check condition** — *C12's and C09's gates read this answer, `halfBlockEligible`, the line-draw and ramp arms … the day one takes the capability directly, its range is dead* — which is a deferral naming its blocker as a symbol. Measured: `isAmbiguous` has **exactly one caller in the whole tree**, `clusterCells`, four hundred lines above it, and `halfBlockEligible` takes `caps.ambiguousWidth` as a parameter at `halfblock.ts:80`. **The condition had already been met and nothing was watching it.** The list is not dead — the measurement claim above it still holds — but the claim it rested on was a gate that does not exist, which is a smaller and different argument, and the docstring now makes it

**F702** — goldens were expected to move in the narrow arm, that being the convention every golden is rendered in. **Zero**, and measured twice: scanning every file under `test/golden/` for a code point whose width changes in either direction gives **0 files affected**, and running `npm run golden` with and without the change gives the identical result — 406 of 407, the single failure reproducing with `text.ts` reverted to HEAD and therefore another lane's. The affected characters in the tree are `⚡` in three test files and one docstring, `✨` in `tools/spinner.js`, and prose in `docs/`. **The exposure is entirely far-side text**, which is the argument the docstring now rests on instead of a literal count of the tree's own characters

**F706** — the brief said *every SVG golden moves, because the layout moves*. **10 move, 234 are byte-identical**, and `SB3` names the same ten. The reserve is zero where nothing is drawn on the right, so a figure with nothing to reserve for does not move — **which is the half of the rule that would otherwise make it the wrong rule**: a margin that grows unconditionally is a different change from one that grows to fit, and only the second is what shipped

**F715** — *`sankey.ts` and `scatter3.ts` both reach the background through `slot(...).colour`* is stated twice in C10 and repeated in I35's limits paragraph. Measured: **`sankey.ts` contains no `slot(` call at all** — it names `ColourRef`s and the resolution happens in `definition.ts`'s `sankeyRows`, a different file — and `scatter3.ts`'s four `slot()` calls are **every one a foreground**. By the time a cell is built it holds resolved `ColourValue`s, and under the default `colourBy: "depth"` those come from a colormap, which is §4e's already-admitted case. **Three documents cite the situation and none cites a measurement**, and the mechanism named does not exist at one of the two sites. Corrected in §4f and §4c.1's table; F714 is the same sentence's other half

**F671** — deleting `validatePalette`'s `carries === "decoration"` skip was the simple rule, and its cost was expected to be an argument about art rather than a number. Measured: light's `spectrum` fails **7 of its 9 stops** against `bg` — 2.36, 3.06, 3.07, 3.30, 3.51, 3.88, 4.00 — and lower again against `bgElev`; dark and high-contrast clear. Applied by hand the **light theme stops loading entirely**, ten rows of `test/contract/theme.test.ts` failing together with a purity test first. **I31's own measurement arriving from the other side**: a floor deletes the low end a sequential ramp exists to have

**F677** — C10 §4 calls `surface.bgElev` *every panel, overlay and confirm* — the second surface text lands on, and the reason a floor checked against `bg` alone has a gap. Measured: `grep -rn '"surface\.' src` returns eight slots and **no `surface.bgElev` at all**. Nothing in the tree paints it, so the floor checked against it is a claim about where blocks land rather than a measured site. Kept in the new pairing because it costs nothing (worst 4.74) and the alternative is §4's own named gap — but **stated rather than assumed**, an invariant being vacuous until its subject exists

**F680** — the count in F665 was restated three times and measured against a narrower authority each time. Against `EastAsianWidth-17.0.0.txt` itself — 2,721 lines, 2,678 explicit rows, single `@missing` default — the accounting is: four Ambiguous marks already in the table (right), **three** Ambiguous in the Latin-1 gap (`§` `·` `×`, the defect), two Neutral reporting 1 correctly (`«` `»`), and one Neutral **over**-counted (`⚠`, 2 inside the geometry deviation). So three of ten, not five of ten and not four of eight, and a fourth mark wrong in the opposite direction. **The conclusion survived all three counts**, which is why none of them was checked; F690 is the lesson. Now asserted one mark at a time (C09 T1.27c), because a total is satisfied by the wrong three

**F657** — F635's rule was motivated by a duplicate row name, and the interesting number is the yield beyond it. Measured: **zero**, in the tree and in its history — 183 mnemonic rows in 23 families across three specs at HEAD with no label declared twice, and **358 commits** touching those specs swept with no duplicate. The `SK10` collision F635 records lived only in lane S's working tree and was renamed to `SK11` before it landed, so **no citation in the tree ever pointed at an ambiguous name**. A clean negative rather than an unchecked one, and not a verdict: the population the gate protects is **666 mnemonic-label citations** across `test/` and `tools/`

**F640** — `pairedRows` is a fourth walk of the run grouping F595 asked to fold in, and it is **deliberate**: it reads `lines` and allocates nothing, which is what keeps `measure` as cheap in split as in unified (C09 I1), where `changedRuns` allocates two arrays per run. A recorded divergence with nothing recording it — someone applying F595's argument one function along would put an allocation on the per-block-per-frame path. The sentence is requested at `height.ts:60`

**F645** — the lane brief said *base commit 106fc7a0 … every gate green … golden 407 frames*, and `npm run golden` in the shared tree failed SB3 with six moved sankey SVGs before the lane touched anything — another lane's in-flight `NODE_LABEL` re-ink, confirmed by reverting `text.ts` to HEAD and seeing the same six. With the brief taken on trust, six moved SVGs read as this lane's blast radius and the honest answer — three snapshots, no SVG, no terminal baseline — would have been lost inside them. One 40-second run

**F601** — the roadmap's *a 50,000-row table has to be measured before C14 can place it — so bound it* named the wrong cost: whole-block `measure` at 50 000 rows is 0.6 ms for `table`, 0.2 `logs`, 20.5 `raw`, 17.7 `code`, and a cap **cannot** bound it because *of 50,000 rows* is what `measure` computes. What the cap bounds is paint, ~0.25 ms a row. C14 §4b carries the table and the correction

**F619** — the brief said *the types and the gate* for I93 were done, from the base commit's message; `validate.ts` still refused any non-PNG `data` under *phase 1 reads PNG only*, so no GIF document could enter a session. Found because the first session-level row was refused before it could render; T2.39 never hands the validator a GIF, so the suite was green with the gate closed. **Fixed** in the merge (F628)

**F620** — the 2026-09-03 re-ruling priced the kitty arm as *every tick is an image upload* without taking the figure: a per-tick retransmit would be 29 662 B at 320x240, 297 KB/s at 10 fps; kitty's animation protocol uploads 116 509 B **once** and 0 B a tick, less than the GIF's own base64. The design the refusal costed was one nobody would build, and the measurement chose a third option it did not list. Roadmap bullet moved to *Already shipped*

**F573** — the GIF refusal rested on *a frame scheduler built around one frame per input batch*, false since C03 §3's `stream` at 33 ms and the orbit's continuous redraw, and on a *90% for 5%* ratio never measured. Measured: `omggif` 38.5 KB, 0 deps; the cost is the `frames` carrier and a frame index on the orbit's wake, ~350 lines. Re-ruled as a cost refusal against zero consumers; R18/R19 registered

**F574** — sankey's refusal cited *edge routing wants a real layout engine* after `graph` shipped a layered router and `elkjs` was in the tree. Measured against `graph`: the layering transfers, nothing else does — no edge weight, a node is a row not a bar, a ribbon is a fill not a mask bit, the axis is the other way. Ruled in C12 §3d as a new form, `sankeyArea`, refused on no consumer; R20/R21

**F575** — `DEPENDENCIES.md`'s *87 packages* counted an install into an empty directory — eslint's own subtree and 74 packages already here; the lockfile delta on this tree is **13**. The row carried it through four re-readings and a trigger that passed four times. Re-refused on the measured grounds: 106 findings and 0 defects over 333 files, and a peer range `<6.1.0` that `ERESOLVE`s against the tree's TypeScript 7.0.2 — the peer-range half is group 14's shape and is not counted there. R11 rewritten, R11b registered, reopen conditions written

**F577** — *every mutation is reversible, and nothing currently is* — the editor has had two undo stacks, structural coalescing and `UNDO_LIMIT = 200` since C17 §6. Narrowed to transcript mutations, which have no inverse: `evict`, `ViewPatch`'s six ops with no delete, `settle` freezing. R22

**F534** — 2-D `Series.marker` was attributed to 1274eb27 without a `git log`; it was 127f19b1's, accepted by both gates and drawn by none. Deleted from `Series`, `SeriesOpts` and the spread; golden 403 tests identical. **Fixed**

**F540** — `keymap.ts`, C22 I75 and commitment 46 all say `←`/`→` fall through to the prompt at `liveBlock`; `dispatch` has no prompt step from there and both keys were dropped — carried through two rulings, measured by nothing until a binding wanted them. **Fixed**

**F551** — SS50's three allowances were said to be dead because the files spell `ambiguous` zero times; for a negative-lookahead rule that is the condition under which they are live. All three measured live by SS53, one firing line each

**F564** — four e2e rows deferred on premises false when written — *nothing pushes a view layer*, *the subscription rung is unruled*, *nothing can ask for depth 1* — each answered by a mechanism already in the tree. All four live, each killed by disabling its mechanism against a rebuilt `dist/`; `ps-watch` added because the view had been opening on the corpus's own refusal. **Fixed**

**F507** — F469's performance rows do not reproduce, and **the controls are what make that a finding rather than a slower box**: `P1` (projection) and `trianglesOf` touch no sample grid and come back at 0.9× and 1.1×, so the host is not the difference. `P2` (raster) is 2.6–3.3× and the whole frame 3.5–4.6× — 12.969 ms measured at **44.9**, 124.2 at **572.6** — because F498 multiplied the sample grid by eight and no budget was re-taken. **The ruling the numbers were taken for gets stronger and its reason gets weaker**: auto-orbit is off on *39% of a core*, and one frame is now past the 33 ms window on its own. `tools/bench/` holds four benches and none is 3D, so §12 has been a written tier with budgets phrased as assertions and nothing asserting them — which is how a budget goes stale in the direction nobody checks

**F503** — the orthographic projection divides by nothing, so the unit cube measures `−0.187 … 1.187` at distance 4, 6, 10 **and** 20 — four cameras, one answer, 37% of the figure off the plot and no member a caller could set to frame it. The licence was **one line of a type comment** — *`distance` is ignored by the second* — which one grep of `docs/`, `src/` and `test/` returns as its only instance, with no invariant, no argument and no row. **C12 I86 already contradicted it** in a document that cites C04 §3 by name: a `distance` of zero draws nothing on this arm too, and it does so by moving the eye. Measured, `distance` moves an orthographic sample's *depth* from 2.77 to 18.77 while its screen position does not move at all — **true about the scale, false about the member**, and that gap is the size of the defect. The remedy is an *equality* rather than a bound: both arms fold one focal length, so dividing by the distance to the target makes them the same expression at the target plane, where every wrong scale that happens to fit is inside a bound. **The cheapest instance of the instrument yet** — one grep, one line, the contradiction already written down one document away

**F463** — *an open surface's signed volume is exactly 0, which is why the volume test is only available where `closed` is given* was carried into step 7 as settled, with a measurement behind it: a flat quad, `0.000000`. **A 9×9 Gaussian measures 0.1742 and a 21×21 measures 0.1785** — it converges rather than vanishing, because the sum is the cone volume from the origin and only a surface through the origin cancels. **The zero belongs to unit space rather than to openness**: `unitOf` centres each axis on the extent, a planar patch's extent-centre lies on the patch, so every planar surface normalises onto a plane through the origin. Three of six fixtures returned zero for that reason and read as corroboration. It moves the disposition — `closed` is **refused at the gate** on the height-field arm rather than detected at runtime. **The record it was carried on was a previous turn's probe**, which is the weakest form and reads like one of the strongest, because it arrives with a figure

**F448** — the design note's §4 says *compute which of the eight box corners is furthest from the camera and draw from there*, **so the axes never occlude the data**. Implemented literally it draws them through the middle: measured over all eight vertices, the far corner projects to screen **(0.500, 0.527)** — the exact centre — and the corner near in x and y and far in z projects to **(0.500, 0.888)**, the bottom vertex of the outline, where every 3D library a reader has seen puts x and y. **The rule is wrong and the reason is right**, which is the shape worth naming: F84's class is a justification that survives being read carefully, and this is a justification that is *sound* while the rule attached to it does the opposite. Neither half is catchable by reading — the note has been read many times — because they only disagree once something is drawn. **Both are still three sign tests**, so F444's point survives as *one source of truth, two derived answers* rather than *one answer, two consumers*. **And a second half measured on the way**: the edge-on degenerate is **orthographic**, not perspective — a line along the view ray keeps 3.37 cells under a divide because its near end is nearer and therefore larger, against 0.00 without one, so the rule fires at zero under one projection and hands off to the collision rule under the other

**F440** — `CAMERA_DEFAULT.distance` was `10`, written on the commit that ruled the camera type, one step before anything could project. **There was no way to be wrong about it**: no frame, no assertion, no consumer — an invariant is vacuous until its subject exists, and so is a constant. Step 3 gave it one and the picture filled **11% of an 80-column frame**. Measured over the normalised cube's eight corners and a 400-point sphere shell, at three elevations x two azimuths: 10 fills 50% of the height, 6 fills 67% and is the largest that clears the edge, 5.5 already touches at two of six cameras. **The number is a property of the normalisation and not of anybody's data** — `unitOf` maps every axis to `[-1, 1]`, so the bounding sphere always has radius `sqrt(3)` — which is what makes it measurable rather than a taste, and the corners are the binding case where the shell never touches at all. **Corrected to 6**, sweep recorded beside the constant. The old value was not wrong in any way a reader could check, which is the whole finding.

**F441** — adding one member to `PlotForm` produced **23 compile errors in `src/`** and 5 more in `test/` and `tools/`, one per total `Record<PlotForm, …>`. **`scatter` is the wrong answer to eight of them** — `HAS_VALUE_AXIS`, `ORIGIN_DEFAULT`, `HONOURS_AXIS_CROSS`, `HAS_Y_GUTTER`, `HAS_X_TITLE`, `HAS_CALLOUT`, `HAS_POSITION_AXIS` and `STYLE_ARMS` — and the first is F330's exact class: a `Figure` holds one `value` and a 3D scatter has three ranges, so copying the neighbour's `true` would have been caught by luck, since `FV1c` only forbids *ramp and value axis together*. **A `Record` forces an answer and cannot check it**, which is the mechanism's whole limit; F330 measured it from the other end, as five cells that stayed silent for as long as their forms were refused. Answered on their own reasons and the reasons recorded in the cells.

**F432** — a written decision not to rule, because *"ruling it needs a measurement this repository cannot take"*, and the instrument named was a probe run **on the same Ghostty**. **The emulator is irrelevant**: tmux's output is what reaches it, so if the bytes never leave tmux there is nothing for an emulator to do — measurable with a pty and no emulator. Measured on tmux 3.5a with two controls: unwrapped APC **absent**, `ESC[?2026h` **absent**, DCS-wrapped APC **present at tmux's default**. Both capabilities claimed from the identification were false inside tmux and neither is a near miss. Ruled as one gate on the identification, joining `colourDepth` and `mouse`. **The reusable part: a blocker naming *whose* behaviour is unknown can name the wrong party, and it is usually the far one** — ask which process the bytes stop at.

**F425** — the source of F423's misdirection, found by following the citation chain. A note reasons that a drag is cheap *because virtualisation makes a repaint forty rows* — true about the **write**, silent about the **measure**, which a width change makes `O(transcript)`. **Virtualisation is what makes the two diverge**, so the property cited as the reason for cheapness is the one hiding the cost: 544 ms per drag. Five restatements inherited it — the note, A01 D31, C01 T3.19, three sites in C03, and a `RangeError` message — **all citing C03 I2, which names two reasons and not three**, and none of them a violation `make enforce` can see. **D31's wording forbade the fix and its purpose required it**: a debounce waits for the events to stop, a fixed deadline does not, and *continuously correct during a drag* survives one and not the other. **And the blind spot is the walk's** — both artefacts index the component's rules, and an architecture decision one layer above is invisible to either shape.

**F423** — roadmap 19 names a resize drag's cost as *a full Fenwick rebuild*, and the tree is **0.07%** of it. Measured against `dist/` with a monotonic sweep: **544 ms for a 30-event drag at 1 000 entries**, of which the rebuild is 0.007–0.013 ms; the cost is `#cache.clear()` forcing a re-measure of every entry. **The finding is the entry's own falsification condition** — *if a rebuild at a realistic transcript size is sub-millisecond the whole thing is moot, and the honest answer is a recorded negative* — which is rigorous, numeric, falsifiable and **aimed at the 0.07%**: following it literally closes a live defect. It also redirects the fix, because `construct.ts:914` runs `viewport.resize` **before** `scheduler.commit("resize")`, so the C03 window the entry proposes saves the writes and the renders and none of the measures. Underneath: C14 virtualises the render to `O(visible)` and the index still needs every height, so **a width change is the one event costing `O(transcript)` rather than `O(screen)`**. C03 I2's text names `input` and `completion` only, and three sites cite it for `resize`; `make enforce` resolves the citation and cannot check what the invariant says. **Caught before anything was built on it.**

**F416** — fixing F415 did not change the frame: `docker exec` does not propagate `TERM` or `TERM_PROGRAM`, so the container reports `xterm-256color` and C02 answers `none` **correctly**. Two right rules in tension — *run everything in the devcontainer* and *detect the terminal from the environment* — with a boundary the second cannot see across. **Fixed in the harness**, and it is why F415 hid: every reading of this demo has been through a container, so the protocol arm could not have run even with the detection right.

**F417** — the half-block frame is 48681 B against braille's 8141 and kitty's 19048, 82% of it escapes, re-emitted on every scroll frame. Reported by a reader scrolling rather than by any gate. The saving that is unexploited is emitting a colour only when it changes; **not taken**, because F416 moved the reader onto a different arm and tuning against a frame nobody is looking at is the wrong measurement.

**This group is the disposition.** The first five entries are findings that were filed,
believed, and then turned out to be wrong — and a log that quietly dropped them would read as
*eighty-four found* rather than **seventy-nine found and five disproved.**

**F161 is the sixth and it is the group's first arriving from the other end**: the instrument
was pointed at a claim *before* it became a finding, so what is filed is the disproof rather
than the belief. That is the group succeeding rather than growing, and it is worth
distinguishing in the count instead of folding into it.

| | what was claimed | what measuring said |
|---|---|---|
| **F17a** | a live part should freeze on append | freezing it would have broken C23 I9 — **not a defect** |
| **F21b** | the fork was drawn wrong | a design-authority question; the source corrected the drawing — **not a defect** |
| **F339** | C12 §2's closed list of legitimate differences | The row reads *an SVG stroke is a **ratio of the box**, never a constant, or it changes with the output size*. **Twelve `stroke-width` sites in `svg.ts` and every one is a constant** — 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2 — and every one is right: a `viewBox` maps user units onto the output size, so a constant in user units already **is** the ratio. The sentence names the property correctly and forbids the only implementation that has it, and obeying it would be worse — `layout.width * k` gives a `pairplot` sixteen panels of sixteen line weights. **The list's own purpose is to be what a fifth difference is measured against**, so it is the one artefact here that cannot be checked by finding a second copy: there is only ever one. Measured in a grep. **Fixed — the row now states the constant it is** |
| **F58** | `?? 0` reports signal-death as clean success | `authoritativeMeta` overwrites `exitCode` on every route: `999` yields **0**, `SIGTERM` yields **143**. The five coercions never reached a document — **retracted, superseded by F58b** |
| **F66** | docker refuses to remove an image a running container references, and it cannot be forced | `rmi` **untags** without `-f` while the container runs — **retracted**, then the retraction itself amended, because what decides it is whether the tag is the image's *last* reference |
| **F68** | the completion menu paints no background and the transcript reads through it | every cell of the box is written — measured by diffing two captures row by row, columns 0–81 on all three rows — **withdrawn**, published in two READMEs first |
| **F161** | a hanging-continuation mark has four consumers, one of which ships | the character is in **no file** in the repository, and none of the four could take it: one already has a `Glyph` slot, one is text inside a `code` block, one has no renderer, one was a homonym — a log *level* named `trace`. **Caught before it was filed** |
| **F168** | roadmap 44 is C20's persistence one level up, so the shape carries | **the policy carries and the redactor does not.** C20 redacts a *command line* with a tokeniser; a transcript document holds what the far side **printed** — and `inspect.ts:152` puts every container environment variable into a `keyValue` block. A rendered document has no tokens to redact, so finding a secret in one means understanding all seventeen kinds. And a transcript row *mutates* after it is written, which append-only with an index-aligned sidecar assumes it does not. **Caught before a line of it was built**, on a row already corrected once this session |
| **F238** | the error box's message cap is four lines | **four, and the width it binds at is the one nobody looks at.** Wrapping `plot failed to render: …` at the top rung's content width: a typical message is 1 line at 120 columns, 2 at 80 and **3 at 40**; a path is 4 at 40; a three-frame stack is 3 at 80 and **6 at 40**. So four holds a whole stack trace at 80 and **a path and nothing else at 40** — the cap binds exactly where the room is least. **Kept at four and not width-scaled**, because the cap is a property of the reader rather than of the terminal, with the cost stated instead. **Caught before it was fixed**, on the instruction that the number was a guess — and a second, stronger argument for it turned up while confirming something else (F239): capped at four lines the box is at most seven rows, so the worst over-draw inside a bounded container is bounded by a number rather than by the length of an exception |

| **F234** | the elapsed counter's 1 Hz write is the weak joint — a document write, a `rev` bump, a cache miss and a re-render every second | **0.4 frames a second.** Ten seconds of fake time: a spinner alone writes 55 frames, a spinner plus a 1 Hz patch writes **59** — six of the ten writes coalesce into a frame C03 had already scheduled. **The counter is cheapest exactly when it is visible**, because a loading status animates and the ticker is already armed. Two control arms make the number readable: nothing animating and no patch is **0**, a patch alone is exactly **10**. **Caught before anything was built on it** — and the guard survives on a *hygiene* argument instead, since a write changing nothing observable is still a `rev` bump that invalidates C14's height cache. The frame read in the same pass settled `H=2` and turned up one real defect the arithmetic could not: `retrying` without `retryInMs` draws no activity line and therefore **no spinner**, which is every one-shot's failure |

| **F166** | roadmap 44 needs a document serialiser and `src/` has none, so 44 is blocked on 34 | a `ViewDocument` is **JSON by construction** — no function, `Map`, `Set` or `Date` anywhere in the block union — and it round-trips byte-identically through `JSON.stringify` and `validateDocument`, which C13 already calls on every append. What is missing is **one export line** and a round-trip row per kind. Three documents restating one unmeasured belief, and a conflation underneath it: 34's *structured export* is a UX item in a six-item bundle, and 44 read it as a codec. **Caught before anything was built on it** |

**Four of the six were disproved by one instrument, and it is worth stating as a number.**
*Going to find where the claim was written* has now **disproved three claims and produced
four** — F58b, F66's replacement reason, F92 and F161. **This line and `CLAUDE.md`'s had
already drifted apart by one before F161 was added** — F92 was counted there and not here,
which is two records of one number doing what two records of one fact always do. Corrected in
both, and the drift is left recorded rather than tidied away: it is the same class the group
is about, arriving in the group's own summary. Seven results from one habit that costs twenty
minutes makes it the highest-yield thing in the toolkit, and it is the only instrument that
checks the *record* rather than an artefact. The frame-read checks output. The mutation pass
checks tests. The audit checks code. Nothing else asks whether a belief has a source.

**All three were wrong in both directions, which is the shape to watch for.** F58 was not
broken for the reason given *and* concealed a real defect nobody had stated. F66's
impossibility was real and the reason given was not — a container pins its image blob by
digest, which is falsified only by a container outliving its own image rather than by any
docker release. F68's measurement was accurate about the wrong thing: zero background
sequences is a true fact about a capture, and a space written in the default colours *is* a
written cell.

**Repetition is not corroboration.** F58 lived in four documents that cited each other and
never held a measurement; F66 was carried across four steps and was never written down at
all. Four restatements of an unmeasured claim are one unmeasured claim.

**And F68's rule is the one most easily missed**: *a symptom named in a spec is evidence the
authors thought about it, not evidence they got it wrong.* C15 I29 describes text bleeding
through a menu in as many words — as the reason the implementation writes every cell.

---

**F801** — session-mouse rows T4.62c–T4.71c were declared in no spec — C16 §10's tier-4 list stopped at T4.68; reconciled by the lead

**F808** — the mouse-mode pair measured on xterm 398 under Xvfb: one tracking mode, the later select wins, either release clears it; the probe captured nothing until `timeout --foreground`, so every capture carries a typed control

**F975** — **the card on the wall clock survives T5.1d, measured.** The brief said a card put back on `deps.clock()` would fail the replay row; it did not — a positional channel reproduces whatever is computed from it, whichever clock that is. The row that sees the axis is the one that skews one clock alone: T4.68 for the card and T3.64 for the readouts, each red under the restoring mutation. Recorded in T5.1d's spec row and the run's comment, where the prediction had been.

<a id="11"></a>

**Keyed here by the F142 inventory sweep** — the second cohort, filed after this document's own count was last derived. Placed by mechanism from the entry; SP6 proves coverage and not placement, which is its stated limit.

| | why this group |
|---|---|
| **F89** | an Order entry carries a claim its own document records as corrected |
| **F93** | C09 §4a promises a registration path that was never built |
| **F112** | F21's four claims all held, and the fix was a tenth the size |
| **F123** | a promise needs three mechanisms and the finding that named it found one |
| **F134** | corrected twice; the drift is real and shipped by a route neither reading found |
| **F139** | the rule was in the file header, and a finding re-derived it wrongly |

| **F184** | **the palette indexed the row, and the rule saying it should was a code comment** | *A plain bar is one series across N categories and the category **is** what a colour can name* — `definition.ts:998`, and a grep of `docs/`, `src/`, `test/` and `tools/` returns that line and nothing else. True about the grouped bar it was written for; general by nothing, and enough to give a histogram's eight bins eight colours for one distribution. **The correction then over-reached, which is the finding under the finding**: eleven reference renderings draw one colour per series, that fits every row and is the references' *taste* rather than the rule behind it, and taking it whole took the colour off every named band too — reported by the user in the same turn, twice, and correctly. A measurement settles what is true and not what to draw. The partition is the **row axis**: a bin and a lag are cuts this renderer made and have no identity; `control` and `Opex` are names the caller chose and keep their slots. **Fixed** — C12 §3t, I38 and `ROW_IS_AN_IDENTITY`, total over `PlotForm` and deliberately not `SHARES_CELLS`, which is indexed by form so a plain bar and a stacked one share an entry. Eleven mutations caught, and the survivor took two wrong diagnoses before the fixture became a pair |
| **F242** | **`layered` collides like `force` does; two sweeps is better than one** | **Neither, measured over 360 graphs before the design rested on either.** Collisions are **0 at every density and size** — a layer's labels sit side by side, so overlap is not expressible and the failure mode is overflow, which has an answer. That is the same question the `force` refusal turns on (one label pair in six at n=20), re-asked of the layout being kept and answered structurally. And the sweep count is non-monotone: at near-path n=50 two sweeps **raises** the mean, 36.5 to 38.1, worse in **17 of 40** trials. **Ruled** — two sweeps with best-kept, which makes it monotone for one crossing count a sweep; past two the gain is 3-6% where the first buys 80%, and at the sizes that fit a terminal (n ≲ 20) sweep 2 is already at the floor. **And a third gap in the recipe, from checking the instrument rather than reading it**: reversing a two-node cycle onto an edge that already exists duplicates it — 24 in 360, drawn twice and counted twice, and nothing in *layer, order, two sweeps* asks |
| **F244** | **`position: 'absolute'`, before a mosaic's spec rests on it** | **The pinwheel composites whole, honours a declared height exactly, and is a pure function of `(block, width, ctx)`** — 10 renders, 1 distinct, against an impure control reporting 10 of 10 so the count means something. So the mosaic is **not** a `raw`-block path and D2 can be written. **Probe 0 is the one that could have cancelled the phase**: the pinwheel admits no guillotine cut at all, while a *five*-rectangle **slicing** control cuts at `x=1` — same count, same tiling, opposite answer, so the difference is the figure and nested `group`s cannot express it. **Two carried claims corrected.** The key is `(id, rev, width, focus, theme)` with `key\0range\0offsets[\0tick]` in the focus slot — `(entry, rev, width)` is `HeightCache`'s triple, and range, offsets and a per-kind tick are folded in already, so nothing is owed at the call site. And the scroll renderer's *`height` on a Box clips **bottom-anchored*** does not transfer for the reason it appeared to: **the anchor is not the position type, it is `flexShrink`** — one child of five rows in a box of two draws `r0,r1` relative-unshrinkable, `a0,a1` absolute either way, and **`r2,r4`** relative-shrinkable, which is no anchor at all but a drop from the *middle*. The decision stands; the wording understates the fault it is arguing against. **And the frame found what sixteen agreeing `measure`/`render` pairs could not** — at width 40 the figure is **60 cells wide**, because an absolute child is not clipped to its parent's width and C09 I1 is about rows (C09 I34's *over-draw in the other axis*). `overflowX: "hidden"`, which the group renderer already carries, brings it to 40 at no cost in rows |
| **F246** | **a four-item queue, three of which were already answered** | **And the three are stale in three different ways.** *S5*: C23 §3d-bis says the fields *had no producer **until now***, and the carried claim is that sentence without its last two words — both arms are built, frame-read and driven end to end rather than at the seam, `T3.56` saying in its own comment that it writes through `declare` and the sweep and never calls the writer. F86/F89/F92's compression class a fourth time, and the cheapest: the body was right and the summary dropped the tense. *F240's residue*: `selection` has no 4-bit map entry and **is painted anyway** — `selectionStyle` falls back to reverse video at its call site, measured `undefined -> {inverse:true}` at 4 and 1 bit. **A missing map entry and an unpainted surface are not the same fact**, and it is the *better* answer: inversion guarantees contrast against arbitrary text, which is the exact constraint F240 named for a wash and which a curated index cannot meet — so one case already has the mechanism the other was deliberately denied, which is a stronger *these are not one case* than the finding had. Ruled: **the entry is not added**. *The label pass*: `shiftInward` resolves to the deferral comment that names it and nothing else; it is a C12 subsystem, reported at its size rather than attempted at the queue's | **3 closed by measurement, 1 built** |
| **F247** | **the roadmap's *measurement is free*, before images rest on it** | **Closed** (F986) — C09 §4c carries the measurement, taken 2026-08-31 on Ghostty. **The risk was where it could be checked.** The row is true of the terminal and is a claim about **three** width implementations: `cells()`, which every `measure` uses (SS23); Ink's, which lays out the Box; and the terminal's. Two are reachable here and they are the two most likely to be wrong about a plane-16 private-use character. Measured: `cells()` gives **1** per `U+10EEEE` and **0** for the row/column diacritics, Ink lays out exactly what `cells()` measures at n = 1, 2, 4, 8, and the text path survives the surrogate pair — `stripControl` leaves it and `truncate` does not split it. So an image declares a height in cells and participates in the grid; nothing floats. **Blind spot stated**: the third implementation is the protocol's guarantee, not this repo's. **And `.length` is exactly 2x wrong on an image** — SS23's rule with its first systematic subject, where every previous instance was incidental. **A literal BEL reached the probe by being typed** and only the width disagreement surfaced it (F236's class, smallest scale) | **gate open** |
| **F248** | **the three measurements images phase 1 rests on** | **All three came back the way the plan needed, each with a control.** *A partial row rewrite*: Ink re-emits the **full frame** — 96 of 96 placeholders on a one-row change of twelve, 12 cursor-ups, 13 erase-lines — so an image is cheap and the grid never has to survive being written a row at a time. **The first run reported `NOTHING re-emitted` and that was the harness**: a fake stdout without `isTTY` makes Ink write nothing at all, so a zero was indistinguishable from a full frame of nothing. *Truncation and the window*: no cluster split at any width 1-12 at either unicode arm, and a windowed row still names its own row — safe for two different reasons, `truncate` being grapheme-aware and each cell naming row **and** column. **This is the failure mode no other kind has** — every other block degrades by losing content and this one would degrade by addressing a different part of the image. *The decoder*: an 8x4 PNG from `sharp` reads back as `IHDR pHYs IDAT IEND`, and `pHYs` is the useful part — a decoder skips ancillary chunks rather than assuming `IDAT` follows `IHDR` | **shape holds** |
| **F309** | **a new tool: `svg-catalogue.mjs`, phase-catalogue widened to the full corpus** | **It exists**, as `tools/svg-baseline.mjs` — one `.svg` per form·variant over the whole of `CATALOGUE_FORMS`, 178 frames, into a **tracked** directory rather than a gitignored one, with a derived expected count (F256) and **a placard already written for every refusal**. That is §2 almost word for word, and the plan named it NEW two lines under its own instruction *extend the existing tools rather than writing a third*. **The condition was written where the deferral is and the satisfier somewhere else** — CLAUDE.md's table, a fourth row, and reached from the satisfier rather than by anything watching the condition. **The narrower true statement changes the build**: the SVG corpus is text and a paired sheet needs pixels, so what is owed is a compose pass over two already-gated corpora and **no `svg-catalogue.mjs` at all**. The same reading retires the plan's third digest — a hash over *derived* pixels moves whenever either input moves and says which of the two only by not saying, over corpora whose own gates name the frame |
| **F318** | **a `drops` member on `Figure`, named in the design and absent from the tree** | **The tree is right and the design was wrong**, which is the reverse of how a declared-but-missing member reads. `dropped = Math.max(0, longest - layout.areaWidth)` — `longest` is a sample count and **`areaWidth` is cells**. §3aj hazard 3 is *anything measured in cells stays in cells*, and `G1`/`G1b` assert the shared layer's side **by arity**: `niceAxis(range, maxTicks, pin)` and `seriesRange(series, pin, bars)`, neither with a width. `notice` carries the count in its string, so both halves fail for one reason. **And the difference it was reaching for is legitimate** — the terminal drops leading columns because a cell is a quantum; the second arm scales 640 px and has nothing to drop, which is not *has not implemented dropping*. The row belongs in §2's closed list as *they differ and always will*, and fails the day the SVG grows a drop rule. **The ramp half stands and is open.** C23 §8a A4's shape in a type sketch: nine of ten members are properties of the block, so the tenth read as one · **Closed** (F991) — commits `6f556962`, `229b077e` — the ramp half is drawn at C12 §3ak.37; the `drops` half was already ruled |
| **F986** | **the open list, read against HEAD** | nineteen rows carrying *Open* or nothing: **fifteen were already closed**, one was a refusal written down twice and read back never, two are halves and one is whole. A row's status is written where the finding is and the work that closes it is written somewhere else — the deferral shape, in a status column. **680 of 975 rows carry no status marker at all**, so every heading count is computed over the subset that was ever annotated. **Recorded** — the remedy is the habit, not a gate: prose against a tree is the class the audit argues against automating |
| **F991** | **the whole register, read against HEAD** | five readers over the fourteen groups and the singles: **355 rows resolved, 296 already closed**, 7 refused with the refusal already written down, 25 partly, **27 genuinely open**. Counted over the whole file: 607 closed, 36 open, **335 carrying no verdict at all**. Every heading was wrong and all in one direction, because a heading is computed over the rows that were annotated and a row is annotated when someone happens to look at it. The nine corrections that matter are the rows wrong about *which* finding closed them — F342, F316, F135, F23/F129, F593's citation of F642's subject. **Recorded**, and the headings now carry a third column |
| **F866** | a plot's `measure` is O(1) by construction and the design's cost table said 57 µs — the overhead argument rested on a figure 58× out |
| **F884** | seven defects carried in a plan and four of their citations do not resolve at HEAD — three are line drift, one is a dissolution whose mechanism was already repaired, and the survivor drops the condition that makes it free on the arm every session runs |



**F482** — F431 chose the half-block rung by measuring what braille wastes on a **surface** — 6931 of 7254 lit dots interior, every one of them on — and the sentence it wrote is about *a 3D form*. **The question was never asked at the other primitive**, and the seam for the answer was cut at step 2 and never called: `Rung = "half" | "braille"` and `sampleGrid(w, h, "braille")` sat in `project3.ts` with no caller in `src/`. Asked — how much of the half rung's second colour does an **outline** figure spend? — the answer is symmetric: **5.6% to 31.1%** of an outline figure's half-block cells carry two distinct colours, against a shaded surface's **62.5%**, and the control is what says the low numbers belong to the figures rather than to the parser. The resolution it costs is `0.5000` cells of worst deviation against `0.2500`, mean per edge `0.3485` against `0.1965` — exactly half, which a 2× grid in each axis has to give and is therefore a check on the measurement as well as a result. **The class: a measurement that settles a question for one input, quoted as settling it for the type.** Nothing was misstated; the other half was never run, and the half never run is where the unbuilt arm lived

**F483** — C12 §3am refuses box-drawing joins with four arguments, and going to check them before building the arm, **one stands, one is the mechanism, one is circular and one is conditional on a premise the request removes**. The load-bearing one reads as the most decisive: *here the glyph arm is what a terminal gets when it cannot do colour. It is the coarser copy.* **Every clause is true — of the glyph arm**, which is `auto`'s floor, reached by degrading; it is false of an arm a caller names at 24-bit, which restores exactly the condition the paragraph says is gone. The sentence refuses the *route* and reads as refusing the *thing* — MG24's shape, and why it survived being read carefully. **The cheaper tell was argument three**: *the selector is not a member of this form* cites `STYLE_ARMS.plot3d = []`, whose own comment reads *a braille arm joins this entry on the commit that builds one* — a refusal citing a deferral as its evidence. **And the remedy was inside the refusal**: *a second depth rule, equal-or-nearer for the mask and strictly-nearer for the colour, on one buffer* — a complete design, written at the moment of refusing and read for four steps as part of why not. The habit: when picking up a refusal, ask which of its arguments would change under the request in hand

**F485** — the commit that retracted `STYLE_ARMS.plot3d = []` described the braille arm's cost as *the shading is gone* and its picture as a **solid silhouette**. Walked by hand one commit later, **both words are wrong in the direction that flatters the arm**: every interior sample sets a dot, `foldBraille` answers `⣿` — a full block — and the cell takes its nearest sample's shaded colour, so the surface is shaded at one colour a cell instead of two. **The real cost was already measured in the same commit** — F482's 62.5% of a shaded surface's cells carrying two distinct colours is exactly the fraction that loses information. **The wrong word implies the wrong repair**: *shading gone* reads as a reason to refuse the combination, where the picture is merely coarser in one axis. And it was reasoned from a right premise to a wrong conclusion — F431's *the interior samples are all lit and carry nothing* is true of the **braille channel**, which is binary, and stops being true the moment the cell also carries a colour. A sentence about one channel applied to a cell that has two

**F486** — `Point3Series` refused a `marker` field with a reason attached: *it would be a second writer on the channel the depth tier owns — the mark **is** the depth reading, so a caller's shape and the tier's shape are one cell with two claims on it.* **The table is `3 × 5` and the two index different dimensions of it**: `glyphRows` reads `table[tier][column]`, the tier picks the row and a name picks the column, and there is no cell with two claims on it. **A slip about a picture rather than about the code** — *the mark is the depth reading* is true and *only the depth reading* does not follow, and the sentence slides between them in one clause; a reader checking it against C12 I88 finds I88 agrees, because I88 is about the tier and silent about the column. **The test that says so had to be constructed**: five of six rows pass against a renderer that packed the marker over the tier, because they assert the shape is honoured, which is what the wrong implementation does. Only the row that fixes the column and asserts the *row* still varies can tell them apart, and it reads as the least interesting of the six. Same shape as F483 two files apart on one feature — F483's argument was true and conditional, this one false and always false

**F487** — adding one catalogue variant, the first to force the glyph arm at full capability, turned FV1 red with two offenders on a property `plot3d` has had since it shipped. **Two mechanisms meeting**: the reader's gutter is *text before the first box glyph*, and on the half-block arm a 3D frame's axis lines are `▀` so no row has an edge — on the glyph arm they are `│` and the billboarded tick left of one reads as a y-gutter label; and FV1 renders every variant at `FULL` only, where every existing `plot3d` variant takes the half-block arm. Twenty-two variants checked forty-four times, and the arm that would have shown the labels rendered zero times. **The record and the row answer different questions** — `HAS_VALUE_AXIS` is about the `Figure` structure and FV1 is about a frame; for twelve forms they coincide and for the one that draws its scales in the scene they do not. `HAS_Y_GUTTER` separates them, and its size had to be measured before it could be taken: **96 of 168 triples, 57.1% — and 94 of the 96 were already silent, so the reach is 2 and the cost is the ninety-four.** The general shape: **a corpus sweep inherits the blind spots of the capability set it renders at**, and nothing was wrong until a member made a degraded-only arm reachable at that set

**F488** — §6m's row 2 ruled *the frame keeps `mark[]` and wins its cell whole*, citing `glyphRows`' precedence — *`glyph` before `mark`* — in the same sentence. **`glyph` is the data's channel and `mark` is the frame's**, so the parenthetical says *data before frame* and the headline says the opposite. Writing the compose step found it, because the code has to pick one and only one agrees with C12 I90. It survived because the two halves are eleven words apart, the evidence is parenthesised, and nothing in the sentence is false — the eye takes the bolded half. **And the row's finding was real under the wrong ruling**: every earlier arm has one sample a cell, so the braille rung is the first where a cell can carry both a frame mark and data dots. F84's class one artefact along, and the cheapest possible instance — the true statement was inside the same sentence as the decision it contradicts


**F491** — C12 §3am's refusal of box-drawing joins rests on one *mechanism* among four arguments: a strictly-nearer test refuses the second edge **by construction** at exactly the cell a join needs. **Every clause is true and the consequence is a tenth of what the wording implies.** Built both ways on a cube's twelve edges: **36 corners and 8 tees against 29 and 5** — seven corners and three tees, not a figure coming apart at every vertex. And three fixtures built to make it categorical are *identical* under both rules. The reason is the arm's corner routing, needed for a different problem, which produces corner glyphs from a **single** stroke — so a corner is not evidence of two edges meeting, and the row that counted corners survived its own mutation. **The class: a refusal states a mechanism and a consequence, and only the mechanism is checkable from the armchair.** Both were carried as one claim for four steps; *refused by construction* is a statement about a code path and reads as one about a picture
**F896** — SP9 requires every invariant to be *named by a test row* and reads that by scanning each line for `C\d{2}`/`I\d+` tokens, so `it.todo("T3.6 (C28 I15): …")` is a row. **Twenty invariants across seven components are named only by a deferral** — C28 6, C04 4, C22 4, C21 3, and one each in C03, C23, C24 — measured by running SP9 twice with its own reader, once with `it.todo` lines visible and once without: 79 uncited becomes 99. The first count was 21 from a copy of the rule that missed `ownerOf`'s fallback. Two of C28's six have no subject in `src` at all: `replay` and any width on a span appear nowhere, so I15 and I26 are vacuous and every gate reports them covered. **This is why eleven `it.todo` rows in `test/edge/profiler.test.ts` never expired**: their marker names no component, so TD1–TD6 have nothing to watch, and SP9 wants a name, which a todo supplies. Two gates satisfied by the shape of one sentence, each for a different reason, which is why neither reads as a hole. The repair is a figure rather than a gate — twenty-one is mostly honest deferral, and a gate red on arrival is a gate edited to fit — but SP9's reported line cannot tell the two populations apart. Its docblock states one blind spot; this is the second, one step earlier: it does not check that the row **runs**


**F908** — C28 §5's *every clock and `elapsed` read* looked like the design's expensive half when the
component was walked, and the tidier alternative — pin the replayed clocks to the event stream — is
deterministic and self-consistent. It became wrong three weeks after the clause was written: C24 I32
put `last 1.2ms` in the default footer, so a frame now holds a **duration**, and a pinned clock
flattens it to `last <0.1ms` on every replayed frame. **The mirror of the deferral rule** — a
deferral's condition is met elsewhere with nothing watching; here a requirement's justification is
strengthened elsewhere with nothing watching, and both halves are correct throughout. **Fixed** —
I14's second half names it and T1.85 asserts the cell is not the flattened one.

**F1038** — **a residue outlived its answer by one day, and the answer was forty rows further down the same file.** F949 closed with *what T1.83 and T5.1b diverge on after a `make e2e` is still open, in group 12*, and F963 — eight rows above it in this document — had already closed it: a read-count asymmetry under positional clocks, marked closed at the time. Everything the residue reached for pointed the wrong way. *The failing set under load is not stable*, *the changed member is the signal*, *a quiet machine is not less contention* — **all three name contention, and contention was the wrong axis**. What found it was that a verdict had to travel with the assertion before anything could be seen at all: the first form was `expect(out.identical).toBe(true)`, so every red run left one line, *expected false to be true*, and four instances carried no verdict between them. **That is not a load instrument.** A residue named in a summary is a claim like any other, and the cheapest moment to check it is before opening the session it asks for; this one cost a grep · **Closed** — no chain was needed and none was run. F949's row said open in prose and carried no marker at all, which is why the register's own reader counted it as *unstated* rather than open

**F1061** — **a commit message is a deferral site, and it is the only one of the four with no reader.** `CLAUDE.md` names three kinds — a code comment, a roadmap row, a chain of citations — and a commit body fails harder than any of them: it is append-only, never opened again, and invisible to *grep from the satisfier*, because the satisfier is a regenerated file rather than a symbol. Swept over **122 commits**: three candidates, **two with no home outside the message** and both this session's own. `7070929b` wrote *six of 244 SVG goldens move and are not regenerated here* and left the golden gate red for **four commits**, found by a lane running that suite for something else; `ce3eea37`'s residue is now F1062. **The third is the control and it is what the rule turns on** — `aa27561e`'s *not repaired the other way* is pinned by T1.25, so the message describes a watch instead of standing in for one, and all three read identically · **Closed** — both instances repaired, and no gate proposed: the wording is prose and the control is textually indistinguishable from the two defects, which is the citation-resolves-against-the-wrong-thing class the audit argues against automating. The habit is one line — a deferral in a commit message has to exist somewhere else first

## 11 · A gate that passes without checking — **415 entries: 2 open · 326 closed · 87 with no verdict**

**F1031** — **the register's own open set could not be read, and a third disposition nobody's reader knew about was most of what went missing.** A `**Open**` grep answers **16**; the set is **39**. Four spellings are in use — `**Open**`, `**OPEN**`, a bare `| open |` cell, and **`**Partly**`**, which is eighteen of the twenty-three missed and means *not done* in eighteen bodies that each name what still stands. **That is the mutation harness's own third-state defect arriving in the document that says what remains**: F897 reports SURVIVED where the truth is *the suite crashed*, F949 reports `0 rows` where the truth is *did not run*, and here a state that exists became invisible rather than wrong — collapsing, each time, onto the answer that means nothing more to do. **The cost is measured and it is this session's**: seventeen open findings sat undispatched while the survey read as complete, F50 among them — a column that never grows, uncounted beside F701, the finding about the same columns that *was* dispatched. **Fixed** — SP12 gates the open set as a list compared by equality, reading a *marker* (a bold span whose first word is the vocabulary, or a cell holding nothing else) and taking the **last**, which is load-bearing on five real rows of the shape `**OPEN** · **Closed**`. 332 rows stating no disposition are counted and reported, not gated, on SP6's precedent. The reader was rewritten five times, each by a measured false positive; the mutation pass found one vacuous row — a superseded-marker case with only one marker in it — and the same mutation fails it after repair.

**F1016** — **the row's name named C09 §3's table and its assertions named the registry, so the two rows the table had lost were the two nothing could see.** T1.4 read *each of the nineteen kinds measures its documented height* over a `documented` literal commented *§3's table, read back as assertions* — and it read no file: the literal was in equality with `DEFAULT_DEFINITIONS`, the registry in equality with nothing in the document, so §3 could lose any row without moving an assertion. It had lost two, `image` and `patch`. **F228's own comment, four lines below, states the class** — a coverage set drawn from the test's own table covers the table — one level up from where the defect had moved. **Fixed**: the row parses §3's table out of the document, eleven `expect` sites reading the file where none did, membership asserted by equality in both directions and the three delegations as a set. **12 of 12 fabricated violations red**, two of them emptying the reader so the parse rather than the membership reports. Stated blind spot: the Measure column is prose, so only 2 rows of 19 bridge to a measured height and a wrong formula beside a right membership passes.

**F1012** — **a fabricated violation with a hard-coded id expires the day that id becomes real, and it fails while the gate is correct.** SP6's completeness fabrication appended `## F999 — a fabricated finding` to the real ledger and asserted the rule names `F999`; F999 is now filed and keyed, so the heading is a **duplicate of a correct entry**, the rule has nothing to complain about, and the row goes red. **The red points at the wrong thing** — *SP6 fires: a finding filed and keyed nowhere* failing reads as *SP6 has stopped firing*, where the truth is that the row stopped constructing a violation. That is the vacuous-fabrication class arriving in a **control**, which is where it is hardest to see. The id is derived now, `max(## F<n>) + 1` over the ledger the row has already read, with an assertion that the ledger does not hold it. **The asymmetry is the tell**: the sibling row removes a *real* key, so its fabrication is a reference to the corpus and was never at risk — one row's fabrication was a literal and only the literal expired. Zero source lines; twelve findings between F999 and the failure. **Closed**

**F1011** — **a stale control is not a degraded run but no run at all.** `KNOWN_STALE` said `c23-refresh.mjs: 10` and 10 is exactly what was there — measured against the working tree *and* against HEAD's committed copies, the identical ten both ways. What the count cannot say is that **one of the ten is the control**, so the measured *before* is not seventeen caught and two missed: it is `AnchorError` on the first mutation and **0 of 18 applied**, for 33 days. Nine of the ten were one rework — C23 §3c, *the thing that polls is the source, not the part* — and the tenth had **no subject left**: C24 I21 deleted `stream` from `LiveSpec`, so both pair-policing throws went, and re-anchoring it at a nearby line would have reported `caught` while asserting nothing. One repair needed care: `if (src.done || src.inFlight) continue;` occurs twice and the six-space form is a **substring** of the eight-space one, so the anchor carries the `for` header above it. After: exit 0, **17 of 17 caught by name**, control killed. **And the run's own header now records why no survivors is weaker than it reads** — this pass verifies the anchoring, not the rows, and cannot see F1007's class. **The remedy nearly resolved against the wrong invariant**: the SP3 message template says *Write it as `C09 I44`*, and C09 owns a real I20 and a real I44 — all four bare citations were **C23's**, so following the template would have satisfied the rule exactly and produced four citations pointing at the wrong subject. **Closed**

**F1007** — **two mutations survived a row written for them, and neither was a test gap.** `c12-callout-column` reported 2 of 4 survived. Deleting the mirrored-label guard survived YC11, whose cases are `yAxis: false` and `yAxis: "right"` — at `false` **the row label is empty anyway**, so the guard and its absence produce the same cell, and at `"right"` the guard is true. Widening the left column by the callout's width survived every YA row because **those fixtures declare no callout**, so the maximum is the width already there. **A survivor means the rows did not notice, and there are two reasons for that**: the rows are weak, or the mutation had nothing to be wrong about at the inputs they drive. Sharpening the assertions would have produced stronger rows that still could not see either defect; what was missing was a *frame* — `yAxis: "left", yCallout: "last"`, where labels exist beside a column that is not a request. Two cases added, **zero source lines changed**, and the pass goes 3 of 5 to 5 of 5 — **through 4 of 5**, because the entry's own named falsifier fired: the left-column mutation survived the first repair too. Rendering one frame with a callout and one without is the state the mutation needs and not a frame where it can be **seen** — at `yCallout: "last"` over values reaching 10 the scale and the callout are both two cells wide. The relation the row rests on, the callout wider than the scale, is now asserted before the columns are. The added case's own first expectation was wrong — written from the neighbouring case's output rather than this one's — which is the cheap failure and it corrected itself. **Closed**

**F1008** — **a dead declaration outlived its callers by thirty-four days, and the flag that says so was set one directory up.** `c4b2869d` removed every caller of the app's `width()` workaround and left `const width = …` with a twelve-line comment whose premise — *`LocalContext` carries `command` and nothing else* — the same round had falsified; reading the comment is what makes the code look live. The root `tsconfig.json` sets `noUnusedLocals` and **none of the three example trees did**, so `make check` type-checks the examples and is green over both. Set on all three: 22 errors, every one `TS6133`/`TS6196`, **no pre-existing errors to subtract**, 0 after. `noUnusedParameters` deliberately not set — its thirteen extra are the `(argv, ctx)` handler signature and would ask the example to write `_argv`. The twelve dead `ViewDocument` imports are one paste; the two worth the number are **`PIXELS_PER_ROW`, `CELL_H`'s dead twin at the same `28`, cited by the comment forty lines below as the value in use**, and **`refuse`, kept by a sentence saying `T-refuse` exercised it while `T-refuse` built the object by hand** — exported and called now, which also lets the row assert the message. F14 closes with it. **Closed**

**F1000** — **the barrel line had a consumer, and it was not the line worth the finding.** F625's census was stale: `decodePng` has a live non-test consumer that imports it **through this barrel** out of `dist/`, to gate an eight-file PNG corpus of six that must read and two that must refuse — and `decodeImage` cannot stand in, because a PNG-specific refusal reported by the dispatching front door is a dispatch. Of the barrel's nineteen names, ten have no `src/` consumer, which is normal for a component barrel, and **three have no consumer anywhere**. None is reachable by MG25 **by its subject rather than its blind spot**: the rule walks `export function`/`export class`, so two constants and a type alias are outside what it looks at, and C24 I16 and A03 both describe it as covering constants. The line stays and names its consumer; the three orphans are recorded by equality rather than deleted. **Closed**

**F1001** — **the debug sink is fed, and the cheap gate for its class is vacuous against its own founding case.** `deps.debug` was declared, forwarded twice and written by seven sites with nothing draining it. Now a 200-line bounded list drained at `stop()` step 3, after `lifecycle.release()` — first N rather than last N with the dropped count said, a call split into lines because C01's redirect hands over `"a\nb\n"` where the six narration sites hand over one sentence, and each line marked because a drained line may be foreign output. **The gate is recorded and not built**: *never an object-literal key elsewhere in `src/`* fires on 12 of 103 optional members and **`ConstructDeps.debug` is not among them**, because a forwarding hop is textually identical to a supplying one. What would work anchors on the call site — 18 of 33 factories taking a deps type have exactly one `src/` caller — and needs a brace matcher plus a type-level allow-list, since `resolveConfig` alone carries 32 legitimately-optional members. ~200 lines. **Closed**, with the class named

**F997** — **the anchor sweep read every run as text, and text resolves whether or not the file is a program.** `anchorsOf` matched a `from:` and `silenceOf` read the tail, both perfectly happy, so a run with a `const` declared twice reported *1005 anchors · 937 expectations · no run drifted* while dying before its first mutation. `process.execPath --check` now runs per file **ahead of** the tail arm — on the tail arm's own argument one step earlier — and a file that cannot start is abandoned rather than read on, because a stale anchor stacked on an unparseable file invites repairing the wrong one. **No debt list**, on asymmetry: a stale anchor is listed because re-anchoring without running the pass produces a mutation that asserts nothing, and a syntax error has no such trap. **Its blind spot, stated**: it asks whether the file parses, not whether it links — `import { runPas }` is resolved at instantiation and nothing short of executing the module sees it. 187 runs, 187 parsed, 1.0 s → 3.3 s. **Closed**

**F998** — **the rule was ruled once, deferred to the commit that implements it, and that commit never came.** F664 says C09 declares two commitments numbered 41; it declares **three**. F225 had already ruled this class in the same document, fixed that instance and written *the row lands with its implementation and this entry is the prose that goes ahead of it* — a deferral naming a condition nothing watched, so C09 re-acquired the defect and it was found again from scratch. **And F225's own measurement was false when written, in its own favour**: *C09 is the only spec in the tree with duplicates* against twelve numbers across four specs at that commit, of which it saw four across one. SP11 compares a spec's commitment numbers for uniqueness, with `DUPLICATE_COMMITMENTS` compared by equality **both ways**; C09 renumbered to 61 commitments, 61 distinct, no gaps, and zero citations touched. The other nine are on the list with their specs. **Closed**

**F955** — **the transcript's 60 µs a row was one glyph.** The gutter's `│` — one non-ASCII code unit on every patch row — made `cells()`'s ASCII fast path miss, so `displayCells` segmented a 141-cell row and the walk asked the segmenter again per code point: 1 122 of 1 554 fitted rows at 60.8 µs, 43 µs of it iterating segments over ASCII. `cells()` now walks ASCII runs by length and asks the segmenter only at a non-ASCII cluster; `transcript` 69.2 → 13.8 ms over the same 34 frames, 22.9 % → 6.1 % of frame work, `react` 153 → 141 as the control. Closed.

**F956** — **the code-point walk dropped a combining mark at a window's right edge, and no width could see it.** A differential probe of HEAD's `text.ts` against the cluster cursor over seeded rows: 1 912 of 530 208 `sliceCells` cases differed, every one a mark belonging to the cluster before the cut, and both answers have the same width, so no row asserting cells could tell them apart. Closed with the cursor (C09 I63, T3.79).

**F957** — **a Prepend is a fifth non-additive shape, and an escape inside a cluster is the corner the walk does not resolve.** The same probe: U+0600 prepends to the cluster after it, which F939's four did not list; and an SGR landing between a base and its mark is a cluster split by an escape, forbidden upstream by C04 I84 and resolved by the walk's tail rule rather than a ruling. Eight cases recorded in T1.36. **Closed** (F986) — the corner is C09 I64's, ruled at F969: no frame the framework paints carries an escape inside a cluster, measured over 201 104 escapes.

**F964** — **`CLOCK_DERIVED`'s header-clock member never matched a chrome frame.** `\b` cannot follow an SGR's `m`, and the chrome writes `\x1b[38;5;241m09:39:14`; `masked` read 14 on every `spans` replay — seven `last` cells on two sides — and 0 at `counters`, which T5.1b asserted as *nothing masked* and which held only because the member was dead. The row that narrowed the pattern, T1.87, fed a sentence with a space before the digits. Closed: lookarounds refusing a digit or a colon on either side; T1.101 feeds the chrome's bytes, and T5.1b now asserts that the header's clock is what is masked.

**F968** — **a mutation survived T2.42 for as long as the row's children were one row each.** The forced-zero guard on a collapsed box was written for a residue text I104 later fixed by state, and what it still governs is the child window: a child straddling a held offset that the slice cannot cut is kept whole. Single-row children cannot straddle anything, so in the row the guard had nothing to be wrong about; a three-row column group with a held offset draws `r1 r2 r3` above the fold without it. Surviving since the run was written and found only because another lane's rename put the run in the pass list. Closed: the state is in T2.42, the mutation is caught, §3c S2 names the observable.

**F969** — **the cursor's corner is closed by a check over the corpus, and the corpus could not see the class.** C09 I64: no frame the framework paints carries an escape inside a grapheme cluster; the walk's tail rule is a corner, not a path, and the promise is C04 I84's and C07's. `test/support/cluster-escapes.ts` strips the CSI grammar recording each escape's stripped index and reports one strictly inside a segmenter cluster — shown to respond on seven shapes before anything was read with it. Over 395 golden frames and 2 440 terminal-baseline frames, 201 104 escapes and none inside a cluster; and not one combining mark, flag or joiner in 39 828 lines, so the green says only that nothing shipped was split — the same tree held three painters splitting clusters (F970), found by feeding six shapes to every painter instead. Ink drops a zero-width code point that opens a span, so a split reaches a frame as a base without its mark, invisible to any assertion about escapes. **Closed** (F976, F978; F986) — the two residues that were open here, treemap, tree, graph and sankey writing one code point per cell and keep no cluster whole (a family arrives as three faces; pinned per kind by equality in T2.129); and `aः` measures 1 to `cells()` against 2 to xterm and `string-width`, so a padded `raw` row wraps in Ink — the width class the Never list is about.

**F970** — **three painters took a boundary in code units against a cluster the segmenter joins.** highlight.js ends a `number` at `\b\d+`, so a Prepend before a digit and a keycap's digit were split in ten grammars (120 hits); the `terminal` cursor took one code unit at a cell column, leaving half a surrogate inverse and a mark alone or dropped; `plot3d` wrote one span with its own SGR per code point of an axis name. Each hidden by Ink dropping the zero-width piece — a bare digit, a family of three, `cafex` — and by a corpus with no shape to split. Closed: `wholeClusters` moves a token's end on to the cluster's end, the cursor walks graphemes and re-splits the covering cluster out of its spans, the overlay writes by cluster; T2.130–T2.132, each with a restoring mutation in `cluster-escapes.mjs` that fails it.

**F976** — **four label writers were one loop written four times, and its unit was the code point.** treemap, tree, graph and sankey wrote one code point per cell advancing by `cells()` of it, so a zero-width piece was written and then overwritten: a family arrived as three faces, a keycap as a bare digit, `café` decomposed as `cafe`. One writer now — `chargrid.ts`'s `write` walks `graphemes(body)`, a cluster owning the cells it measures with `""` behind a wide one and a cluster measuring nothing dropped with the column unmoved — and the treemap, the sankey and the point labels call it rather than carry the loop (C12 I118, commitment 118). C09 T2.129's `NOT_WHOLE` is `{}`, kept as an equality so a new writer losing a shape must join it; T1.130–T1.134 read each kind before Ink and after; `c12-label-writers.mjs` mutates the writer and each call site back to its private loop. **The guard's removal is invisible to a leading mark** — the next cluster overwrites the cell either way — so the run also gives a zero-width cluster a cell of its own, which is what the leading-mark case sees. F969's first residue. **Closed.**
