**F978** — **a cluster measures as the terminal advances, and a spacing mark is a cell.** `clusterCells` gave every cluster the width of its base code point alone — true of every shape the tree had measured and false of a spacing mark: `aः` measured 1 to `cells()` against 2 to string-width and to xterm, so a `raw` row padded to the width was one cell over by Ink's measure and wrapped into a row the measurer never counted. C09 I65, commitment 57: after the two whole-cluster rules — a selector after the base, a regional pair — the cluster is walked by code point, nothing for `Mn`, `Me`, `Cf` or a modifier after a base, the sum ended by a joiner, two where the property says Wide, one else; `aः`, `कि`, `கொ`, `কা` read 2, `हिन्दी` 5, the family and the keycap unchanged. Swept against string-width 8.2.2: `a` + every one of the 471 `Mc` marks agrees where 440 were under before, and single code points go 24 under to none. T2.133 is F969's own stated falsifier — the `raw` row read two rows on the unmodified file and one after; T1.37 pins the set by equality; T6.111–T6.114 and `c09-text-zero-width.mjs` name the reverts. Recorded limits: a lone modifier stays 2, a lone selector 0, the conjoining jamo not zeroed. F969's second residue. **Closed.**

**F980** — **two mutation runs rotted unwatched, and one exit had meant nothing for a hundred passes.** `c12-sankey`'s fit-test mutation survives at HEAD — in a pristine export too — because the overlap guard, written beside it later, drops the `long-labels` middle name first: the sink's name is written outside-in into the same cells, so the frames with and without the fit test are byte-identical at 40 and at 80. SK12 makes the fit test the only guard — a thirty-cell first name against a gap of seventeen, nothing in its way — red under the mutation on three assertions, green restored. `c12-graph` had exited 1 on three survivors F243 recorded and disposed of, indistinguishable in a summary from a new one; it declares them now in the C19 form and exits 0 unless a survivor is new or an exemption is stale. **Closed.**

**F953** — **the seventh verb shadowed a `profile` both examples declared, and only the proof gate saw it.** C05 I6 makes an app's duplicate of a framework verb a parse error at construction, so when `/profile` joined the seven both reference apps stopped starting — and the framework's 5 841 rows stayed green on every gate, because none holds an app's manifest. docker-tui went nine of 299 red in proof; plots is in no gate but `make test`'s example loop, which never ran because `npm run test` was red first (F941). docker's stub had no handler and is removed; plots' had one drawing the panes as a document and is renamed `/report`. C05 §3 now carries the check — grep every manifest in the tree before a verb joins the list. Closed; the demo's `TuiConfig.profile` is the open question beside it.

**F952** — **a mutation that survived the memo had been surviving all along.** `c14-cap.mjs`'s `MARKER-ROW-WINDOWED` survived the pass after F940's memo; swapped by hand, HEAD's registry gave the same 16 of 16 green, so the memo masked nothing. The clamp it removes is taken over the form's block, already `cap` rows, and cannot bind. Recorded as an expected survivor with the reason; the anchor sweep checks that an anchor resolves, not that its mutation fails, so nothing dates when it went vacuous. **Closed** (F990) — the sweep runs all 181 serially against a snapshot, ranks each green, known-stale or red, and the weekly CI job at six shards is what makes *until the pass is run* a date rather than a hope.

**F990** — **181 mutation runs and no gate that runs one.** F952's class closed by an instrument: `tools/mutate/sweep.mjs` runs every run serially against a snapshot of `src/` and `tools/enforce/`, restores between them, and gives each a verdict — green, known-stale against a debt list **compared by equality**, or red. Sharded round-robin because runs are not equal in cost: `--shard 1/6` takes 31 of 181, and the weekly job runs six in parallel. The debt it inherits is **35 stale anchors across 16 runs**, two of them controls. **Its blind spot, stated**: a run that exits 0 having caught nothing is green here — whether a row still *sees* is the anchors sweep's job, and the two are separate instruments on purpose. **Closed**

**F945** — **`paneTitle` was published with no consumer anywhere, and MG24 could not see it.** `src/index.ts` re-exports it beside `profilePane` and `PANES`; at HEAD before the drawing round it had two occurrences in the tree, its definition and that re-export — no caller in `src/`, `test/`, `tools/` or `examples/` for the whole of its life. MG24 checks members of exported interfaces and is blind to a root function re-export. The view is now its consumer (the layer's title row); the rule's blind spot is recorded and not closed. Closed as a finding about `paneTitle`.

**F948** — **the third Status line, fifteen hours after the round that named the class.** C28's header read *Spec'd 2026-09-06, unbuilt* against 4270 lines in `src/shell/profiling/`, 48 invariants all cited, and a `COMPONENT_SOURCES` row; F891 (`e38819e1`) was committed fifteen hours after C28 landed (`b62b64df`) and corrected C26 and C27 without reading C28. The check is buildable — a `COMPONENT_SOURCES` path that resolves forbids a Status matching *unbuilt* — with its blind spot named in the finding: a *Built* line with wrong figures, C26's conjunction, a component with no row. The line is corrected; the rule is not built.

**F941** — **C28 T1.61 asserted the two overlay layouts on purpose.** `paint()` and `cursorFor()` each called `placedLayers`, and the row pinned `2 × frames` with a comment naming P11 as the reason — the T2.28b shape F855 and F856 recorded: a row that watches the remedy rather than the condition is green for exactly as long as the defect is. Now *exactly once*, under C22 I96, with `render-frame.ts` computing one layout and handing it to both. Closed.

**F939** — **the cursor steps by code point and the measurer counts by cluster, and four emoji shapes fall between them.** The walk F937's fix had to preserve asks `cells()` of each code point while `displayCells` asks the segmenter of the whole; a ZWJ family, a keycap, a flag and a VS16 emoji are counted differently by the two, so `fitStyled` cuts a 14-cell row to 9 cells at width 15 and returns 3 cells for a 2-cell budget — the wrap hazard. Pinned by T3.79 as a record row until the remedy landed with F955: `fitStyled` and `sliceCells` take three pieces — an escape, an ASCII run, one cluster whole or nothing — and T3.79 asserts the four shapes as the measurer counts them (C09 I63); I20's family row needed no change. **Closed.**

**F938** — **the quadratic cursor had two sites and a third mechanism, and the rule is what closes it.** F937 named `fitStyled:203`; `sliceCells:283` held the same read one function down, and both rescanned the remainder through a `g`-flagged pattern from `lastIndex`. A code-point read in place and a sticky pattern: 400 cells went from **1.118 ms to 0.0095 ms** a call, 50 cells from 0.027 to 0.0015, byte-identical output on the probe. SS60 forbids the idiom across `src/` with no exceptions and a stated blind spot; C09 I60 is the sentence and T3.77 its row. Closes F937. · **Closed** (F991) — commit `f92ae903` — SS60 and C09 I60, T3.77 and T6.104

**F937** — **every row of every frame walks a quadratic cursor.** `exact(text, width)` — `fitStyled` in `presentation/text.ts` — runs on every row of every frame, 1700 times in the profiling fixture, and is **53 % of all frame work**. One line does it: `const ch = [...text.slice(i)][0] ?? "";` allocates a copy of the rest of the string *and* an array of every remaining code point, per character, to read one. Measured against `String.fromCodePoint(text.codePointAt(i))`, which is flat: **3× at 40 cells, 17× at 80, 219× at 200, 1081× at 400**. `fitStyled` tracks it — 0.0242, 0.0264, 0.1451, 0.6483 ms per call — so the frame gets more expensive per row as the terminal gets **wider**, the axis a full-screen TUI can least avoid. **The fast path does not save it**: a row already exactly `width` costs 0.0208 ms, one cell short costs 0.1271 and one cell over 0.1576, so a row needing nothing but spaces appended pays the whole walk to discover it — and rows arrive from Ink short of the width, so that is the ordinary case. The profiler agrees at 0.21–0.27 ms per row. **Keyed here for want of a better group, and the distinction matters**: this is not a gate passing without checking, it is *no cost gate existing at all*. The code is correct, has no failing case, and every test of `fitStyled` passes. The old P11 list had *a regex `.replace` per row per frame* one function away — found by reading, fixed, now 0.3 ms — while the far larger per-row pass beside it went unexamined, because **reading finds what looks expensive and measuring finds what is**. **Closed** (F938; F986) — SS60 and C09 I60, `quadratic-cursor.mjs` every mutation caught

**F932** — **a tally is not a set.** Both survivors of the forty-six's mutation pass are one mechanism: a `new Set` taken over a corpus of source *sites* and compared by equality. C07 T2.8 asserts the distinct sources of `command:` in `registry.ts` are `["ctx.command"]` — removing the funnel's assignment leaves **4 sites where there were 5**, all still `ctx.command`, and the set is unchanged. C23 T1.60 asserts the distinct `producerContext(...)` arguments in `execution.ts` are `["deps.region().height", "null"]` — moving one route between them leaves **4 and 3** where there were 5 and 2, and the set is unchanged. **A set records the vocabulary and reads as though it records the assignment**: it fires on a new value and on an emptied bucket, and never on a site changing sides — which is the whole of C07 I16 and C23 I41, both being claims about *which sites*. Both rows had a second half; T1.60 has no behavioural half at all, because a partition of routes is only legible in the source. The sweep bounds the class: thirty rows in `test/` compare a set against an expected set, and every other one asks *how many distinct* or *which names occur*, where a set is right. Fixed — both compare a **tally**, value and count, and both now fail on the mutation they passed. **Closed**

**F933** — **the row that named the regression never reached the route that regressed.** C07 T2.8's comment says the identity route is the one that regressed and drives it by name — with a payload carrying no `status` and no `meta`. `identityDocument` **validates rather than sniffs**, deliberately and for I5's reason, so that payload is refused, `adapt` falls to the fallback, and the fallback assigns `command` at its own site. **Both readings answer `/ps`**, so the row agreed with itself while measuring the wrong route, and the mutation removing I16's assignment from the single funnel survived it. Measured through `meta.adapter`, the field that tells the routes apart because I13 carries the far side's across on this route alone: three payloads reach `fallback` and only the one carrying all nine `meta` fields reaches `far`. Fixed by carrying a whole document and **asserting the route before the answer**. The shape to carry: a row whose subject is *which of N paths ran* must assert the path, because the paths agree on the field under test — that is usually why the invariant exists — and the setup that fails to select the path is the one that looks simplest. **Closed**

**F931** — C20 I25 says *every rule reaches inside a quoted compound and into URL query values*. Writing that row — three rules × two compounds — measured **five of six cells leaking**, and the sixth was incidental: `curl https://x/y?q=<40-char token>` wrote the token to the history file **in full**. The cause is F84's class in a comment — *a URL … names a resource, and the part of one that carries a secret is an assignment the text scan already catches*, which is **true of a named secret** (`?token=` is caught by the text scan's `SECRET.test(name)` guard) and **false of an unnamed one**, where only the entropy net could fire and the net sits behind the very exemption the sentence justifies. **The sentence is true and it is about the other rule.** Why nothing saw it: the compound split runs on `/\s/.test(slot.text)`, so a *quoted* compound has whitespace to split on and a URL does not — the one compound the existing mechanism cannot reach is the one with no row. Fixed by splitting a URL's query and fragment into its pieces and running the same rules over them; the **path** stays exempt, and a path segment leaks with or without a scheme, so the two now agree. **The fix's own defect was caught by the existing suite**: offsets taken from `slot.text` (unquoted by `tokenise`) against a `slot.start` that indexes the quoted line put every splice one character early — the text came out right and `fired` gained a duplicate `P2`, which the contract row compares by equality. **Closed**


**F925** — `degradesTo1Bit` **could not sweep two of the four kinds that hold blocks**, and both exemptions said in their own words that it could: *"the children are swept as blocks in their own right"*, on `scroll` and on `mosaic`, where `visit` recurses for `panel` and `group` and nothing else. It was not silent, which is what made it findable — `carriesATone` is deep, so F102's guard fired on a *descendant's* tone — and it was wrong in both directions from that one cause: a **legitimate** document holding a properly toned notice inside a scroll was **refused**, and a **real** offence was refused with the container named instead of the offender. **The guard was written for leaves**: a leaf's own fields are its whole subtree, so a deep scan and an own-fields scan are the same measurement there, and for a container they differ by exactly the children an arm sweeps. All four get the arm now, with the premise kept and scoped to the container's own fields — which gives `panel` and `group` a premise check they never had, having had an arm instead. **Nothing had run into it because nothing had built one**: three call sites, all `panel`, and C24's T5.5 — `degradesTo1Bit` over every document the reference app produces — is specified and unwritten. C24 I13, T2.12. **Fixed**

**F927** — C24 I10 says the runtime entry exports no function performing I/O except `createTui`, and T2.6 — *a source scan finds no I/O in the runtime entry outside `createTui`* — **would have passed on any tree as specified**: `src/index.ts` is a barrel, 497 lines and 44 `export` statements with no executable statement in it, so a scan *of* it finds nothing whatever the surface does. Resolved through the entry instead — each of the 31 value exports to its defining module — two come back: `session.ts`, which is `createTui`, and `builders/index.ts`, which is `b`. `b.image({ path })` calls `readFileSync` at construction. It is a published convenience and the comment above it is a good one, arguing that the read belongs in `shell/` and never in `presentation/`, where a renderer doing I/O at frame cadence would make `measure` and `render` disagree — **which is where the read belongs, and the invariant asks whether the surface may do it at all**. F926's shape twice in one session, and this one had no wrong answer behind it, only an unwritten exception: I10 names `b` with the reason, and T2.6 compares the reaching set by **equality**, so a third is a failure and a retired second is one too. C24 I10, T2.6. **Fixed**

**F921** — a comment cited a bare `I83` for a gate owned by `I111`, and **SP3 is why it was found**: a bare number can be written without deciding anything, and answering *which spec owns this* is what sent someone to read C04 I83 and see it is about spans. **SP3 did not catch it and cannot** — `C04 I83`, qualified and wrong, resolves and passes. The rule supplies the **occasion**, not the check, and CLAUDE.md's claim that no mechanism reaches this class stands. Recorded for the shape: a rule's value can exceed its stated subject when what it costs is a decision, and its reach here is the bare citations only. **Fixed**

**F920** — `PtyFactory` exists so a consumer passes `node-pty` itself, and two documents say so in those words; the compile-level check that would have measured it was T2.8, deferred. It does not compile: `Readonly<>` is a homomorphic mapped type, so `spawn(...)` is rewritten from a **method** into a **property with a function type**, method bivariance is off, and `readonly string[]` is refused against `node-pty`'s `string[] | string`. **One word, and it was protecting nothing** — the only caller builds `["-c", command]` inline and drops it. **The producer could not have found it**: every internal caller holds a freshly built array, which satisfies both spellings, so nine passing tests exercise `spawnPty` under either type and the variance is visible only from a consumer holding the real package. **The other half is the deferral's own text.** I15 names two types — *every member of `PtyProcess` **and** `PtyFactory`* — and T2.8 asked for a check that *`IPty` is assignable to `PtyProcess`*: the half that passes. Written as specified the row would have been green and cited against I15, and a reader checking the citation finds a row that names the invariant and covers half of it. **Nothing checks that a test row's subject is the whole of the invariant it cites**, and the citation is what makes the gap invisible. `check` green with the modifier dropped; T2.8 now asserts both halves and T6.18 names the restoration. **Fixed**

**F923** — converting the first eighteen deferrals found three whose **own assertion** was wrong, none of them wrong about its blocker: C04 T2.118's *a seventh block key* against nine, C21 T2.8's *`IPty` is assignable to `PtyProcess`* against an I15 that names two types (the second did not compile, F920), and C22 T2.100's *`config.pty` read at exactly one site* against two. **Three different directions** — a drifted count, a subject narrower than the invariant cited, and a claim true per object and false as the grep the row describes, which is F92's shape. **The cause is that a deferred row's text is never executed**: a running row is checked against the tree on every commit, an `it.todo` is a sentence, and TD1–TD6 watch the blocker while SP9 watches the invariant's name — nothing reads what the row claims. Two of the three were visible only because the row was **written**, by `tsc` refusing and by running the grep the sentence describes; reading them would have found neither. **Fixed**, and the conversion rule is now to resolve every number, type and path against HEAD first

**F924** — **every successful command on the PTY arm settles as an error**, and has since the arm landed. `node-pty` reports the signal as a *number* and uses **0 for none**; C21's `signal === undefined ? null : \`SIG${signal}\`` turns that into `"SIG0"`, and C23 computes `failed = … || exit.signal !== null`. A correct screen under a status card reading *Killed by SIG0.* The second defect is the form: a piped child resolves with Node's `"SIGTERM"` and a PTY child resolved with `"SIG15"`, where `runner.ts`'s own comment says the point is *the same shape a piped child resolves with, so a caller reads one `Exit`*. **Nine passing rows said otherwise because every one of them hands a fake, and every fake resolves `{code: 0, signal: null}` by hand** — the fixture supplying the behaviour under test, and supplying the value the real package does not produce. The port's type made it easy: `onExit` declares `signal?: number`, so a fake omitting the field is well-typed and omission is the one case the code handles. **Found from a frame that was on screen for a different reason** — the row's own quoting bug is what put a real child's exit on a screen at all. C21 I19, commitment 15, T2.9, T6.19. **Fixed**

**F919** — forty-four `it.todo` rows resolved against HEAD rather than read. **Every blocker the emulator rows name is built**: `spawnPty`, `hasPty` and `spawnShell` in `src/data/process/runner.ts`, consumed at `execution.ts:805-817`; `PtyFactory` at `types.ts:107`; a 198-line `terminalDefinition`. **Four are second records of rows that are written and running** — C28 T3.6 and C23 T3.63 close, C23 T2.47 and T4.64 are *partial* and reframed to their residue: T4.64 asked for the child resized **before** the emulator and the written row asserts only that the two are told the same width, which is green under the defect the deferral was about. **The guard for exactly this is green over the set it cannot see** — `todo-expiry.mjs`'s six arms are all keyed on a component id, every one of the forty-four carries `not deferred on a component`, and `ACKNOWLEDGED_BACKLOG` is empty, so TD0 passes by equality: A03 §2's vacuity class inside the guard, SS26's shape, cited in that file's own header. CLAUDE.md is right that *until X is threaded through* cannot be matched against *X exists* — **but two blockers named a file path and one named a grep**, both checkable with no prose matching, and both had been false since `replay.ts` landed the same session. T6.8 and T6.15 converted and each verified by mutating `compareFrames`. And a defect in the instrument, found in it: a bare T-number scan reported 38 duplicates of which **31 were collisions**, because T-numbers are per-component — and they collide *within* a component too (C04 two T4.1s, C09 three T4.2s), so the key is component, number **and** subject, and only a reader supplies the last. 44 rows to 40; 5,665 running to 5,668

**F918** — `NO-EXIT-FLUSH` was carried in `c28-profiler.mjs` as an absence with a reason, and the reason was true: its only witness was T5.1, and the harness cannot run tier 5, which executes against `dist/` — a `src/` mutation is invisible to it without a build per mutation. **It was one step short, and the step is visible from the spec's side rather than the harness's**: the hook had a fifteen-line comment in `config.ts` and **no invariant anywhere**, and A03 SP1 catches a commitment with no invariant only when the commitment is written *down* — this one was written in a code comment, where no rule looks. The missing mutation, the missing tier-1 row and the missing invariant are one absence seen three times, and the recorded reason named the third-order symptom. With C28 I48 written the row is sixty lines at tier 1 — capture `process.listeners("exit")` across a `resolveConfig`, call the **listener** rather than `process.exit`, assert a pending clock batch reaches the sink with `end` last — needing no PTY, no build and no `dist/`. Two mutations anchor to it and are killed by **different** assertions: the deleted hook by the listener count, the emptied one — `() => {}`, present in a diff and doing nothing — by the flush. **The reusable part is the question**: *why can no test reach this* was answered with a correct fact about the harness, and *what claim would a test be written from* had no answer at all · **Closed** (F991) — C28 I48 and commitment 29, T1.91, with an anchored mutation

**F917** — `b62b64df` added `/profile` to the plots example — a manifest entry, a handler, and no row in `plots.test.ts`'s `DOCUMENTS`, which is asserted **by equality** against the manifest for exactly that reason. **The gate was red from the commit that landed it** and went unread because the session reported the suite green from a run that did not include `examples/plots`: *run the whole suite, not a chosen subset*, arriving as a workspace boundary rather than a directory list. **The sixth form of the gate-not-run class and the first where the gate is in a different workspace from the change that broke it.** Fixing it produced the second half — the builder was inline in `main.ts`, so the coverage row could only be written by re-typing the handler's body, the two-records defect the table exists to prevent appearing inside the test that prevents it; extracted to `commands.ts` beside every sibling. And a gap underneath: the row can construct the *absent* arm only, because `ProfileReport` is reachable through `ctx.profile()` and nowhere else — `@fmx/calcium/testing` publishes `checkBudget`, `checkPhases` and `checkLeaks`, three functions that **take** a report to a consumer with no way to make one. `profilePane`'s doc comment calls it *a pure function from a report to blocks*, which is true and is why the missing half is invisible: the function is testable and its input is not

**F915** — C17 I24's first draft keyed the layout memo on the buffer, the width, both gutter figures **and the size of the chip table**, justified by three true clauses: `drawAs` resolves a sentinel through `#chips`, §5a never prunes it, its size moves exactly when an answer could. The term is dead — `insertChip` is the table's only writer and it mints a fresh sentinel and inserts it in the same call, so the table can never grow while the buffer stands still and `chips.size` can never differ on a comparison where `text` matches. **A key term no input can make differ is A03 §2's vacuity class wearing a memo's clothes**: it survives review because the sentence explaining it is correct. Found by writing the row that had to construct the state — the spec had already committed T1.43 to *a chip registered with the buffer untouched changes the answer*, which is unreachable through the public surface, and **a row that cannot be written is the strongest signal a spec sentence is wrong**. The remedy is not a smaller key but a stated blind spot: the precondition is a property of the *writer*, so a second writer registering without inserting would leave the memo stale and nothing in C17 would see it — T1.43's last row pins the writer that exists and says it cannot watch one that does not

**F905** — `make instruments` compares its inventory by equality and its own comment promises *an instrument added without a fixture fails here on the day it lands*. It did: **45 found, 40 with a fixture**, from commit `8ed2ccde` — whose message is about `byEntry` and C28 I42 and which carried 697 lines of a foreign-PTY capture tool in five files it never mentions. The gate was right and nobody ran it, which is the **fifth form** of this class and the first where the gate itself is correct: the other four are a wrong corpus, a gate that did not run, thirteen days red on CI unread, and a scope stopping a layer above its subject. Found by *an untouched file appearing in a diff* one level out — a target naming five files from a session that had not touched them. Read as five instruments it is five fixtures; read against the tree it is **one instrument in five files** wanting four different answers — a fixture, two `null` drivers, a host-only recorder with its reason, and a control moved to `NOT_INSTRUMENTS`. Six rows over the three pure exports, each verified by mutation on landing. Now **44 instruments, every one with a fixture · 555 rows** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `a1138740` — 44 instruments, each with a fixture

**F913** — C28 T5.4's fixture read `report.misses["nothing-changed"]`, and the report is `cache → reason → count` — so the read asks for a *cache* by that name and is `undefined` on every run. It was found only because the row asserted `toBeTypeOf("number")`; written the other way round, as *is it zero*, `undefined ?? 0` would have passed for ever. **The spec carried the same reading** in §10's row text, so two documents agreed about a shape neither had checked against the report. The corrected row is stronger than the original: determinism over the **whole** map with `toStrictEqual`, which holds whatever the counts are, and the absence asserted per cache — absence rather than zero, because the recorder omits a reason that never fired (C28 I13)

**F904** — `roadmap-status.mjs` checks a `path:line` citation for being **non-blank** and checks its cell's backticked symbol against the **whole file**, and joins neither to the other — so a line number drifts silently while the file grows above it and the gate reports *every claim resolves*. Surfaced when an unrelated 47-line shift pushed roadmap 20's `construct.ts:1224` onto a blank line, the one condition the gate can see; chasing it, **both of that row's citations were already wrong at HEAD** — `refresh.ts:327` for a declaration at 344, `construct.ts:1224` for a wiring at 1352. Measured over the document at a six-line window: **43/74 line citations carry one of their own cell's symbols**, and 29 of the 31 adrift are in files this session never touched. Roadmap 15 names `#setCopyMode` at `session.ts:586` and it is at 1096; it names C03's `suspend` at `frame-scheduler.ts:263` and it is at 315. **Reported, not gated** — any radius is a judgement, and a rule that guesses one gets widened until it says nothing; the figure sits beside the tool's two existing signals. Two correct checks over one citation, satisfied by different facts, with nothing joining them — F903's shape one level out · **Closed** (F991) — **Recorded as a signal** — commit `370d2a29`, RS14; reported and not gated by design
**The composition, measured 2026-09-04 at `86d3b191`.** The heading read *157: 96 closed, 61 open*
and the total was out by **66**. Under SP6's own definition of *keyed* — a bolded id anywhere in the
section — group 11 keys **223** distinct ids: 222 open an entry (77 as prose, 145 as a table row)
and one, F272b, is keyed beside F272 rather than opening one. **The closed half was current and the
open half was not**, which is the shape to watch for: 96 is still what a scan for an unqualified
*Fixed* or *Closed* returns, so the sentence read as maintained while two-thirds of the arrivals
since it was derived went uncounted. F142 in this group is the row this fired on, and it is one of
the rows that states nothing beyond its own sentence.

**No entry carries a disposition field**, so the split is a reading and not a derivation. The rule
used: *an entry is closed when its own text states a completed disposition for the finding it keys*
— `**Fixed**`, `Closed by`, `**Ruled**`, `Renamed to`, `Implemented`, `Wired` — and open otherwise,
**the partial ones included**: *Fixed two of three* (F422), *Fixed for ghostty; WezTerm and Konsole
owed* (F415), *Instance fixed … class open* (F159, F160), *Closed for the tick* (F710). Ten entries
state a partial disposition and **fifty-eight state none at all**. So 96 / 61 was never re-derivable
by a reader, and neither is 105 / 117 without the rule beside it — which is why the rule is written
here and not only the number.

**And the split that predicts the next findings** — of the 117 open, how many say what their gate
still does *not* reach:

| | | named instances |
|---|---|---|
| **a blind spot stated** — the entry names the residue, the limit, the control, or a measured zero yield | **88** | F349 (*Stated blind spot — a collision needs two variants differing in one field*), F434 (*Not gated: `keyed` has no strong enough definition*), F479 (*Recorded as a known limit with its measurement*), F181 (*blind spot recorded at MG27's declaration*) |
| **borderline** — measured, with the residue implied rather than written | **12** | F373, F453, F370, F294, F147, F56 |
| **nobody has questioned it** — the instance and nothing else: no reach, no control, no residue, no disposition | **17** | F127, F104, F142, F87, F94, F95, F99, F102, F105, F109, F114, F115, F121, F131, F133, F136, F2 |

**Sixteen of the seventeen arrived in one batch** — the F142 inventory sweep's second cohort at the
end of this section, *placed by mechanism from the entry*, with SP6 proving coverage and not
placement. The un-questioned residue is not spread through the group; it is one table nobody has been
back to. **A cohort filed to satisfy a coverage gate is where the next finding is**, because the gate
it was filed for is the one gate that cannot ask whether the row says anything.

**Ranked by reach, the three to read first.** The argument for a place is what a fabricated violation
or a frame would ask, not severity:

1. **F127 — MG3 has never walked `import type`.** The largest corpus in the bucket and the cheapest
   probe. MG3 is the layering rule, over every import in `src/`, and CLAUDE.md calls acyclicity and
   L0's two halves the tree's central invariant. The fabricated violation is one line — a type-only
   import from `data/` into `shell/` — and `make enforce` either goes red or the rule has a hole
   exactly the width of `import type`, over a corpus the row does not state.
2. **F104 — `block()` is transparent to excess properties, so C04's narrowings land unenforced.**
   The corpus is every narrowing C04 states, on the published builder. Two instances of the shape
   have since been measured elsewhere and both were real — F479's `tone: "chartreuse"` accepted by
   seven carriers with `slot()` returning `{}`, and F589's `colormap` accepted on a `rule` — so the
   general row is the one entry here with two independent confirmations and no measurement of its own.
3. **F142 — a derived count is derived once, and nothing re-derives it.** It is the row this
   measurement fired on and it is still firing. The ranking table's per-group column now disagrees
   with **8 of 15** sections — −1, +1, −1, +1, +2, −1, −6, +6 — against F434's measured 6 of 14, and
   **the total still passes because the errors cancel**. Group 13 declares 6 ids and keys **none**;
   group 14 declares 24 and keys 30; row 11 reads 224 against 223.

**Why row 11's `224` is not corrected here.** SP6's only assertion over the ranking table is the sum
against the distinct ids keyed, and the sum is exact today at 735. Correcting one row to its measured
value makes the sum 734 and turns `make enforce` red, so no row can be fixed alone. And the
per-section counts sum to **736**, because F30 is keyed in both group 4 and group 7: **a truthful
per-row column and the total check are not simultaneously satisfiable while any id is keyed twice.**
That is the half of F434's recorded limit that was not stated — it recorded that the column is
unchecked, not that it is unfixable a row at a time.

**F725** — F710 named **two** frames where a callout overprints a right-hand value label. Sweeping all **212 drawn baselines** for `<text>` pairs whose painted bands overlap: **10 pairs in 6 frames**, and `line-callout-single` — **one series, one callout, the simplest figure the corpus has** — is one of them. Every pair is **2.415 px** apart, which is the difference between `end[1] + SVG_FONT_SIZE / 3` and `y + SVG_FONT_SIZE / 3` and nothing else, so the class is *every frame that draws a callout beside a right axis* and a series' last sample is very often at an axis end. **The geometric row `RM1` agreed with all ten for as long as they existed**, every string being inside its own `viewBox` — the measured instance of *containment is not correctness*, and the reason `RC1` had to be a different row rather than a stricter one. Closed by C12 I114 / §3ak.50 and `RC1`; F726 is the third writer the same sweep found and cannot be fixed the same way, and F729 is the same class with both writers being the axis emitter, which `RC1`'s shape cannot reach — keyed in 7 and 14

**F701** — the under-count was expected to show as a row that overruns its width. Rendering C11's `ps` table at width 60 with `⚡ events` and `⭐ starred` as cell text and **reading the rows rather than the totals**: every row before the change *drew* 60 cells, so no assertion about a row's width could see anything. What is wrong is **inside** the frame — the `family` column padded `⚡ events` as though it were 9 cells, so `status` begins at cell 27 on the two emoji rows and at cell 26 on the plain one: **one frame, two answers to where a column starts**. And the row hands C22 a string that *measures* 59, so a frame padding to 60 by the same measure emits 61 drawn cells, which is the wrap one layer up. **Both halves are invisible to a count**, and the frame-read is the only instrument that reached either · **Closed by F1019** — the under-count is gone at **both** conventions and the residue quoted here was one commit stale; the sentence survives between the header and the rows, and is repaired by `fitAt` at all three padding sites

**F1019** — **the recorded residue was one commit stale, and the sentence it was written for was true about a different pair of lines.** F701 closed with *the under-count survives at `ambiguousWidth: "narrow"`*; measured at HEAD, all 49 of F685's set measure 2 at **both** conventions, because `WIDE_RANGES` answers before the convention is consulted. F685 was measured at `f01c4f35+lanes5`; the table that answers it landed at `311ab18e+lanes6` — **the same commit F701 was written at** — so the annotation carried a figure one commit past its own fix, and `isWide`'s docstring already said so in the tree. **The live half is structural, which is why a trace could not reach it**: C09's `fit` takes `Pick<TerminalCapabilities, "unicode">`, so it forwards its record to `truncate` — which cuts at the session's convention — and then calls `pad` with none, defaulting to narrow, while `rowSpans` pads at the session's convention. At wide with labels `Δt`/`°C` the header began `note` at cell **36** and both rows at **34**, and the header carried a truncation marker where nothing was too long for its column. **Every line was 44 cells before and after.** Also settled here, because the width question needed an authority: `cells()` agrees with `string-width` 8.2.2 on all fourteen samples and Ink resolves string-width as a direct dependency, so the framework and the compositor already agree; `xterm-headless` disagrees on nine, and the reason is that its only Unicode provider is **version 6**, predating the Unicode 9 emoji Wide assignments — the instrument that says otherwise is the stale one. **The fixture had to be changed before the mutation could fail**: the first draft right-aligned `µs`, and the mutation dropping the convention **survived**, because U+00B5 is not Ambiguous and the character covering the padding could not move · **Closed** — `fitAt(text, width, ctx)` at all three sites, I21/I22 and commitments 19/20, T3.20 asserting column ink offsets per row as a set at both conventions with an ASCII control. Its blocker is in another component and travels as its own finding: `fit`'s one remaining caller draws **43 cells in a 40-cell block** at wide, right border out of frame

**F1030** — **the blocker was checked rather than restated, both of its premises held, and neither had ever been the obstacle.** F279 refused a `to`-half gate on two grounds: TypeScript 7.0.2's JS entry point no longer exports `createSourceFile`, and `esbuild` is present only as vite's transitive dependency. Measured at HEAD: `Object.keys(await import("typescript"))` is `default, version, versionMajorMinor`, `createSourceFile` is `undefined`, and esbuild resolves at 0.25.12 through vite **and** 0.28.2 through `tsx` — one parent short, so the conclusion stands. **Both premises survive and the refusal was still wrong**, because the satisfier is in the runtime rather than in this repository: `node:module`'s `stripTypeScriptTypes` throws `SyntaxError` on malformed input during the strip, which makes it a parser for this purpose. `mode: "transform"` rather than `"strip"`, and the difference is one construct. Apply-and-parse over the corpus: **1 947 of 1 974 pairs, 6.8 s, zero false positives**; shipped it costs 4.5 s, 5.5 → 10.0. F279's cheap-gate figure re-derived on the way: **207 mismatches across 108 of 192 runs**, not 31 of 942 — the refusal was **more** right than when it was written, and the entry still could not see the answer · **Closed** — `anchorsOf` widened, `parseOf` added, MA9 and a run file. Stated limits: `parseOf` asks whether the result is a program, not whether it means anything; no `.tsx`, no `enum`

**F1037** — **F277 and F279 are not one gate, and the clause of F277 that *is* mechanical is the sentence saying it is not.** They are two mechanisms: F279 is *the `to` half is unread*, and closes with a parser; F277 is *the anchor is textually perfect and its callers moved* — `TC2` anchored on a line the curve family had left, still present because the **bar** family runs it. Nothing textual reaches that; it is the citation-resolving-against-the-wrong-invariant class, and `docs/COMMITMENT_INVARIANT_AUDIT.md` §Fourth pass says why no mechanism for it should be built. One gate would have been the wrong shape. But F277's own claim that *the report cannot tell F219 and F277 apart* was mechanical and false: anchor multiplicity travels on the row now, and the survivor line names the third disposition instead of two · **Closed** — a unique anchor is absence of evidence for F219, not evidence for F277, and `hits` reads the primary edit only; both stated rather than implied

**F1025** — **the residue's largest shape was the sweep reading a nested document flat, and its headline class has no instances at all.** Run as F732 wrote it the sweep gives **124 pairs in 9 frames**, exactly F732's 126-in-11 less the right band's 2-in-2 that I115 closed — but F733 names *six* frames, and `calendar-hour` alone is 23 of the 124 and is in no entry. **84 of the 124 pairs are the instrument rather than the figure**: `pairplot-default` and `smallmultiples-default` are the corpus's only nested documents, four `<svg x="0|160|320|480">` panels each, and a flat reader collapses them onto the first. Resolved, the residue is **40**, and *identical texts at identical coordinates* has **zero** instances. `RC8`'s own header had diagnosed this and worked around it by excluding the facet frames by name, and the register never moved — **a workaround in the tree is not a disposition in the record**. Under it, two real defects with one cause each: `positionAxisAt` appends the domain maximum after the niced ticks, and where the terminal arm's `xTickRow` drops a caption that cannot keep its cell, the SVG arm drew both — `31` through `30` as one unreadable glyph; and the anchor was indexed by `i` while the position reads `facing`, so at `origin: "bottom-right"` the maximum grew 14.84 px into the gutter onto a value label. **I121 gave the ordinate the abut rule and cited *the abscissa's precedent* for the budget while leaving the abscissa's placement half unwritten.** Two of the residue's shapes are not defects and only reading the frames says so — `calendar-hour` at 11.467 px and `sankey-crowded` at 11.600 px render legibly, the em bound over-reporting on a column of glyphs with no descenders · **Closed** — I123, commitment 123, `RC10`/`RC11`, T6.105; row overprints 10 → 0 at both shipped canvases and `RC8` sweeps 848 frames where it swept 840. **Two survivors indict this finding's own spec sentences** — *a refusal reserves nothing* and *cleared against the kept set* are vacuous on a monotone axis whose only drop is the last caption, and the ruling says so now. 6 of 244 goldens move

**F1029** — **golden now composes a frame, and the first thing it caught was a guard the suite had a row for and could not construct.** The census was re-derived before anything was built and had moved twice: twelve golden files, eleven through `renderToLines`, two importing `src/shell/`, **none composing a frame**. F163's own row still carried the first count with the correction a hundred lines below it in this same document. Built as `test/support/frame-golden.ts` driving `buildSession` into a real `Session` and reading it back three ways — grid, style map, bytes — with 45 snapshots over 15 scene-arms and the theme arm derived from `Object.keys(defaultTheme)`, which puts `high-contrast` in a golden frame for the first time. **Ten mutations, and three findings out of them.** The harness fixture is a **symmetric camera**: with the default `name: "prism", binary: "prism"`, exchanging the header chips moves **0 of 45**; with `name: "calcium"` it moves **34** — measured by regenerating, not reasoned. `chrome.ts`'s `tone: "muted"` on the binary chip **forbids nothing**, because `simple.ts` resolves `chip?.tone ?? "muted"` and it *is* the default, so both chips render at the same ink and deleting it moves 0 of 45. And `paint.ts`'s `shows(window, span.row)` guard is asserted by nothing: T4.26 calls itself *the row the mutation pass demanded* and stays green when the guard is dropped, along with **48 rows across three files**, because its single span is inside the window and the guard cannot bind. The frames were read in colour, and the first read produced three groups of byte-identical PNGs from thirteen distinct inputs — the probe's own `parseLine` branching on `params[0]` alone, so a compound SGR read as a bare reset · **Open** — the two dispositions it asks for are outside the lane that found them, in `chrome.ts` and `session-paint.test.ts`, and roadmap 49's row lives in a file the lane did not own · **Closed** — disposition 2 by F1072, which found the census this row did not take: the inert tone is **one of five**, all five inert alone, so no single word was the defect and the property they are one instance of had no invariant. Disposition 3 by F1073, and it was **false when written**: T1.21b in `session-composite.test.ts` constructs exactly that state and fails on exactly that mutation, and had done for twenty-seven days. The forty-eight rows are honest and the conclusion drawn from them is not — a mutation's verdict is only as wide as the file list it was run over. Stated limits: the corpus test records rather than checks, the grid is one display cell short on rows carrying zero-width code points, and the snapshots were generated against a tree eight lanes were editing — a red frame must be **read**, never `-u`'d

**F1033** — **the finding was closed by work its own file records four lines above it, and the row still read `Open`.** F675 asked for a width reservation in `svgLayout`; F703's sweep, re-run against the committed goldens with facet coordinates resolved, finds **0 strings off the page in 0 frames** against F703's 13-in-7 and F675's *67 px, 10% of the figure*. Because *on the page* is a property satisfied by **cutting**, the ellipses were counted as well rather than assumed away: **one** in 244 goldens, which is the cap's own licensed instance, and `RC6` is green on it · **Closed** — nothing was written. Both closures already have gates (`RM1`, and the refusal-set equality assertion in the plot-arm test), and adding a row to a gate that already holds would be manufacturing work

**F1034** — **the closure grepped a marker and not the subject, so a landed fix was recorded as though a symbol resolved that is in no file in the tree.** F294's device was *the condition is a symbol rather than a judgement*, which is the right device. Grepped: `horizonFigure` resolves; **`contourFigure` occurs in no file**. What met the condition is `fieldFigure`, `(block: Plot) => Figure`, exactly the demanded shape under a different name — and the closure was written as though both resolved, in a notes document (*`contourFigure` … exists, which was the symbol this section said to grep*, plus *47 keys* against a measured **48**) and in the renderer's own comment, which sat beside its own correction of the family name. Everything F294 predicted was wrong in the measurable direction: *nineteen forms are refused* is **1**, all three forms draw, and both predicted families were wrong. **The pair with F1025 is the point**: there a workaround sat in the tree and the register never moved; here a fix landed and the record grepped the marker instead of the subject · **Closed** — the renderer's comment now names `fieldFigure`, records that `contourFigure` is in no file, and states the class. Two handoffs outside the lane: a notes document carrying 47 against 48 and the non-existent symbol, and the baseline tool's header carrying **three** stale clauses in one sentence — nineteen forms measured at one, three non-default origins that all draw, and an `ohlc` that is a block member rather than a `PlotForm`, in the header of the tool that generates the corpus every SVG row sweeps

**F703** — the brief reported one defect — a callout at `x=620.4` in a 640 viewBox on three `line-callout-*` frames. Measuring **every `<text>` in all 244 committed frames** against its own `viewBox`: **13 strings in 7 frames are off the page**, and the worst is not one of the three — `line-both-axes-narrow`'s callout runs **240.5 px past the edge, 38% of the figure**, and renders as `eu`. The right-hand **value label** `100` overruns by 2.1 px in six frames, **which is why this is not a callout defect with a callout fix**: the margin is short for everything drawn in it. The one graceful case is `heatmap-both-axes-narrow`, 0.5 px past `x=0` inside §3ak.41's clip, doing its job. F675 measured one frame of the same defect and the corpus sweep is what sized it; the constant behind it is F704 · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I113 with five rows and a revert row

**F705** — §3ak.41 had already *fixed* the left gutter by growing it to `widest × 12 × 0.6 + 6`, so the left side was the settled half. Measured: `boxplot-default`'s `sepal_length` is `end`-anchored at `x=86.4` and is **86.70 px wide at DejaVu's true advance — it starts at −0.30**, and at SF Mono's 0.6182 at **−2.6**, a third of a glyph gone. **Under the clip, silently, which is the exact failure mode §3ak.41 was written against**: a fix that removes the visible half of a defect leaves the sub-pixel half looking like success. The bound moves that label's start to +6.9

**F707** — `rightRoom` grants `3 × 12 × 0.65 + 6` and `fitLabel` divides the same three numbers back out, and **`23.400000000000006 / 7.8` is `2.9999…`** — so `100` shipped as `1…` **in a margin sized for it**. Found by **reading the diff of the moved frames**, not by any count: every frame still rendered, every coordinate was inside the page, and the golden would have recorded it faithfully. Two derivations of one product meeting at equality is a shape, not an accident of these numbers; the remedy is a `1e-9` tolerance, nine orders below a glyph. Row `RM5` · **Closed** (F991) — RM5 under C12 I113, with a tolerance mutation

**F709** — `line-callout-name` and `line-callout-both` are separate frames because §3ak.47 made the three callout modes draw three documents. **Both render the identical image**: the callouts are clipped to `alp` / `bet` at the page edge, so the difference the byte-compare certifies — ` 0.8774` against ` 99.12` — is **entirely off-page**. A frame gate that compares bytes cannot report that two of its frames are one picture, and the two frames had been standing as evidence that the modes differ · **Closed** (F991) — the run reads `alp` again under T6.90

**F710** — `line-callout-multiseries`' top-right corner renders **`90012`** — `99.12` written over `100` — and `line-both-axes-narrow`'s callout `e` sits on the label `0`. **Both texts are at the coordinates their own rules give them and both are inside the canvas, so no arithmetic assertion sees it**; it is visible only as a picture, and it is unchanged by the margin fix because both move right together. The terminal arm has a rule for exactly this — I48's contention ladder, *one series' last reading, and three that contend for rows* — and the SVG arm implements none of it. Owed, with the frame named; C12 §3ak.49b. **Corrected at d29cb2cf+lanes7: six frames and ten pairs, not two, every pair 2.415 px apart (F725) — and a third writer contends in the same band, the legend, which I48 explicitly forbids displacing, so that half is recorded and not fixed (F726).** Closed for the tick by I114 / §3ak.50 and `RC1`

**F713** — `gutterRoom` reserves from `figure.value?.labels ?? []` and the emitter writes `axis.labels[i] ?? String(tick)`, so **an axis whose `labels` array is short reserves for nothing and draws a number**. `rightRoom` does it correctly after the margin work, so the two sides of the box now measure differently. **No instance in the corpus** — every axis in it labels every tick — which is what makes it invisible to every gate and what makes it worth recording rather than fixing: fixing it moves guttered frames on a case that is fine, which is §3ak.41's own ruling about narrowing. C12 §3ak.49b. **Corrected at d29cb2cf+lanes7: the premise is false (F727).** `ValueAxis.labels` is total over `ticks` by construction — one producer, three call sites, no object literal in the tree — so the fallback branch is unreachable, the two reserves are the same function and the mirrored fix would have changed nothing. The real hazard is a **silently ellipsised** gutter label if the two ever part, which nothing watched and `RC6` watches now

**F721** — a guard on a rendering path was expected to be covered by the golden corpus. Hand-running `SANKEY-UNGUARDED` — `sankeyArea`'s `assertPictureGlyph` call deleted — **313 of 314 rows passed** across `theme.test.ts`, `terminal-baseline.test.ts` and `plot-forms.test.ts`, the single failure being **T1.37, the row written for it**. **No input the tree produces trips the guard**, which is exactly what makes it a guard worth having and exactly what makes a green run indistinguishable from its absence. T6.92; `SANKEY-UNGUARDED` and `PLOT3D-UNGUARDED` in the run file · **Closed** (F991) — the `SANKEY-UNGUARDED` mutation and T6.92

**F669** — the brief's sweep cell — *is there any other text in either arm whose ink and ground are not a validated pair* — was expected to turn up nothing or one more site. **Ten.** In the SVG arm the callout, the treemap/heatmap tile label, the graph node label, the outline label one slot per depth, and the label-at-a-point branch; in the **terminal arm** the callout column, a flame or icicle frame's name inside its own bar, a pie's legend rows and a run's label. Every one is `categorical` as ink or as ground and **none was a validated pair**. The terminal callout is the same figure in the same slot as the SVG one, which is D11 holding — so the arm that moves the *sites* moves ten across a seam that must agree and the arm that moves the *check* moves one function. **The sweep is what decided the ruling.** Open · **Closed** (F991) — commit `21218402` — the C10 §4g pairing

**F670** — C10 I15's third clause — *a `decoration` palette … is lint-restricted to declared art* — is a claim about a **class**, and its mechanism covers one member: SS21's pattern is `/["'`]spectrum\.\w|palettes\s*\.\s*spectrum/` and matches `spectrum` and nothing else. `categorical` is the other `decoration` palette, the framework paints it as text at **ten** sites, and no scan, floor or type reaches any of them. The citation sits beside the *spectrum* palette in `tokens-dark.ts:193`, which is why it reads as covering the clause — a claim about a class, a mechanism covering one member, and a citation that resolves. Closed by `validateDecorationText` and a reworded I15

**F675** — the SVG arm places a callout at `end[0] + LABEL_GAP` with `text-anchor="start"` and reserves a **fixed** 25.6 px between the plot box and the viewBox, while the terminal arm reserves the cells a callout needs before the layout exists (`calloutWidth`, C12 I48). In the shipped golden `line-callout-both.svg`, `alpha 0.8774` starts at x = 620.4 in a 640-wide canvas — about 86 px of glyphs ending near **707**, so **67 px, 10% of the figure's width, is off the page**, with `line-callout-{name,single}.svg` the same and a right-hand y-axis label overrunning by 2 px. Read as a picture the callout is clipped mid-string at the frame's edge, and **every byte-compare golden records it faithfully and none can object** — a snapshot records, it does not check. Not repaired, the fix being a width reservation in `svgLayout`; filed as a request. Open · **Closed by F1033** — **it was already closed and by an entry four lines above it in this file.** F703's sweep re-run against the committed goldens with facet coordinates resolved finds **0 strings off the page in 0 frames**, against F703's 13-in-7 and this row's *67 px, 10% of the figure*; `alpha 0.8774` has moved from x 620.4 to 546.4, ink ending at 635.4 inside a 640 canvas. Because *on the page* is satisfied by cutting, the ellipses were counted too: **one** in 244 goldens, the cap's own licensed instance, and `RC6` is green on it

**F676** — C10 I21 admits a picture cell — *a palette ref may be a background for a cell that carries no text* — and it is expected to be held by the type, the way `wash` is. It is not: `sankey.ts:506` and `scatter3.ts:1459` both build the background through `slot(ref, …).colour` rather than `resolveBackground`, so *carries no text* is a property of the caller. And the margin is the whole argument — the worst `categorical × categorical` pair measures **1.00 on all three shipped themes**, because a categorical palette is authored for hue and I17 only forbids two slots being *equal*. A half-block cell whose two owners are 1.00 apart is invisible in greyscale and to a reader who cannot separate those hues. Recorded; named as the mutation the run file cannot score. **Closed by F1017**, and its two halves closed separately. The first — *nothing enforces that the cell carries no text* — was closed by `isPictureGlyph`, which refuses a background on a glyph outside `U+0020`, Block Elements and Braille Patterns at both constructors; what is still owed there is the compile-time refusal, held open in C10 §4g.4 with its two blockers named. The second — *legible only by hue* — was never a measurement, and measured it fails: seven pairs under the floor on every shipped theme, worst ΔE2000 **0.6**. C10 §4j and I39 hold the debt list. The type remedy stays open under F991; the hue claim does not.

**F685** — **49 of the code points kept Ambiguous by `DRAWN_AS_GEOMETRY` are Wide in the property** (`⚡` `⚪` `⛄` `⛔`, `♈`..`♓`, `☰`..`☷`). At `ambiguousWidth: "wide"` they measure 2, which is right **for the wrong reason**; at `"narrow"` they measure 1, which is wrong, and the deviation is why no test at the wide convention can see it. **A guard that is accidentally correct in the mode people test and wrong in the mode people ship** is worth naming as a shape: the deviation is not neutral about the gap it overlaps. Open · **Closed** (F991) — **Refused, deviation recorded** — `DRAWN_AS_GEOMETRY` stands with its reason, and the narrow-mode wrongness is the cost

**F687** — `partSeparator` returned `' · '` on the unicode arm from a parameter typed `Pick<TerminalCapabilities, "unicode">`, so the function **could not ask** the question its answer depends on: U+00B7 is Ambiguous and two cells at `wide`, where the renderer counted one. `npm test` went 5,108 green to two red the moment the width table was fixed, U6f (C12 I54) reporting `heatmap/default draws a two-cell glyph at wide`. **F292's shape four functions along**, and `categoryMarks` twelve lines below in the same file takes the full alphabet and switches on `ambiguousWidth` — the right neighbour got it right and the narrow parameter is what stopped anyone noticing the wrong one

**F688** — `plot-y-axis.test.ts` YA4's own comment states its premise — *`café` is Ambiguous, so a terminal reporting `wide` measures it at five cells and a default at four, the exact drift §3f names*. At f01c4f35 `é` was not in the table, `cells("café","wide")` was **4**, and the drift the row exists to catch **could not occur**; it passed for that reason. With the table fixed it fails on `firsts.size` = 2, which is the C12 I47 defect. **A vacuous test is indistinguishable from a satisfied one until the thing it depends on starts behaving**, and the instrument that found it was not a review of the test but a change to the substrate underneath it. F692 is what the row turned out to be carrying underneath that

**F689** — `c02-ambiguous.mjs` row 2 — *block elements are not ambiguous* — was re-anchored onto the `DRAWN_AS_GEOMETRY` row when the range moved there, and **MA4 went green while the mutation stopped mutating**: deleting the geometry row leaves T2.52 passing 5 of 5, because `AMBIGUOUS_RANGES` now carries U+2580..U+258F and U+2592..U+2595 from the property itself. **A valid anchor that is no longer a mutation is exactly what MA4 cannot see** — it checks that anchors resolve, not that they change an answer. Re-anchored a second time as a guard inside `isAmbiguous`, which is what the row's name claims, and verified by hand. The general shape is new: **deriving a table from its source can make a deletion behaviour-preserving**, so every mutation whose subject is one row of that table needs re-running rather than re-pointing · **Closed** (F991) — re-anchored into `isAmbiguous` and verified by hand

**F691** — `partSeparator`'s wide arm now takes the ASCII form — the hyphen is Neutral, one cell under both conventions, already in the function, and a new mark would owe SS47 a justification for a distinction no reader of a wide terminal can see. **Fixing it broke G4**, which scans `svg.ts` for the literals `ambiguousWidth` and `TerminalCapabilities` precisely so the image path cannot acquire a terminal fact; a capability record passed to the SVG arm to be ignored is exactly what the rule refuses. So the shared caption takes a **separator** rather than a capability and its marks, and `marks.ts` exports `IMAGE_SEPARATOR` for the arm with no cell grid. The rule was right and the **routing** was the repair — the gate named the seam and the fix moved to the seam's own side of it · **Closed** (F991) — `IMAGE_SEPARATOR` with one reader

**F649** — C10 §4's *if a surface ever paints text on it, that surface is wrong or the exclusion is* names a trigger and no watcher, so it was satisfied by nobody looking: the SVG arm has painted every axis tick, axis title, legend row, callout, notice, empty-figure message and sankey label on `surface.bgDeep` since it was written, C12 §3ap.7 filed the debt as *not this component's to fix*, and nothing in C10 moved. A03 §2's vacuity class in prose — the conditional could not be violated, so it read as obeyed. The repair is a row that asks the property (T2.27), not a better sentence. **Closed**

**F651** — nothing constrains which side of `bg` a theme's `bgDeep` sits on, because nothing measures it. All three shipped themes, 27 slots, both grounds: dark recesses **away** from the tones and every ratio rises; high-contrast's `bgDeep` is **lighter** than its `bg`; light recesses **toward** them and **twelve slots fail**, `tone.muted` at **2.44** under its own 2.5 floor. Two themes in three passing is what made the exclusion look harmless, and it is a property of each theme's authoring rather than of the slot. Asserted as a set in C10 T1.34. **Closed**

**F653** — `svg.ts:1132` inks a treemap or heatmap tile's label in **`ground`** over the tile's series fill, so the pair is `surface × categorical` and no C10 §4 pairing holds it — a ground used as ink, measured by nothing. Its ratio against the *page* is 1.00 by construction, which is why T2.27 has to exempt it by name; against the fills it is 6.26 dark, 5.17 light, 7.55 high-contrast. Owed to C12, whose ink it is. Open · **Closed** (F991) — commit `21218402` — C10 §4g and I35

**F656** — unwiring an SP rule from `tools/enforce/index.mjs` fails **nothing** — A03 commitment 14's fabricated violation calls the checker directly and 14b's inventory compares rule *ids* against a list, so a rule that is off is invisible from all three directions it should be visible from. Measured on the new rule and on the pre-existing `checkTestRowIds` both: **no new failures** with the spread deleted, and SP7 has been in the gate since it landed with nothing checking that it still was. Closed as a class rather than an instance — SP10 reads `index.mjs` and asserts a carrier per rule, the carrier table compared to `SPEC_RULES` **by equality**. Stated limit: it proves the gate *calls* the checker, not that it gates on the result, SP8 reporting without gating. **Closed**

**F659** — `noticeDefinition.render` draws `glyphLead` at `index === 0` and spaces on rows 1..n, so a `quote` glyph slot gives a three-row quotation its mark **once** and a blank rail under it — every gutter-shaped mark is a bullet. Every count agrees between the wanted frame and the drawn one — same rows, same width, same reserved columns — so `measure`, the row property and C09 I1 all pass with the wrong one, and **the frame is the only instrument that separates them**. Closed by `GLYPH_RAIL` (C09 I41): a token property, no schema and no geometry change. The reversing mutation fails T2.107 and nothing else. **Closed**

**F664** — `docs/components/C09_block_library.md` §8 declares two commitments numbered **41** — the animated image and the run-tone one — and `make enforce` is green: SP1 resolves each commitment's citation and never looks at its number, so the failure SP2 exists to prevent (*1..n in document order*) is unwatched one list over. Left alone rather than renumbered, a shared document mid-lane being the wrong place to renumber. Filed as a request. Open · **Closed** (F998) — SP11, and C09 declared **three** duplicates rather than two; renumbered 41,42,43 → 59,60,61, and nine more across four specs are on a debt list compared by equality

**F634** — `paint-order="stroke"` is what puts the halo **under** the fill; drop the attribute and keep the stroke and every label is a ground-coloured blob, while both hex attributes sit exactly where a rule reading them expects. Run by hand: the ink mutation kills **24** SK11 rows, the halo mutation 24, and this one **12** — the ratio half is blind to it by construction. The `paint-order` assertion is separate rather than folded in, and the 12-against-24 asymmetry is T6.89 rather than a number nobody wrote down. **Closed**

**F635** — two rows both called `SK10` in one spec passes `make enforce` — *338 files · 26 specs · 15 728 invariant references resolved · no violations* with both in the file. Caught by reading a grep. `T6.87 → SK10 fails` would resolve against whichever row a reader found first and both readings are plausible: SP5's *existence, not aim* one artefact along, and the definition side has no rule at all where the citation side is exact. Renamed to `SK11`; the gate is owed. Open · **Closed** (F991) — commit `f6828b84` — SP10, with its own mutation run

**F637** — restating a patch unit's `rows` as its **line** count passed **99 rows across eight files** — every contract, integration, edge, unit and golden row for the window — because every row bounds the window from above and an over-stated unit shows *less* diff and never more. Containment is satisfied by every wrong answer inside the bounds, and the goldens render the whole patch and never a window. `T1.20c` states the **equality across the seam**, its expectation coming from `height.ts`, and it is the only row that kills it. **Closed**

**F644** — C09 T3.10b — *no wrapped row exceeds the width it was wrapped at* — is the wrapper's property row and passes on F590 and F591 both, because a **short** row fits. The property nothing asserted is *no row could have taken the first word of the next row*: swept over 14 strings × widths 1–40, **102 of 560 pairs and 161 row joins** violated before the arm, **0 and 0** after. The count is the argument for sweeping rather than pinning — two pinned rows would have closed the instance and left the class. T3.10b2. **Closed**

**F647** — the blind spot above was checked against the nearest component with the same shape and was not there: the same mutation against `table/definition.ts`'s `unitsOf` failed **six rows immediately**, T2.14 among them, because the table's `measure` computes its height by its own arithmetic while the patch's `measure` and `unitsOf` shared the wrong number. So the class is narrower than it reads — what distinguishes the two is whether the expectation has a source independent of its subject. Keyed here for the row; F637 in this group is the instance · **Closed** (F991) — **Recorded** — it narrows F637 to an instance

**F588** — C04 T2.36's own example — `"alpha beta gamma"` at 12 with `gamma` valued is one row more — could not fail: the plain wrapper already breaks before `gamma`. A single-word valued token never moves a prose row count; the count moves only where the row has no break point (`"x(abcde)yz"` at 6, 2 → 3). *The finding's own example checked by nothing*, in a spec row. Rewritten. **Closed**

**F589** — `validateBlock({ kind: "rule", colormap: "viridis" })` was `ok: true` and the value survived on the block — `checkColormapName` asked `raw` and `notice` and never `rule`, F207's *accepted and ignored* at the gate. Refused now (C04 I90). **Closed**

**F594** — sixteen patch goldens × four widths × two themes × two modes were all blind to C25 I10 — every case built through `patchOf` / `hunkOf` and `block()`, none through `b.patch`, where the writer lives; `b.patch(` had one caller in the tree, a context-only hunk. The one C25 feature whose whole output is an SGR attribute inside a golden row was recorded by no golden. One case through `b.patch` now. **Closed**

**F604** — `import { MARKER_ID } from ".../transcript/index.js"` resolved to `undefined` under vitest's transform rather than failing — the name is not exported from the barrel — so the row failed reading *expected "transcript:evicted" to be undefined*, the store right and the constant nothing. `tsc` would have caught it; vitest did not. The test deep-imports `cap.ts`. Open · **Closed** (F991) — `tsconfig.json` includes `test`, so `make check` sees it and `make check` is a CI job

**F625** — `decodePng`'s consumers dropped to `codec.ts` itself and tests when `image.ts` and `transmit-image.ts` moved to `decodeImage`, and MG25 stayed green — it counts occurrences with comments stripped, and `decodeImage` calls `decodePng` one screen below. A barrel export kept alive by its own file. Open, recorded so nobody takes it for a seam · **Closed** (F1000) — the census was stale: a live non-test consumer imports it **through the barrel** out of `dist/`. The line stays and names its consumer, and three genuinely orphaned names MG25 cannot see are recorded by equality

**F628** — the I93 gate was declared done by a commit message, carried by a brief into five lanes, and was not: the type had landed and the validator still refused non-PNG bytes. T2.39 — the gate half's own row — measures a PNG under a different digest and never hands the validator a GIF, so the row that stood for the gate was green with it closed. **Closed** in the merge; keyed here for the row, and F619 in group 10 for the claim

**F569** — 403 golden rows passed unchanged through the `code`/`raw` window because `test/golden/blocks.test.ts` renders `ONE_PER_KIND` whole and the one `code` fixture is two lines — a fact about the fixtures, not evidence about the windows, and the instruction assumed the gate could have moved. The frame-reads that see the window are T1.18 and T2.22, byte for byte against the whole rendering · **Closed** (F991) — **Recorded** — the frame reads are the rows that see the window

**F581** — the states golden strips SGR before it records, so a span cannot move it: it holds geometry and only geometry, and *no golden frame moved* when spans landed is the gate's blind spot. The bytes are asserted in the edge rows, in Ink's form, and the attribute order at `paint()` (F580)

**F585** — `truncateParts` reserved `limit - 1` for the `…` that `truncate` had learned to measure — F292's second instance one function along, at the same single rung, unreached because the second function had no `wide` corpus. Now measured through `clusterCells`. **Fixed**

**F552** — SS53's first run over 36 allow entries found five dead — one for the whole life of its rule, one the brief did not name — and its first draft measured with a comment-strip that deletes the marker a negative lookahead reads. `lineFires` is one function now. Removed, `why` rewritten; the coverage rows carry the one-file-per-directory residue · **Closed** (F991) — SS53's `lineFires` is one function, and five dead entries went

**F553** — MG2 had a row and no rule for the whole life of the suite, under a `PENDING_RULES` entry reading *nothing — implementable today*. Implemented; the tree had one cycle, `shell/paint.ts` ↔ `shell/composite.ts`, since broken, so `ACKNOWLEDGED_CYCLES` is empty by equality. **Fixed**

**F554** — `PENDING_RULES`' string arm skipped SS18 while C10 and twenty components landed; a string beginning with a component id is now read as a `waitsOn` and fails when built. SS18 implemented over adapters, fixtures and shell, clean. **Fixed**

**F555** — TD1–TD6 had a test and no gate — `index.mjs` never imported `todo-expiry.mjs`, and the unit tier is not on the pre-commit path. Wired; the unit file asserts the call sites by reading `index.mjs`, since calling the functions is what could not see the gap. **Fixed**

**F557** — SS54's seeding, writing premises as symbols, found two claims wrong in the record: `DEPENDENCIES.md` names `node:readline` as the alternative in use (zero occurrences; C06 splits on `indexOf`) and C12 I71 names `contourFigure`, which exists only in comments. The `typescript-eslint` decision is owed and unowned · **Partly** (F991) — the register is seeded and C12 I71 corrected; the `typescript-eslint` decision is owed and unowned · **Closed** (F1056) — **already closed at HEAD, and both halves of the residue are false.** The decision was taken 2026-09-03 with all five justification parts and `npm view` figures, and it is watched by two refusal rows. This entry and the one that decided it are the same day, seven commits apart; the sweep carried the sentence forward without re-deriving it

**F562** — no test constructed the last resort — C07 §11 cites T3.7 and `grep 'last-resort' test/` returned nothing. T1.21 reaches it through `finish`'s own second validation and is the first row to assert `meta.adapter === "last-resort"`. **Fixed**

**F563** — the floor-suppression branch had one witness, `logs`, whose window carries nothing — so the kind with the most to lose, `table`, had never been drawn floored. T1.22 constructs both siblings and reads both frames. **Fixed**

**F510** — MG25 refuses *an export nothing consumes* and its declaration regex reads `export function|async function|class`, so `type`, `interface` and `const` are outside its subject — **661 declarations covered, 754 not**, and MG25's own logic over the uncovered arms finds **ten** unconsumed exports it cannot see. **F84's shape exactly**, one rule along. A second gap stacks with it and is independent: the consumer count is occurrences of the **bare name across all of `src/`**, so two unrelated declarations of one name excuse each other — measured on `Rung`, which F498 left dead in `project3.ts` and whose homonym in `definition.ts` carries the count past `uses > 1`. **The allow-list is not the weak part**: `UNCONSUMED_FUNCTIONS` is equality-gated and its one live entry is exactly what the shipped arm reports, which is the control saying this scan measures the rule and not something else · **Closed** (F991) — **Refused, limits recorded** — MG25 is functions and classes by design

**F481** — `AT1`'s first draft asserted the strongest-looking thing available: **the muted cell count falls by exactly what the accent count gains.** Exact arithmetic, both directions — and it **survived both mutations it was written to catch**. Conservation is not attribution: moving colour from one part of the frame to another satisfies the equation, which is what the equation says. The first mutation left the ticks and label carrying the tone; the second moved colour onto a box the fixture had turned off, so it could not have been seen in any case. The repair is two arms and neither is an equation. **And fixing it turned up a rule the walk did not have**: with `box3: "full"` the accent count falls from 9 to 1, because the box strokes first and a tie goes to whoever drew first — the box occludes the axis lines it coincides with. *Assert the artefact, not a proxy*, in its sharpest form: a conserved total looks like it constrains two numbers and constrains one · **Closed** (F991) — commits `894956ff`, `7228dbfe`

**F479** — `Tone` is a ten-member union and **the runtime gate does not check membership**. Measured with `tone: "chartreuse"`: notice, plot series, `points3`, `lines3`, `surfaces3` and a table cell **all accept it**. And `slot("tone.chartreuse", …)` returns `{}` — no colour, no throw — so the mark draws *uncoloured*: not a refused document and not a wrong colour, but the third outcome, which leaves no trace. **Found while walking a fourth tone field into existence**, and the first instinct — validate the new field — would have been the wrong repair: one member of one union on one of seven carriers teaches a rule that holds in one place. Recorded as a known limit with its measurement rather than half-closed; the check belongs to C04's validator across every carrier at once

**F465** — `EDGE_HALF = 0.7` says a sample is on an edge below 0.7 of one sample away. Widening it to **3** survived every assertion in the row that owns it, because the fixture's cells are fifteen samples across: a six-sample band still leaves interior, and every relation the row asserts is between two masks that **widen together**. **The quantity the constant is about appears in none of them.** What states it is a ratio against the geometry — edge samples over the projected perimeter is the band's width in samples, `0.916` at 0.7 and four times that at 3, resolution- and mesh-independent. **A constant expressed in units of X is not tested by counting Xs**, because the count scales with the fixture and the constant does not · **Closed** (F991) — the ratio-against-perimeter assertion

**F426** — a window of a `code` block is a **different parse**, not a cut token. Measured: a block comment opening above the slice loses the `comment` slot entirely and its lines render as live code. `measure` never tokenises, so **C09 I26 holds perfectly at every window while the rendering is wrong** — containment is not correctness, through the invariant written to make windows safe. **A width travels with a window and a parse does not** (C09 I25a): `patch` pins `numberWidth`, `keyValue` now pins `keyWidth`, `code` is refused. The repo had written the answer — *`widest` a whole `keyValue` and `tokenise` a whole code block* — and the two names are not the same kind of blocker.

**F431** — the 3D design note's premise, measured before the design was read. *A braille dot is square, so a projection into the dot grid needs no aspect correction* — **true, off by 5%, and irrelevant**. Four numbers said the dot grid holds the silhouette (0.3% and 1.5% disagreement against the pixel truth, the handle's hole four dots clear, 163 shade levels of 186); **the frame is a smudge**. The explanation is one line: *6931 of 7254 lit dots are interior, and every one of them is on* — inside the outline the braille channel is a constant, and the whole interior is the 80×24 cell colours. **C09 commitment 35 had already ruled it** — *a half block spends a cell on two full colours where braille spends it on eight dots, so a photograph arrives as a photograph* — and a shaded 3D render is a photograph. The half-block arm at 80×48, a quarter of the samples, shows every structure the braille arm loses. **3D is a terminal form on the half-block rung**, and the premise is a property of the cell rather than of braille: `4.205 × 4.0` and `8.41 × 8.0` are the same 1.051 : 1.

**F434** — SP6's recorded limit, measured. The ranking's per-group column is unchecked, and **6 of 14 rows disagree with the ids keyed in their own section** while the total passes **because the errors cancel** (−1, +1, −3, +1, +4, −1).
