The failure mode is one natural move — entry beside the related finding, count on the owning group — and it happened **twice in one session**, caught only by reading the diff. Not gated: *keyed* has no strong enough definition (the loose reading counts prose mentions; the strict one orphans 8 of 435 ids), and a gate over numbers nobody maintains is red on arrival and edited to fit. The limit carries its figures now. · **Closed** (F991) — **Recorded** — the limit carries its figures and is deliberately not gated

**F430** — MG27's field walk ends at the first nested `Readonly<{ … }>`, so every member declared after one is invisible: 3 types, 6 fields. **The diagnostic points the wrong way** — an invisible field never reaches `unreached`, so the bidirectional arm reports its exemption as *stale*, "which the builder now sets", about a field no builder mentions. A reader following the message deletes a correct entry. **`KeyValue.keyWidth` is the cost**: the pin `keyValue`'s window rests on, twin to `patch.numberWidth` and its five-line `BUILDER_OMISSIONS` reason, with no entry and never asked for one. **The regex's optional `\n` was added by a correction** whose comment records the shape it fixed and not the one it broke. Fixed by brace-matching; the first run found that one gap and nothing else.

**F429** — the fix for a known interaction, and the assumption underneath it was never stated. C09 §6b had ruled that a slice taken in declaration order shows different rows than the range C14 addressed; the remedy — sort, then slice — assumes `sortedRows` is the identity on an already-sorted slice. **`kindOf` reads the values present**, so a window that drops the one non-numeric value re-classifies its own column: `2 · 10 · abc` renders `10 · 2 · abc` whole and **`2 · 10`** windowed, reversed, with every count and every height correct. **Two cheaper repairs are both wrong the same way** — stripping `sort` loses the header's arrow, and marking the column `sortable: false` changes what the table *offers*, which is the objection the walk had already made against stripping `actions`. Ruled as `Table.presorted` (C11 I19).

**F427** — two negative claims, both true when written. C25 I21a says the generic window check *does not reach a patch at all, because a patch does not declare `BlockDefinition.window`*, and it declares one. The conformance file's header says `logs` is *the only kind that declares a window*. **Together they left the generic I26 property covering one kind while a spec said it could not reach the other and a test said there was no other** — two documents agreeing, neither checked. For a claim of the form *nothing does X*, the file holding it cannot be the one making it. · **Closed by F1050** — and the corpus had lost a second kind while the claim stood: `patch` **and `terminal`** are both absent, and **nothing anywhere compares `CORPUS` to `DIVIDES`**. A negative claim's corpus is the thing to check, because *nothing does X* passes hardest the day the corpus stops containing X

**F422** — `make instruments` compares by equality and failed on the day three instruments landed without fixtures; `make all` includes it, and **has been red for three commits**. **The second instance in the same target**, with the first recorded in that target's own comment four lines from where the fixtures were owed — *a gate is covered by where it sits in the default path*, and being in `make all`'s list is not the same as the list being run. **Fixed two of three**; `probe.py` runs at import and opens `/dev/tty` at module scope, so its parsers cannot be imported, and the expiry is named as a symbol rather than taken — the restructure needs a real terminal to verify.

**F420** — `heatmap.ts`'s `layoutFor` sizes its row-label column from `series[].label`, and `/monitor` declared `categories`, which no matrix form reads. Eight rows of correct colour with **no ordinate**, at every width, with the labels declared and discarded. C12 I18 already forbids the frame — *a heatmap's row labels **are** its ordinate*, and A2's drop order ends *an unlabelled matrix is never rendered* — but the ladder never fires, because there were no labels to drop. **docker's `cpu-history` has always used `label`**: two consumers, one right, and nothing between them. **Fixed in both surfaces**, with the C04 refusal owed as a ruling — `categories` on a matrix form should be refused at construction the way an unknown colormap is.

**F418** — F415's shape one column over. `detectColourDepth` matches the substrings `256color` and `truecolor` and knows no terminal by name, so **`TERM=xterm-kitty` is detected as a 16-colour terminal** by the same table that answers `kitty` for its graphics protocol four lines up and names ghostty for `synchronisedUpdate`. Three capabilities, three vocabularies about one terminal. Masked because both terminals set `COLORTERM`; it surfaced the moment F416's harness fix propagated `TERM` without it, dropping the container to 4-bit and taking every continuous colormap with it. **Open** — the fix is a shared terminal table, which is a C02 ruling. **Ruled and closed for the identification** — C02 I11 and commitment 14: one `identifyTerminal`, read by all three, asserted structurally because three lists that agree pass every agreement test. It found two things the entry missed: *latent* was wrong (**`ssh` forwards `TERM` and not `COLORTERM`**, so the case is live and not ours), and `TERM=xterm-ghostty` alone answered `imageProtocol: kitty` with `synchronisedUpdate: false` — **a disagreement a test asserted as correct** under a comment that is true about the code and false about Ghostty. The fourth instance is named and instrumented rather than ruled: identification is not capability, and only `mouse` asks whether a sequence reaches the emulator · **Closed by F1021** — and that sentence had drifted before it was closed: `identifyTerminal` is read by **four** capabilities, not three, and the tmux half of the fourth instance closed under F432. Ruled as `sources`, a third member of the detection record naming five kinds for *what would falsify the answer* — `declared`, `stated`, `inferred`, `assumed`, `unreachable` — **produced by the rule that produces the value**, so a fourth list cannot drift into a component just repaired for having three

**F415** — `detectImageProtocol` returns `kitty` for `TERM=xterm-kitty` and nothing else, so the entire protocol arm was unreachable on Ghostty, WezTerm and Konsole — three of the four terminals the plan named. T1.7 asserts exactly the three cases the function implements, so rule and test agree and neither knows about a fourth terminal; and every other test injects capabilities directly, which is correct and leaves the detection as the one thing nothing exercises. **The knowledge was four lines up in the same table** — `synchronisedUpdate` has named ghostty since v1. **Fixed for ghostty on a measurement**; WezTerm and Konsole owed, with the probe named as their expiry.

**F454** — `writeDepth` compared a `double` against a `Float32Array` slot, so storing rounds and comparing does not: a second writer handing over the **identical** value passed `z < d.z[i]` whenever that rounding went up. **About half of all ties, decided by the last bits of a number nothing reads** — and *strictly nearer, so a tie is stable, so first-drawn wins* is the sentence three separate rules rest on, including the frame-drawn-last fix one finding along. **Nothing that compares depths can see it**: as doubles the two are equal, so an assertion that they tie passes. It showed as a missing marker glyph, and *which* marker went missing changed when an unrelated rounding convention changed. `Math.fround` on both sides is the whole fix. **The reusable shape**: a value stored in one precision and compared in another has an equality that is a coin flip, and a `Float32Array` beside a `double` is the ordinary way to write a depth buffer · **Closed** (F991) — `Math.fround` on both sides, pinned by a mutation

**F453** — `strokeSeg` placed a sample with `Math.round` and every other writer in the same file uses `Math.floor`, so sample `i` covered `[i − 0.5, i + 0.5)` in one convention and `[i, i + 1)` in the other — **disagreeing for half of all coordinates**. A trajectory drifts off its own markers by up to a sample, systematically; and the rule that depends on the agreement (*points draw before lines, so a marker is never swallowed*) was decorative, because a tie needs both writers to be naming the same cell. **F445 one function over** — there the block origin was floored after subtracting half its width, and neither convention is visibly wrong alone · **Closed** (F991) — the two sites and the mutation that pins them

**F450** — three guards went into the 3D reference frame; **one had never worked, one no longer has a subject, and one never had one**, and the assertions written for all three agreed. `clipProject` moved an endpoint behind the eye to the near plane and `project` culls on `z <= NEAR` **inclusive**, so the clipped point was refused by the function the clip exists to satisfy and every segment it was written to save was dropped anyway. **Invisible for two independent reasons**: the clipped remainder is thrown outside `[0,1]²` at five of six distances swept, so a frame comparison agrees whether a segment is clipped, dropped, or clipped-then-dropped. **And the negative recorded in the spec was measured against the broken path** — *0 visible at every distance*, re-measured at **1 of 3 at `distance: 1.7`** once the clip worked: a probe run against a function that returns `null` measures the `null`. The label gap no longer reproduces `10.5` (24 azimuths, none abut) because three unrelated changes on the same commit moved the labels apart, and the anchor depth test fires nowhere because the anchors are pushed *outward*, toward the reader, by construction — both kept on asymmetry with their figures and the date. **The reusable part**: all three assertions were about a *consequence* — ink present, labels fewer, a run no longer than four characters — and a consequence is reachable by other mechanisms. **A guard whose only test is a consequence is one the next change can silence without failing anything**

**F449** — the 3D tick labels use the collision rule every other axis here uses — *drop the later, ordered by extent*, where collision means the cells **overlap** — and the frame read `10.5`. It is `1` and `0.5` at adjacent columns: two disjoint claims, both legal, both drawn, one token that is neither. **Two labels touching are unreadable without overlapping**, and an occupancy set reserving exactly the label's cells cannot say so. **Fixed** — the reservation is the label plus a blank each side, which is the spacing the y-gutter already has one dimension down and which nobody had written as a rule because there it falls out of the layout. **The axis names had it one level up**: placed past each positive end, and x and y both run *to* the anchor corner, so two names and a shared tick landed on one cell and the frame read `xy1` — they sit at their midpoints now. **The instrument is the frame and nothing else could be**: both are legal geometry, correct arithmetic, and a row every *is the label present* assertion agrees with

**F447** — `make all` is `check … e2e`, `check` type-checks the examples, the examples resolve `@fmx/calcium` to `dist/`, and **`dist/` is built by `e2e`** — the last target. So on any commit that widens a public type, `check` reads the *previous* build and passes. Measured with timestamps: `plot3d` joined `PlotForm`, check went green, `e2e` rebuilt `dist/` five minutes before the commit, and the example's `Record<PlotForm, Entry>` failed on the **next** commit's first check — **attributed to whatever landed in between**, which is the expensive half. **Not a stale cache but an ordering**: the examples are the only population that reads the *build*, and they are right to, because that is what a consumer does and R01 R4.4's reuse claim is exactly that. **Fixed** — `check` builds before the example loop, one `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json` that `e2e` pays again. **And the 24th forced declaration is the other half**: F441 counted 23 by running `tsc --noEmit` at the root, which is the root workspace only, so the count was right about what it measured and silent about a population it never reached — *a number derived from one command describes that command*

**F446** — the `plot3d` pass ran ten mutations and **two survived**, neither about the code. SC7 asserted `new Set(colours).size > 1` — *both clouds appear* — which a renderer with **no depth test at all** satisfies, because the loser still draws everywhere the winner is not. SC8 asserted `cs.size === 1` — *one reading, one colour* — which is true at the ramp's floor, at its top and at its middle. **One mechanism twice**, and both comments named the right property while both assertions counted: *containment is not correctness*, one level up, in rows written by someone holding that sentence. **Fixed by identifying**: the contested cell against the near cloud's own colour, and the zero span against `continuousColour(map, 0.5)` with the floor asserted to differ. **A fourth disposition for a survivor** — not *the code is wrong*, not *a second defect masks the first*, not *the spec sentence forbids nothing*, but **the assertion is about the wrong property and the comment above it names the right one**, which review cannot separate because the comment reads as the assertion

**F445** — the raster arm placed each point's `bw x bh` block at `floor(x) - (bw >> 1)`, half a sample **left and up** at every near point. **`make refdiff` found it**, not the suite: our helix sat two columns left of matplotlib's `scatter3D` with the same shape, and centring the block's origin took the external number 8.8% → 7.8%. **Three attempts at a row that catches it all failed for one reason**: a bounding box is unmoved by a sub-sample shift, and every cloud symmetric enough to assert against has a degenerate depth span, so every point lands in the middle tier where `bw` is 1 and the two placements are arithmetically identical. The row was **removed** rather than shipped passing both ways, and the mutation is recorded in the run file as one that kills nothing, with the reason. **The class lives where an independent renderer of the same geometry is the only reader** — invisible to arithmetic, to a stripped frame, and to a golden frame recorded after the defect existed · **Closed** (F991) — **Recorded** — the row was removed with its reason

**F444** — `legendPlacement`'s count read `(segments?.length ?? 0) || series.length` and `identityOf`, in another file, read *segments, or series if it has none*. **The same sentence twice**, agreeing byte for byte on every form, because there were exactly two carriers and both clauses enumerate them. A **third** carrier is what separates them: `plot3d` holds its identities in `points3` and only under `colourBy: "series"`, so `identityOf` learned a third arm and the copy could not — leaving a legend whose *presence* and *contents* rules sit in two places with nothing to keep them in step. The failure would have been a key that never draws while the labels exist. **The signal was a correct fix changing nothing observable**; the question that reaches it is *which callers do not call the function*, and the answer was the one resolver whose whole job is the same question. **A reimplemented rule keeps its birthday clauses**, in its cheapest form. **Fixed** — one call, behaviour-identical on all forty-four existing forms, which is what made it invisible

**F442** — the 3D raster's first draft used the **colour** as the drawn flag: `ink[i] === undefined` meaning *nothing there*. `continuousColour` returns `undefined` below its own floor, so a sample the depth buffer had accepted would compose as blank — a point silently deleted, block the right height, every geometry assertion passing. **It is unreachable today and that is the finding rather than the reprieve**: `halfBlockEligible` requires `colourDepth >= 8` and `CONTINUOUS_FLOOR` is 8. Two thresholds in two components, written by two different arguments — how many colours a cell needs to carry a picture, and when a perceptual ramp stops being one — that happen to be the same number, with no reason to consult each other. Either moving alone turns a drawn point into a blank. **Fixed** — the depth buffer is the source of drawn-ness and the colour is what it is drawn in, `undefined` included; the glyph arm was already right because its table index and its colour are separate arrays

**F439** — PR8 exists to refuse a scratch buffer and its first draft asserted two **proxies**: `b.z !== a.z` and `b` cleared to `Infinity`. A scratch array handed out as `SCRATCH.subarray(0, n)` passes both — `subarray` returns a fresh view object per call, and the `fill` clears the view. **Two green assertions over one array.** The property a shared buffer cannot have is that an *earlier* render survives a later one, which is what the row asserts now. **Found by the mutation pass and by nothing else** — the row passed and the thing it exists to refuse walked through it. **And the rule that would have caught it cannot be widened**: SS24's `why` says *binding* and its pattern says `let|var`, so a `const` bound to a mutable is invisible — measured at one instance in the whole scope, `DIM_FACTORS`, which is a pure memo over frozen data. Widening would fire on the legitimate case and leave an exemption reading as permission. Blind spot recorded beside the rule with the figure · **Closed** (F991) — PR8's non-interference assertion, with SS24's blind spot recorded

**F437** — SP1 decided a citation's owner by **position**: `GROUP` took every paren group that did not *open* with `→` and read every `I\d+` inside as local, so an arrow in the *middle* sent a cross-reference to the local arm. **Wrong in both directions and the direction that shipped was the false pass** — 8 mixed groups in commitments, 8 foreign tokens, **8 of 8 silent because the citing spec declared the same number**, and a false failure the first time one did not. A second defect in the same arm read `/^(I\d+[a-z]?)/` off the start of a multi-token target, dropping 10 of 20 tokens across 10 groups. **The existing fixture was true and tested one of the two ways to write a cross-reference** — an arrow-opening group — which is F84's class inside a test. Re-measured **through the repaired rule** rather than through a hand parser: all 8 perturbed, all 8 reported against their own target, corpus clean. The repair's own regression — `(→ A02 §1)` becoming *cites nothing* on 8 commitments — has its own row. Blind spot stated and demonstrated: an arrow-less `(C11 I17, I9)` stays local, because the arrow is the mark and guessing would be wrong in the other direction. **Fixed**, 6/6 mutations with a control

| | the gate | what it was not checking |
|---|---|---|
| **F408** | **every row in `refresh.test.ts`, for a part that is not at the top level** | every live part any real surface nests — `/faults`, `/monitor`, S13's dashboard | `currentPanel` read the block back with `doc.blocks.find(…)`, **top level only**, while `liveDeclarations` recurses and says why in its own comment: *S13's dashboard is five panels inside a `group`, so a walk over top-level blocks alone finds none of them — which would be a dashboard that renders its placeholders and never ticks.* **One question a step apart, and the recursion was added on one side.** Writes were fine — a patch addresses by id and the transcript resolves through the tree — so a nested part declared, rendered, fetched and patched, and every sweep that must **read the block in place** found nothing: the elapsed counter (C23 I52, landed at F227 and never run in a real session since), the staleness re-title (I35), and the retry countdown. Every row here puts its panel at the top level and no real surface does. **Fixed** — the read walks on C04's `hasChildren`; `T3.55c` nests a part and failed before it. **Fixed** |
| **F407** | **the sweep, for anything that is not a *loading* box** | every failing live part in every app | `retryInMs` was written once, at the failure, so `retrying in 12s` stood still for the whole backoff and jumped — measured, seventeen frames with the spinner advancing and the number fixed. Every ingredient was present: the driver knows `dueAt`, the sweep runs, the field is a field, and the only arm asked for `loading`. **The bit that made a safe fix possible was being spent**: `partOf` resolved `renderError`'s default at declaration, which is the resolution `renderLoading` beside it deliberately avoids, so the driver could not tell its own block from a consumer's. `renderError` is `| null` now and the fallback moved to `refresh.ts` — **the shape C24 §5 already called tidier and did not take**. The attempt counter was **correct**: 1→3s, 2→6s, 3→12s, 4→24s, doubling exactly. And the first row written for it asserted the write and not the **arming**, which the harness satisfies either way. **Fixed** |
| **F404** | **the collision sweep's own note, for a member the explanation did not cover** | I75 grouped `violin/braille` with `violin/braille-filled` — a pair differing in **exactly** `plotFill` — and the note beside the group explained all four members as *the terminal's choices about how to spend a cell … a resolution fact rather than a dropped field*. Three of the four are. **A pixel curve fills or strokes at any resolution**, and `densityFigure` emitted `fill: true` unconditionally, so the member crossed nowhere. **An explanation that fits most of a set absorbs the member it does not**, and it survives review because a reader checks it against the examples in front of them. I80 had it filed as *blocked until `violin` draws* and the family had drawn since F383, so it sat on two lists and neither said owed. **Fixed** — fills on `plotFill === "solid"`, the terminal's own test; C04 I59 refuses `solid` on the line arm, so the arms now agree on the absent case too. 16 SVG frames moved, one attribute each; the terminal baseline did not. Distinct documents 149 → **152**. **Fixed** |
| **F403** | **`G1c`, for whether it still stands for its own subject** | §3ak.37 says the violin refusal expires *the day `violinRows` stops taking a width*. It does still take one — the terminal arm is per-cell — and the refusal went anyway, through a shared fixed-resolution curve. This arc's spec commit concluded the guard was therefore **stale and standing for nothing**, from its doc comment. **Reading the assertions**: the row was inverted *with* the fix, keeps the arity as a **control**, and adds the claim that matters — the same point count at two output widths. **A row's doc comment describes it as written**, and an inverted row keeps the paragraph that motivated its control, so *the comment says arity* and *the row asserts arity* are both true of a row whose subject has moved. Corrected one commit late. **Fixed** |
| **F401a** | **`roadmap-status`, for whether a cited line holds what the row says** | Entry 10 cited `ctx.ask` at `src/shell/execution.ts:616`; it is at **806** and had been for some time. The verifier asserts the line is **non-blank**, and 616 held something — so a citation wrong by 190 lines passed every run. **RS2c's own recorded limit arriving as an instance**, and within one file rather than across two. Exposed by adding one `import` far above, which is the only thing that could: the citation resolves against no symbol, so nothing but a shift onto whitespace moves it. Corrected. **Not closed as a class** — matching *"`ctx.ask` at line N"* against the identifier at line N is the citation-resolves-against-the-wrong-thing problem `COMMITMENT_INVARIANT_AUDIT.md` argues against automating. **Fixed** |
| **F400** | **the plots suite, for the document its 53 rungs land in** | `/all` and `/form <name>` drew **nothing at all** for a session while thirteen rows were green, one of them asserting **95 figures by equality**. F396 gave every form its rungs and every rung kept the form's own `` `f-${form}` `` id, so `/all` held twenty `f-line` and `/form violin` twelve `f-violin`; C04 I14 refuses the **whole document**, above every block that could have reported it. Every assertion was about a **piece** — each rung built alone, compared to its default, counted — and the composition lived in `main.ts`, which calls `tui.start()` at module scope, so **the one artefact that could have failed was the one no test could construct**. Fourth instance of the duplicate-id class here (F372, F373, F399). Fixed by a seam: the *table* still cannot name an id, the *composer* must. `main.ts` is wiring only; every document is built in `src/commands.ts` and `T-doc2` runs all 97 through `expectDocument().isValid()`, with `T-doc1` gating the coverage table against the manifest by equality. **Two fabrications corrected the gate itself**: the fixture invented a `meta` the shell publishes (`completeLocal`), and the live-part row validated a rendered child *alone*, where a child collides with nothing — widened to the host's blocks plus the child, all three historical instances fail it. **Found by a reader typing two commands.** **Fixed** |
| **F381** | **every gate, for the chunked transmission's alignment** | `chunked()` reserves 4042 payload bytes an escape — **not a multiple of four** — and kitty decodes base64 **per chunk**, so every continuation is misaligned. One chunk drew, two drew **nothing**, measured by bisecting the raster in a real kitty. The escape is well-formed under every reading available here: nine chunks, options on the first, `m=1` through `m=0`, right box. **The header reasoned its way to the right doubt and stopped a step short** — it names the 4096 cap as a protocol claim *not measurable here*, and alignment is a second claim in the same class that nothing named. **Three defects in one arm, each hiding the next**: F379's bound meant it never ran, so F380's `c=1` was invisible; fixing the box let it run, so this became visible — and only at a raster needing two escapes. **Fixed** |
| **F379** | **every gate, for an arm bounded at forty cells** | `MAX_PLACEHOLDER_SPAN` was the diacritic table's length and the table shipped as a **40-entry prefix of kitty's 297**, so `placementRows` refused every image wider than forty cells and the renderer fell back to the dither — deliberately, correctly, and on everything. **The arm had never drawn a pixel** after a walk, an invariant, a validator arm, a renderer, three probes and IK2's assertions, all of which pass: IK2 checks the escape's *form*, and a refusal that falls back is well-formed. **The justifying sentence is true about the wrong axis** — *forty entries cover forty rows and forty columns, which is past any height a transcript block declares* — right about height, silent about width, and a block's width is the terminal's. MG24's shape (F84). Found by running it in a real kitty, which is what C09 §4c names and nothing had done. **Fixed** — all 297, from kitty's own table, not guessed: combining class 230 gives 510 in a different order |
| **F380** | **`image-kitty.test.ts`, for the parameter that sizes the placement** | With the table fixed the arm is taken and **nothing draws**. One APC emitted, `c=1,r=14`, against **784 placeholders spanning 56 columns** — the transmission declared a one-cell-wide image, from a literal `1` at three call sites. **The comment above them is wrong twice and reads as care**: *the renderer derives the same numbers from the same block* (it derives them from the block **and the width**, so there was one computation and one constant, not two) and *`c` and `r` are advisory to kitty* (a claim with no record — `c` sizes the virtual placement). Every existing assertion passes on the old code, because `c=1` is a well-formed escape. **Fixed** — the frame's width threaded through, the box from `imageCells` |
| **F373** | **`validateDocument`, on the document a patch produces** | `validate.ts` says *I2, I3, I14 and I27 are established here and nowhere else*, and the transcript's `patch` commits `applyPatch`'s result without revalidating. Measured: patch #1 **succeeds** and yields a document `validateDocument` refuses — `id "walk" appears 2 times (C04 I14)` — and patch #2 is then rejected with the same invariant's message. **The defect is detected one tick late**, at the point where nothing can attribute it: the patch that created the invalid state passed, and the one that merely met it failed. Reachable from the published builder with nothing warning, because a live panel and the block it renders taking one name is the obvious thing to write · **Closed** (F991) — the three resolving sites refuse a duplicate id |
| **F369** | **the reader map every ruling in this arc opened with** | *How many times does each member appear in `svg.ts`* — eleven came back `svg=0` and it was read as *the arm does not read it*. **It measures which members the file names.** A member reaching the arm through a shared `Plot → Plot` transform is never written there, and `calendarUnit` and `startDate` are exactly that: `svg.ts` calls `drawnBlock`, which routes `calendar` to `calendarRows`, **moved out of a terminal renderer by F322**. Measured by rendering instead of grepping: five blocks differing only in these two draw **five distinct documents**. Both crossed a year ago and the arc counted them owed for six commits. The remainder is **two**, not four. **F355's member sweep shares the blind spot** — it reads the type against the files — and a grep over names cannot see a value arriving through a transform, which is what this whole pass has been building · **Closed** (F991) — commit `b9dc68fd` — C12 I82 |
| **F370** | **the two fixtures, and the order they were run in** | `xTitle` and `axisCross` are the genuinely owed two, and neither is isolated by the corpus: `line/x-title` sets `xLabels` beside the title, `line/axis-cross` sets `xMin`/`xMax` beside the crossing. Two variants added, each named for what its block sets — `x-captions` (three captions, no title) and `straddle-zero` (both axes straddle zero, rules at the edge) — **and the collision sweep run on them before either member was touched**, which is what caught `compact-box-solid` earlier in this arc. Two collisions reported by the instrument rather than by inspection; four distinct terminal frames · **Closed** (F991) — commit `b9dc68fd` — G12a and G12b |
| **F368** | **`legendOf`, for every block whose callout names its series** | `yCallout: "name"` and `"both"` write the identity at the line's end, and `legendPlacement` has removed the legend for them since §3ag — *a name at the line's end **is** the legend*. **`legendOf`, the crossed resolver, never carried that clause**, so this arm drew `alpha` and `beta` in a legend the terminal removes, identically for all three callout variants: the collision group F349 already reported and nobody had read. **The second half is a wrong picture, not an absence** — it would have drawn the legend *beside* the callouts once they landed, the identity three times. Closed by moving the clause and crossing the member: **the strings** are `calloutOf`'s, **the rung** stays in the terminal (below the colour floor the family stacks into labelled strips, so a name there is a third copy), and **the anchor** is each arm's own — a cell row and a pixel y, the same question in two units. 1840 terminal frames, 0 moved |
| **F367** | **my own AD13 comment, and two of the four remaining rulings** | *Three cheap ones, because the sweep already reports them* — and one of the three was wrong in my own note inside AD13. **`calendar/day` and `day-stretch` differ in `matrixAnchor`, not `calendarUnit`**: both set `calendarUnit: "day"`, so the member the sweep isolates is the anchor, and `calendarUnit`'s four variants draw different grids and never collide. **`matrixAnchor` is legitimate** — all four anchors give one document, every cell 7.487px, and both of its jobs are cell quanta: `stretch` against `uniform` distributes 53 columns over 74 cells (*a doubling at a pitch of one*, says its own doc) where 640/53 divides exactly, and `window` against `left` decides what to drop, which **F318 already ruled** — this arm scales and has nothing to drop. **I80's third instance**, composing with the other two rather than repeating them. **`yCallout` is owed**: the terminal writes `█ beta` on the row a series ends at, this arm draws a legend and its texts are identical across all three callout variants — direct labelling is not a cell idiom, and it simply does not read the member |
| **F366** | **`plotCorners`, and the two probes that read it backwards** | I80 asks *does the choice exist because of cells?* Corner cells against **width** are stable — 37 at 40, 60 and 80 — which reads as *tracks the data*. The second arm draws a `<path>` of fifty `L` segments, and `stroke-linejoin` is a corner vocabulary, which reads as *expressible, so owed*. **Against height it settles**: 15, 37, 61, 79 at heights 4, 8, 16, 24, for a curve with **three** turning points. They are **staircase steps** — a sloped line quantised onto a character grid — not the curve's corners. **The tree has no ambiguity**: the terminal draws `╭──────────┴──┬────────╮` because a grid cannot draw a diagonal, and this arm draws `M352 47.2 L220.8 116`, one straight segment with no corner to round. **Legitimate, I80's second instance.** Reusable half is about the probe: both wrong readings came from varying the axis the artefact does not depend on — width is the obvious parameter and the staircase is a function of height · **Closed** (F991) — G10b, commit `37981c4a` |
| **F365** | **F355's own pairing of `plotFill` with `plotBox`** | Both were owed under one note — *every variant is a `violin`, which this arm refuses* — and the two are not alike. **`plotBox` is the `boxplot` form's**: `definition.ts` reads it at 2398 and 2405 inside `boxplot:`, so a fixture was buildable all along and the corpus placement was an accident. Built as a pair, the terminal draws `███` against `━━━` at `plotDetail: "compact"` and **nothing at the default detail** — which is its whole meaning: a one-row box has no top or bottom edge and spends its single row on mass or on a stroke. **Legitimate, measured**: this arm's IQR rect is `fill-opacity="0.35"` *and* `stroke-width="1.5"`, so it carries both and has nothing to choose. **A second kind of legitimate** — F355's four *are* quantities in cells; this is a choice **forced by** cells. **`plotFill` is violin-only** (four sites, all in the `violin:` entry) and a pixel density can be filled or stroked, so it has a meaning here and nowhere to be read: **blocked on `violin` drawing**, a dependency rather than a decision |
| **F364** | **C12 I54, for every string the caller supplies** | *Every frame at `unicode: "ascii"` is ASCII* — and AA1 sweeps the whole corpus and finds nothing, because **no fixture ever put a non-ASCII character in caller text**. Measured with one ellipsis each: `emptyMessage`, `xTitle`, `xLabels`, a series label and a category name — **five for five leak**. The renderer degrades its own glyph tables and passes the caller's words through. **A gate phrased over a corpus inherits its blind spots**: AA1 is right about the four mechanisms it was written against and blind to the class where no table is consulted. **Found by a fixture not looking for it** — `line/empty-message` was added so `emptyMessage` would have a corpus instance and its text ended in `…`. Reproduction is AA1b; the fix is a ruling with reach (it changes a consumer's own words at a rung they did not choose) and is owed · **Partly** (F991) — AA1b is the reproduction; the ruling on degrading caller-supplied text is owed | · **Closed** (F1052) — refused at C12, with the reason **replaced** rather than added to. Three things this entry says are wrong: the class is **12 of 12 responding kinds** and not the plot's five, so it is C09's question and not C12's; the invariant it quotes says an *alphabet* is chosen by capability, not that every frame is ASCII; and **the operation a fix would call does not exist** — zero folds in `src/` or `tools/`, and the one `…` substitution swaps the marker the framework appends when it truncates. Reopen when a fold exists as an operation and C09 rules for the class
| **F363** | **the second arm, for the difference between no data and no support** | `plotToSvg` ends `if (body.length === 0) return null`, so `line/empty` and `violin/default` — a form this arm does not draw at all — are **byte-identical**, in the same 33-strong collision group. The terminal holds the declared height and centres `No data.` in the muted tone. **The comment above that line is right about the defect and wrong about the remedy**: it was written against *five gridlines labelled 0 to 1 over an empty box*, and drawing nothing at all fixes the false axis by collapsing two states a reader needs apart — F259 says a refusal is for a figure that **cannot** be drawn, and one with nothing in it can. `emptyRows`' own comment names the consumer: a `--watch` that has not reported an epoch. **`emptyMessage` rides on it** — no corpus instance at all, and no possible reader while the whole state is a `null` · **Closed** (F991) — commit `ff654d07` — C12 I79, G9a |
| **F362** | **`quartileRange`, for every declared bound on a distribution form** | C12 I35's second half is *the caller's pin is what that axis is*, and a boxplot pinned to `-50 … 150` gives `0 … 100` — identical to the unpinned one. **`seriesRange` ends in `pinnedRange(min, max, pin)` and `quartileRange` takes no pin**, so the curve family honours a declared bound and `boxplot`, `violin`, `forest` and `ridgeline` do not; `dumbbell` escapes only because it routes through `seriesRange`. **F274's shape on the other axis** — a capability added where it was needed, and the second extent function never asked the same question. **Found because SP9 demanded a row**: I35 was one of the three F357's matcher called covered, and writing a row that checks it took two assertions, of which the second failed. **Not fixed** — no corpus variant pins a distribution form, so the one-line fix would move no frame, no document and no golden byte. The fixture comes first |
| **F361** | **F357's own figure, and 11% of every invariant in the repository** | F357 argued the convention was held by hand from *76 of 76 cited* — a `\bI75\b` match against the test corpus, which resolves against twenty-six specs. **Three C12 invariants had no citation at all** when it was written (I32, I35, I69) and it reported full coverage: the fourth manufactured number in this arc, and the one a finding about unmeasured claims used to make its case. Attribution took four attempts — `ownerOf` reaches **6 of 2342** test files for C12, so elsewhere a citation must be qualified, which is SP3's job. Sound reading, SP3's own run-on walk: **86 of 768 invariants are named by no test row**, worst at C24 (14 of 28) and C26 (8 of 20). **Two matchers, one total, different members** — the total is not the evidence, the list is. Closed by **SP9** with an equality-compared exemption list. **What it cannot check is F357's finding**: that a citation *covers* anything |
| **F360** | **the x-row reader, for every tick row crowded to a single space** | The split is on **two or more spaces** — what tells `first  mid  last` from `1      250     500` — and the renderer does not guarantee that gap. `line/x-log` at 40 columns draws `1                   100 200 500`, so three readings arrive as one caption, land in `identityLabels`, and manufacture a disagreement against an arm that has none. **Nineteen groups across the corpus were already in that state** before the pair that found it existed — `aspect-square`, `legend-left`, `annotation-label`, `axis-cross-corners`, `candlestick-overlay`, `stackedarea`, two `streamgraph`, and four facets each of `smallmultiples` and `pairplot`. Corrected: those four forms close outright, `line` goes 13/78 to 8/78, AD4's open count 49 → 45. **Same class as F358 one predicate along** — there the alphabet was not the renderer's, here the alphabet is right and the tokenisation is a guess about layout. **I77's stated blind spot names it exactly**, and was walked into within the hour of being written · **Closed** (F991) — commit `3bb2eafd` |
| **F359** | **SP8's resolver, for a bare section reference after a foreign one** | Writing I77 moved the counter to **135 of 6037 across 84 targets** from 134 across 83, and the new target was `A03 §3ak.43` — a section of A03 nobody wrote. A bare `§x` binds to the **nearest preceding document name**, and the entry says *A03 §2's note about SP1* two sentences earlier, so a citation of a neighbour re-homes every later bare reference in the same entry. **It fails in the direction that reads as correct**: a bare `§3ak.43` inside C12 is the ordinary way to name your own section and resolves everywhere else. **Found by reading a counter that never fails a build** — SP8 is reported and not gated, so +1 dangling is exactly what a correct new citation looks like to anyone not watching. Qualified as `C12 §3ak.43`, the counter returned to 134/83 · **Closed by F1049** — the same sentence, the same file and the same ratchet as F857, so it is one finding and not two. SP8's dangling arm went **134/83 → 321/170** while the row read as addressed |

The cause is a character class: `RULE_ONLY` never had the dotted vocabulary the renderer uses, so a real rule fails the predicate and a figure glyph clears F334's `> 2`. **F334 is correct about what it fixed** — a blank row is two glyphs — and could not have found this, because a third glyph had never arrived. **Third instance of one class**, after F321's edge glyph and F334's blank row, both closed on the instance. **And AD10, the control that certified the reader, is written in `├───┤` and `│  │  │` — neither of which the renderer draws**, so a fabricated violation inherited the rule's vocabulary the way SP1's inherited its misreading. | **F159** | **MG24's member walk** | it reads members off their own lines, so a type declared on a *single* line presents none and is watched by nothing. Neither the keyword nor the name is the discriminator — the **line shape** is, and **40 published object types under `src/` are single-line**. F84's sibling: a rule widened along the axis one finding named can stay narrow along one it did not, and the earlier fix is what makes the clean result read as coverage. **Instance fixed** — both C26 types reformatted, with the reason recorded at each declaration; **class open** · **Closed** (F991) — commit `db3d09fc` — a member is a segment and not a line; **the class is closed** |
| **F160** | **MG24's member matching** | it compares members **by name**, so `ElementReport.kindsCovered` reported a *different* type's exemption stale. The harmless direction; the same looseness means **a genuinely unconsumed member is satisfied the moment any unrelated type anywhere declares a field with that name** — and `id`, `kind`, `width`, `rows` are exactly the names a new type carries. Nothing in a clean run tells the two apart. **Instance renamed**, blind spot recorded at the declaration; **class open** — the fix is keying by `(owner, name)` · **Closed** (F991) — **Refused with figures** — commit `3b21f11c`: `(owner, name)` needs a receiver type and would not key uniquely, and the exactness line is the standing residue |
| **F252** | the framework — **an escape with a length limit, emitted whole.** kitty caps a direct transmission at 4096 bytes and continues with `m=1` until a final `m=0`; phase 1's `transmit` emitted one escape carrying the entire payload. **IK2's structural assertions all passed**, because the corpus fixture is an 8x8 PNG — seventy bytes of base64, legal at that size and illegal at any useful one — and **no in-repo test has a terminal**, so this is the plane-16 class. Found by building the composited arm, whose raw RGBA is *larger* than the PNG it replaces (7595 bytes against 1741): thinking about payload size raised the question and the question found the shipped defect. Taken on the asymmetry rather than on certainty — chunked is correct under both readings and unchunked draws **nothing**, blamed on the image. **And the reserve was off by one in the fix**: `- 8` against a 9-byte frame, so every first chunk came out at 4097. The row that caught it asserted **the bound** rather than the chunking — an assertion about the mechanism agrees with an off-by-one | **fixed** |
| **F256** | the framework — **the gate was written, found, run, and passed against the violation it names.** C12 §3aj is the best-placed gate here: written two phases early, put beside the geometry it governs, four hazards, five rows, its own preconditions. Both preconditions held — catalogue reproducible at 1780 files over two runs, `niceAxis` unit-free so the refactor was small as predicted — and the refactor passed at **0 of 377 golden rows and 0 of 1780 catalogue frames**. **Then the same gate passed hazard 1's own violation**: moving the flat-line answer into the normalised layer is wrong at *every even row count* (`floor(last/2)` against `round(0.5·last)`) and moved nothing. Counting the branch says why — **0 hits in either corpus**, because neither contains a constant series. **A03 §2's vacuity class arriving in a gate**, and in the good one: a gate phrased over a corpus inherits its blind spots without inheriting a way to see them. What separated the readings was a **fabricated violation** — the one instrument A03 asks for everywhere else and this gate did not have. Remedy is a companion rather than a rewording: rows that construct what the corpus does not, plus a pass that says they can fail. **And one row was removed rather than declared an expected survivor** — byte-identical and uncatchable, and a permanent survivor turns a pass's signal off for good | **fixed** (§3aj.1) |
| **F257** | the framework's own gates — **three commit messages cited a gitignored directory as evidence.** `docs/catalogue/` is in `.gitignore`, so `git status --porcelain docs/catalogue | wc -l` returns **0 whether a frame moved or not** — and it appears in three commits from one session as half of §3aj's evidence. **The same bit for *clean* and for *cannot see***, which is the class F256 was filed about, committed three times by the author of F256. **The conclusion survives on the other half**: `test/golden/` is tracked, 20 snapshots, 377 rows, and the flat-line branch count went through the renderer rather than through git. The catalogue sentence was decoration reading as corroboration. **It survived three writings because `git status <path>` looks like it checks that path**, and a blind check and a passing check are both an empty string. Remedy is a digest rather than a discipline — `tools/catalogue-hash.mjs`, 956 frames, reproducible. **Plus an ordering hazard found the same hour**: `plot-catalogue.mjs` clears the directory before writing, so running it second deletes the 66 phase frames — watched, 66 became 1 | **fixed** |

**F82 was found producing this file** and it is the group's sharpest instance, because the
gate that failed is the one verifying the sentence three sections down. `citations` was
incremented inside the violation branch, so it counted *failures* — a second name for
`violations.length`, reporting **0** against a tree holding hundreds. Zero is also what it
reports if the regex matches nothing, if the scope excludes every citing file, or if the walk
comes back empty: **the exact two failures SP5 had already shipped, and the field existed to
tell them apart.**

**Then the working counter found the next one within a minute.** `415` looked low against the
tree, and it was: **thirteen top-level documents under `examples/docker/` were outside the
scope entirely — 250 citations, of which this file holds 175.** The most-cited artefact after
the ledger, and the one whose whole job is to cite. That is the rule's *first* vacuity
repeating — a scope naming the directories thought to matter instead of covering the directory
and naming exceptions — so it is three instances of one class in one rule. All 250 resolve, so
nothing was hidden; what was missing was the guarantee, not a defect.

**Its test agreed, and the way it agreed is the part to keep.**

```ts
expect(v.citations, "it looked at the citation and accepted it").toBe(0);
```

The message states the intent; the number asserts the opposite. Both were written in one
sitting from one understanding and neither was checked against the other — **F65's shape, an
artefact wrong about itself, arriving in a test rather than in a drawing.** The corpus row
meanwhile asserted `scanned > 50`, which counts *files opened* and stays high through both
failure modes; only `citations` shows that the regex and the scope work **together**.

Fixed, and mutated: reverting the increment kills both new rows and neither `fires` row —
which is why four green rows and a dead field coexisted for as long as they did.

### The four open ones are about a rule's *reach*, and they fail in different directions

**F83 is a definition too weak inside its scope.** A02 Seam 4 is about a component complete on
its own side with nothing on the other — and the implementation is the *same* side. The file
boundary was taken as a proxy for the seam and within a component it is not one. 28 members
never cross their own component; `HistoryStore.rerun` has no caller in `src/` — a test
calls it, and that qualifier is the difference between a claim and a slogan — and
`TransportRouter.busy` **survived its own removal** — `router.ts:64` says a guard *"replaced
`busy`"* and `construct.ts:1024` counts *"seventeen until `busy`"*, so the tree records the
deletion twice and the member is still there.

**F84 is a scope too narrow with a definition that works.** Its present contents are almost
empty — 0 members dead outright, and the 67 that never cross a component are dominated by
deferrals already documented, `ToolDef.oneShot` having three paragraphs in C22 §4. Three
survive: `GlyphSet.teeLeft`, `teeRight` and `hollow`, declared glyph slots with no drawer.

**Filed anyway, and this is the group's argument in one line: a rule whose scope excludes
three-quarters of its subject is a rule whose clean result means much less than it reads,
whatever it happens to contain today.**

**F159 is the third, and it is the one that could only be found after F84 was fixed.** MG24
now walks type aliases — which is what closes F84's axis and what makes its result read as
covered. It still reads members off their own lines, so a declaration written on one line
presents none: forty published object types under `src/` are outside the rule by their
formatting alone, and nothing chose that. **A rule widened along the axis a finding named
can stay narrow along one it did not, and the earlier fix is precisely what hides the
remainder.** That is F82's sentence about a counter, applied to a
scope — and it is why this group's disposition is never "fix the instance".

**F60 is three findings stacked and only the third is new.** F36 and F37 are why the deep
imports exist; this is that the reach was aimed at a *repository*, so a workaround for a
missing export silently excluded itself from the check that exists to test the package.
**A workaround that cannot survive the boundary it works around is a second defect wearing
the first one's clothes.**

`tools/capture.py` now spawns the bin rather than the module, so every frame this repository
reads goes through the entry point a user has.

**F489** — adding a third rung turned up **two latent defects and one new one, all the same shape**: a variable meaning two things while the two happened to be equal. `grid.width` was the cell width *and* the sample width — `frameOf` places a label's row from `rows` (cells) and its column from `grid.width` (samples), and both existing arms made them equal, so the asymmetry sat in one function unremarked until a rung that is `width × 2` scattered every label to the margins. And `half ? undefined : densityGlyph(…)` fired on the braille rung, where the surface's second channel makes no sense, so every surface sample wrote a glyph the compose step then withheld from the dot grid. **The second is the instructive one because the picture was plausible** — a stipple, which is what a reader half-expecting braille to stipple would accept. What said otherwise was a histogram of the eight dot positions: **3 against 76** before, 59 to 61 after, with the wireframe's even row as the control. **A boolean naming one of two arms becomes a lie the moment there is a third, and does not become a compile error**; the remedy is a named union plus a second predicate for *paints a sub-cell sample*, and the audit was `grep "half\b"` over one file · **Closed** (F991) — a named union and a second predicate, with six rows

**F490** — BR4's first draft measured a marker's **bounding box in cells** and bounded it between the half rung's box and one cell more, with a correct reason for the tolerance: the two rungs round a block's origin into different lattices. **The mutation pass killed it** — replacing the doubled tier table with the half rung's, quartering every marker's area, moved the bounding box by nothing the tolerance could see. **Because a mark has a floor of one cell**, and what quartering destroys is *sub-cell coverage*, which a box measured in cells is precisely the quantity that cannot see. The assertion that bites is a full cell: a near marker at the doubled table is `4 × 4` dots — one cell, all eight positions — and at the half table `2 × 2`, which cannot fill a cell at any alignment. **8 against 0**, with its own control that non-full cells exist. **The class: when the property is an area, do not assert an extent** — they agree on every input above the floor, which is every input a fixture picks, and the tolerance that survives lattice noise is the same one that makes the row blind · **Closed** (F991) — BR4 asserts a full cell beside the box

**F1027** — **a rule asked *is this built* with a form TypeScript also uses for *this is declared*, and its two errors cancelled.** MG24's record arm tested for a `name:` site, which is **neither necessary nor sufficient**: 6 691 of 18 551 `name:` occurrences in `src/` sit outside any object literal, and 538 shorthand `{ …, name }` constructions were invisible to it. The cancellation is why nobody saw it — `Layout.callouts` read as *consumed* on a **parameter annotation** while its real construction one line below went unseen, so the rule's answer was right and both of its reasons were wrong. Both of F218's stated blockers resolve at HEAD: F105 and F160 are Closed–Refused and **not one of their four refused tightenings touches this defect**, all four moving the consumer scope instead; F84's widening had already landed; and F218's own remedy is unbuildable, `typescript@7.0.2` exporting only `version` and `versionMajorMinor`. **The loud instance no longer reproduces** — re-fabricating `let blocked` gives 0 violations, the equality arm's scoping having landed since — **and the quiet half had never been fixed**, which is the shape worth keeping: an entry whose demonstration expires reads as an entry that was addressed · **Closed** — seven members were being answered for by an annotation, five of them unexempt, and three exemptions were held open by the shorthand blindness; the exemption list moves 104 → 106. The rule now **reports its own reach**: a file whose bracket scan does not balance keeps the loose test and appears on the violation list, 0 of 371 today

**F1028** — **a rule scoped to one file of six, and every figure the entry rests on had moved.** MG27 reads one builder file; `src/shell/builders/` has six, two of which build blocks. `BUILDER_OMISSIONS` is **7 entries and none is `plot.*`** — F263 closed the ten it was written for and the scope stayed behind. **The scope fix moves zero violations today**, which is the honest result and worth stating rather than implying a yield. `FigureOpts`' six-of-twelve is real, but `build()` spreads **six** and not three. **The remedy was corrected rather than executed**: `b.plot` refuses on `plotStyle` and `plotDetail` and on none of the other four, and `validateDocument`'s only caller in `src/` is the persistence path on untrusted input — a locally built block is never validated — so forwarding all twelve would have routed round the only gate rather than reaching it. `b.figure` takes ten of twelve · **Closed** — **a third instance fell out of running it**: MG28's fabrication carried the same one-file scope and had stopped fabricating, its colon arm was unanchored where its shorthand arm was not — F1027's disease one rule along — and an architecture claim that the heatmap is reachable only through `block()` is false, `setForm` predating the `heatmap` arm by two days

**F1051** — **the bubble's size channel has a fourth symptom, and it is a control that does the wrong thing.** F271's three reproduce at HEAD — the ordinate's floor is a size, the channel is rasterised, the legend names it. The fourth is in no entry: the reader-facing toggle reaches the channel, so hiding the entry the legend calls `size` removes a rasterisation nobody asked to see **while the sizes keep sizing the bubbles and keep owning the axis**, because the renderer reads the series directly and never asks whether it is hidden. Not a figure that looks wrong — a control acting on a symptom of the defect rather than on data. **The harness nearly hid it**: two runs returned byte-identical frames for two different hide requests, a collision from passing an array where a record was wanted, which no per-property comparison could see · **Closed** — the C04 ruling is taken and its row watches the *condition*, pinning the count of offending forms at one so a second form fails rather than passing silently. The implementation landed under F271: eleven golden frames and the snapshot moved and nothing else did, and the count this row's ruling pins is now zero

**F1052** — **F364's leak is twelve block kinds wide and the operation its fix would call does not exist.** One ellipsis driven through thirteen text-bearing kinds: twelve respond and **all twelve leak**, so the five F364 names are five instances of a framework-wide class — its sentence is true about the renderer and names the wrong subject, which made a C09 question a C12 one. `asciify|foldTo|deunicode|unidecode` returns **zero** hits across `src/` and `tools/`, and the one ellipsis substitution in the tree swaps the marker the framework appends when it truncates, not a character a caller wrote. **And the invariant never said what F364 quotes** — it rules that an *alphabet* is chosen by capability, where the citation reads *every frame at ascii is ASCII* · **Closed** — refused with the cost, the reason replaced rather than added to: a general fold is a transliteration table and it is lossy exactly where it matters. The control looking for a kind that already degrades caller text found none and caught **four vacuous passes** on the way

**F1053** — **the two sentences that kept `busy` exempt are homonyms of another component's member.** *The tree documents the deletion twice* rested on one file that never said it and one file about C16's `RouterDeps.busy` — **a different member on a different interface in a file of the same basename**. Grepping the transport router for its half returns **0 hits**. A count of consumers is an argument only if the consumers share a shape, and neither citation shares a component with its subject. **The ruling had already been taken the other way**: C16 §5 and C23 I5 make C23's guard authoritative and C06's a backstop, and what C16 removed was its own member · **Closed** — C06 I26 declares it, because the guard is a closure binding and six rows have nothing else to compare I13 against. Written in the spec rather than left to a gate, because C28's decorator has to *be* a router and so re-exposes every member, which is F882's own general form

**F1056** — **measured already closed; the number is spent.** F557's residue said the `typescript-eslint` decision was owed and unowned, and **both halves are false at HEAD** and have been since 2026-09-03: the decision is recorded with all five justification parts and `npm view` figures, and two refusal rows watch it — one keyed on the package's absence, one on the TypeScript major it would be reopened for. F557 and the entry that decided it are the **same day, seven commits apart**; F557's sentence was true when written and false by that evening, and the sweep carried it forward without re-deriving it · **Closed** — nothing was written and nothing should have been. Recorded so the number cannot be issued again, on F1040's precedent

**F1063** — **a bound whose subject is a population's size cannot be made safe by choosing a better size.** A roadmap row guards a ratio with a population floor. Its own comment records the first two failures — the floor was 10, *a bound pinned near today's count fails on success*, so it was moved to 4 — and the population reached **exactly 4** on the session that closed the entries. **Each repair chose a better number and the number was never the problem**: the work moves that population one way, so any floor is a date. **And the floor was guarding a claim the row had stopped making** — the ratio inverted into a subset relation on 2026-09-04, and a subset relation is meaningful at any population including zero, so the bound's whole purpose went with that edit and nobody removed it · **Closed** — replaced by a relation the run states twice and the row never checked: the signal's denominator equals the header's own confirmed-OPEN count, so it covers the whole population rather than sampling it. **A second instance the same hour makes it a rule rather than an anecdote**: the register's own open-set row asserted *the reader sees more open findings than a naive grep*, and that inverted too — closing a `**Partly**` row appends `**Closed**` and leaves the marker, so the grep stops under-counting and starts over-counting. **14 against 11, standing for 6 missed and 9 over-counted** — fifteen disagreements nearly cancelling into three, which is why the residues are now asserted by set and the totals are not compared at all

**F1066** — **the ordering half of the commitments list was refused on a figure that was wrong when it was written.** Two documents agreed, citing *eleven of twenty-eight specs* — **a count, a ruling and two documents are what a ruling looks like from outside**. Re-measured at the commit that wrote the sentence rather than at HEAD, because only that reading separates a drifted corpus from a claim that was never true: **9 by descent, 10 by the other reading, at all three commits**. And the reason beside it is a correct sentence justifying a different decision — *a renumber of two of the largest documents* refuses the **repair** and says nothing about the **check**, which the rule forty lines up already separates with a debt list for a defect it cannot repair either. **The hole was live**: descent keys went 32 → 34, so three commitments landed out of order with the gate green on every one · **Closed** — SP13, with the unit the **descent pair** rather than a first divergence, because 43 items are out of place across 34 descents and one sits 25 positions from home. Two specs renumbered, seven listed with a reason, and the load-bearing one is that three are the duplicate-run rule's subject

**F1067** — **a part that never agreed with its own total, restated in two documents.** The lettered-commitment blind spot is correctly totalled at **22** across four specs and says one of them holds **sixteen**; the true breakdown is 2 + 1 + **18** + 1, and the arithmetic that disproves it is on the same line in both documents · **Closed** — corrected and **asserted rather than written**, so the figure fails if it drifts. A note for the next reader: that spec's lettered run is itself out of order by four descents no rule can see, on top of the six SP13 reports for the same file

**F1068** — **a fabrication disarmed from outside for the second time in one day, and its stated limit is the mechanism.** The fixture cites a bare invariant number in a file the citation rule attributes to C02, which works for exactly as long as C02 lacks that number. The first expiry is on record — a lane gave C02 an I13 — and the repair **translated the number to I14 and wrote the new expiry into the comment**. Another lane's C02 rulings added I14 hours later. **Two instances of one class in one day, both caught only by the row failing**: a stated limit names a condition and nothing watches it, which is the deferral rule arriving inside a test fixture · **Closed** — the number is derived as the lowest C02 does not declare, asserted absent before use. The shape the architecture document asks for is preserved; **the digit never was the defect**, and the row failed identically for I13 and for I14

**F1075** — **the contract has one consumer, it lives where the producer's typecheck cannot read it, and the gate that would have read it shares a name with one that does not.** `derive.compute` is declared `(data, prev, attempts)` and `examples/plots/test/plots.test.ts:466` called it with two — `error TS2554`, for **23 commits**. The whole tree has **two** callers: `refresh.ts:833`, the framework's own, and that one, which is the only consumer of the contract anywhere and sits in a workspace with its own `tsconfig`, so the root `npx tsc --noEmit` returns **0** at the same commit. The declaration's note is F84's class exactly — *additive, so every existing fold compiles unchanged* is **true** about a fold, which may take fewer parameters than its type, and says nothing about a **caller**, which must supply all three. **The half worth keeping is the gate**: `make check` is `npm run check` **plus** `npm run build` **plus** `npm run check` in every directory under `examples/` with a `package.json`, and this session's verification chain ran the npm script and reported `check=0` under the gate's name. Measured at both ends — `make check` exits 2 at HEAD and at `a5c747d0`, in a fresh detached worktree, so the worktree was never masking it. `make test` has the same shape. And CI runs `make check` with a comment saying why, but `gh run list` shows no run on this branch at all · **Closed** — the call site takes `0`, the first tick's value. *A gate that exists and is not run*, arriving through a **name collision** rather than a missing row: wired three times over — Makefile target, CI step, and the comment explaining the population — and defeated by a shorter command with the same name that reports the same green. The test that catches it is that the target's recipe is more than `npm run <its own name>`, true of `check` and `test` and of nothing else in `all`

**F1076** — **a class named by enumeration is the size of the enumeration: five named, twenty-four declared, eight unchecked.** C04's `colormap` clause names five members as *unions for the same reason* and F213 measured those five; `Plot` declares **24**, of which **eight** had no membership rule at the document gate and five of those appear in no document at all. **Five clauses would have closed a third of it**, and the twenty-fifth member would have been unchecked on the day it landed. **No textual rule could have found it, measured before wiring one**: *every literal appears in `validate.ts`* misses `plotDetail`, whose three values are common words, and fires falsely on `yFormat`, `plotStyle` and `graphLayout`, which check through a `Set` or against one literal — wrong in both directions on one corpus, which is why the gate reads a structure. `PLOT_UNIONS` is the class as data and **is** the mechanism rather than a list beside one: sixteen bespoke membership checks collapsed to a one-line `isKnownPlotValue` guard each, so a member in the table is checked by construction and a form rule stays silent about a value the document does not contain · **Closed** — MG31 both directions, five rows, three mutations, and the one that turns everything false fails **only the control**, which is the argument for the control existing. Two of F213's five were already closed and its row never said so (F1073's class, third instance). Three mutation runs re-anchored, two of them caught by the anchors sweep rather than by me. Recorded and not closed: `construct.ts` keeps a second `Y_FORMATS` copy for the builder's half

**F1077** — **the deferral signal was repaired in one file and left blind to the class.** F907 ruled that *a describe title is not a row either* — it had found `describe("C28 I45 — per-kind input gauges")` over a block of pure `it.todo`s, where the todos stripped correctly and the title carried the citation through — and the remedy was to edit that one file. `withoutTodos`'s comment states all three clauses (a todo title, a describe title, a comment) and the code implemented two. **Surfaced by using the instrument rather than auditing it**: a spec-alone commit put four `it.todo`s under `describe("C04 I118 …")`, nothing ran behind I118, and the figure did not move — 33 before, 33 after. **The corpus splits three ways and the wording picks none of them**: of 1129 `describe` blocks, **5** are hollow and 1124 hold a row that runs, while **37** invariants are named only by a title over a block that does run — so stripping every title would take the figure to **70** on a corpus with no holes in it, which is wrong in the other direction. The fix reads the **block**, not the title · **Closed** — and the repair has **no measurable effect today**, which is what makes EC5's fabricated violation the only thing that can drive it; EC6 is the control, since a filter blanking every title passes EC5 and is wrong about all 37. The block's end is checked against an independent brace-depth walk over all 1129 blocks, with no disagreement

**F1072** — **the inert tone was one of five, and the vacuity was not the word that was there but the one that was missing.** F1029 read `tone: "muted"` on the header's binary chip as a sentence that cannot be violated and asked for a ruling. The census it did not take: `chrome.ts` emits **eight** chips, **seven** name a tone, and **five of those seven are `muted` — which is the default**, so each is inert alone and dropping the clock's moves the same **0 of 46** golden snapshots that dropping the binary's does. So the five are not five words to delete: they are one property — **C22 pins its chrome's appearance rather than inheriting C09's `?? "muted"`** — and what was wrong is that one chip of eight opted out of it, and it was the one whose intended tone differed. Ruled `{ label: name, tone: "default" }`, read from the frame rather than reasoned: **19 of 46 snapshots moved, 15 style maps and 4 byte rows, and no grid** — a tone being appearance and never geometry. C22 gains §6l.6 J's clause, **I97** and commitment 67; T1.46b asserts the class on the walk and the two inks on `tone(…, theme, caps)` rather than on the two words, because `tone: "muted"` on the name spells a difference and paints none · **Closed** — four mutations, the two clauses failing separately, and the label list asserted before the walk because a walk that found nothing satisfies every loop below it. Recorded against the ruling: it adds a third level of emphasis to a row that had two, and chrome is meant to recede

**F1069** — **a scope was widened in the walk and not in the filter, so 645 files were added and removed three lines apart.** The corpus walks `tools/` and `test/` and the predicate three lines later keeps **0** of the 645, which hold **2 688** finding citations across 423 files. **The justification is correct, which is why nobody looked** — five sentences above the walk explain the widening, give its figure and say *measured before widening so that it lands green rather than red*, and that measurement was real and never reached the predicate that decides. **Nothing could tell the two states apart**: a walk that adds 645 files and a filter that removes them reports exactly what a correct scope reports. The one document that was right — the architecture row already lists all five directories — had nothing joining it to the code, because the test that reconciles that column is scoped to the other rule family · **Closed** — repaired and measured **before** applying it: 11 violations against 3, all eight of the difference the round's own unfiled numbers, **zero pre-existing in the 645**. The widening put the fabricating file inside the rule's own corpus, so the sentinel is built at run time and exists only in memory

**F1080** — **the group heading is a second record of the open set, and nothing checks it.** Closing F158 and F1024 meant editing group 8 from `1 open · 48 closed` to `closed · 49 closed` and group 9 from `1 open · 67 closed` to `closed · 68 closed` by hand, with nothing that would have gone red had neither been touched. A second pair one level up: group 11's heading read `396 entries` against **395** in the `ids keyed` column, and SP6 was green because it sums that column against `keyed.size` — a +1 in one row cancelling a −1 in another, which is what a summed gate cannot see. **The first draft of this entry was wrong and its error is recorded in it**: it read the ranking table's `consumers` column as a disposition column and rewrote four cells, when group 8's `4 open` against one open finding was the tell and the column's definition was four lines above · **Closed** — built as SP14, and only the tally half: the `ids keyed` half stays with `checkTriageInventory`'s recorded non-gate, whose argument holds — *keyed* has no strong definition and a disposition does. Run before wiring, it found §13 out by one, §12 stating no verdict count, §4 and §6 stating only a total, and §3 spelling the field `unread`; all five repaired, and a heading stating no tally is now a violation rather than a pass. **A fifth form of a gate that exists and is not run fell out of the mutation pass** and is closed in the same commit: deleting only the spread while leaving the binding failed nothing, so the meta-row now requires each carrier's result to reach `violations`, with `SP8` named as the one report-only rule in a list compared by equality. Stated blind spot: the consumers column must not be compared to anything, because its `unread` is not `with no verdict`

**F1082** — **`Plot` has sixty-eight members and no record of which are checked, so a member arrives unchecked and unreported.** Found by the walk for F271, before the channel it was about existed: moving a bubble's sizes out of `series` also moves them out of `series`' own `requireFiniteNumbers`, and nothing carries that over — a member outside `series` is validated by whatever someone wrote for it. Measured over the declaration, comments stripped: fourteen of sixty-eight never appear in `validate.ts` at all — and appearing is a proxy. Driving five wrong values at every member across all forty-eight forms of `PlotForm`, over fixtures first shown to validate clean: **twenty of sixty-six optional members are refused by no form** — `xLabels xMin xMax xFormat yMin yMax emptyMessage categories quartiles offsets totals startDate bands facets segments xScale yScale camera bandwidth axisStyle3`. The proxy was wrong in **both** directions: six members it counted as present are unchecked, and `axes`, which it counted as absent, is refused. **Three ways a check reads as present**, each defeating a different reader — *absent* fails a grep; *read then skipped* passes one, and is `plotAxisCrossErrors` reaching `yMin` three guards deep and then using `typeof lo !== "number"` to **skip** rather than to refuse; *gated behind a sibling* passes a grep **and** carries a detailed refusal, and is `startDate`, whose whole check sits under `if (unit === undefined) return;` — measured, `form: "calendar"` with `startDate: 12345` and no `calendarUnit` is accepted, and the same document with `calendarUnit: "day"` is refused. Two are the same shape as the channel that started this: `offsets` is `readonly number[]` and `totals` is `readonly boolean[]`, both declared, both reachable from the builder, both ungated. **And `facets` is `readonly Plot[]`** — child plots, so every rule in the file stops at the first nesting. **I118 is the half that was built**: MG31 compares `PLOT_UNIONS` against `Plot`'s string-literal unions by equality in both directions, so a union added to the type is *unchecked and reported*. The other members have no such table, which is the same rule with its subject widened, and the reason the gap is invisible is that a missing check reads exactly like a satisfied one. **The instance this walk created was caught only because the walk asked**: `sizes` gets its `requireFiniteNumbers` call and would have been the fifteenth otherwise. **What a wrong value does, one form per member at width sixty**: nothing escapes containment; three become an ERROR card (`yMin` `yMax` `categories`); **seven draw a different frame with no error at all** (`xLabels` `xMin` `xMax` `quartiles` `totals` `bands` `facets`); ten are inert at that form and value. The seven are the finding — containment catches what crashes and says nothing about what lies. **And the two edges are unconstrained relative to each other**: `yMin: 6, yMax: 0` draws a collapsed axis, gutter reading `6` three times with the series flattened to a straight line, while `yMin: "x"` on the same block is loud. The mistyped value is the one you see. Open — the fix is the coverage table MG31's shape suggests, plus a check per uncovered member, and the count is what makes it a project rather than a line · **Closed** — C04 I120. Sixty-nine of sixty-nine members carry a row or a driven exemption, and the sweep's own twenty was a floor by four: its predicate was existential over forms and values, so `axes`, `annotations` and `light3` answered *yes* on a different value and **`origin3` answered on a pair rule about `axes3`** — a claim about co-occurrence, satisfied and gone. `checkAnnotations`' `if (!isRecord(a)) continue;` is *read then skipped* a third time. **The control refused eight of my own legitimate values**, each by a correct rule naming a constraint the table did not know. **The mutation survivor was the finding**: removing the `facets` arm from `childBlocksOf` left every row green, because the table's wrong value for `facets` is refused for not being a record — so the recursion this entry named was the half nothing tested, and a well-formed facet with a bad member inside is what separates them. The exemption list was itself an unchecked way to silence a member, found by reasoning about a mutation before running it. **And a finding this nearly acquired and measured away**: `ONE_PER_FORM` draws 19 of 69 members, the whole drawing corpus draws **67**
