**F1085** — **MG31 states its blind spot and then asserts the corpus does not reach it, and the corpus had reached it for twenty-five days.** The rule compares `PLOT_UNIONS` against `Plot`'s string-literal unions by equality both ways, which is right; its doc comment then names the two escapes a textual parse has — a member across two lines, a union built by reference — and says *both forms are absent from `Plot` today*. `xFormat?: Plot["yFormat"]` is the second form, landed `c839d514` on 2026-08-16; the sentence landed `b0d5f3ae` on 2026-09-10. **Not stale — false when written.** The member is in no table, `plotUnionErrors` loops a table that has never heard of it, and `validateDocument` accepts `xFormat: "__unlikely__"` while refusing the identical value on `yFormat`, the member it is declared from. **The separable lesson**: naming a limit is a claim about the rule and review can check it; claiming the corpus does not reach the limit is a claim about the corpus and only a measurement can — and the second sentence is the one a reader uses to decide the first does not matter. It is *a negative claim inverts a resolver's verdict* one level up: **there is no such member** reads most convincingly the day it stops being true, because the gate is green either way. One grep over the body the rule already brace-matches. **Open** — the instance is one table entry; the class is MG31 resolving `Plot["other"]` against its referent, after which the blind spot has one clause and it is a count the rule takes rather than a claim a reader trusts · **Closed** — MG31 resolves `Plot["other"]` against its referent through `plotByReferenceMembers`, shared with T2.132 so rule and row cannot drift; three fabrications fire and the control is silent; `xFormat` is in `PLOT_UNIONS`, making the table **25** entries against 24 inline unions, two counts now held separately. **The corpus is counted rather than asserted absent**, pinned at exactly one by-reference member — the arm cannot pass by having nothing to resolve. Removing the resolution fails T2.132 twice **and turns enforce red the other way**. Residue, permanent rather than pending: the same form on `AxisSpec3.format` and `BarSpec.format` is on nested types outside MG31's subject, closed by hand checks under F1082 — the class is closed and the gate reaches a third of it

**F1086** — **A04 §6's table promises `install → check → enforce → audit → test` on every branch push, the job guards are written for exactly that case, and the trigger excludes it.** `on: push: branches: [main]` plus `pull_request`, so a push to any other branch fires **nothing** — not the fast chain, not `enforce`, which §6 says stays on every push regardless because it costs five seconds. Meanwhile `full`, `proof` and `degraded` each carry `if: github.event_name == 'pull_request' || github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || …`, a distinction with no subject unless branch pushes trigger, and `fast` carries no `if:` at all because on the design as written it *is* what a branch push runs. **Two records agree with each other and the third decides**, which is why it was invisible: every artefact a reader checks corroborates the gap's absence. Measured — `gh run list --branch feat/profiler` returns nothing while `main` has runs, so the workflow works through pull requests, the path that happens to satisfy the trigger. **This is F812's blocker rather than something beside it**: its disposition has read *the next run is the measurement* while the branch doing the work could not produce one, and a finding waiting on evidence a mechanism cannot generate reads exactly like a finding waiting on someone's time. A fifth form of *a gate that exists and is not run*, and the most self-concealing of the five. Remedy is `on: push:` unrestricted — what §6 already specifies, with the existing guards doing the job they were written for — and **no spec edit**, because the spec is the record that is right. One consequence named rather than discovered: a push to a branch with an open PR then runs `fast` twice, once per event, which is the design as written; a `concurrency` group is a different change with its own row. **Open** — the trigger is fixed, and the measurement is the first run · **Closed** — on the second run this branch has ever had. The first was the fix's own push and it was red, on F1087 and F1088, neither of which is about the trigger and both of which had been in the tree for as long as the branch, green on every developer machine, waiting for a runner to read them. The second is green across `install`, `enforce`, `check`, `instruments`, `audit`, `regime` and `test` in eight minutes fifty-nine — a figure that is itself F1090. **The entry's own measurement was two findings deep before it could report on itself**, which is the argument for the trigger rather than against it. `degraded`, `full`, `proof` and `publish` skipped, which is the design and is what F1089 is about — and the close-out for `main` is a separate reading, `gh run list --branch main --limit 1` after the merge, because `main`'s copy still carries the old filter · **Closed on `main`** — `148c1df6`, run 34524961642. `main`'s `ci.yml` now reads `branches: ["**"]`, so the copy carrying the old filter is gone. Five of six jobs green — `fast`, `proof` and all three `degraded` — and `full` red on T5.21 alone, which is F812's set and now F1096's measurement rather than the trigger's. **The reading the finding asked for is the one it got**: the expensive tier ran on `main`, where the old table made it the only place it could run, and it ran on the pull request first, which is what that row was added for

**F1087** — **`test/contract/process.test.ts` spawns a real child whose last statement writes `out/handoff-<pid>.json`, and `out/` is neither tracked nor gitignored — a scratch directory every developer machine grows and a fresh checkout does not have.** The child throws `ENOENT`, exits 1, and the assertion reports a **process** fact — *the child ran to completion* — about a missing directory. Reproduced: the same `writeFileSync` in an empty directory exits 1. **The fourth form of *a gate that exists and is not run*, with a different file** — the 2026-09-05 instance was two `instruments` fixtures reading gitignored output, green everywhere and `ENOENT` on every runner. It had never fired because `make test` sits in the `fast` job, which until F1086 never ran on a branch, and on `main` the workspace has usually been through a job that creates `out/`. Nothing in `test/` creates it; the three other files naming the path use it as a fixture string or a `captureDir` the profiler makes recursively. **Open** — the row makes the directory it writes into · **Closed** — `mkdirSync("out", { recursive: true })` before the report path, and the proof is a reproduction rather than a diagnosis: a worktree at the filing commit with no `out/` gives the runner's line back exactly — same assertion, same `expected 1 to be +0` — and the fixed file copied into that same worktree turns it green. The row is shown to respond to the thing under test in the state the runner has, which is `test/support/README.md`'s rule applied to a fixture that is a directory rather than a file

**F1089** — **a trigger's reach is a property of the ref the file is on, and going to find out how to run the expensive tier turned up a workflow with no triggers at all.** F812's measurement lives in `full`, the only job installing `xvfb xdotool xterm kitty`, without which C02 T5.7 and C16 T5.9 skip by name — *not a pass, and not the gate either*, in that job's own words. Measured on run 34492226500, the one this branch has: `fast` failed and `degraded`, `full`, `proof` and `publish` all **skipped**, the guard being a pull request, `main` or a tag. So the question was whether `workflow_dispatch` could reach it — the key `mutation-sweep.yml` already carries and A04 §6's table already writes a row for. **GitHub answers in an error message**: `gh run list --workflow mutation-sweep.yml` is `HTTP 404: workflow mutation-sweep.yml not found on the default branch`, and `gh workflow list --all` returns one workflow. `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` register from the default branch alone; that file landed `887de60d` on 2026-09-09 and exists on `feat/profiler` only, so it has **neither of its two triggers** — not the weekly cron, not the dispatch, not the `only:` input written to steer it. §6's row for it is the whole answer to F980's eighteen stale anchors of two hundred and eighty-three, and it describes a gate that has never been capable of firing. **And nothing would have reported it**: the first Sunday is three days out, and a cron that does not fire produces no run, no annotation and no red — A03 §2's vacuity class in a scheduler, where a gate that cannot run and a gate with nothing to report are the same absence, and `gh workflow list` is the one line that tells them apart. **So F812's blocker is a merge rather than a workflow edit**, measured rather than assumed: adding the key here would register nothing until it is on `main`, so the expensive tier is unreachable from a feature branch by any means available on that branch. The cost premise was checked while it stood in the way and is false for this repository — §6 opens with *private repos have a monthly Actions budget* and `Calcium` is public, its `billable` figure **zero** across five jobs against a run duration of four hundred and fifty-four seconds, and populated with zeroes rather than absent, so the figure is reported and it is nothing. §6's second argument survives and is the real one: it is about what a push runs automatically, and says nothing about what a dispatch runs on demand. **A ninth form of a gate that exists and is not run, and the first where the gate is unregistered rather than unrun** — every earlier form is visible in the repository, and this one is invisible there by construction, the file being correct, complete and on the wrong ref. **Open** — the record and §6's missing sentence land here; both triggers need the file on `main`, which is a decision rather than a repair · **Closed** — `148c1df6` merged the branch and neither file changed, only the ref: `gh workflow list --all` returns two workflows where it returned one, and the API's `total_count` is two where `gh run list --workflow mutation-sweep.yml` was `HTTP 404: not found on the default branch`. **Registering is not running and this is the ninth form of a gate that exists and is not run**, so the close-out is a dispatch rather than a listing — `gh workflow run mutation-sweep -f only=c12-a` returned run 34525436331, six shards, the `only` input reaching the sweep and the job body working end to end. The first time that workflow has ever run, and its cron is still ahead of it. A listing would have closed the wrong half: the finding's own words are *the first form where the gate is unregistered rather than unrun*

**F1090** — **A04 §6's branch-push row promises two minutes, names five stages, and the job runs seven in nine.** Measured on run 34497559553, the first green run this branch has ever had and F1086's closing measurement: `install` sixteen seconds, `enforce` ten, `check` seven, **`instruments` a hundred and fifty-one**, `audit` eleven, `regime` two, `test` three hundred and thirty-three, and the whole job five hundred and thirty-nine with checkout and set-up. §6's five named stages come to **three hundred and seventy-seven** — six minutes seventeen, or three point one times the promise; the job is four point five times it. The red run before it brackets the figure rather than contradicting it, at two hundred and ninety-four for the same five on a `make test` that failed. **And two of the seven stages are not in the arrow**: `instruments` is twenty-eight per cent of the job and costs more than `install`, `enforce`, `check` and `audit` together, and `regime` is two seconds. Both joined `fast` in `7f399391` on 2026-08-12, a commit whose subject is *CI runs the seventh target, and a budget says which regime it was set in* — it added the target to the job and not to the table, and the arrow is from the initial commit on 2026-07-28 and has never been edited. Twenty-nine days out of date, and the record that moved is the one nobody reads for a budget. **Where the two minutes came from: nowhere measured.** F1088's class, with one difference that makes it worse — F1088's figure was a constant, so a row could time out against it and did, while this one is prose in a table, so nothing asserts it, nothing can go red, and the only thing that ever disagrees is a run nobody was getting. **Rearranging cannot reach it**: moving `instruments` to `full` leaves three hundred and seventy-seven, and `make test` alone is three hundred and thirty-three and is the gate the row exists to promise. **How it was found is the part worth keeping** — the paragraph two lines above this row was corrected an hour earlier, in F1089's spec half, and the row underneath it was not read while the run that falsifies it was the one being waited on. *Read the abstract against its own section*, with the parts swapped: not a summary that dropped a body's condition, but a correction that stopped at the sentence it came in for. **Open** — the row takes the measured figures and the true stage list; whether `instruments` belongs in `fast` at all is a separate question with its own cost argument and is named rather than folded in · **Closed** — the row carries nine minutes measured and names `instruments` and `regime` beside the arrow rather than inside it, because that arrow is the shape all three repos share; the step table, the dates and the commit that moved one record and not the other are in the section under it. **What the promise was for is restated rather than deleted** — the row is the inner loop, so what belongs in it is what changes a decision within the push, a test that survives the figure being wrong where a budget in seconds would only have gone stale again

**F1092** — **an evidence table of nine readers: eight cited, all eight wrong at HEAD, one that never resolved at any commit, and the gate reported the line that landed on whitespace.** Merging `main` shifted `construct.ts` by twenty lines and `session.ts` by nine, and `make instruments` went red with a single line — `body: shell/construct.ts:1576 is blank`. Going to fix that one citation is where the finding is. Entry 30 argues a structured buffer is four components' change because `editor.text` leaves C17 as a plain string at a list of sites; measured at `437aaa79`, the commit that wrote the table, **the tree held nine sites, the table listed eight, and the prose above it said seven** — three numbers, no two equal, in one screen of one document. The population **has not moved**: nine then, nine now, so the count was wrong on the day and the citations drifted around it. **The member never in the table is the strongest row** — `keys.ts:397`, now `:486`, is `const before = deps.editor.text` followed by `setText`, an **edit round-trip**, where every other reader only consumes the string; the entry's whole question is whether the sentinel design stays C17-local, and the row bearing hardest on it was the one nobody listed. **And one citation was false when written**: `construct.ts:2523` is cited twice, in the table and in the prose sentence about where resolution happens, and on that day the file was one thousand six hundred and fifty-nine lines long with the submission site at 1215 reading `editor.text`. Swept over every commit touching the file since, `pipeline?.submit(stores.editor.resolved)` has been at 2525 since it appeared and has **never once been at 2523**. **Why one of eight**: the gate says what it does in its own output — *checked for the file existing and the line being non-blank only* — and non-blank is satisfied by every wrong line that is non-blank; `:1576` landed on whitespace and the other seven land in the middle of other functions. *Containment is not correctness*, the containment being *the line has characters on it*. **The sibling signal already measures the real thing and is not gated**: *citation anchorage · 39/74 line citations carry one of their own cell's symbols within six lines*, which is **thirty-five adrift**, reported every build into a line nobody reads as a verdict. **Open** — entry 30's table lands here with nine rows, the lines they resolve to, the count corrected in all four places and the missing member named; gating anchorage today turns the build red on thirty-five rows across entries nobody is working on, which is a repair campaign rather than a merge, and those thirty-five are the residue, named rather than closed. **What found it was not the gate** but resolving each cited claim against HEAD before trusting the row — and it caught a claim of mine in the same hour, a first repair asserting *seven then, nine now, and the two arrivals*, written from inference and false, both arrivals having been readers on the day with one simply never listed · **Closed** — thirty-six citations re-pointed across the roadmap, and the two that were not drift are named: `types.ts:446`'s member was real at `63257076` and deleted by `90a717cd`, the very commit its cell documents, so the cell now says so in prose, and `validate.ts:284` is correct, the six-line window simply cannot see that `suggestName`'s one-line wrapper is what the site calls. **Thirty-five to five and the five are one class** — the citation is right and the cell's symbols name something other than the site — which is the residue read rather than counted, and reading it is what produced F1101, F1102 and F1103. The signal now reports seventy-three of seventy-eight, the population having grown because a matcher that could read only one of three spellings had been skipping whole cells before judging them. Entry thirty's table stands at nine rows, every citation resolving at HEAD, with `keys.ts:486` listed and its edit round-trip named

**F1095** — **the two files that decide whether any gate runs are read by no gate.** `grep -rln '\.github' tools/ Makefile` returns nothing: `make enforce` runs sixty-one source scans, a module graph, a commitment audit, a findings-register check and a todo-expiry sweep, and not one of them opens `ci.yml` or `mutation-sweep.yml`, the two files that decide whether any of it runs. **Four findings in one day came out of that directory** — F1086's trigger excluding branch pushes, in the tree forty-four days; F1089's workflow with neither of its triggers, invisible by construction; F1090's stage list naming five where the job runs seven, twenty-nine days stale; F1094's job installing four packages for rows that need five — and every one was found by a person going to look. **A fifth came out of the draft rule before it was wired**: of the ten `make` targets a job runs, nine are named in A04 §6 and `proof` is not, and `proof` is a whole job with its own runner and a pull-request guard, named in A04 only at §1 as a noun and §3 as a sentence about packing. That is F1090's defect a third time and the largest — not a stage missing from a list of stages but a job missing from the account of the jobs — and F807 is why it matters, `proof` having been CI-only in practice and red on an npm crash for as long as the fast job hid it. **A sixth is live and vacuous**: `mutation-sweep.yml` says it carries the same terminals `full` installs and the two lines diverged at `dab8bbc1`, F1094's own fix, which put `imagemagick` in one of them; measured rather than assumed, no mutation run reaches an ImageMagick row, so the claim is false without yet being costly, and the repair is the comment rather than the package because the day a run does reach one, F1094's precondition refuses by name. **This is SS31's shape** — a document about a file drifts from it, and the drift is invisible to a reader holding either one — and §5 is the second measurement, eight of this repository's targets absent from the table that is supposed to be the Makefile contract and two of the table's naming other repositories', which is what makes §5 cross-repo and stops a two-way comparison being the right rule there. **Open** — SS62, one direction only, scoped to §6 rather than to the document because scoping it to the document makes it pass today; measured over the corpus before wiring, ten targets, nine ok, one firing on a defect rather than a convention. **Stated blind spot, and it is the larger half**: a trigger's reach is a property of the ref a file is on rather than of the file, so nothing local separates F1086's tree from the fixed one and nothing at all reports whether `mutation-sweep.yml` is registered; SS62 covers the third instance and the fifth and neither of the two that hid best, and the instrument for those is the habit §6 already carries in prose — `gh workflow list --all` in the close-out of any change to that table · **Closed** — `tools/enforce/workflows.mjs` wired into `make enforce` beside SS31 for SS31's reason, five rows in `enforce-workflows.test.ts` and four hand mutations each killing one: `names()` returning true, `targetsRun` returning nothing, and both empty arms passing. A fifth mutation one file out — renaming the fabrication's title so it no longer says *fires* — turns the new registration row red, and that row exists because **SS62 spent one chain in exactly the state it was written to prevent**: inventoried in A03, implemented in a module the reconciliation could not see, `make enforce` green and `make test` red on *A03 inventories SS62 and nothing implements them*. The gate that caught it is in the suite rather than in `enforce`, which is *run the whole suite, not a chosen subset* arriving on this finding's own rule. A04 §6 gains the `proof` row in its own commit before the code, the sweep's comment stops claiming a parity no gate resolved, and the corpus reads ten of ten. **The two blind spots stay as named residue**

**F1097** — **the sweep's first run ever went red on two shards, and both reds are the job rather than the tree.** Dispatching the workflow to close F1089 fired it for the first time: four shards green, two red, and neither a survivor — both rows read `caught 0 survived 0 expected 0`, which is a run that measured nothing. **The first is a gitignored directory the job never generates.** `c12-ascii-alphabet` drives `plot-catalogue.test.ts`; `docs/catalogue/` is generated and gitignored, and `make test` carries `catalogue` as a prerequisite with the reason written beside it — *the degraded jobs run `make test` alone, so the dependency has to travel with the target*. It did not travel, because the job runs `node tools/mutate/sweep.mjs` directly while `make mutate` exists and its own comment says CI uses it. A04 §5 forbids that in its own words: *CI runs the same targets a developer runs. Not equivalent commands — the same ones.* **And the target would not have been enough** — `make mutate` has no prerequisites either, so two records claim the dependency and neither carries it. Reproduced exactly with the catalogue moved aside: `exit 1, nine seconds, caught zero` against the runner's ten, green at eighty-two seconds with six caught when restored, and the reason is the harness's own vacuity guard — *the unmutated suite already fails, so no row below means anything*. **The guard worked and the job made the suite red.** F1087's shape with a different directory. **The second is a per-run timeout sized on a desk**: `c12-arm-seam` killed at `sweep.mjs`'s forty-five-minute bound, measured here at **two thousand two hundred and thirty-seven seconds against two thousand seven hundred** — eighty-three per cent of the budget on the only regime the author could see, and the runner is 2.7 to 4.1 times this container, so a hundred and one to a hundred and fifty-four minutes against forty-five. The kill is arithmetic rather than a hang and the run cannot pass on a runner at any load; F1088's ruling applies unchanged, a timeout being a claim that must hold on the slowest regime that runs it. The run itself is healthy at ninety caught and none survived, and **the snapshot net held** — the `LEFT THE TREE MUTATED` line is the recovery reporting, not a leak. **The third is why the first two looked alike**: `sweep.mjs` writes every run's log under `out/mutate-sweep/` and the workflow says those logs *are in the job log above*, which is false — nothing prints them and nothing uploads them, so a red shard gives one summary line and no reason, and the whole diagnosis of the first red exists only because it was reproduced on a machine that keeps the file. Third instance in that file of F1095's shape, a claim written in a workflow that no gate resolves. **Open** — `make mutate` gains `catalogue`, the job runs the target, and a red run prints the tail of its own log; the timeout is **not** raised, both figures and the ratio are recorded against it, and whether the sweep's cost model survives a runner is named as the measurement it is rather than folded in — the workflow promises about an hour a shard and one run of thirty-two costs thirty-seven minutes here · **Partly** — the catalogue and the log are repaired and driven both ways: `make -n` shows SHARD and ONLY passing through and a real run through the target comes back green at seventy-eight seconds with six caught after regenerating two thousand four hundred and forty frames, and `redTails` takes an injected disk with five rows in MS7 and three hand mutations each killing one. **The timeout stays open and is the reason this is not closed**: F1088's ruling would put the bound at the runner's 4.1 times, about nine thousand two hundred seconds, but a shard runs thirty-two of these against a three-hundred-minute job timeout, so a bound that admits this run breaks the shard. The number cannot be chosen without measuring what a whole shard costs on a runner, which is the first thing the next full sweep reports now that it can run at all · **Closed** (F1105) — the sweep ran and the number is the number already there. One hundred and ninety-two runs: median thirty-three seconds, p90 one hundred and sixteen, so forty-five minutes is twenty-three times the p90 and admits one hundred and ninety-one of them. **One run fails and it is an outlier rather than a regime**: `c12-arm-seam` carries ninety mutations, more than any file in the catalogue, and was killed at the bound on the runner as it was here. The remedy is to split it — one hundred and fifty-four minutes would put one run at half a three-hundred-minute job and break the shard — and F1105 owns that alongside the seventeen other reds. **The ratio this was sized by is the wrong instrument**, measured on a render-cost row and applied to runs whose median is thirty-three seconds, with the third slowest coming in *faster* on the runner than in this container

**F1098** — **the element count sums two operations, and the ratio built on it is labelled *measured*.** `element(kind, id)` opens a span named `kind#id` and nothing else, and the registry decoration opens one in the `measure` wrapper and one in the `render` wrapper — so `NodeStat.calls` is measure-opens plus render-opens, and `profile.mjs` divides it by `frames` and prints `<-- measured more than once per frame`. Measured with a counter per operation over the same thirty-five-frame session: `group#chrome.header` is zero measures and thirty-five renders, `table#profile-table` is sixty-seven measures and no renders, and the header's pills are thirty-five of each. **So a block both measured and rendered has a floor of two point zero** and the marker fires on eight of the nine rows, while the two rows genuinely measured twice inside a frame — the footer's pills, at seventy measures over thirty-five frames — read three point zero, one step above a floor the table never states. **C28 I31's own sentence is the false one**: it says the count is kept beside `frames` so a node recomputed within one frame is distinguishable from one measured once per frame, and no constant threshold delivers that, because the floor is a property of the node — one for the header group, two for the header pills, three for the footer pills. **It is F892's symptom from a second merge**: I42 records the same formatter sentence naming a recomputation at a node where there is none, repaired then by keying on the entry pair; that merged two entries, this merges two operations, and the class is a ratio whose numerator is a union. **The filing was wrong about the mechanism and this corrects it** — it blamed the height memo, and a memo hit never reaches the `measure` property, which `registry.ts`'s own header states in six words; the wrong reading came from `registry-probe.ts`'s gloss *questions rather than reads*, which admits both senses, and the hand tally agreed with it because thirty-five renders and thirty-five memo hits are the same number in that session. Remedy: `element(kind, id, op)` with a required operation, `NodeStat` carrying measures and renders with the sum kept for the seam-entry column `panes.ts` already describes correctly, and the printed ratio becoming measures over frames — under which this session flags the footer's two pills alone, which is compose's `measureSequence` before the paint pass, named by C22 I82 and already asserted in `session-paint.test.ts`. **Closed** — `element(kind, id, op)` with `measures` and `renders` beside `calls`, and `measures / frames` as the printed ratio: four of nine rows flagged where eight were, and `group#chrome.header` prints zero point zero, which no earlier version of the table could say. The four that remain are real — the footer's two pills at compose's `measureSequence` plus paint's, and the entry's table and plot at two measures per frame with no render through the property. **A second site was found by adding the two seams up**: the per-entry table's *elements measured* column prints `byEntry`'s close count, two hundred and three for one entry against two hundred and two measures, and it is renamed rather than split because the sum is taken over both seams and a count over one would be a mean of neither. Asking why that was the section that was wrong is F1099

**F1099** — **two of the report's six sections are computed in the script, and both of them carried the mislabel.** `make profile` prints six sections; three come from `src/testing/profile.ts` as a `check*` paired with a `format*` — the appendix, the two phase tables, the leak table — and three are built inline. The two inline tables are the element table F1098 corrected and the per-entry table, whose *elements measured* column prints `byEntry`'s close count: two hundred and three for the one entry this session draws, which is sixty-eight plus one plus sixty-seven plus sixty-seven to the unit, measures and renders together. **The ruling this applies was already taken once**, and `profile.mjs`'s own header carries it — the phase table moved into the harness because a reading computed in a script is a reading no row can be written against, which is how its negative residue of minus four hundred and sixty point five milliseconds went unasserted (F888, C28 I41). The two tables beside it stayed where they were, and they are the two that were wrong, so this is a ruling applied to one of the three sections it covered rather than a rule inferred from a correlation of six. **Why a script is where a label rots**: a `check*` returns a value a row can assert and a `format*` renders it, while inline the number is interpolated into a template string on its way to stdout and the only assertable thing is the whole line. The instruments fixture runs the tool and reads its exit code; none of its nine rows can see a column heading. Remedy: `checkElements`/`formatElements` and `checkEntries`/`formatEntries` beside the three siblings, runner-free with a report as input (C28 I37), with the marker's threshold published rather than a literal — and the row that matters asserts that a report whose blocks are each measured once and rendered once produces no flagged rows, which is F1098 made unable to return at the report seam rather than only at the aggregate. **Closed** — `checkElementCost`/`formatElementCost` and `checkEntryCost`/`formatEntryCost` in `src/testing/profile.ts` with `REPEATED_ABOVE` published, and `tools/profile.mjs` computing no table of its own. **The names carry `Cost` because `checkElements` and `ElementReport` are already C26's**, and the compiler refused the second definition — MG24's name exactness arriving as a duplicate identifier one layer before the scan. Three rows, each unwritable before the move, and the middle one carries the finding inside the finding: `repeated` is taken over the whole population, and any fixture of ten nodes or fewer makes that indistinguishable from a count taken after the truncation, so the arm is **twelve nodes with the crossing one cheapest** — outside the table entirely, the count the only thing that says it exists. Its first draft was wrong the way the rule warns about: measuring a node twice doubles its self time, which put the repeated node third rather than last, a fixture agreeing with the defect instead of responding to it. Four mutations recorded and each run by hand — the ratio reading the union, the count after the truncation, the unclaimed row's absent count drawn as a zero, the threshold made inclusive — each killing exactly the row it names. **Stated blind spot**: I55 is asserted by rows and enforced by no scan, so nothing goes red the day a seventh section is added inline; the scan that would close it has to tell a section that computes from one that quotes, which is a distinction the invariant draws in prose and a line regex cannot

**F1101** — **the roadmap writes a symbol three ways and the anchorage matcher reads one of them.** `IDENT` in `roadmap-status.mjs` takes a bare backticked identifier, and the document also writes `cells()` and `#anchor` — forty-seven call-form spans and seven hash-prefixed ones, two of the latter being palette cells rather than fields. **The window is a judgement and the spelling is not**: F904 refuses to widen the six-line radius by name, because a rule that guesses a radius gets widened until it says nothing, while `body()` names `body` and `#setCopyMode` is written with its hash in `session.ts` — dropping the parentheses and keeping the hash is how each appears in `src/`, so neither guesses. **The silent half is the population, not the verdict**: a cell whose symbols are only ever written in the two unread forms has no idents at all and is skipped before it is judged, so widening took the count from seventy-five citations to seventy-eight and **every one of the three arrivals was adrift** — `viewport.ts:347` for `#afterContent`, declared at three hundred and eighty-seven; `paint.ts:303` for `ghost()`, read at four hundred and forty-four; `keys.ts:441`, cited twice, for `afterEdit()` at five hundred and thirty. Nothing else in the repository reaches them: all three lines exist and are non-blank, which is all the gated arm checks, and all three symbols appear somewhere in their cited file, which is all the symbol arm checks — F904's own shape, two correct checks over one citation with nothing joining them. A matcher that sees one encoding reports absence when the value changes form, and here it reported nothing at all. **Closed** — the pattern takes both forms, `#fafafa` is excluded by the CSS hex lengths rather than by name so a field called `#anchor` is still a symbol, and the gated `problems` arm resolved every newly extracted ident against its cited files with none missing

**F1102** — **a citation repaired onto a second wrong line reads exactly like one repaired correctly.** F1092's campaign moved entry nineteen's citation from `viewport.ts:197` to two hundred and twenty-four, and two hundred and twenty-four is step zero's guard — `if (size.width === this.#width && size.height === this.#height) return;`. The cell's sentence is *rebuilds **only when the width changed***, and the width-gated rebuild is at two hundred and thirty-seven; the cell names step zero separately in its own clause three lines later. **The repair anchored the sentence on the line belonging to the neighbouring clause.** The signal cannot tell: `resize` is declared at two hundred and nine and is the cell's only extracted symbol, so both windows miss it and the citation was adrift before the repair and adrift after with byte-identical output. A campaign driven by the count would have called it done, and one driven by the adrift set reaching zero would have been pushed to widen the window — the failure F904 refuses by name. **What found it was being made to justify the residue**: closing on *thirty-five to five* takes a number, while writing down why these five are acceptable takes reading each cell's sentence against its site, and four of the five survived that and the fifth did not. The general form is that **a repair verified by the instrument that prompted it is verified against that instrument's blind spot too** — anchorage answers *is one of this cell's symbols near this line* and never *is this the line the sentence is about*, so a move inside the blind region changes neither answer. **Closed** — two hundred and twenty-four to two hundred and thirty-seven, and the rule that reaches the class is the one already written down, *ask where a settled claim is written down*, run on a repair rather than on a finding

**F1103** — **a correction to a population left the sub-count over that same population untouched, and the member it added is the row the correction called strongest.** F1092's remedy reads *the count corrected in all four places*; three were, and the fourth — entry thirty's indented design block, eleven hundred lines below the table — still said seven sites. Going there turned up three more stale claims in the same paragraph that F1092 named none of: the index sub-count as *three of the seven*, the submission site as `construct.ts:2523` when it has never once been anything but two thousand five hundred and twenty-five, and the same sub-count again as a comment in `src/shell/construct.ts`. **The sub-count contradicted its own parenthetical** — *three of the seven read a buffer index alongside the string (`contextAt` three times, `selectionSpans`)* lists four members beside a number that says three, a sentence carrying its own counter-example inside the document whose finding is a miscount. **And the fifth member is F1092's own strongest row**: `keys.ts:486`, `applyEdit`, reads the buffer into `before` and writes back `before.slice(0, edit.start) + edit.text + before.slice(edit.end)` with `edit.start + edit.text.length` as the cursor, and those offsets come from `accept` over a `contextAt` taken on the unresolved string — so under the resolving `text` getter section eight-a refuses, the other four read at a wrong offset and **this one writes at one**. F1092 called it the row bearing hardest on the entry's whole question and the one nobody listed, then listed it as a reader without noticing it belongs to the sub-count the question is actually about. **The mechanism is that a correction lands on the sentence that was measured and stops there**: the population was measured at nine so its four restatements were chased, while the sub-count is taken over that same population and was never itself measured, so nothing pointed at it and it survived in two documents and one source comment — a count derived from a corrected count is not corrected by the correction. **No instrument reaches it**: `construct.ts:2523` is a comment line and therefore non-blank, the cell's symbols appear in the file, and neither check the gate runs can fail on a comment. **Closed** — nine in all four places, five index readers named with `applyEdit` among them, the citation moved to two thousand five hundred and twenty-five, and the comment in `src/` corrected

**F1104** — **C04 obliges a glyph on a `warn` cell, C11's bar branch returns before reading it, and four goldens record the absence.** The first automated mutation sweep left a survivor on `docker-dashboard.mjs` — *the glyph slot is not reserved, so the column reflows when a container gets hot* — and the survivor is right: its subject is inert. `cells.ts` pushes `valueBar(cell.bar, planned.width, …)` and returns **forty lines above** the line that reads `cell.glyph`, so a bar cell's glyph has never been drawn, and `spark` is the same early return eight lines up. **C04 I6 obliges the thing C11 discards** — any `Cell` toned `error` or `warn` carries a glyph, enforced at construction, so `examples/docker` must supply one and `loadTone` does above sixty. No spec permits the drop: C11 says nothing about a bar cell's glyph and C04 I50c says only that `Cell.bar` replaces the cell's **text** and takes the planned width, while the ordinary path is explicit that a glyph is part of the cell's width rather than an addition to it — so it is not text and I50c does not reach it. **Four golden frames record it and two of them have no colour**: `worker` carries `tone: "error", glyph: "warn"` and draws `########### 101.2%` at dark-ascii, where the severity band is carried by nothing — C12 I25 arriving in C11's file, *two things a reader must tell apart differ by mark or by name, never by tone alone*, with fifty-nine and sixty-one per cent differing by one cell of fill and the band declared at sixty. A snapshot records and does not check, for the second time in this register. **The consumer reserved the slot and the run ate it**: `GLYPH_SLOT` is two cells inside `CPU_WIDTH` and inside both columns' `minWidth`, and the cell measured from the frame is `[██████████ 780.0%]` at seventeen — run ten, separator one, number six — becoming `[████████ 780.0%]` at fifteen once the slot is removed. `valueBar` is handed `planned.width` and **re-derives the split itself**, so `GLYPH_SLOT + BAR_CELLS + 1 + 6` names a layout the renderer does not produce and `BAR_CELLS` is eight at neither setting. That is why the mutation survives — removing the slot shortens the run by two and changes nothing else, so no assertion about elision can see it, and the glyph has nowhere to go because the bar occupies every cell. The app's own comment records the hand-drawn predecessor rendering `✗ ████████ 101.2%`, so the glyph was lost when `Cell.bar` took the drawing and the reservation stayed. **The first draft of this entry read the column as nineteen against a minimum of seventeen and called the fixture slack**; it is neither, `COLUMN_GAP` is two and C11 I22 allocates a non-flex column exactly its minimum, and both wrong legs came from a probe printing the distance to the next header and calling it a width — a finding's own example is checked by nothing, and the check is to reproduce the figure. **Two more defects fall out of measuring it.** The replacement row is a tautology — `test/repo/cpu-cell.test.ts` slices the frame to `cpuWidth()` and asserts the slice is at most `cpuWidth()`, satisfied by construction for every value — and its own comment says it was written to escape exactly that, so the symmetry moved out of the module and into the test's own reader, A03 section two's vacuity class in the file documenting it. And the constants name a layout nothing produces: `BAR_CELLS` is documented as the run and the run is ten cells at `CPU_WIDTH`, which is what makes the remedy C11's rather than the app's — the app cannot reserve anything from out there. **Closed** — C11 I23, commitment twenty-one, T1.26 to T1.28, T3.21 and T6.18, with `c11-cell-glyph.mjs`'s three mutations and `docker-dashboard.mjs`'s C4 now caught. **The frame falsified two rulings on the way.** The too-narrow clause went: rendered at three a toned cell draws `▲ …` where an untoned one draws `10…` for 101.2, and C12 I20 already ruled that a truncated number is a different number, so the mark is dropped only where it does not fit. And the lead is the **column's** allowance rather than the cell's — the constant that started this said so all along, *reserved whether or not a glyph is drawn* — because spent per cell a container at fifty-nine per cent draws its run in ten cells and one at sixty-one in eight, which is C12 I20's own inversion in the other allowance, *ninety-nine draws thirty-seven and one hundred draws thirty-six, every count right*. A third row could not fail at all: T3.21 wanted a two-cell glyph at wide and C09 I48 makes every glyph one cell either way, so its blind spot is stated and C09 T2.5 is what goes red the day a token's renderings differ. T1.28's first draft made the twin mistake twice in four lines, asserting an offset the number holds under either design — the observable is where the **run** begins. **`GLYPH_SLOT + BAR_CELLS + 1 + 6` describes the frame for the first time**, so C4 is rewritten against `BAR_CELLS`, the one figure that does not move when the slot is removed. Residue stated: the reservation costs two cells of run in every fixture sized before I23, so `states.ts`'s `api` at four point two per cent falls from one filled cell to none — `valueBar` has no minimum-ink step for `fill` the way C12 I16 has one for a ramp, which is C12's question and not C11's

**F1105** — **the sweep's first complete run: one hundred and ninety-two runs, eighteen red, and the extrapolation the durations falsify.** Six shards, about eighty-four minutes wall and three hundred and twenty-six job-minutes: one hundred and sixty-three green, eleven known-stale, **eighteen red**, thirteen survivors of which one is expected, and twelve stale anchors **two of which are ambiguous** — *its anchor matches 2x, `replace()` took the first*, so the mutation applies somewhere the author did not choose and the row that would catch it never runs, which is `assert s.count(old) == 1` missing from the mutation harness that this repo applies to its own edit scripts. **F980's argument paid in one run**: by hand a pass is a session per component, which is how eighteen of two hundred and eighty-three anchors went stale with two controls among them. **And it answers the question F1097 stayed open for** — *the number cannot be chosen without measuring what a whole shard costs on a runner*. All one hundred and ninety-two: median thirty-three seconds, mean one hundred, p90 one hundred and sixteen, second slowest one thousand nine hundred and twenty-two at seventy-one per cent of the bound, third one thousand six hundred and four at fifty-nine, slowest **the bound itself** — `c12-arm-seam`, killed at two thousand seven hundred with nothing caught and one file restored from the snapshot. **So the number is the number already there**: forty-five minutes is twenty-three times the p90 and admits one hundred and ninety-one of one hundred and ninety-two, and what fails is one outlier at sixty times the median carrying **ninety mutations**, more than any other file in the catalogue. **The ratio was the wrong instrument for it.** F1097 sized the problem at *one hundred and one to one hundred and fifty-four minutes* from a two point seven to four point one machine ratio; the direction held and the magnitude is unmeasurable from a killed run, but that ratio was measured on a render-cost row and the runs it is applied to land at a median of thirty-three seconds with the third slowest at twenty-seven minutes against a thirty-seven minute in-container figure — **faster on the runner, not three times slower**. A ratio measured on one workload is about that workload. The remedy is a split rather than a bound: one hundred and fifty-four minutes would put one run at half a three-hundred-minute job and break the shard, while splitting the ninety-mutation file leaves the bound governing the runs that do not need it, and `c23-running-card` at seventy-one per cent and `c28-profiler` at fifty-nine are the next two, so there is one run's headroom and no more. **Open** — this entry owns the list of eighteen; one is closed, `docker-dashboard.mjs`, whose `C4` survivor was F1104 and whose two stale anchors were one edit each, so its `KNOWN_STALE` entry is gone

**F1106** — **a mutation's `from` is checked and its `to` is not, so a rotted mutation reads exactly like a weak test.** `c12-lines3d.mjs`'s LN6 — *the frame is drawn before the data* — survived the first complete sweep, and its anchor matches, so nothing reported a problem. **Its `to` is broken in two independent ways.** It injects `frameOf(block, scene, grid, rows, depth, ctx, …)` where the real call four hundred lines below passes `{ w, rows }`, because the fourth parameter is `area: Readonly<{ w: number; rows: number }>` — a shape that changed at F489 and C12 I100, *`rows` was already here and `w` was not, and the asymmetry is the defect* — so `area.w` and `area.rows` are undefined inside the injected draw. And it **adds** a draw where the defect was a **move**: the data loop is at line nine hundred and ninety-nine and the real call at one thousand one hundred and seventy-eight, and the mutation inserts before the loop without removing the call after it, so the later draw wins and even with the right arguments the net effect is nothing. Either alone makes it unable to fail. **The class is that the anchors sweep checks a mutation can be applied and never that what it applies still means what it meant**: `from` is matched against the tree, which is the whole of what `KNOWN_STALE` counts, and `to` is an opaque string — so a rotted `to` applies cleanly, runs the suite and reports a survivor, indistinguishable from a row that is genuinely weak. F277 says to ask why a mutation cannot reach the test before rewriting the test, and this is the answer being *the mutation* for the second time in one sweep, after F1104's answer was *the subject is inert*. **Closed** — `tscTypecheck` is built in `mutate.mjs` and `runPass` asks it of a survivor and nothing else, the mutated tree being still on disk at exactly the point the reader is about to be told the tests are weak; a failing row reads `DID NOT TYPE` with the compiler's line beside it, is counted apart from the survivors and still fails the gate, and the baseline is measured rather than assumed — the first type error restores the tree and asks the same question of it, because the clean-*suite* guard does not cover a tree that type-checks red and runs green, which is the whole of the finding. Four rows: MH11, MH11b, MH11c, and MH11d which is the instrument itself against a real two-file tree, red then green in four hundred and fifty-four milliseconds. Four permanent mutations in the self-run and five by hand, all caught. End to end on the row that produced it: `c12-lines3d` said *one survived — a finding about the tests* and now names `scatter3.ts(999,45): error TS2345`. **Scoped to survivors on purpose and not only for cost** — a rotted `to` on a caught row misleads nobody, since the row is green and the named test failed; it could have been caught for the wrong reason, which is real and weaker, and asking every mutation rather than every survivor is two thousand and two checks against thirteen. **Two corrections, both from measuring what had just shipped.** The claim was overbroad — it read *the tree that ran is not the tree the mutation describes*, and **types are erased**, so the mutated code always runs exactly as written, LN6 included; what a type error says is that the `to` is **not expressible against the current tree**, which is evidence it was written against an older one, so the row is *suspect* rather than *unmeasured*. And the blind spot stated as an edge is the majority: applying every mutation in F1105's seventeen red runs, two hundred and twenty-four with a `to`, gives sixty-one red of which **forty-four are TS6133**, a binding with no reader — so the unused family is now excluded on a principled line, an unused binding being erased and unable to change what runs, while TS18047 stays in because a deleted null guard is a legal mutation and the compiler is right that the code may now throw. **Both arms of that exclusion were vacuous when written** and the mutation pass is what said so: MH11d's temporary project set `strict` and not `noUnusedLocals`, so it could not emit a TS6133 at all and the row asserting an orphan is not a rotted `to` passed with the filter removed — a fixture written for the check, on the same day, that had not been shown to respond to it. **Stated blind spot that remains**: an additive mutation type-checks perfectly and is inert for a reason no compiler can see, which is LN6's second defect. The cost figure this entry first carried, *thirteen of one hundred and ninety-two*, is a ratio that does not exist — thirteen counts rows and one hundred and ninety-two counts runs, and the denominator a survivor rate wants is the two thousand and two mutations. Separately measured and not diagnosed: replacing the tie guard `if (!nearer && mark[i] === undefined) return;` with an unconditional fall-through fails nothing, all nine rows passing with the frame taking every cell it reaches, so the guard is either unconstrained or LN6 is not about it — one hypothesis, cheaply tested, falsified, and the right replacement mutation wants the by-hand walk rather than a guess

**F1107** — **the corpus, type-checked: two hundred and twenty-four mutations, eighteen whose `to` is not expressible, and one that calls a function in no file.** F1106 built the check; this points it at the corpus once. Every mutation with a `to` in F1105's seventeen red runs, applied one at a time against a tree confirmed green first, type-checked, restored: **two hundred and twenty-four applied, sixty-one red to `tsc`, eighteen red after the unused family is excluded**, spread over ten of the seventeen runs. The parser is `anchors.mjs`'s own `anchorsOf` lifted verbatim and cross-checked against the anchor count the gate prints before anything is measured — the first draft hardcoded two thousand and two, refused at two thousand and six, and was right to, four mutations having been added since the number was copied. Forty-four are TS6133 and excluded; four are TS18047, a deleted null guard, which is the mutation's own point; the remaining thirteen are argument, field and shape refusals. **The one confirmed instance**: `c22-construct`'s *construct the lifecycle before the stores* replaces `at("runner", …)` with `atLate("runner", …)`, and `atLate` is in no file in `src/` — at run time a `ReferenceError`, so the row is caught by a crash rather than by the ordering it names, and its `expect` is a claim about which instrument caught it that is not true. That is precisely the *caught for the wrong reason* harm F1106 named as real-but-weaker and did not measure. **Open** — seventeen candidates are unread, four of them probably legitimate; the rest want what `atLate` got, which is a reading per row and not a run. And the census says nothing about the seventeen reds' actual survivors, which is what F1105 owns

## 12 · A time-based assertion under contention — **2 open** · 15 closed · none with no verdict · new at F80

**F1100** — **F963's stated-not-fixed residue reproduced on the runner, with its own named precondition met.** `fast` went red on PR forty-eight's pull-request run and green on its push run on the same commit, and the row left its whole verdict: T5.1c asserts `consumed.wall === recorded.wall` and it read fifty-four against fifty-six, two wall positions recorded that the replay never consumed, with mono one short, load at one point four zero, no stray fixture processes, and the divergence on the **last** frame — one hundred and seventy-four bytes recorded against three hundred and twenty-two replayed, after the Ctrl-D, with the session killed rather than exited. **F963 named this by its precondition and did not assert it**: the transcript head's duration figure is wall-derived and unmasked, *invisible below one second, and a far-side call of a second or more would diverge under any residual misalignment*. Every recording F963 was written against runs its answer at eighty-four to one hundred and fifty-nine milliseconds; **this run's answer took two thousand six hundred and fifty-eight**, so the figure invisible below a second had two and a half seconds to be visible in. That is what makes it a number rather than a re-run — a residue stated as *would diverge if X*, nothing watching X, and a runner that supplied X. **Two candidates and one observation**, which is why the verdict is recorded rather than diagnosed: the killed exit means the recorded tail may be short of a clean replay's, which is a second explanation for the last frame's size with nothing in this sample separating it from the first. Not repaired here — F963's own remedy is mirroring on mono, which needs C06 to measure `durationMs` on `elapsed`, and its own sentence calls that a C06 design question rather than a replay one; a parity change from a single runner sample would be a repair aimed at one reading. **Open**

**F1088** — **`CORPUS_BUDGET_MS` is 10 s, sized on an idle developer machine in the devcontainer, and the runner is 4.1×.** T3.15 timed out at exactly the budget on the first CI run this branch has ever had — and **the measurement was already in the same job**, printed thirty seconds earlier by `make regime`, the instrument `budget.ts` built for precisely this: *361 ms/pass here against 89 ms recorded … 4.1×*, with a notice saying *read this ratio before touching the budget*. The arithmetic then says the row could never have passed there: T3.15 records its own cost as **4.78 s against 10 s, measured**, which at 4.1× is **≈19.6 s** — twice the bound, not marginal. The same ratio puts `edge/transcript`'s 3.2 s worst at **13.1 s**, so two of the three files sharing this budget are above it on the runner. **A correct repair sized against the wrong regime**: the 4.78 s is new, from F1014's fix for `layout`'s memo turning a minimum-of-five into a minimum over four cache hits — right, and three times more expensive, measured and recorded against the only regime the author could see, on a branch where no runner could disagree. **This is what F1086 costs when it is left open.** `budget.ts`'s own taxonomy makes this a **timeout** (*how long a test may take before it is called stuck*) rather than a product claim like `DOCUMENT_BUDGET_MS`, so it must hold on the slowest regime that runs it; the performance claim is untouched and stays where it was, in the row's `insertMs` under two thousand and its ratio under three. **Open** — raised from the measured ratio, and the row's own runner figure is the next run's to print · **Closed** — sixty seconds, from the arithmetic the failing job printed itself, and the same mechanism found on a second constant with three figures against it. `make test` went red twice running on **MA4** and **MS3**, which each shell the whole hundred-and-ninety-two-run mutation sweep out to a child: the sweep alone is nine point four seconds, MA4 was **twenty-five point one** inside the last green `make all` and **thirty point three** on the very next run, against a global thirty. Not the unstable-under-load class — it reproduced two of two and the sweep is green alone. Twenty-five point one against thirty is one point one nine times, which is the sentence `budget.ts` opens with about a different row, so it had been green at eighty-four per cent of its budget and this session's own additions were enough. `SWEEP_BUDGET_MS` is a hundred and twenty seconds — a little under five times the loaded figure, the ratio `SCAN_BUDGET_MS` takes over its worst row, and a hang still surfaces inside two minutes. **And a third thing fell out of reading the file**: its headroom table said *vitest's five-second default* while `vitest.config.ts` has set thirty since 2026-08-22, F967's class, labelled rather than rewritten because those sentences are the record of why explicit budgets exist. Residue, recorded and not fixed: MS3 pays the same sweep a second time, so the suite runs it twice for two assertions about one output

**F929** — `make instruments` was red twice in four runs on an unchanged tree, and **a different profiler row each time**: T5.1b's replay-identity assertion once, T1.83's capability re-derivation the next. Both red runs report `tools/profile.mjs` **0 rows** rather than a failure count, and **556** rows against the green runs' **562**. **The changed member is the evidence**: a row failing identically every time is that row's defect; a different one each time, in one file, with the instrument reporting nothing at all, is the harness. Run alone the row passes twice; the whole file passes as a file; the target starts no load generator, and what both red runs share is a preceding gate chain. Recorded rather than diagnosed — the assertions are **byte identity**, not timings, so *contention* is the reading and not yet the cause. **Recorded so the next reader does not debug the row**: the first move on a red T1.83 is to run it again and watch whether the member moves. What would settle it is a run under deliberate load with the profiler file isolated, and a harness that can tell *starved child* from *genuine divergence* — which it currently cannot, and that half is worth fixing either way. **Open** · **Closed by F1018** — the count exists on vitest's own summary bracket, per run, needing no golden 562; F949 had already fixed the counter's other half and its residue was that the counter read the buckets rather than the bracket, so a crashed child read as *errored after its rows*. Sixth state, `lost rows`

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **F262** | **six rows whose verdict is a function of machine load** | `vitest.config.ts` sets no `testTimeout`, so 5000ms governs, and six rows parse or shell over the whole tree: the module graph, the commitment scan, the anchor sweep across 98 runs, the catalogue's SGR parser. **`MA4` is 1539ms alone and 5033–7345ms in the suite; `T6.5/T6.6` is 1723ms alone and 5114–5489ms.** Three consecutive runs, three different failing sets — five rows, then three, then two — every one passing alone. **The config already reasons about flakiness and this walked past it**: its comment is about a clock a test asks for, and the clock these race is one nobody chose. **It cost three red runs against a change that moved zero frames**, which is what a real regression looks like from outside. **Fixed** — `testTimeout: 30_000` with both figures beside it, a ceiling rather than six arguments because the rule is *a row that reads the whole tree needs room* and a per-row list stops seeing the seventh. Three runs after: green, green, green |

**Calcium's own suite, not the app's**, and it is here because a failure of this kind names an
enforcement rule and sends a reader looking for a violation that does not exist.

| | the victim shape |
|---|---|
| **F69** | a **threshold** — T5.3a asserts a background stream advanced by more than three while sixty keystrokes are processed. On a contended runner it advanced by one. Same commit, red on a branch and green on `main` four minutes later · **Closed** (F1004) — the predicate moved into `waitForFrame`, so the row fails by timing out rather than by comparing two numbers that were never in a relation |
| **F1004** | **the class, and eleven rows that are not in it** — a control written as a magnitude measures the machine. Tier 5 holds twelve counts compared against a non-zero number; reading each for *what sets the number* leaves T5.3a and one neighbour. C03 T5.1's `> 20` frames/s looks like the class and is not — its floor sits under a ceiling and the pair **is** the invariant, where a magnitude is only the load's measurement when it stands in for something else. **C10 T5.4 was the neighbour**: *fifty toggles produced frames* was `frames.length > 10`, and the number of frames fifty submissions coalesce into is C03's window; the row's subject is that every completed frame is 24 rows, and its loop skips the last as possibly still arriving — so with one frame on the screen it checks nothing and passes. The control now counts what the loop examined. **Its blind spot, stated**: T5.3a's surviving `expect` restates the wait's predicate and cannot fail if the wait returned, so the row's teeth are the timeout and the assertion is a message. **Closed** |
| **F73** | a **timeout** and a **ratio** — six tier-2 source scans timed out at 15 s having taken 24 s; C17 T3.15 read 6.0 against a limit of 3 on a 1 MB paste. **Read 4.05 again on 2026-08-15**, on a docs-only commit that touched no `src/`, green on an immediate re-run at load average 3.0 — a second measurement of the same row, and the one that makes *not an algorithmic regression* a fact rather than an inference · **Closed** (F991) — commits `83d6a87b` (the timeout) and `716f7905` (the ratio, F877) |
| **F877** | **one timing of each size** — T3.15's ratio is the whole assertion and a single descheduled slice inside the larger call is the whole reading; recorded failing at 6.0, 4.05, 3.17 and 3.03 across three sessions with the reading written down each time and the instrument never touched. Minimum of five → 2.18, 2.49, 1.99, 1.73, 2.11 against an unchanged bound of 3. **Fixed** |
| **F320** | **a fixture that types at a clock** | `examples/minimal`'s PTY harness sends `/list` at **1.5 s** and its Enter at 3.5 s; the package takes **3.35 / 3.79 / 4.35 s** to import `dist` and reach the prompt, measured three times. Both writes went into a terminal with no application listening, and the capture ends with `❯ /list` — the prompt drawn *after* the keystrokes — which reads exactly like a shell ignoring a command. **It passed on a quick machine and failed on a busy one**, so the row was asserting the host. **Three wrong hypotheses first, every one about the environment**: a half-written `dist` from a killed `make e2e` (a clean rebuild did not fix it), a stale symlink (it resolves correctly), and **1098 zombie processes** from the same killed run — real, reaped by restarting the container, and not the cause. A stash and a checkout to the previous commit failed there too, which cleared the day's work and left the harness. **The question that ended it was about the fixture rather than the environment** — *what is this row waiting for?* Nothing. **Fixed**: the command goes when the prompt appears, three green runs, and a control whose prompt never appears still captures the banner and no table. **Stated limit**: a 20 s outer deadline replaces the 9 s window, so a hung shell takes 20 s to fail |

**The ratio is the one worth naming**, because it fails in a way that reads as an algorithmic
regression rather than as a busy machine. So the class is not *slow rows time out*: it is
**anything whose assertion is about time.**

`make all` reproduces T5.6 with no load generator at all — three times for three, at 291–301 s
against 214–225 s for every subset, which is what says this is the machine rather than an
ordering defect.

**`make load-down` keeps its place on the asymmetry, not the odds**, and both measurements are
recorded: the step-8 re-measure did not reproduce, and F69's pair four minutes apart did. A row
that fails under contention will fail eventually whether or not anyone introduced it.

---

**F809** — a render-cost row red at 54.6 ms against a 50 ms ceiling on a runner measured at 2.7×, 17–22 ms locally; one named ceiling of 150 with both figures, F262's shape · **Closed** (F991) — commits `df06c00b`, `9427564b` — the ceiling carries both figures

**F812** — the emulator gate green in the devcontainer and red on the runner: kitty answering Shift on every key, xterm with no window in eight seconds; neither reproduced on the runner's own distribution, so the fixture is hardened on every axis the failures touch and the next run is the measurement · **Partly** (F991) — the fixture is hardened, and F839 measured red, red, green: the guard does not reach the mechanism · the run that would settle it cannot be produced from this branch: `full` is guarded to a pull request, `main` or a tag, and F1089 measured why a fourth trigger is not the answer — `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` register from the default branch alone, so adding one here would register nothing. The blocker is a merge, which is a decision rather than a repair, and the row says so now instead of reading as though it waits on someone's time · the blocker is cleared — `148c1df6` merged, and the set has been measured twice more. **The kitty rows are green on both**: T5.5, T5.5b and T5.7 passed on pull request 46's `full` and on `main`'s own run, one failure in a hundred and thirty-three, which is two consecutive greens after `d2e57aaa` moved the modifier clear after the settle. **T5.21 is the member that remains and it is no longer a flake**: `main`'s run failed it having used the whole five-second poll, with lines one to thirty-four of two hundred and the JSON complete rather than truncated, which is a feed that stopped rather than a patch that was late. That is F1096, and the diagnosis moves there with the two figures that separate its readings

**F983** — **a ratio between two axes measured a third thing, and fell under the suite's load the day that thing was fixed.** C28 T1.42 asserted ten times the plot's height moves the `plot.area` span 1.5× harder than ten times the samples; the span brackets the merge, whose per-column spread was quadratic in the width and linear in the rows, so the height axis was the merge's and not the raster's. F981 made the merge linear: alone the row still clears the margin at 2.1×, under the chain it read 3.9× against 4.0×. Removed rather than retuned — the ONE-PHASE mutation it guarded is exact on the kept frame's tree (`plot.area` holds no phase, the three are siblings), and *equal cells is not equal work* is a bound at 100× the samples (under 40×, a per-cell-per-sample renderer at fifty or more, one pass measured 4.9–12.4× across both trees). **Closed.**

**F1081** — **two assertions in one row raced on one knob, in opposite directions, and only the one that lost was visible.** T5.1d records behind a far side sleeping 1 200 ms so the readout's one-second wake draws a frame inside the call; `make instruments` was red on it once, with `frame:476` — the wake — absent between the input's echo and the `far` line. Green alone seven times, six of them under deliberate load to 19.9, and **the load reading is falsified rather than merely unconfirmed**: the margin between the wake and the answer *grew* with load — 222 ms quiet against 372–519 ms at nineteen — because the far side's answer path slows faster than a timer does. What those same six runs did show is the **other** race. The answer reached 1 959 ms, forty-one milliseconds short of the second boundary that turns the settled head's `· 1s · 20 rows`, asserted as a literal, into `2s`. **Two races on one knob, pulling opposite ways**: widening the sleep buys the wake its margin and spends the settled figure's, and the figure's headroom was never anyone's subject because it had never been lost. Fixed — the sleep goes to 2 500 ms so **two** wakes land inside the call rather than one and a fifth; the settled figure is read out of the frame rather than pinned, so which second it falls in stops being a claim about the harness's arithmetic; and the wake is looked for among the frames recorded **before** the `far` line, which is what F974 says and what a search over every frame was a proxy for — that third one is a restatement rather than a check, and the mutation pass says so: dropping the `break` fails nothing, because no running head is drawn after the answer settles. **The single red is recorded and not diagnosed**, which the repair survives: it holds under a late timer, under an unlucky phase and under two commits folded into one frame, and not under the one hypothesis still standing — `armParts` arms the readout's wake only while `deps.visible({ kind: "entry", id })`, so an entry not yet visible at registration arms no timer at all and waits on `visibilityChanged`. That is where the next reader starts. **And the red left one line that could say none of this**: the three assertions running before the replay carry `timeline(rec)`, which holds no times, while `explain` — built for exactly this by F929 and F949 — needs a verdict and is unreachable until after one. The row whose whole subject is a race printed the only message in the file with no clock in it. `harness()` extracted, so a pre-replay message carries `took`, the PTY reads, the load and the strays, and is built only when its assertion fails. **Fixed**

<a id="13"></a>
**F1083** — **narrowing a racy reading by kind lowered its rate and kept its shape.** C24 T1.9 asserts that importing the published profiling face registers no timer, by reading `process.getActiveResourcesInfo()` either side of an `await import(...)`. Its own comment records the first version of this: *the total moved on its own here — 5 before the import and 4 after, a handle the harness released while the module was loading* — and the repair was to count `Timeout` and `Immediate` only. The shape survived the narrowing. Measured on this tree, three runs of three inside the suite: `after` 2 against `before` 3, a foreign timer firing during the import, green alone every time. **A count of a process-wide kind across an `await` is a race with every other timer in the process**, and choosing a narrower kind picks a smaller population rather than a private one. Fixed as a bound — a timer this import registers can only raise the count, so `<=` is the invariant with the race removed rather than a weaker claim about it, and the upward control moves with it. **Its blind spot is stated rather than closed**: an import registering one in the same window as a foreign one expiring nets to zero, and `getActiveResourcesInfo` returns kinds and not identities, so nothing here can separate them — the structural assertion beside it, that the namespace publishes nothing callable, is what makes that case unreachable in practice. **Surfaced by an unrelated change**, which is the other half worth recording: F271's plot work shifted the suite's timing enough to turn a row that had been green into one that was red three times running, and a row whose verdict moves under a change that cannot reach its subject is reporting on the harness. **Fixed**

**F1084** — **a ratio is fragile in one direction and the row that gated on one said the opposite.** C09 T3.85 guards F955's fix by timing a 200-cell patch-gutter row against a 200-cell CJK row and requiring the quotient above **6**; its comment calls that *the transcript's 60 µs a row, as a ratio a loaded machine can still reproduce*. Inside a full `make all` it measured **5.7** — 0.0883 ms against 0.0156 — with the fix it exists to protect fully in place and the pre-fix figure at 2.8. **The denominator is a fixed floor**, which the sentence had backwards: measured at three sizes, the gutter row is *sub-linear* — 200, 800 and 3 200 cells cost 0.0065, 0.0117 and 0.0211 ms, sixteen times the cells for 3.2× the time — while the CJK row is linear, 0.056 → 0.287 → 0.939. So most of the 200-cell denominator is constant, and any additive per-call cost a busy machine adds compresses the quotient toward one. A ratio is only robust when both operands scale with the load. **Fixed by measuring the thing the ratio was a proxy for**, which the row's own comment names: *the number of clusters that reach the segmenter, one against a hundred*. Counted through `Segments.prototype.containing`, restored in a `finally`, it is **2 against 200** — each non-plain cluster is found once and measured once, the doubling being `fitStyled`'s `clusterAt` and `pieceCells`' own walk — exact, load-free, and a stronger claim than any duration. The timing stays as reported evidence with the control it always had, that the CJK row costs more at all. **Fixed**

**F1091** — **the ratio that has to separate two from four spans one point three eight to three point zero six, and the noise scales with the operand.** C17 T3.15's linearity claim is `big.ms / small.ms` under three, and the row's own comment says why: *linear is two and quadratic is four; the bound sits at three, in the middle, which is right* — one unit of headroom either side. Five runs of the row alone give **2.38, 2.35, 1.38, 1.84, 2.13**, a spread of a whole unit on an instrument that must resolve one; inside a full `make all` the record is 2.14 green and 3.06 red, and the comment already carries 3.03 and 3.17 red before that, repaired twice — a minimum of five, then a minimum of three over fresh editors once F1014's memo made the repeats cache hits. **What both repairs missed is that the noise is not the machine being busy.** In the red run the small walk read three hundred and twenty-eight against a solo range of two-fifty-two to three-sixty — inside it — while the big walk read one thousand and four against a solo four-ninety-six to six-eleven. A busy machine slows both and the ratio survives; **this perturbation lands on the larger operand only**, because twice the text is twice the allocation and the big walk pays a collection the small one does not, which no minimum over N can remove because every one of the N big readings pays it. **F1084's class entered from the other side**: that ratio had a denominator that was a fixed floor, this one has both operands scaling and the noise scaling with them — one rule absorbing both, *a ratio is an instrument only when its noise is common to both operands*. **The repair is F1084's too**, measure the thing the ratio was a proxy for: the walk calls `drawAs` exactly once per cluster at one site, and `layout.ts` exports `displayRows(text, width, gutter, drawAs)`, so a counting `drawAs` returning its input gives the inner loop's trip count — **1000032 visits against 1000032 graphemes, and 500016 against 500016**, exact and load-free. **The fabricated violation fires and the duration does not**: a second `drawAs` call ahead of the real one gives two million and sixty-four against one million and thirty-two while the walk's time reads 667 ms against 668 clean, so no timing could have seen it — which is the argument that the count is a different instrument rather than a cheaper proxy. Blind spot stated: the count is over iterations, so a change making each step proportional to the rows is quadratic and invisible here, and the timing that would have moved is evidence now rather than an assertion; nothing in the suite reaches that class, and the alternative is a bound that has been red three times and green by luck. **Open** — the count becomes the assertion, the durations stay printed with the resolution control, and C17 §10's row names the measurement so the next reader does not restore the timing · **Closed** — both controls driven rather than asserted: the doubled visit fails the count row naming it, and removing the `drawAs` call fails the row that says the counter fires at all, which is what an equality against zero would have satisfied in silence; `src/` was restored from a copy and its md5 compared both times. Three runs read five hundred thousand and sixteen to one million and thirty-two identically while the printed durations gave ratios of 1.24, 1.94 and 1.89. **And the row is cheaper**, four point seven eight seconds to **three point zero four** alone, because the count costs two walks and two segmentations where the minimum over three cost six — a third of the pressure F1088 put on `CORPUS_BUDGET_MS`, handed back by the row that caused it
