**F1096** — **the wait was right to exist and wrong about why, and it is the wait that says so.** `d2e57aaa` put a five-second poll under T5.21's tail assertion on a premise about a race — the patch carrying the child's last chunk landing a turn after `settled` returns — which was reasonable, unmeasured, and consistent with the two runner samples it was written from. On `main`'s own run after the merge the row failed having used **five thousand and forty-three milliseconds against a five-thousand deadline**, with the block holding lines one to thirty-four of two hundred and the JSON complete rather than truncated: `"follow":true}]` closes the array. **A patch landing a turn late is fixed by waiting a turn; a feed that delivers thirty-four lines and stops is not fixed by waiting at all**, so the repair was right to exist and wrong about why — and the only reason the distinction is known is that the repair produced the figure. A fix that changes nothing indicts the diagnosis, with the sign reversed: this one changed the evidence. **Two readings remain and they want opposite repairs** — a feed that stops wants a drain the route does not perform before it releases the guard, a feed that is slow wants a longer wait, which is the bound this row already widened once — and every sample so far reports only the final state, which both produce. **This machine cannot produce either**: green at eight hundred and seventy-six milliseconds every time, including with `settled`'s phase-one budget of twenty milliseconds starved to zero, because the poll underneath covers for an early `settled` locally. The local instrument reads *two hundred to two hundred numbered lines of two hundred in five thousand and ten milliseconds, one patch, grew by zero*, and two things in it are worth keeping — the transcript is already complete before the poll begins, so the wait has never once been load-bearing here, and the whole two hundred lines arrive in **one** patch, so a runner holding thirty-four may be holding one short chunk rather than a sequence that stalled. **Open** — no bound is widened; the row keeps its five seconds and gains the two figures that separate the readings, so the next red is a diagnosis rather than a repetition, and the falsified premise is corrected in the same edit rather than left in the file as the reason for the code beneath it. F812's set, and the member with a mechanism-shaped question rather than a flake · **Closed** — the instrument answered on its first firing: **forty-six to forty-six numbered lines of two hundred in five thousand and thirteen milliseconds, one patch, grew by zero**, and thirty-four on the sample before it, a cut point that moves because it is a chunk boundary rather than a cap. **`grew by 0` is a stopped feed**, and the row picked one of the two readings on its first red, which is the whole argument for reporting instead of choosing. **The mechanism is one flag carrying two conditions**: `accepting` means both *the child may still write* and *the emulator is still alive*, the outer check wants the first and closes early which is right, and the inner check wants the second and read the first — so every link queued before the exit and not yet run when it fired returned without writing, and the drain awaited a chain of no-ops. **C23 I67 was satisfied by it word for word** — *every write is awaited* was true of every one of them — so the clause moves to name the observable, which is A03 §2's vacuity class arriving in an invariant. T3.80 constructs the window rather than racing for it and fails on the old flag with the runner's own sentence; **T3.80b is the control the guard never had**, removing it failing nothing across all five emulator suites at ninety-five rows green, its hazard the opposite one and reachable through an `exited` that rejects with chunks still queued. The two rows together are why the flag changed rather than went

## 13 · Text the framework emits — **closed** · 8 closed · 1 with no verdict

**F995** — **absent was reported as the wrong type, and the ruling was already in this repository pointing the other way.** A `notice` with no `tone` and a `notice` whose `tone` is `42` both read *"tone" must be a string* — true both times, and the first sends a reader to a value there is no value for. `src/shell/config.ts` distinguishes exactly this for `createTui`'s required fields, and half the argument had been applied — to the config an app author types, and not to the validator every adapter's output passes through. **The count found by reading was wrong**: 47 sites by *which function emits the sentence*, and **12 more** by deleting each key of the one-per-kind corpus in turn, each with a bespoke predicate no grep for a helper reaches. **One of the twelve is the same defect reversed** — *`digest` is derived and must be present* emitted for a digest that was present and was a number. Absence is `=== undefined`, never `key in b`, because `in` gives one document two sentences either side of `JSON.stringify`; `null` is present and wrong. 56 call sites carry the split, 42 required keys are reachable, 41 are split, and one conditional requirement is exempt by equality. **Closed**

**F996** — **the far side failed and the notice named the app author's adapter — and the finding's own remedy splits on the wrong axis.** F152 proposed suppressing when `outcome.status === "error"`, inferred from two instances that are both far-side failures with empty stdout. Driving all four cells: **ok with empty stdout still blames the adapter**, and **error with a payload suppresses a real adapter defect**. Wrong in both directions, with `cancelled` mapping to `partial` as a third miss. The axis is `stdoutRaw.trim() === ""` — not `stdout === undefined`, which means *unparseable* and is true of a real payload, and not the status. **Two instances is the minimum for noticing a rule, not evidence for one.** C07 I23, with the cause before the consequence. And the mutation pass **corrected a reason rather than finding a gap**: the set assertion was justified as *a per-cell assertion cannot say exactly these two*, and converting it to membership checks survived, because the complement is asserted too — what the set buys is the fifth cell, which is now its own mutation. **Closed**

**F911** — `formatFrameCost` produced `last 9.6ms` and `last <0.1ms`, ten cells and eleven, so the footer's right-hand cell **jittered by one column** whenever the frame cost crossed 10 ms and every cell after it shifted. Found through replay, where masking the value left the frames still unequal — a mask cannot absorb the padding beside the cell, because it sits after the SGR reset and swallowing an escape would hide a real divergence. The fix is `padStart(5)` in the chrome rather than a wider mask: **a measurement drawn at a width that tracks its value moves everything beside it**, which is a rendering defect independent of the gate that found it · **Closed** (F991) — commit `dc79ab21` — the figure is padded to a fixed width

**F55**. `PROMPT = "❯ "` and `b.live`'s default `loading…` are constants concatenated into a
frame with nothing between them and the terminal. C09 §4 argues at length that a glyph is a
slot and never a character, *because* a block-supplied one was emitted verbatim and broke under
`LANG=C`. These are L4 text, outside that vocabulary, and they break the same way.

**Consumer count is the ranking here and it undercounts this one**: there is exactly one
prompt, it is on every frame the shell ever draws, it is on the line the reader types into, and
no application can replace it. It is low on the list because it wants the same ruling C09 §4
already made — folding it in is cheaper than making a second one.

**Keyed here on 2026-09-04, moved from the F142 cohort table under §14 where they had been counted against the wrong group** — the section keyed no id at all (`**F55.**` carried its full stop inside the bold, which SP6's reader does not match), so its ranking row said 6 over a section the reader saw as empty, and group 14 read 30 against 24.

| | why this group |
|---|---|
| **F55** | the framework draws two characters it does not substitute — this group's subject |
| **F152** | the framework emits a sentence about the wrong subject — the far side failed and the notice names the app author's adapter · **Closed** (F996) — C07 I23, and **F152's own remedy was wrong**: splitting on `status === "error"` still blames the adapter for an ok-with-empty-stdout and suppresses a real adapter defect on error-with-payload. The axis is an empty `stdoutRaw` |
| **F153** | the framework emits a sentence about the wrong fault — an absent required field reported as a value of the wrong type · **Closed** (F995) — C04 I114, and the class is 42 required keys rather than the three instances: reading for the shared helper found 47 sites and driving the corpus found 12 more |
| **F215** | the framework emits a sentence about a vocabulary one fork out of date — a rejected theme preference is *not dark or light* on a set that holds three, and no test asserts the string · **Closed** (F991) — commit `cb835781` — C22 I40 and T1.20e |

## 14 · A constant, a channel or a rule that outlived its configuration — **closed** · 37 closed · 2 with no verdict · new at F492

**F992** — **the bar's value labels had no collision guard, and neither did the sibling cited as having one.** The premise check paid twice: the writer is not `bar.ts`, whose `valueBar` is C11's table cell and cannot compose `4.17.4`; and `columnLabels`' `start >= cells(row)` **forbids an overlap and permits exact adjacency**, so `mon tue wed thu` at eighteen cells drew `montuewedthu`. One rule, two writers, neither had it. C12 I120 and §6p, ruled with **a classification table and a trace** — and the trace found the cell the table cannot: *a refusal reserves nothing*, so a run of contending labels degrades to alternate survivors rather than to its first alone, and `5.25.15.6` becomes `5.2   5.6`. The guard could not live where the finding said, because the composer sees no neighbour and cannot tell a digit from a ramp glyph by reading its own output: the builder asks and the composer grants, one claimer per composed row. `4.17.4` reproduced literally at 81 configurations before and is gone after, with `4.1` surviving whole. **Closed**

**F993** — **the SVG arm had no abut rule, and the remedy the entry named would have moved the range.** The mechanism holds at HEAD and the figures do not — 11 labels at 27.52 px, not 19 at 15.29. And a per-arm ordinate budget **relocates every sample**: six budgets over `{−12.4, 7.9}` give five different niced ranges, where the abscissa can afford one only because `positionAxisAt` nices *inside* the domain. So the budget stays shared and the SVG gains the abut rule it never had (C12 I121). 0/2/3/3/4 overprinting pairs at five widths before, 0 at all five after. **Two instrument corrections are recorded beside it**: a first sweep reported 30 pairs at 0.00 px because facets are nested `<svg x=…>` and a flat reader collapses them. **Closed**

**The mechanism, in one sentence: a decision that was correct for every configuration that
existed, kept after another one arrived.** Not a mistake at the time and not a mistake anyone
made later — the thing it depended on moved, and nothing watches a dependency that was never
written as one. Every member reads as deliberate, which is why none was found by review.

**F729** — the SVG arm's tick count is `ticksFor(plotAreaRows(block))` — derived from the block's declared **row** height — while the canvas height is whatever `svgLayout` was given, and the corpus only ever renders at 320. A rule configured for cells, running against a fixed pixel canvas. At `height: 40` that is **19 right-hand labels at 15.29 px pitch with a 12 px font — 3.29 px of leading** — and it saturates at 19, so nothing overprints and **the ceiling is the only reason**. Take the same block to a shorter canvas and the value labels overprint **each other**: 11.47 px at `svgLayout(640, 240)`, 9.55 at 200, 7.64 at 160, 5.73 at 120. **`RC1` cannot see it** — it indexes callout against label, so label-against-label is outside its shape however many frames it sweeps — which is F725's class with both writers being the axis emitter. Owed a row and a ruling: a width crossing the seam in the direction §3aj hazard 3 does not name. Open · **Closed** (F993) — C12 I121, and **the remedy the entry named is wrong**: a per-arm ordinate budget moves the niced range, six budgets giving five ranges, so it would relocate every sample rather than thin the labels. 0/2/3/3/4 overprinting pairs before, 0 at all five after

**F704** — `SVG_EM = 0.6` was in the file documented as *this arm's own estimate*, and the request proposed building the reserve on it. Measured from `hmtx ÷ head.unitsPerEm` on the shipped `.ttf` files and confirmed by rendering 20 glyphs at size 120 through the same `sharp` the goldens go through: **0.6001** Courier New, Andale Mono, Monaco; **0.6021** DejaVu Sans Mono (the container's `fc-match monospace`) and Menlo; **0.6182** SF Mono. **Every measured face is above 0.6, in the one direction that clips.** The arm emits `font-family="monospace"` — a generic family, so the face is the renderer's choice and the metric is **unknowable at emit time** — and the constant is used only for reserving room and for deciding how many characters fit in room, both of which want an **upper bound**. `SVG_EM_MAX = 0.65` is 5% above the widest measured, costs 4.6 px of blank on the corpus's widest string, and **states its own falsification: a face above 0.65**. F703 is the 13 strings it was clipping and F705 the head it was cutting sub-pixel · **Closed** (F991) — `SVG_EM_MAX` measured over six faces, C12 I113

**F652** — `categorical.carries === "decoration"` on every shipped theme, so C10 I15 exempts its eight slots from contrast validation and lint-restricts them to *declared art* — and `svg.ts:1158` paints a line's end-of-series callout in `categorical.cN` as a `<text>` fill on the page, under F382's ruling that both arms agree with the legend. So eight slots are checked against **nothing, on any surface**, while carrying the only text that names a series. §4's own conditional one axis over: a palette exempted because it carries no meaning, and a surface that paints it as meaning. The figures are comfortable — worst 6.26 dark, 5.17 light, 7.55 high-contrast — and T2.27 is now the only row that asks, because it reads fills off the document rather than palettes off the theme · **Closed** (F991) — commit `21218402` — C10 §4g and I35 bind `categorical` to both text surfaces

**F661** — SS39 — *a block names a glyph slot* — carries the fourteen `Glyph` token names as a **literal alternation**, so adding `quote` and `nested` to the union, to `GLYPH_TABLE` and to `GLYPH_MEMBERS` (both `satisfies Record<Glyph, true>`, so the compiler holds two of the three copies) made the rule report the new tokens as defects. The vocabulary is declared in three places of which two are checked and one is a string; both copies read as correct and the one that goes stale is the one nothing asserts against. Extended by hand with a note; the class fix is to derive the list from `GLYPH_TOKENS`, which the scan table's shape — a flat array of regexes with no import of `src/` — does not allow today. Filed as a request. Open · **Closed** (F991) — **Ruled** — SS39 keeps its literal list because the failure direction differs, recorded at `validate.ts`

**F632** — `contrast.ts` excludes `surface.bgDeep` from `textSurfaces` under a comment reading *`bgDeep` is excluded because it carries no text; if a surface ever paints text on it, that surface is wrong or the exclusion is* — and `plotToSvg` fills the whole page with `GROUND: ColourRef = "surface.bgDeep"` and then writes every axis label, node label, tile label and the reversed notice onto it. `tone.muted` on `bgDeep` is **3.02** dark and **2.44** light, the light one under `muted`'s own 2.5 floor, and C10's validation reports neither because the pairing it checks is `bg`/`bgElev`. The exclusion outlived the arm that arrived under it; C12 I112 clears 4.5 on both variants so nothing is unreadable today. Owed to C10. Open · **Closed** (F991) — commit `0af3c0b0` — the page moved off `bgDeep` and the comment records it

**F610** — `b.plot`'s I69 guard read `drawn !== "graph"` — correct while `graph` was the member's only reader, and kept when C04 I92 gave `sankey` the same member *at both gates*. The validator admitted `{ form: "sankey", graph }` and the builder threw; T2.38 asks one gate. A string comparison against the first consumer's name is the configuration this guard outlived. **Fixed**

**F576** — eight spinner sets were refused on `EA=Ambiguous` before roadmap 51 made ambiguous width a capability and gave `SPINNER_SETS` a `narrowOnly` tier; only `growVertical` had crossed. Every frame of all eight measured 1 narrow / 2 wide, and three rows had named a subset of frames as ambiguous where every frame is. Seven sets plus `pulse` added; T2.70–T2.75 green, mutation kills T2.70. **Fixed**. (`typescript-eslint`'s peer range, F575, is the same shape — a refusal that outlived the compiler it was written against — and is keyed in group 10 with its count)

**F521** — `waffleGrid` rounded each segment independently against `pos < 100` — the identity at `scale === 1`, which was the corpus's only waffle for a year. `50/50/1` lost its sliver and `1/1/1` drew a hundredth square the legend did not name, in both arms, from one shared function the table had correctly ruled *shared*. Largest-remainder with the earlier segment winning a tie, C12 I108; 22 baselines. **Fixed**

**F523** — `annotationMarks` opened `if (a.kind !== "line") return []` under a comment reading *this arm does not draw annotations*, false from the day `line` crossed; `band`, `confidence` and `whiskers` never reached the SVG, and the sweep blamed `fill` for a mark that was not there. C12 I109; six baselines. **Fixed**

**F525** — the violin's IQR box sits on `layer: "annotation"` for its width, not its meaning — the layer is what escapes `SLOT_SHARE` — and painting claims translucent behind the data made it vanish into its density. Told apart by `seriesIndex`, which no claim carries; nineteen baselines. **Fixed**, with the honest fix — a rect that declines the inset without borrowing a layer — owed

**F529** — `yScale: "symlog"` and `"time"` reached their tick algorithms for the first time and moved no sample — a scale that selects ticks without placing samples is a scale in name. Ruled by the follow-through as C04 I81: the range carries the scale and both arms place through it. **Fixed**

**F506** — F498 merged the two sample grids into one and removed `sampleGrid`'s rung parameter; **`80 × 48` went on being the recorded grid in five places afterwards** — the comment heading the function whose parameter went away, C12 §3al's table, I84, commitment 84, the `PR7`/`CAM4` rows, and every performance budget in the 3D design note. It returns `160 × 192`: **30,720 samples against the recorded 3,840, eight times**, and a 120 KB depth buffer recorded as 15. **The implementation and its test were both corrected and none of the prose was** — `PR7`'s body asserts the new rule and explains why, while the spec entry naming that row still says its control is *that the two arms disagree*, a control that cannot be constructed because there is one arm. **`PR7` and `CAM4` are the same row under two ids** and `CAM4` is in no test file, which is where the correction stopped: the duplicate is not merely redundant, it is the copy that stays wrong. This is F498's own ruling — *when the thing a derived constant compensates for goes away, the compensation is the defect* — pointed at the documents, by the finding that named the mechanism and did not sweep for it · **Closed** (F991) — the two grids are one and the rung parameter is gone

**F504** — a catalogue spreads a rung's override last so a rung can *replace* a field rather than only add to one — correct for sixteen members and wrong for the seventeenth, because `camera` is the one override that is an **object whose fields are independent statements** (C04 I75 made it `Partial<Camera>` for that reason). A rung naming an elevation and a distance discards the azimuth the animation's phase lives in, so four of seventeen rungs draw stills under `/live` and thirteen orbit. **The type cannot say so** — `Partial<Camera>` spread over `Partial<Camera>` typechecks — and it **reads as deliberate on three of the four**, whose fixed azimuths carry comments recording the measurement that chose them. The fourth is what gives it away: it sets no azimuth at all and still does not turn · **Closed** (F991) — the camera is the block's and the spread preserves it, with a doc row

**F492** — a per-cell colour split, individually optimal in every cell, alternates on noise where a gradient puts two masks at nearly equal error, and the frame reads as vertical seams no cell contains. A raster is read as a **field** and no per-cell rule can see one; the remedy is a decision that cannot alternate rather than a better score · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104 and I105 with the sites in `scatter3.ts` and `surface3.ts`

**F493** — a sample exactly on two triangles' shared edge has a zero weight in both, and floating point decides which gets `-1e-13` and drops it. Latent for the life of the fill, invisible at four sample rows a cell, and cross-hatching the surface at eight — **a numerical fault is found by the change that raises resolution, not by the one that introduces it**. The tolerance has to be proportional to the triangle's own area, since an absolute epsilon is wrong at both ends of a mesh's size range · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F494** — half-cell triangles `◢◣◤◥` chosen per cell by a score against the cell's own coverage **serrate**, because neighbouring cells disagree about the angle and a silhouette is read as a run. And the alphabet was wrong anyway: a cell is twice as tall as it is wide, so the hypotenuse is one row per column where the offending edges are three times shallower. Removed, with the reasoning kept, because the next reader will have the idea · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F495** — an aspect correction reading *a sample is square on the sub-cell arms*: true at 2×4, a lie at 2×8. The whole figure squashed, and **both the surface and the box squashed with it** because they are drawn from one basis — but they round differently, so the visible symptom was *the surface does not reach the braille bounds*. A disagreement between two things drawn from one basis cannot be a rasterisation artefact, which points at the basis in one step · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F496** — a classifier taking the nine-level ladder only where two sample columns agreed drew **three** eighths on a Gaussian and sent every other edge cell to a half-cell staircase. Making the ladder the default lifted it to 19 and **the picture barely changed**, which is the finding: a block element splits a cell with one horizontal line, so a run moves the boundary once per cell and a shallow silhouette is a staircase whatever the level count. **The ceiling is arithmetic, not tuning** — four iterations went into the quantisation before it was stated · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F497** — the reference frame's samples are kept out of the dot grid so the axis is not drawn twice, but its glyph is only drawn in a cell with **no** data dots, so the exclusion buys nothing and costs the data. A z axis in front of a dome took a black stripe from peak to base, every cell of it a correct `⢸` — cells lit, colours right, extent unchanged, and **no count-based assertion sees it**. Two wrong repairs first, the second **circular**: it judged the data's footprint using the frame's own samples · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F498** — a marker tier table written as *doubled in each axis* to compensate for two sampling grids, kept after the two became one. **When the thing a derived constant compensates for goes away, the compensation is the defect.** Beside it, points and surfaces sharing one `kind`, so a mark quantised to eighths changed size with where in the cell it fell; and a compose writing sample row `y` into dot row `y`, an identity while the two agreed and a doubling once they did not · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F499** — one dot grid asked *is this cell the data's?* and *what does it draw?*. Different questions: the first must exclude the frame's samples and the second must not · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F500** — one array carrying both a surface's density glyph and the frame's stroke, with the local that read it named `framed`. Giving the frame its own structure looked like taking out everything the array held, so the argument was **replaced** rather than joined and a surface on the ASCII rung drew **nothing** — blank rather than degraded. **A channel with two owners wants two names**, or the next person to split it splits it in favour of whichever one the identifier mentions · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F501** — the plot's ground became a *foreground* the day a top-mass cell had to be drawn as its complement with the colours exchanged. A reader taking foregrounds alone sees a figure lit from above that is nothing of the kind, and a **headlight**, whose whole claim is that it has no direction, measured 1.24. `parseLine` had carried `background` all along — the frame was right and the reading was half of it. With it, a fixture that had stopped having its property: 160 faces over 72 cells, so both shading arms saturate and the row asserts the size of the frame · **Closed** (F991) — C12 I102, I104, I105

**F502** — three rows whose premises had been true and were not, and **a ruling falsified by a font**. F204 removed a guessed dot radius and ruled that braille emit a `<text>` *exactly as box drawing does*; `fc-list ":charset=2800"` returns no mono face, so braille alone fell through to a proportional font and every frame since showed that fallback's design. **Right about the defect and wrong about the remedy** — what survives both is geometry that is not a guess · **Closed** (F991) — four rows carry it at HEAD, across three test files

**F792** — `ToolCallSpec.output` stays unconsumed and the equality arm demanded a third exemption go — `UNCONSUMED_FUNCTIONS.toolCallDoc` expired at its first `src/` call · **Closed** (F991) — **Measured** — `toolCallDoc` left the list at its first `src/` call and `ToolCallSpec.output` stayed with the reason

**F798** — *any input disarms `⌃c`* met *1003 reports every move* — a resting hand would have made the double-`⌃c` exit unreachable; `button: "none"` does not disarm (T3.8d) · **Closed** (F991) — C16 §4a row **t**, T3.8d

**F972** — **C06's `durationMs` was a difference of two wall-clock readings.** `Clock = { now, schedule }` dates from before `elapsed` existed, and a duration belongs on the monotonic clock. `Clock.now` becomes `Clock.elapsed`, the subprocess transport's five reads move with it, the root hands `config.elapsed`, and the replay stand-in mirrors the pair on mono — and `tsc` named two consumers the change list did not: `src/data/fixtures/handler.ts`, whose `Clock["now"]` was a wall-time hook and not C06's clock, and a support-harness test. C06 I19, commitment 19, T1.13 added to the spec where it was absent, T6.17 reworded. Closed. · **Closed** (F991) — commit `791a448b` — `Clock = { elapsed, schedule }` and every transport site reads `elapsed()`

**F973** — **the running card and the readouts took their figures from the wall clock.** `deps.clock() − startedAt` on both routes, the approval restart and every `cardOver`; the readouts' and parts' `startedAt`, both `since`, `titleOf`, `lastOk` and the stale age. `deps.elapsed` joins the pipeline's and the refresh driver's deps, wired from `config.elapsed`; the deadlines — `dueAt`, the retry countdown, the stall watch with its whole-minute figure — stay on `deps.clock`, being measured against the timer's axis, and C23 says so. The harnesses gained `mono` and `skew(ms)`; a third harness holding its own deps literal behind `as unknown as` lacked the field silently. C23 I54, commitment 56, T3.64, T4.68, T6.99. Closed. · **Closed** (F991) — commit `791a448b` — the card and the readouts read the injected clock

**F977** — **`mergedRow` indexes a point-label overlay by code point after the cells were joined.** `pointLabelRows` builds a cell array — a cluster in one cell, `""` behind a wide one — and hands it on as `r.join("")`; `mergedRow` reads it as `[...row][x]` and `behind()` walks the merged spans per code point, so a `""` contributes nothing and a family's five code points are five columns: at 40 a family label leaves its row 37 cells with every glyph after it three left, `図表` shifts the tail two right and clamps the frame's edge off the row, `peak` shifts nothing. The wide-glyph half shipped before F976 — TL13's own `図表` row ends in the clamp's `…`, and its `/[│|]$/` filter skips exactly that row — and the cluster half changed sign with it. The fix is the merge's, in `definition.ts`: the overlay crossing as cells, or the walk by cluster as `write` already does; the row is the probe's comparison, a labelled row against the same row unlabelled at every cell right of the slot — C12 T1.135, landed with F981. **Closed** (F981).

**F979** — **the zero-width table was the third hand-written Unicode table in `text.ts`, and a unit row asserted its defect as the rule.** C09 §5 records the disease for `isAmbiguous` and `isWide` and closes each by deriving the table from its source; `isZeroWidth` was sixteen typed ranges, 659 code points, checked against nothing: 1,607 marks and format characters outside it measured a cell each, and two Thai letters `า` `ำ` inside its `0x0e31..0x0e3a` measured none, so `กา` was one cell for two — with 22 unassigned code points, the only under-counts against Ink in the single-code-point sweep. `ZERO_WIDTH_RANGES` is every `Mn`, `Me` and `Cf` of Unicode 17.0 minus U+00AD, 375 ranges, re-derived at test time and compared by equality (T1.38). T1.36 had read *a spacing mark stays with its base* — the finding written as a test's reason, green for exactly as long as the defect was, the T2.28b shape in a unit row. **Closed.**

**F981** — **a layer meets the merge as cells, and the gridline pass is the merge.** `rowCells` in `text.ts` inverts the label writer's join where the width lives — each cluster at its first cell, `""` behind a wide one, a fast set of one-cell-per-unit ranges checked against the tables by C09 T1.40 — and `mergedRow` derives every layer's row once and reads it by cell index where it had spread the whole row per column and indexed by code point; `behind()` is folded in per cell with its two rules kept, so every frame without a wide or multi-code-point label is byte-identical (407 goldens, no snapshot written). The family row at 40 reads 40 where it read 37, `図表`'s keeps the frame's edge; the definition's render at 200 columns 7.70 → 2.91 ms, the width ratio 3.06 → 1.69. TL13 asserts every row now. C12 I119, commitment 119, T1.135–T1.138, T6.98–T6.100; `c12-label-merge.mjs`. F977 closed. **Closed.**

**F982** — **the radar's category labels are a sixth writer, one code point per slot, read by code point on both arms.** Found by F981's check of which layers carry text: `circle.ts`'s `labelRows` writes a category one code point per cell and `radarQuadFigure` reads the row by code point, so on a six-category radar at 60 columns a family label moves `east`, which shares its row, three cells left over the disc's glyphs on both arms, `図表` two right on the braille arm, and the line arm's row runs to the width and clamps. C12 I118 named five writers and the radar is not among them; the fix is the radar's — `write` for the slots and `rowCells` for the read. **Closed** (F984).

**F984** — **the radar's labels through the one writer and its line arm reading cells: F982 closed, both arms the control's row cell for cell.** `labelRows` calls `chargrid.ts`'s `write` and `radarQuadFigure` derives its label row through `rowCells` once per row; on the frame F982 measured, the row a family or `図表` shares with `east` reads 45 cells on both arms with `east` at its column, where it read 42 and 47, and 60 into the clamp. C12 I118's list grows and I119 names the arm; T1.139, T1.140, T6.101, T6.102; `c12-radar-captions.mjs`. **Closed.**

**F985** — **both x-axis caption writers had the radar's shape, and SS61 closes the class.** Found by sweeping every spread in `plot/` for F982's shape: `xAxis`'s `place` and `xTickRow` placed a caption by `cells()` and wrote `[...text]` one code point per cell, one of them under a `cells-ok` mark, so with a family at the left the centre and right captions sat three cells left of ticks that had not moved, and with `図表` two right into the clamp. Both go through `write`; the tick-label arm cannot show it, a formatted number being ASCII, and its restoration is the run's expected survivor. SS61 — a string spread read at any index but SS60's first, in `src/`, no exceptions — fires on the three shipped lines and states the two-statement shape as its blind spot; corpus after the fix, nothing in `src/`. T1.141 (XA10), T6.103, T6.104. **Closed.**

**Keyed here by the F142 inventory sweep** — the second cohort, filed after this document's own count was last derived. Placed by mechanism from the entry; SP6 proves coverage and not placement, which is its stated limit.

| **F378** | **the dither, for any figure with a painted ground** | `plotToSvg` → `b.image` at `imageProtocol: "none"` gives **511 of 784 cells inked** and no findable curve. The SVG paints its own `#141414` — right for a browser — so the PNG's mean luminance is **22 of 255**, and an ordered dither turns a flat 8% ground into a regular 8%-dense texture the figure is then drawn inside. **A pairing rather than a defect in either part**: C09 I36's dither is built for photographs, which have no flat ground, and a rendered figure is nearly all ground. Consumer-side remedy measured — a `linear` map of 20 → 0 takes it to **42 inked cells** and the curve appears. Recorded in `examples/plots`, because normalising a figure's ground is the caller's knowledge about their own picture · **Closed** (F991) — the consumer-side pairing is recorded in the example's own SVG arm |
| **F375** | **a bar's value labels, at a dashboard's density** | `4.17.4` is `4.1` and `7.4`; `3.83.4` is two numbers; `4.34.16.8` is three. A reader does not see a crowded row, they see **one number that is not any of the values**. **The same renderer has the rule one label row away** — `columnLabels` guards the category names with `start >= cells(row)` and drops rather than abut — so this is a missing clause, not an open question, with the drop-or-abut choice already made beside it. Found by looking at the demo at the width its own layout gives a bar: 53 cells, four categories, four series · **Closed** (F992) — C12 I120, and the sibling cited as holding the rule did not: `start >= cells(row)` forbids an overlap and permits exact adjacency, so `mon tue wed thu` in eighteen cells drew `montuewedthu`. One rule, two writers, neither had it |


**F1093** — **a budget written to widen a five-second default became a narrowing when the default went to thirty.** C12 T2.1 sweeps twelve fuzz corpora across every width from one to two hundred through three rasteriser entry points, one corpus being a hundred thousand points, and it carries the only hand-written row timeout in the tree with a comment defending it: *an explicit budget, because 3.2 s against a 5 s default is not a margin … twenty seconds says the seconds are expected*. Every sentence of that is right and the number it produced now does the opposite of what it was for, because `vitest.config.ts` set `testTimeout` to thirty thousand on 2026-08-22 — so the override stopped being a widening of five and became **a third off** the limit the row would otherwise have had, in a file whose comment says the row exists because the default was too small. Measured: the row is **6936 ms** in the devcontainer and **20960 ms** on the runner, against its explicit twenty thousand and a global thirty. **It would have passed on the default it was written to escape** — the failure is the override, not the runner and not the row. **The population is exactly one**: swept over `test/`, every other explicit row timeout is at or above the global, almost all of them in tier five where a real PTY needs its own budget, and four candidates that looked sub-global on a first pass are not row timeouts at all — two arguments to `waitForFrame`, one to `elapsedNeeded`, one an assertion bound. **F967's class, third instance in a day, and the first that is a number rather than prose**: a stale sentence misleads a reader, a stale number fails the build on the one machine that was not available to disagree when it was written. **And the direction is the surprise** — an override reads as *more room*, this one is less, and nothing in the syntax says so; twenty thousand beside a comment about a five-second default reads as generous at every glance. **Open** — `CORPUS_BUDGET_MS`, which is what the row is in `budget.ts`'s own taxonomy, sixty seconds against a runner figure of twenty-one; the number leaves the file so the next regime change moves it once, and the comment keeps its argument and gains the measurement the argument was missing · **Closed** — and the one way this repair could fail silently is the constant not being read, a wrong argument position leaving the row on the global and green, which is indistinguishable from the fix working. Driven rather than assumed: with the constant set to one the row fails with `Test timed out in 1ms`, and `budget.ts` was restored from a copy with its md5 compared. The row runs in two thousand three hundred and nine milliseconds alone and six thousand nine hundred and thirty-six inside the suite, against sixty thousand

**F1054** — **`SankeyCell` is two arms now and the runtime keeper is on the only path to the type.** F717 left the union owed because another file reads three fields off it *and this lane does not own that file* — **both files are the same directory**, so the condition was satisfied by the partition rather than by a commit. The brand's only constructor wraps the assertion, so a later caller hand-building a background-bearing cell gets a compile error where it used to get a green suite and a wrong frame; the brand is on the channel and not the alphabet, because the ASCII repertoire's top glyph is a rule character rather than a fill · **Closed** — **frame read rather than counted**: 77 cells carry a background at five rows and every one is the upper-half block, with a graph whose layers never share a cell as the control at 0, where asserting the property would have been vacuous. Its row is two compile-error directives, so the fabricated violation is `tsc`'s

## What this triage cannot see

**Most of what found things this step is not reachable by sorting a list.** Measured over
docker-tui's step 8, eleven findings, and **not one came from a test written to look for it**:
reading a frame (4), writing a second consumer from the public surface (3), the mutation pass
(2), an untouched file appearing in a diff (1), asking where a settled claim was written (1).

Every instrument that found something is **a way of looking rather than a thing asserted**, and
a triage is a thing asserted. Specifically:

- **Reading a green gate's counters instead of its exit status.** **F82 was found in the act
  of verifying this file**, and it is the newest instrument here: `make enforce` printed
  success through three separate vacuities in one rule, and each time the evidence was a
  number the rule already returned and nobody read. A triage sorts what a gate let through;
  it has no way to ask whether the gate looked.
- **A frame-read.** F62, F67, F74, F75, F79 — five of the last twenty-six. Nothing in a sorted
  list tells you a frame was never read, and F67 is the proof: golden frames sweep 60/80/120/160
  columns and the height axis has no equivalent, so a document rendering to zero visible rows
  produces a frame that is *correct*.
- **Writing a second consumer.** F7, F56, F58, F70. The ranking **is** consumer count, and
  ranking by it cannot produce one. `examples/minimal` is forty lines written from the public
  surface with no house style to copy, and it hit F58's wall on its first compile — which is the
  evidence that a thing is the API's shape rather than a habit.
- **A mutation pass.** It indicts tests, and sometimes prose: a sentence that cannot be violated
  reads exactly like one that is obeyed. A triage reads prose as given, which is A03 §2's
  vacuity class pointed at this document.
- **An untouched file appearing in a diff.** F56's mode change is evidence about a mechanism
  nobody knew was running. No category here would hold it.
- **Going to find where a claim was written** — group 10, and this is the self-referential one.
  **A triage is a fourth document that cites the other three.** The instrument with the highest
  yield in this repository is the one this file is most exposed to, because grouping and ranking
  is exactly the operation that lends a claim authority it has not earned.
- **A finding that was never filed at all.** Not hypothetical. **F81 spent a whole step as a
  comment** at `examples/docker/src/progress.ts:31`, ending *"filed rather than worked around"* — and it was not
  filed. So the strongest group on this list looked like three consumers rather than four, and
  nothing that sorts `FINDINGS.md` could have known. SP5 checks that every citation resolves to
  a finding that exists; **nothing checks whether something that should be a finding is one.**

---

## How this file was checked

**The inventory is derived, and now it is derived by something that runs.** `SP6` in
`tools/enforce/findings.mjs` takes the distinct ids from `FINDINGS.md`, takes the ids keyed in
the group sections here, and **compares the two sets by equality** — so a finding filed and not
keyed fails `make enforce`, and the sum in the ranking table is checked against what the groups
actually hold rather than against itself.

**It was a snapshot until then, and it had gone 55 findings stale.** This paragraph used to
read *"yields 89 ids and every one is keyed in a group above — 10 + 12 + … = 89"*. Measured
when SP6 was written: **145 distinct findings, 55 keyed nowhere**, and the sum still reached
89, so the arithmetic offered as evidence passed exactly as it did on the day it was true. That
is F87's proxy one level out — F87 found a total cannot see a duplicate placed twice and
counted once, and this is the same total unable to see an id never placed at all. F142.

**Every group heading was recomputed from its own rows on 2026-09-10**, with `dispositionOf` —
the reader SP12 uses — rather than by hand, and **eleven of fifteen were out**. Group 11 said 378
entries and holds 393; group 7 claimed *F537, F726 and F934's five open* and all five read closed;
groups 1 and 12 said **closed** over a row that reads open, and group 8 said four open where one
is. That is not new drift with a finding of its own — it is exactly what F991 measured and
prescribed, *a heading is computed over the rows that were annotated, and a row is annotated when
someone happens to look at it*. The prescription is the third column: **no verdict** is carried
separately everywhere now, so an unread row cannot pass as closed by arithmetic. Nothing gates it,
for SP6's reason two paragraphs down — the numbers are recomputed when the file is opened, and the
date above is what a reader checks them against.

**What the column counts, stated because it is not what the header used to say.** `ids keyed`
is *ids appearing in bold inside a group's section*. This document keys in two forms — a table
row's first cell, and bold in prose — and nothing distinguishes a key from a mention: table
cells alone find 78 and leave groups 6 and 13 keying nothing at all. So the number, and SP6,
prove **coverage and not placement**, exactly as SP5 checks a citation's existence and not its
aim. Tightening the key form to table rows only is real work and is not done here; until it is,
a finding bolded in passing inside a group it does not belong to is counted there.

**The groups are not disjoint, and an earlier version of this paragraph claimed they were.**
F30 is keyed in group 4 and again in group 7 — it genuinely is both a verdict the union lacks
and a drawing that asserted one — so group 7 names fifteen ids and counts fourteen, excluding
the one already counted. **The sum reaches 89 either way**, which is the point: a total over
group sizes cannot see a duplicate that was placed twice and counted once. That is F87, and it
is this document's own check failing the rule it was built from — *assert the artefact, not a
proxy*. A disjointness claim wants `sort | uniq -d`, which is one line and was never run.

That check exists because the F55 version had a heading reading *5 surfaces* over a row listing
six. **A count that disagrees with its own row reads as authority and is the cheapest possible
error to make** — which is also F65's finding, one document out. Deriving the list rather than
transcribing it is the pattern for any future inventory here; deriving it does not make it a
partition.

**The residue pass's 55 entries (F512–F566) were keyed in one move each**, on F434's rule: the row goes in the group and the same group's column moves by one, so the column sums to 570 because each of eight groups gained exactly what it keyed — 13, 10, 3, 6, 4, 7, 4 and 8 — and not because errors cancelled. Measured before the merge at 515 = 515 and after at 570 = 570, from SP6's own counters.

**The owed three's 21 entries (F567–F587) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 2 gained one (F586), 7 one (F578), 9 two (F568, F584), 10 four (F573, F574, F575, F577), 11 three (F569, F581, F585), 14 one (F576) and Singles nine — 1 + 1 + 2 + 4 + 3 + 1 + 9 = 21. F575 has group 14's shape as well as group 10's and is **mentioned** in 14 without bold, so it is keyed once and the column moves once; F434's failure mode is exactly the row bolded in both. Measured before at 570 = 570 and after at 591 = 591, from SP6's own counters.

**The seven owed items' 42 entries (F588–F629) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 2 gained one (F624), 7 one (F621), 9 five (F598, F609, F616, F622, F623), 10 three (F601, F619, F620), 11 six (F588, F589, F594, F604, F625, F628), 14 one (F610) and Singles twenty-five — 1 + 1 + 5 + 3 + 6 + 1 + 25 = 42. F619 and F628 are one defect from two sides — the claim and the row that stood for it — and are keyed in 10 and 11 respectively, each mentioned in the other without bold so the column moves once per id. Measured before at 591 = 591 and after at 633 = 633, from SP6's own counters.

**F667 is one more, keyed in Singles** (Singles 75 → 76, the ranking sum 670 → 671): the resolver
holding two records of one status and comparing neither. It is a single because its consumer is the
one instrument, and it is filed after the lanes because the lead found it while confirming a lane's
own roadmap edit had landed — the Order column still said `PART` where the evidence table said
`BUILT`, and `roadmap-status` printed *every claim resolves* on that tree.

**The three lanes' 19 entries (F630–F648) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 7 gained two (F631, F643), 9 four (F633, F638, F639, F646), 10 two (F640, F645), 11 five (F634, F635, F637, F644, F647), 14 one (F632) and Singles five — 2 + 4 + 2 + 5 + 1 + 5 = 19. F637 and F647 are one question from two sides — the blind spot and the check that narrowed it — and are both group 11's, keyed once each with the other mentioned without bold. Lane W's file numbered its own findings F591 and F593–F597, which are ledger numbers already: its F591 is the ledger's F591 (fixed and keyed since the seven owed items) and appears nowhere new, and its F593–F597 are renumbered to F642–F646. F591's body cited *what turned up F593* and now cites F642 — a citation that resolved against a real but unrelated finding, which is the class SP5 states it cannot see. Measured before at 633 = 633 and after at 652 = 652, from SP6's own counters.

The ledger ran to F724, so the assignment from F725 upward in the file's own order is the **identity** and the rewrite is provably a no-op — asserted rather than assumed (`rewrites == 0`), with the out-of-map citations compared **by equality** before and after: measured with `grep -on`, the entry bodies carry `F713` once (F727's heading), `F725` once and `F728` once, all three in F729's body or a heading, and the preamble the parse does not reach carries the rest. The lane's own note is the reason to record it: **a lane that keeps counting from its brief lands on someone else's body, and SP5 checks a citation's existence and not its aim** — its first draft numbered from F714 and collided with six live findings. **Two existing entries were corrected in place, each an asserted replacement with the disproved claim kept visible**: F710's two frames are six and ten pairs (F725), and F713's premise is false outright (F727), so both entries carry a correction paragraph and both rows here carry the correction rather than leaving a falsified claim standing in a second document — F86 and F89's class, which is a claim surviving because only the body was fixed. **Dispositions were checked against the repository where they move**, not against `FINDINGS.md`'s
prose — that being the record whose authority is in question. Scoped to findings whose
disposition changed and to groups 1–4. Findings in the low-ranked groups carry their filed
disposition unverified, and this sentence is that limit stated rather than left implicit.

`make enforce` resolves every `Fnn` cited above, including F81 and F82 — **306 files, 665
citations, 0 violations, and those numbers are the check.** This file is inside that scope
for the first time, at 175 citations of its own; until F82 it was not, so the sentence this
paragraph makes would have been false had it been written yesterday.

Reading SP5's counters rather than its exit status is what found F82: `enforce` was green, as
it has been through all three of that rule's vacuities, and the number beside the green
disagreed with the tree. The double-count that widening introduced was caught the same way —
`415 + 250 = 665` against a reported `781`, a difference of exactly the ledger's 116
self-citations.

**Which is the frame-read applied to an enforcement rule** — the exit status is the
assertion and the counters are the frame — and it belongs in the list below as much as in
group 11.

## What this moves in `docs/ROADMAP.md`

ROADMAP was drawn from the F55 version and its ranking predates F56–F81. **Named entry by
entry, so step 3 acts on it rather than re-deriving it:**

| ROADMAP entry | at F55 | at F81 | what moved it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** the producer-context contract | 6 | **9** | **F77** makes it structural — asking forces a route change. F13 + F58b are one fact from two sides, and F58b's fix is a *narrower* type where ROADMAP's F58 note asked for a wider one |
| **2** three builder fields, F27's shape | 5 (3 closed) | **12** (7 closed) | F7, F17, F70, F71 are the same shape; **F78** is the sharpest open instance and adds the pair-of-throws problem, which is not a field |
| **3** absence vs failure | 8 | **8** (5 closed) | no count change, but **F67** is the first instance where the framework itself says nothing — no crash, no refusal, no exit — and **F61** is the first where the emptiness is a bug rather than a state |
| **4** a change axis distinct from `Tone` | 3 | **4** | **F81**, and a fourth independent consumer is the threshold this list is ranked on. It also supplies the 1-bit answer twice over: `/diff` shipped text, `/build` shipped a word in a column, neither knowing about the other |
| — | — | **absent** | **three groups with no ROADMAP entry**: the instrument (6), a claim carried without a record (5), a time-based assertion under contention (2) |

**Nothing demotes, and that is the finding about the ranking.** All four moved because a fourth
or fifth consumer turned up, not because anything got more severe — the F55 triage's own
mechanism arriving again, exactly as it should.

**The actionable divergence is that group 9 outranks ROADMAP's entry 4 on consumer count and is
not on the list at all.** A whole group about the apparatus every other finding was measured
with — the size is in the ranking table and is derived, not restated here — and its disposition — *every instrument gets a fixture it must reproduce* — is cheaper
than any of the four entries ROADMAP already carries.

ROADMAP is **not** edited here. Step 3 rewrites it from this file.
